[[sep-vplan-area-7]]
==== 7. Key Manager vault

[[sep_km_otbn_sideload_kat_test]]
===== 7.1 `sep_km_otbn_sideload_kat_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense, `+esrc_noise_force`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_otbn_sideload_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: Proves the full key-distribution chain from the real entropy datapath to a real cryptographic consumer: the OpenTitan entropy stack seeds the Key Manager, the Key Manager's firmware accepts a known 384-bit key over its mailbox and sideloads it to OTBN over the dedicated key bus, and an OTBN program running on the real core reconstructs the key from its two shares and writes it to data memory, where the host reads it back and compares against the known value. Nothing on the key's own proof path is forced or read through a backdoor, so an exact data-memory match shows that the key physically traversed the sideload bus and was consumed. Key-bus isolation is proven by construction — only the Key Manager and OTBN are released from reset, with AES, HMAC and KMAC held parked for the whole transfer.

*Test Coverage*: Known-key Key Manager to OTBN sideload; DMEM reconstruct equals the key.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid production eFuse image and run real fuse sense.
. Park OTBN, AES, HMAC and KMAC; bring up the entropy stack with strict golden checking.
. Release the Key Manager and wait for its firmware to report ready over the mailbox.
. Load a known 384-bit key with `CMD_KEY_LOAD`; require a non-zero handle.
. Release OTBN and wait for its post-reset secure wipe to reach idle.
. Issue `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` for that handle with OTBN as destination.
. Load the key-dump program into instruction memory, execute it, poll to idle.
. Read the result and share dump out of data memory and compare.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-A
|`CMD_KEY_LOAD` accepts the known key and returns a non-zero handle.
|CHK-B
|`CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` succeeds with OTBN as destination.
|CHK-ISO
|Only the Key Manager and OTBN are out of reset during the transfer. AES, HMAC and KMAC are parked. The check is a mask over those bits, not an equality on the whole register. Adams Bridge stays released: the Key Manager firmware does not reach ready with that domain held, and the Adams Bridge sideload leaf is the isolation claim for that engine.
|CHK-C
|The OTBN program loads, executes to idle, and reports no error bits.
|CHK-F
|The two raw shares are non-trivial and distinct, neither equals the key, and their exclusive-or reconstructs it — so the masking is real, not degenerate.
|CHK0
|Key Manager firmware boots and reports ready over the mailbox.
|CHK-D/E
|Data memory holds the exact known 384-bit key, and the high result word padding is zero — the known-answer test itself.
|CHK1..CHK3
|The entropy stack matches its golden model bit-exactly at the decorrelator, compress and seed stages, with zero mismatches.
|CHK4
|Generated entropy blocks match the golden model. Generate segmentation is `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`, not this `glen=32` budget.
|CHK5
|The Key Manager and OTBN both take real entropy beats from the shared bus. Alive-mode beat count; bit-exact per-sink compare is the small KM ROM tests (`sep_esrc_e2e_smoke_test`, `sep_drbg_gen_segmentation_test`), not `rom_main`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

'''''

[[sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test]]
===== 7.2 `sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_km_aes_sideload_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: Proves that a known key loaded over the Key Manager mailbox and sideloaded to AES encrypts to the independent golden and decrypts back to the plaintext, and that the software-key path reaches the same golden.

*Test Coverage*: Known-key Key Manager to AES sideload known-answer.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a production fuse image; park OTBN, KMAC and HMAC; leave AES released. The isolation check is a mask over those bits, not an equality on the whole register. Adams Bridge stays released so the Key Manager firmware can reach ready.
. Bring up real entropy; release the Key Manager and wait for firmware ready.
. Load the known 256-bit key for AES; require a non-zero handle.
. Encrypt the plaintext with a decoy software key; check against golden.
. Transfer the key to AES; read back the reset register for the isolation record.
. Read the public key registers and require zero, with a readable register in the same window as a positive control.
. Encrypt in sideload mode; decrypt the result in sideload mode.
. Load the known key through software and encrypt again, proving the engine reaches the same golden independently of the sideload route.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-A
|The key is staged and a non-zero handle returned.
|CHK-B
|The transfer to AES succeeds.
|CHK-ISO
|Only the Key Manager and AES are released during the transfer. OTBN, HMAC and KMAC are parked. The check is a mask over those bits, not an equality on the whole register. Adams Bridge stays released: the Key Manager firmware does not reach ready with that domain held, and the Adams Bridge sideload leaf is the isolation claim for that engine.
|CHK-NEG
|The decoy-key ciphertext matches its own golden, so the comparison baseline is sound.
|CHK-F
|The sideload ciphertext equals the independent golden for the known key.
|CHK-H
|The software-key path reaches the same golden ciphertext, so the engine is correct independently of the sideload route. `CHK-F` proves the delivered key bit-exactly. Guards that only relate two results already pinned to the same golden are not claimed here — they hold with the simulator off.
|CHK-RT
|Decrypting the sideload ciphertext recovers the original plaintext.
|CHK0
|Key Manager firmware boots on real entropy and reports ready over the mailbox.
|CHK1..CHK3
|The entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at the decorrelator, compress and seed stages.
|CHK5
|Both the Key Manager and AES draw real entropy beats. Alive-mode beat count; bit-exact per-sink compare is the small KM ROM tests, not `rom_main`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Observation*: `PUB-OBSERVATION`. The test asserts that the public key registers read zero, but `aes.hjson` declares `KEY_SHARE0`/`KEY_SHARE1` `swaccess = wo`, so a frontdoor read returns zero whether a key was sideloaded, mirrored elsewhere, or never delivered, and that half cannot fail. It also asserts that a STATUS read over the same frontdoor is non-zero, which fails if the read path is dead. Software-unreadability here is a property of the register description, not a runtime behaviour this leaf can demonstrate. The key-protection evidence that *can* fail is CHK-F: the sideload key encrypts to the golden ciphertext.

'''''

[[sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test]]
===== 7.3 `sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_km_hmac_sideload_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: Proves that a known key loaded over the Key Manager mailbox and sideloaded to HMAC produces the independent HMAC-SHA256 golden, and that the digest differs from the golden of the decoy software key.

*Test Coverage*: Known-key Key Manager to HMAC sideload known-answer.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up real entropy; boot the Key Manager; load the known key for HMAC.
. Transfer the key; read back the reset register for the isolation record.
. Read the public key registers and require zero.
. Write a seeded software decoy key to the public key registers. Each decoy word differs from the Key Manager key word at the same index.
. Configure keyed SHA-256; start; feed the message; process; read the digest.
. Require the digest to differ from the decoy golden, then to equal the golden of the Key Manager key under the pinned convention.
. Clear the done event and confirm it reads back clear.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-A
|The key is staged with a non-zero handle.
|CHK-B
|The transfer to HMAC succeeds.
|CHK-ISO
|Only the Key Manager and HMAC are released during the transfer. AES, KMAC and OTBN are parked. The check is a mask over those bits, not an equality on the whole register. Adams Bridge stays released: the Key Manager firmware does not reach ready with that domain held, and the Adams Bridge sideload leaf is the isolation claim for that engine.
|CHK-DECOY
|The digest differs from the independent HMAC-SHA256 golden of the software decoy key, under both the software-key and the sideload key byte conventions. The decoy is in the public key registers while the sideloaded key is valid, so the check fails if the engine uses the register key in place of the sideloaded key.
|CHK-MAC
|The digest equals the independent HMAC-SHA256 golden for the known key.
|CHK-RW1C
|The done event clears on write-one-to-clear.
|CHK0
|Key Manager firmware boots on real entropy and reports ready over the mailbox.
|CHK1..CHK3
|The entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at the decorrelator, compress and seed stages.
|CHK5
|The Key Manager draws real entropy beats from the shared bus while the key is staged: the `CHK5_km` beat count after the `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` response is higher than the count taken just before `CMD_KEY_LOAD`. Alive-mode beat count; bit-exact per-sink compare is the small KM ROM tests, not `rom_main`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Observation*: `PUB-OBSERVATION`. The test asserts that the public key registers read zero, but `hmac.hjson` declares `KEY` `swaccess = wo`, so a frontdoor read returns zero whether a key was sideloaded, mirrored elsewhere, or never delivered, and that half cannot fail. It also asserts that a STATUS read over the same frontdoor is non-zero, which fails if the read path is dead. Software-unreadability here is a property of the register description, not a runtime behaviour this leaf can demonstrate. The key-protection evidence that *can* fail is CHK-MAC: the sideload key produces the golden digest.

'''''

[[sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test]]
===== 7.4 `sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test`

*Phase*: 1

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse sense

*Source Module*: `sep_km_kmac_sideload_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Objective*: Proves that a known key loaded over the Key Manager mailbox and sideloaded to KMAC produces the software-key digest, that this digest equals the independent KMAC256 golden, and that it differs from the decoy-key digest.

*Test Coverage*: Known-key Key Manager to KMAC sideload known-answer.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Real-sense a production image; park the engines; bring up real entropy.
. Boot the Key Manager; load the known key for KMAC.
. Release KMAC and run a decoy-key operation for the reference digest.
. Read back the reset register; transfer the key.
. Read the public key registers and require zero.
. Run the operation in sideload mode, then again with the known key in software.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-A
|The key is staged with a non-zero handle.
|CHK-B
|The transfer to KMAC succeeds.
|CHK-NEG
|The decoy-key digest is not all zero, so it is a real observation and CHK-SIDE does not compare against garbage. No KMAC golden is applied to this digest; it is an alive check, not a value check.
|CHK-ISO
|Only the Key Manager and KMAC are released during the transfer. AES, HMAC and OTBN are parked. The check is a mask over those bits, not an equality on the whole register. Adams Bridge stays released: the Key Manager firmware does not reach ready with that domain held, and the Adams Bridge sideload leaf is the isolation claim for that engine.
|CHK-SIDE
|The sideload digest differs from the decoy-key digest.
|CHK-MAC
|The sideload digest equals the software-key digest for the same known key.
|CHK-MAC-GOLDEN
|The software-key digest equals KMAC256(K, X, L=256, S="") from `env/sep_kmac_golden.py` (NIST SP 800-185). The sequence programs `PREFIX` as `encode_string("KMAC") \|\| encode_string("")` and ends the message with the 3-byte `right_encode(256)`, so a wrong encoding fails here and not only in a comparison of the engine with itself.
|CHK-ENT
|The engine draws real masking entropy. Alive-mode beat count. Bit-exact per-sink compare is the small KM ROM tests, not `rom_main`. The scoreboard reports it as `CHK5_km` / `CHK5_kmac`.
|CHK0
|Key Manager firmware boots on real entropy and reports ready over the mailbox.
|CHK1..CHK3
|The entropy stack matches its golden bit-exactly at the decorrelator, compress and seed stages.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Observation*: `PUB-OBSERVATION`. The test asserts that the public key registers read zero, but `kmac.hjson` declares `KEY_SHARE0`/`KEY_SHARE1` `swaccess = wo`, so a frontdoor read returns zero whether a key was sideloaded, mirrored elsewhere, or never delivered, and that half cannot fail. It also asserts that a STATUS read over the same frontdoor is non-zero, which fails if the read path is dead. Software-unreadability here is a property of the register description, not a runtime behaviour this leaf can demonstrate. The key-protection evidence that *can* fail is CHK-SIDE and CHK-MAC.

*Not claimed*: fail-closed `CFG.sideload=1` START with no valid Key Manager key
(`ErrKeyNotValid`), and a later software-key digest after `CMD.err_processed`
recovery. <<sep-vplan-scope>> lists them.

'''''

[[sep_km_isolate_termination_test]]
===== 7.5 `sep_km_isolate_termination_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_iso.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_isolate_termination_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — one directed path, three directed legs.

*Objective*: What a Key Manager master transaction receives when its own AXI-Lite path to a destination is isolated. The KM CPU reaches the `km_*` paths; the SEP host port reaches only the `host_*` paths. The KM CPU image stores to the same HMAC wrapper key address three times: clean while the path is open, an AXI error while `km_hmac` is isolated, clean again after release.

*Test Coverage*: `km_hmac` isolate termination, and that isolation does not wedge the KM path.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Dedicated `km_rom_iso` on the KM CPU bus, not `rom_main`. The image publishes a phase marker in KM SRAM word0 and blocks on an inbound mailbox word between legs, so the host controls when each leg runs.
. Each leg W1Cs `IRQ_STATUS` AXI_SLVERR and AXI_DECERR and requires them to read back clear before its store. The bits are sticky, so without that precondition a leg would also be satisfied by an earlier fault anywhere on the KM CPU bus.
. Leg A with HMAC released: the store completes with neither bit set.
. The host parks HMAC by read-modify-write of `SW_RESET_N`, preserving the Key Manager's own release bit, then waits for the `km_hmac` isolate-completion bit before releasing the image into leg B. A leg B that raced the coordinator would store into a still-open path.
. Leg B: the same store raises AXI_SLVERR or AXI_DECERR.
. The host releases HMAC; leg C repeats the store and requires it clean.

[NOTE]
====
Legs A and C cannot fail on their own — "no error" is also what a dead poll loop
reports — and are not offered as evidence by themselves. They are the controls
that make `CHK-KM-ISO-TERM` discriminating: the same instruction at the same
address in the same image, differing only in whether the host holds HMAC in
reset. Without them the error leg would be satisfied by an address that always
errors.
====

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,6",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |What it proves
|CHK-KM-ISO-PRECOND
|Every leg began with both AXI error bits clear, so the bit a leg reports belongs to that leg's store.
|CHK-KM-PATH-LIVE
|With HMAC released, the KM store to the HMAC key aperture completes with no AXI error — the path is open.
|CHK-KM-ISO-TERM
|With `km_hmac` isolated, the same store raises AXI_SLVERR or AXI_DECERR. It neither completes nor hangs.
|CHK-KM-ISO-REOPEN
|After release the same store is clean again, so isolation did not leave the KM path wedged.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Functional Coverage*: `sep_crypto_isolate_cg` in <<sep-fcov-isolate,SEP_FCOV>>. This leaf fills all three bins: it isolates a KM path, presents a KM master transaction to it, and reopens it. The bins are edges rather than states, because both isolate bits hold high for the whole reset window and a state bin would fill even on a run whose reset preceded the drain.

*Not claimed*: the other five KM destination paths (`km_otbn`, `km_aes`,
`km_kmac`, `km_abr`, `km_efuse`). They share the mechanism; only `km_hmac` is
demonstrated. The response code is graded as "an AXI error", not pinned to
SLVERR specifically, because the KM CSR status bit the image polls reports the
two codes as one field.

'''''

[[sep_km_ic_reset_release_test]]
===== 7.6 `sep_km_ic_reset_release_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom.parhex`. VCS only (`tools = ["vcs"]` in `testlists/km.toml`).

*Source Module*: `sep_km_ic_reset_release_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, IC_RESET TDR (`jtag_ic_reset_reg` in `tb_top`), KM CPU image

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE -- one directed walk.

*Objective*: The Key Manager ROM request stays known across an IC_RESET override of the Key Manager. `hw/top/sep_ip_integration.sv` connects that request to the `prim_rom` chip select, and `prim_rom` asserts `noXOnCsI` on it on every clock (`vendor/lowRISC/opentitan/upstream/hw/ip/prim_generic/rtl/prim_rom.sv`). The port is `km_jtag_rst_n`, the TDO-end port of the SEP slice (`doc/integrator/src/smu.adoc`, SEP slice). The walk follows the IC_RESET field table in `hw/ip/jtag/jtag_ptap/doc/architecture.adoc`: stage `reset_control=0` with `reset_enable=1`, apply `reset_enable=0`, then release control and disable the override in separate Update-DR operations.

*Test Coverage*: stage, apply and release of the `km_jtag_rst_n` override while the KM fetches from its ROM; the request through a window that starts at the first fetch after the release.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring-up with the KM held in software reset. TRST the TDR, select it, and require the shifted-out word to be the TDR reset value.
. Release the KM through `SW_RESET_N`. The smoke image stores its SRAM marker and spins, so every loop turn is a ROM fetch.
. Stage and apply the override, one Update-DR each. Release `reset_control` while the override remains enabled, then disable the override in a second Update-DR. The host samples the KM ROM request counter after each operation.
. After the release, count the cycles to the first ROM request, then run a 4000-cycle fetch window.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,6",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |What it proves
|CHK-TDR-SELECT
|After TRST the TDR shifts out its reset value (every `reset_enable` and `reset_control` at 1). That the TDR word reaches the Key Manager reset is shown by CHK-TDR-APPLY.
|CHK-KM-LIVE
|The KM left software reset: the ROM request count rises and KM SRAM word0 holds the smoke marker.
|CHK-TDR-STAGE
|`reset_control=0` with `reset_enable=1` does not stop the KM: the count still rises.
|CHK-TDR-APPLY
|`reset_enable=0`, `reset_control=0` holds the KM: the count does not move over 64 cycles.
|CHK-TDR-RELEASE
|Releasing `reset_control` while the override remains enabled restarts the KM: a ROM request comes within 64 cycles of that Update-DR. Returning `reset_enable` to 1 in a separate Update-DR does not stop the requests. The log gives the latency and both counter values.
|CHK-KM-ROM-WINDOW
|At least 64 ROM requests in the 4000-cycle window after the release. `noXOnCsI` grades the request on every cycle of it; a firing prints `Error:` and the run fails.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes, and the VCS log has no `noXOnCsI` failure.

*Anti-vacuity*: `noXOnCsI` is compiled into the VCS `lsu_stub_all_live` model: `prim_assert.sv` defines `OCAH_OT_INC_ASSERT` for a flow that defines neither `VERILATOR` nor `SYNTHESIS`, and the SEP VCS build defines neither. The cocotb checkers cannot see X; they only prove the override reached the KM and that the KM fetches through the graded window.

*Not claimed*: any four-state property on Verilator, which is two-state and compiles no RTL assertions (see Known Limitations, "RTL assertions are backend-dependent"). The other seven SEP-slice ports. The KM ROM image is always loaded on this bench; a request that is X because the ROM array holds X is a bench condition and is not graded here.

'''''

[[sep_km_key_policy_vault_test]]
===== 7.7 `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_vault.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — slot, SRAM lock region (1..31) and the seal/free slot pair from the run seed, logged on the `km vault:` line; extent endpoints 0 and 63 and the lock/W1C sequence are directed.

*Objective*: The key/policy vault refuses what it should: a store to a locked SRAM region is dropped with the readback unchanged, the matching `SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` bit sets, a store past the KPV register map raises `IRQ_STATUS` AXI_SLVERR only, and the status bit clears W1C. The seal walk is here too: the same erase must retire a sealed slot and free an unsealed one.

*Test Coverage*: Vault lock, seal, retire, erase, and SRAM write-lock on the KM CPU bus.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up in `no_cpu`, release the KM CPU on the dedicated `km_rom_vault` image, and post the seeded config word (slot, SRAM region, seal slot, free slot) through the mailbox.
. The ROM stores key data to the seed slot before any lock and requires AXI SLVERR to stay clear. It sets `CTRL.lock_write` on slot 0, the seed slot and slot 63 (CHK-SLOT).
. The ROM repeats the same key-data store to the write-locked seed slot and requires AXI SLVERR (CHK-LOCKWR).
. The ROM reads the seed slot while it holds only `lock_write` and requires the stored word with AXI SLVERR clear. It sets `lock_use`, requires the same read to raise AXI SLVERR and return zero, and stores that data in KM SRAM word 1 (CHK-LOCKUSE).
. The ROM stores to `0x13108` and requires AXI SLVERR with AXI DECERR clear (CHK-EXTENT).
. The ROM write-locks the seeded SRAM region and stores to it. It requires the write dropped and `SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` plus `IRQ_STATUS.SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_ERR` set, then W1C-clears both to 0 (CHK-DROP, CHK-VIOL, CHK-IRQ, CHK-W1C).
. The ROM seals the seal slot with one `CTRL` write, then erases it twice and requires the slot retired after each erase (CHK-SEAL, CHK-RETIRE, CHK-RETSTICK).
. The ROM preloads two key words in the free slot, marks it with `lock_write`, and erases it. It requires `CTRL` all clear and both words changed and different from each other (CHK-FREE, CHK-ERASEDATA).
. The ROM sets `IRQ_SET.DRBG_ERR_SET`, requires `IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR`, then W1C-clears it (CHK-IRQSET).
. The host polls KM SRAM word 0 for the result magic and decodes each flag. It requires the echoed config fold to match (CHK-RANDCFG) and KM SRAM word 1 to read 0 through `km_sram_probe_o` (CHK-LOCKUSE).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SLOT
|`CTRL.lock_write` sticks on slots 0, the seed slot, and 63.
|CHK-LOCKWR
|A key-data write to a write-locked slot raises AXI SLVERR. This is the LOCK path, not the decode path CHK-EXTENT grades: a legal key-entry address on a locked slot. The sticky error bits are cleared and confirmed clean first, so an earlier fault cannot satisfy it. A control store comes first, to the same address before `lock_write` is set: with AXI_SLVERR cleared and confirmed clean, the store must land and AXI_SLVERR must stay clear for the same poll bound. Without the control, a design that answered SLVERR on every key-entry store would pass.
|CHK-LOCKUSE
|A key-data read of a `lock_use` slot raises SLVERR and returns zero data. Both halves are required: the refusal alone would not show the data path is also forced. The zero is graded twice so that X data cannot pass: the ROM takes its pass branch only on a known zero (the 4-state PicoRV32 does not take a branch on an X condition), and the ROM stores the read data in KM SRAM word 1, which the host reads through `km_sram_probe_o` with no unknown bits allowed and requires to be 0. A control read comes first, on the same slot while `CTRL` holds only `lock_write`: it must return the stored `0xA11CE000` with AXI_SLVERR clear. `lock_write` is write-1-set, so it stays set under the `lock_use` read, and without the control a design that refused reads on `lock_write` would pass. The control value also shows the refused CHK-LOCKWR write did not land.
|CHK-EXTENT
|Store to `0x13108` sets `IRQ_STATUS` AXI_SLVERR and leaves AXI_DECERR clear. `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/key_manager.rdl` (memory-map summary): an unmapped offset inside a window answers SLVERR.
|CHK-DROP
|Write to the locked SRAM region is dropped (readback unchanged).
|CHK-VIOL
|`SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION` reads EXACTLY the locked region's bit. Compared whole rather than masked, so a decoder that set every region's bit fails.
|CHK-IRQ
|`IRQ_STATUS.SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_ERR` is set and no ROM/SRAM parity or ROM-write error is raised alongside it.
|CHK-W1C
|Violation and IRQ bits read back 0 after W1C.
|CHK-SEAL
|One `CTRL` write on the seed's seal slot sets `seal` and raises `lock_write` with it, and `lock_use` and `erase` read 0. So the `lock_use` that CHK-RETIRE requires is set by the erase, not by the seal.
|CHK-RETIRE
|Erasing that sealed slot leaves it retired: `lock_write` held and `lock_use` gained.
|CHK-RETSTICK
|A SECOND erase of the retired slot leaves it retired. Retirement is stated to hold until warm reset, so it is graded as a sticky property rather than a one-shot transition.
|CHK-FREE
|An unsealed slot is marked with `lock_write`, and the erase clears `CTRL` entirely, so the slot is reusable. The mark matters: the reset value is already clear, so without it a no-op erase would pass. The contrast is the point: the same erase, one sealed and one not.
|CHK-ERASEDATA
|The erase overwrites the stored key words, not just the CTRL bookkeeping. Two words are preloaded before the slot is marked; after the erase both differ from the preload AND from each other, so a fill that never ran or that is stuck at one value fails.
|CHK-IRQSET
|`IRQ_SET.DRBG_ERR_SET` raises `IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR`, and W1C clears it. This grades the software-inject path and the status plumbing ONLY -- it is not coverage of the DRBG fault source, and must not be recorded as such.
|CHK-RANDCFG
|The ROM echoes an eight-bit fold of the WHOLE config word, so all four seeded operands are proven received. Echoing only the slot would let a ROM that ignored the region or either seal-walk slot produce an identical result word.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: The test runs a dedicated `km_rom_vault` image on the KM CPU bus, not `rom_main`. The seal walk lives here rather than on the command set because no mailbox command seals a slot: over that surface an erase always frees, so the retire path has no vehicle there. PicoRV32 is RV32E (`x0`–`x15` only). The store at `0x13108` is past `KM_KPV_SIZE` and still inside the 8 KB KPV window. `km_csr.rdl` `SRAM_LOCK` defines 32 regions. The seeded region is never region 0, which holds the result words: `SRAM_LOCK` is write-1-set until warm reset, so a lock there would drop the final result store.

*Precondition*: the vault image must publish its result magic in KM SRAM word 0 within the cycle budget, and the graded word is re-read with no unknown bits. Every checker decodes that word, so a KM CPU that never ran stops the test with `CHK-LIVE FAIL` before any checker is graded. There is no `CHK-LIVE` PASS record.

*Aperture*: the vault is on the KM CPU bus, so the ROM evaluates these
contracts and the host reads one summary word from KM SRAM. The host observes a
result word, not the individual registers. What keeps it from being a firmware
self-report is the seeded configuration -- the slot, region and slot pair are
chosen by the host, and the result word carries a fold of the whole config word
the ROM received, so a firmware that reported success unconditionally would
still have to have consumed every one of the host's operands. Every pass/fail
decision in the ROM reaches its flag only through a taken branch on a known
compare result; the fall-through goes to the fail label. The 4-state PicoRV32
does not take a branch on an X condition, so an X register readback or an X
status bit leaves its flag clear and the named checker FAILs.

'''''

[[sep_km_command_set_rand_test]]
===== 7.8 `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. Generate / transfer / revoke-or-shred /
illegal-to-error walk every seed, with both generated key sizes (8 and 12
words). The extra transfer dest (one of HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN), the
out-of-policy destination (outside `AES | xfer_dest`), and the undefined
command IDs come from the run seed. The
undefined IDs always include the five IDs immediately outside the defined
runs. One seed writes one classic dest cell plus the directed ABR cell; the
entry does not claim all four classic dests in one invocation.

*Objective*: the KM mailbox command set is this vehicle's surface — generate,
transfer, revoke-or-shred, illegal-to-error, version queries, OTP cold lock,
recoverable-fault ACK, and a directed ABR seed transfer. The sideload KATs prove
only that a consumer can consume a key; none of them exercises the command
interface that produces, moves and destroys one.

*Test Coverage*: Generate, transfer, revoke-or-shred, version, OTP lock, recoverable ACK, ABR dest, and illegal-to-error on the KM mailbox.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image and sense it for real: the KM firmware reads OTP
at boot. Park OTBN, KMAC and HMAC, leave AES released as the consume witness, so
a transferred key can only land where the test says it lands.
. Bring entropy up strict, then release the KM and wait for `RESP_KM_READY`.
. Query `CMD_HW_VER`, `CMD_ROM_VER` and `CMD_SRAM_VER`.
. Generate an 8-word key and a 12-word key for AES; check the handles and the
echoed argument fields.
. Load a known key with `DEST_VALID = AES | xfer_dest`, transfer it to AES, and
encrypt: the ciphertext is compared against an independent AES-256-ECB golden.
. Transfer the same handle to the seed-selected dest (`xfer_dest`) in a
dedicated mailbox beat. When that dest is not AES, release that engine for
the key-bus write (OTBN waits idle after release), then park it again.
Consume of HMAC / KMAC / OTBN stays on the sideload KATs.
. Load an 8-word seed and transfer it to dest ABR ML-DSA seed
(`0x10`). Every seed walks this cell. Consume of that seed is
`sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`.
. Transfer the same handle to an engine outside its permitted set.
. Shred the AES key, then load and transfer a second, distinct known key and
encrypt again.
. Transfer the generated handle to AES, revoke it, then transfer on it again.
. Revoke the reserved null handle; walk seeded undefined command IDs; send a
defined command with the wrong payload length. Probe with `CMD_STAT` after each
refusal, and once after the undefined-ID walk.
. Lock one identity OTP bit with `CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD`; refuse a reserved bit
and a zero-length payload.
. Pulse SEP mailbox `CTRL.FLUSH`, receive `RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT`, ACK it, and
prove `CMD_STAT` clears the latched bit. The following transfer and shred are
the key commands that must be accepted after the ACK.
. Transfer the second known key to AES again and encrypt one block, then shred
the AES key and start the same block: the engine must produce no output.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`. This is the bench build with the boot and unrecoverable-fault SRAM wipes compiled out; see Known Limitations, "KM application ROM wipe paths".
|CHK-HW-VER
|`CMD_HW_VER` returns success and `RETURN_ARG` equals the KMCSR VERSION word 1.0.0.
|CHK-ROM-VER
|`CMD_ROM_VER` returns success and the packed ROM 1.1.0 word: patch `[7:0]`, minor `[15:8]`, major `[23:16]`, `[31:24]` zero, per the return-argument layout in `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc` (`km-cmd-rom-ver`). The KM firmware spec does not state a release number, so 1.1.0 is the DV-owned expected value. The test holds it as a constant and does not read it from the KM firmware headers, because the ROM builds its reply from those headers.
|CHK-SRAM-VER
|`CMD_SRAM_VER` returns the failure code: this image is ROM-only.
|CHK-GEN
|`CMD_KEY_GENERATE` returns success and a non-null handle, and its return argument echoes the requested size and destination mask.
|CHK-SAMPLER
|`CMD_KEY_GENERATE` raises the observed EDN->KM beat count on the DRBG scoreboard `CHK5_km` stream. The KMCSR sampler counters are not SEP-reachable.
|CHK-UNIQ
|A second generate, of the other key size, returns a different handle and echoes its size.
|CHK-XFER
|`CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` of a known loaded key succeeds and the AES ciphertext equals the independent golden.
|CHK-XFER-DEST
|A dedicated `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` of that same handle to the seed-selected dest returns success and echoes the dest. Consume of HMAC / KMAC / OTBN is not claimed here.
|CHK-ABR-DEST
|A directed `CMD_KEY_LOAD` + `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` of an 8-word seed to dest ABR ML-DSA seed returns success and echoes that dest. Every seed walks this cell. Consume is not claimed here.
|CHK-DEST
|A transfer to an engine outside the key's permitted set is refused with the failure code, not an argument-validation code. The refusal is attributable: the target engine is released from reset and waited to idle for that beat, so being parked is not the cause, and an unconditional control beat transfers the same handle to a destination inside its permitted set and requires success, so an absent handle -- which carries the same failure code -- is not the cause either.
|CHK-SHRED
|`CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` succeeds with its destination echoed, and the engine re-keys: a second distinct key transferred afterwards encrypts to its own golden and not to the first key's.
|CHK-REVOKE
|`CMD_KEY_REVOKE` succeeds with the handle echoed.
|CHK-CLOSED
|A transfer on the revoked handle is refused with the failure code: revoke fails closed. Allow leg: the same transfer of the generated handle to AES, its permitted destination, returns success with the handle and destination echoed right before the revoke, so the refusal comes from the revoke and not from a KM that refuses generated-key transfers.
|CHK-NULL
|Revoking the reserved null handle is refused with the invalid-argument code.
|CHK-ILLEGAL
|Every seeded undefined command ID returns the invalid-command code.
|CHK-LEN
|A defined command carrying the wrong payload length returns the invalid-length code.
|CHK-FRAME
|The three framing rejections, each with the value it carries: a corrupt header CRC-8 returns RC_HEADER_CRC with the CRC the KM computed, a wrong sequence number returns RC_CMD_NOSEQ with the number it expected, and a corrupt payload CRC-32C returns RC_PAYLOAD_CRC. The sequence-counter side effects differ between the three and are handled in the driver.
|CHK-OTP
|`CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD` of one identity bit returns success with that bit set in the echo; a reserved bit returns the invalid-argument code; a zero-length payload returns the invalid-length code.
|CHK-RECOV
|SEP `CTRL.FLUSH` posts `RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT` with the flushed-by-SEP code; `CMD_STAT` reports the latched bit; aggregator bit 31 is 1; `CMD_RECOV_ACK` clears the STAT bit and the aggregator bit. The following transfer and shred are the key commands accepted after the ACK.
|CHK-GONE
|A shredded engine refuses to start. After a final `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` the AES produces no output within a bounded window, which is the only frontdoor evidence the shred reached the key: the engine key registers are write-only. Positive control: right before the shred, the second known key is transferred to AES again and the same sideload start gives output inside the same bounded window and encrypts to its golden, so the key is live at the shred, the window is long enough for a keyed block, and the shred is the only change between the two starts. Runs last, because an engine parked waiting for a key stays parked.
|CHK-ALIVE
|After every refusal, and once after the undefined-ID walk, `CMD_STAT` succeeds and reports no latched recoverable error. One probe covers the walk: each ID answers with its own invalid-command code, so the KM is still in its loop after the ID before it, and a recoverable fault stays latched until `CMD_RECOV_ACK`, so a fault that any ID raised is still set at the probe. The status word is the response's `RETURN_ARG` (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, "0x03 - CMD_STAT"), and a response without one fails rather than reading as status 0. The second half is what gives CHK-DEST and CHK-CLOSED their meaning: while a recoverable fault is pending the KM refuses every key command with the same failure code those two expect, before it looks at the arguments at all, so a faulted KM would otherwise satisfy both without the policy they name being exercised.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: sideload KATs stay consume proofs; this vehicle owns the mailbox command
set. The mailbox PROTOCOL is its own entry: see
`sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`. The seal
walk is on `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`, not here: no mailbox command seals a
slot, so over this surface an erase always frees.

*Anti-vacuity*: every frame is built with a correct header CRC-8, a correct
declared length and a correct payload CRC-32C, so a refusal is a verdict on the
command ID or its arguments and never on framing. The refusal checkers assert
the exact documented code rather than "not success", so a rejection for the
wrong reason does not read as a pass. The two known keys are checked to encrypt
the plaintext differently before either is used, so CHK-SHRED's
before-and-after comparison cannot be satisfied by two identical goldens.

*Not claimed*: the DRBG fault SOURCE. The organic timeout and AXI-Stream error
conditions cannot be provoked from the SEP fabric, and at SEP level such a fault
is unrecoverable -- the KM emits an unsolicited fault response -- so it
is neither a readable status nor a return code from here. The interrupt plumbing
for that same bit IS reachable and is graded on the vault vehicle's CHK-IRQSET,
via the architected `IRQ_SET.DRBG_ERR_SET` inject; the two must not be conflated,
and CHK-IRQSET is not coverage of the source.

The mailbox is a transport here, not the subject. Its register surface -- depth,
full, the four overflow/underflow stickies, `IRQ_STATUS`, and `SEP_CTRL`'s
response modes and flush -- is owned by `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`.

'''''

[[sep_km_wipe_reset_test]]
===== 7.9 `sep_km_wipe_reset_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_wipe_reset_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE.

*Objective*: recover the KM after a `SW_RESET_N` warm pulse, then close it
with the SEP emergency wipe.

*Test Coverage*: KM warm-reset + `RESP_KM_READY`; `KM_WIPE_CTRL` unrecoverable fault.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`, and load a known key.
. Park then release the KM `SW_RESET_N` bit. Resynchronize mailbox sequence
counters. Wait for `RESP_KM_READY` and load the same known key again.
. Write `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state`. Receive `RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`. This is the bench build with the boot and unrecoverable-fault SRAM wipes compiled out; see Known Limitations, "KM application ROM wipe paths".
|CHK-PRE
|`CMD_KEY_LOAD` succeeds before the warm pulse.
|CHK-RESET
|After park/release the KM announces `RESP_KM_READY` and `CMD_KEY_LOAD` succeeds.
|CHK-WIPE
|A write of `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state` posts `RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT` with a one-word payload equal to the `wipe_state` fault code (-1, sign-extended to `0xFFFFFFFF`) from the Fault Codes table in `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, and aggregator bit 30 (PIC source 31) is 1. A fault from any other cause carries a different code and fails.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: KPV-zero after wipe is not claimed -- those arrays have no SEP
frontdoor.

*Anti-vacuity*: the post-reset load is a value-bearing command, not only the
ready banner. Wipe is last: after the fault frame the test does not issue
another mailbox command.

'''''

[[sep_km_handover_test]]
===== 7.10 `sep_km_handover_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_handover_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE.

*Objective*: the handover mailbox contract, a SRAM load, and `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` after warm reset.

*Test Coverage*: `CMD_EXEC_ROM` inhibit; `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` 14-word stream; `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` after warm reset.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`.
. Refuse empty `CMD_SRAM_EXEC`, `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` with `FW_WORDS=0`, and
`CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` with reserved bits set.
. `CMD_EXEC_ROM` succeeds once; a later `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC`, `CMD_SRAM_EXEC`
and `CMD_EXEC_ROM` each return the failure code. A warm reset clears the
inhibit.
. On a fresh epoch, send `CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` with `FW_WORDS=14`, receive
success, then stream the 14-word blob plus CRC-32C.
. Warm-reset the KM. `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` returns success.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`. This is the bench build with the boot and unrecoverable-fault SRAM wipes compiled out; see Known Limitations, "KM application ROM wipe paths".
|CHK-EMPTY
|`CMD_SRAM_EXEC` with no image returns the invalid-argument code.
|CHK-ARG
|`CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` with `FW_WORDS=0` or reserved `[31:16]` set returns the invalid-argument code.
|CHK-INHIBIT
|The first `CMD_EXEC_ROM` succeeds; later handover commands return the failure code.
|CHK-INHIBIT-RST
|A warm reset clears the inhibit; `CMD_EXEC_ROM` succeeds again.
|CHK-LOAD
|`CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC` returns success before the stream; the 14-word image plus CRC-32C is posted.
|CHK-EXEC
|After a warm reset `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` returns success.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: KMCSR `TEST_SIGNATURE` is not SEP-reachable.

*Anti-vacuity*: inhibit cells assert the failure code, not "not success". The
load response is taken before the stream, matching the ROM handler. CHK-EMPTY
refuses `CMD_SRAM_EXEC` with no image, so CHK-EXEC success is against that
refusal, not an always-success command.

'''''

[[sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test]]
===== 7.11 `sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_abr_seed_sideload_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. `SepAbrKeygenCfg` is the single source of truth for
masking entropy on both compare KEYGENs. The seed words are directed so the
two KEYGENs stay comparable.

*Objective*: a KM-sideloaded ABR ML-DSA seed produces the same public key as
a direct software-seed KEYGEN of the same seed, and not the first seed's
public key.

*Test Coverage*: KM dest ABR ML-DSA seed consume through the KV seed-read control.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`.
. Run a direct-seed ML-DSA-87 KEYGEN on the first seed with
`cfg.entropy` and capture the public key.
. `ZEROIZE`. Repeat KEYGEN with the inverted entropy vector. The two public
keys must match -- `ABR_ENTROPY` is SCA masking and must not change the
outputs.
. `ZEROIZE`. Run a direct-seed KEYGEN on a second, distinct seed
and require a public key different from the first. This is the reference for
the sideload compare.
. `ZEROIZE`. Run a KEYGEN with no software seed and no KV seed-read enable and
require a public key different from the reference, so the seed register does
not hold the second seed.
. `ZEROIZE`, then `CMD_KEY_LOAD` + `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` of the second seed to dest
ABR ML-DSA seed.
. Write the same masking entropy, set the KV seed-read enable, KEYGEN, and
compare the public key word-for-word with the reference.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`. This is the bench build with the boot and unrecoverable-fault SRAM wipes compiled out; see Known Limitations, "KM application ROM wipe paths".
|CHK-RANDCFG
|A KEYGEN that writes `cfg.entropy` and a KEYGEN that writes the inverted vector produce the same public key. Both compare KEYGENs write `cfg.entropy`.
|CHK-DIRECT
|The direct-seed KEYGEN produces a non-zero public key.
|CHK-XFER
|`CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` dest ABR ML-DSA seed returns success and echoes the dest.
|CHK-PK-REF
|The two direct-seed KEYGENs produce different public keys. Without this the sideload compare could not tell the second seed from the first.
|CHK-CLEAR
|After `ZEROIZE`, a KEYGEN with no software seed and no KV seed-read enable does not reproduce the reference public key, so the seed register does not still hold the second seed when the sideload runs.
|CHK-PK
|The KM-sideloaded KEYGEN public key equals the reference public key word-for-word, and is not the first seed's public key.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: ML-DSA only. The ML-KEM dest bits and the `CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER`
consume are claimed by `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`.

*Anti-vacuity*: both compare public keys must be non-zero. The inverted
vector is not equal to `cfg.entropy`. Key Manager word `i` and
`MLDSA_SEED[i]` carry the same dword (`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`,
"ABR seed word order"), and each seed has eight pairwise-distinct words, so
CHK-PK fails a delivery that loses, keeps stale or reorders a seed word -- a
dword reversal included. `ABR_ENTROPY` is write-only; this row grades the RDL "no change on
the outputs" rule, not a consume probe.

'''''

[[sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test]]
===== 7.12 `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, `env/sep_aes_golden.py`, the
`abr_mlkem_seed_z_probe_o` observation probe (SEP_TB_ARCH exception list)

*Randomization*: none. Every seed, message and command is directed: each leg
is a value-equality compare between a sideloaded and a direct run, and a
random input on either side would remove the compare.

*Objective*: every ML-KEM lane of the Key-Vault facade carries what the KM
pushed into the sideload CSR, word `i` to register index `i`, and the shared
key Adams Bridge posts back over the write lane is the engine's own shared key,
consumable by the KM.

*Test Coverage*: the 16-dword seed read lane carrying D then Z, the message
read lane, and the shared-key write lane with its `KEY_VALID` latch and its
interrupt, plus the `CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER` refusal when no key has been posted
and the vault-confidentiality rule that withholds the shared key from software.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`. Read `MLKEM_NAME` and log it as information only: `abr_reg.rdl`
gives it no reset and no SEP document gives its value, so it is not graded. A
dead decode reads both reference keys as zero and fails `CHK-KEM-SEED-REF`.
. KEYGEN on the ALT `(D, Z)` pair written directly to the registers and keep
the encapsulation key. `ZEROIZE`, then KEYGEN on the REF pair and require a
different encapsulation key. Read the engine's `Z` through
`abr_mlkem_seed_z_probe_o` and require the REF `Z`. The registers are left
holding REF.
. `ZEROIZE`. Read the engine's `Z` and require that it is not the ALT `Z`.
`CMD_KEY_LOAD` + `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` the ALT `D` to dest
`abr_mlkem_seed_d` and the ALT `Z` to dest `abr_mlkem_seed_z`, set the KV
seed-read enable, and KEYGEN with no register write. The encapsulation key
must equal the ALT one and differ from the REF one, and the engine's `Z` must
equal the ALT `Z`.
. ENCAPS twice over that key, once on each message, written directly, and
require the two ciphertexts to differ. The first of the two also establishes
that the shared key is readable when nothing came from the vault. The register
is left holding REF.
. `ZEROIZE`, sideload the ALT message to dest `abr_mlkem_msg`, set the KV
message-read enable, and ENCAPS. The ciphertext must equal the ALT one, and
the shared key of that same ENCAPS must then read zero.
. With nothing yet posted on the write lane, `CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER` must be
refused. Then set the KV shared-key write enable and ENCAPS again. The KM must
post the unsolicited `RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY` announcement, and the same
command must then succeed with a non-zero KPV handle.
. `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` that handle to AES and encrypt one block with the
delivered key selected. The ciphertext must equal the AES-256 golden keyed with
the shared key read back from the register-driven ENCAPS of the same
encapsulation key and message.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`. This is the bench build with the boot and unrecoverable-fault SRAM wipes compiled out; see Known Limitations, "KM application ROM wipe paths".
|CHK-KEM-SEED-REF
|The ALT and REF `(D, Z)` pairs produce different encapsulation keys, and after the REF KEYGEN the engine's `Z` (`abr_mlkem_seed_z_probe_o`) equals the REF `Z`. Without the first the sideload compare could not distinguish a delivered seed from a stale one; the second is the positive control that the probe follows the `Z` the engine holds.
|CHK-KEM-SEED-SIDELOAD
|A KEYGEN whose seed arrives only over the KV seed lane produces the encapsulation key of the direct-register KEYGEN on the same words in the same order, and not the key of the pair the registers last held; and the engine's `Z` after that KEYGEN equals the ALT `Z` word for word; before the KV read the engine does not hold the ALT `Z`. The encapsulation key depends on `D` only and software cannot read `Z` back, so the key grades the `D` half (the low eight dwords) and the probe grades the `Z` half (the high eight). Key Manager word `i` is register index `i` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`, "ABR seed word order").
|CHK-KEM-MSG-SIDELOAD
|An ENCAPS whose message arrives only over the KV message lane produces the ciphertext of the direct-register ENCAPS on the same message, and not that of the message the register last held. The compare is on the ciphertext, not the shared key: the ciphertext is public and a deterministic function of the encapsulation key and the message, while the shared key is not readable on exactly this path -- see the next row.
|CHK-KEM-SK-CONFIDENTIAL
|The shared key reads back over the bus after a fully register-driven ENCAPS, and reads zero once the message came from the vault. The ML-KEM shared key is consumed directly by the Key Manager and shared-key storage is secret-bearing Class 2 logic (`doc/crypto.adoc`, `doc/attack_countermeasures.adoc`), so a key the vault sourced must not be readable by software over the CSR aperture. The register-driven read is the positive control: without it a broken readback path would pass the zero check, and it is what makes this a conditional withholding rather than a readback that never worked.
|CHK-KEM-SK-NOTIFY
|The writeback raises the shared-key interrupt and the KM firmware posts the unsolicited `RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY` frame. The facade pulses `IRQ_STATUS.key_valid` on the final writeback dword and the wrapper gates it onto the shared-key interrupt, so this frame is the only host-side view of that path -- the consume below could succeed with the interrupt dead.
|CHK-KEM-SK-WRITEBACK
|`CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER` is refused while the sideload block's `KEY_VALID` is clear, and succeeds with a non-zero handle after an ENCAPS with the write lane armed. The refusal is the negative control: without it the success would not show that the writeback set `KEY_VALID`.
|CHK-KEM-SK-VALUE
|`MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY[i]` holds word `i` of the engine's own `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY` register array (`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`). The KEY words sit on the KM-private bus, so AES grades them: the consumed handle, transferred to AES, encrypts one block to the AES-256 golden keyed with the shared key the register-driven ENCAPS of the same inputs read back. The golden of the dword-reversed key differs, and a failure reports when the ciphertext matches it.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: the KV `read_entry` / `write_entry` and `dest_valid` fields are not
claimed -- SEP has no Caliptra Key Vault, so the facade serves one block per
lane and ignores entry selection. The read-error response a block raises while
its `KEY_VALID` is clear is not claimed here.

*Evidence*: `CHK-KEM-MSG-REF` labels the two direct-register reference ENCAPS runs of CHK-KEM-MSG-SIDELOAD; a timeout, an engine error or an all-zero ciphertext in either one reports under that label.

*Anti-vacuity*: every captured encapsulation key and ciphertext is required to
be non-zero. The `Z` probe reads the REF `Z` after the REF KEYGEN and a value
other than the ALT `Z` before the KV read, so the ALT `Z` compare after the
sideloaded KEYGEN can fail. The shared key read on the register-driven path is required to be
non-zero, and it precedes the check that the vault-sourced shared key reads
zero. Each sideload leg is preceded by a run on different words, so the
register the lane bypasses holds the wrong value when the lane is read -- a
lane that delivers nothing fails on the inequality before the equality is
reached. The ML-KEM identity words are logged as information only. Every seed
half and message has eight pairwise-distinct words, and `D` and `Z` share no
word, so a dword reversal, any other word permutation and a swap between `D`
and `Z` all fail. The reference shared key is checked not to be a dword
palindrome before CHK-KEM-SK-VALUE uses it. The
shared-key consume carries its own refusal leg rather than resting on a single
success.

'''''

[[sep_km_abr_mldsa_kat_test]]
===== 7.13 `sep_km_abr_mldsa_kat_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_abr_mldsa_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, `env/sep_abr_nist.py` (the ACVP vectors of
`fw/tests/common/abr_nist_vectors.h`)

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. `SepAbrKeygenCfg` gives the masking entropy. The
seed is the ACVP vector.

*Objective*: an ML-DSA-87 seed delivered by the Key Manager reaches the engine
in the documented word order: Key Manager word `i` is `MLDSA_SEED[i]`, and
index 0 holds the first four bytes of the FIPS 204 seed
(`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`, "ABR seed word order"). The ACVP keyGen
vector is the golden, so the grade does not depend on the software register
path.

*Test Coverage*: KM dest ABR ML-DSA seed, the ML-DSA KV seed read, and KEYGEN
on the delivered seed against an independent known answer.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`.
. `ZEROIZE` the engine.
. `CMD_KEY_LOAD` the ACVP keyGen seed, in the word order
<<sep_abr_mldsa_keygen_kat_test>> writes to `MLDSA_SEED`, and
`CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` it to dest ABR ML-DSA seed.
. Set the KV seed-read enable and poll `kv_mldsa_seed_rd_status` until `VALID`
or a non-`SUCCESS` `ERROR`.
. Write the masking entropy, KEYGEN with no seed register write, and compare
the 648-word public key with the ACVP public key.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK0
|`rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces `RESP_KM_READY`. This is the bench build with the boot and unrecoverable-fault SRAM wipes compiled out; see Known Limitations, "KM application ROM wipe paths".
|CHK-XFER
|`CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` of the ACVP seed to dest ABR ML-DSA seed returns success and echoes the handle and dest.
|CHK-KV
|The KV seed read completes: `VALID` with `ERROR` = `SUCCESS` (`kv_error_e` encoding of `kv_def.rdl`).
|CHK-KM-KAT
|The KEYGEN public key equals the ACVP keyGen public key, all 648 words.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: ML-DSA keyGen only. The ML-KEM lanes and the shared-key writeback
are graded against the software register path by
<<sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test>>.

*Anti-vacuity*: the ACVP seed is never written to the ABR registers, so a
public key equal to the ACVP public key can only come from the delivered seed.
The eight seed words are pairwise distinct (asserted at import), so a dword
reversal or any other word permutation in delivery changes the seed and fails
CHK-KM-KAT.

'''''

[[sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test]]
===== 7.14 `sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, real fuse-sense, `+esrc_noise_force`,
`+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, `env/sep_aes_golden.py`, `env/sep_hmac_golden.py`,
`env/sep_kmac_golden.py`, the `abr_mlkem_seed_z_probe_o` observation probe
(SEP_TB_ARCH exception list)

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. `SepKmShareWalkCfg` is the single source of truth
for both rounds of keys, the AES plaintext, the HMAC and KMAC messages, the
HMAC software keys and the ML-DSA masking entropy; the goldens and the
direct-register ABR references are built from the same object. ABR seeds are
seeded, with eight pairwise-distinct words per block.

*Objective*: AES, HMAC, KMAC and OTBN each consume the exact key the KM
delivered, and each ABR destination consumes the delivered words, word `i` at
register index `i` (`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`, "ABR seed word order").
Every destination takes a second, different key after an engine shred, and
shows the documented invalid-key behaviour while its `KEY_CTRL.KEY_VALID` is
clear.

*Test Coverage*: all eight `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` destinations -- AES, HMAC, KMAC,
OTBN and the four ABR blocks `MLDSA_SEED`, `MLKEM_SEED_D`, `MLKEM_SEED_Z`,
`MLKEM_MSG` -- loaded twice each, and `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` on all eight at once,
twice. The invalid-key column of the key-delivery summary in `doc/crypto.adoc`
for each accelerator.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a valid PROD OTP image, bring entropy up, release the KM, wait for
`RESP_KM_READY`. Release AES, HMAC, KMAC and OTBN and wait for each to be idle:
each wrapper key block sits in its engine's software-reset domain.
Precondition, not graded here.
. Round 1. `CMD_KEY_LOAD` + `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` one seeded key to each of the
eight destinations. The firmware response (success, handle and destination
echoed) is a precondition; the delivered value is graded in the next step.
. AES ECB-256 encrypt with `CTRL_SHADOWED.sideload` (CHK-AES). HMAC-SHA256 (CHK-HMAC)
with a different software key in the KEY CSRs. Keyed KMAC256 with
`CFG_SHADOWED.sideload` (CHK-KMAC). The OTBN key-dump program (CHK-OTBN). For
ABR, `ZEROIZE`, set the KV read enable, wait for the KV status to settle, run
the KV-fed KEYGEN or ENCAPS, then `ZEROIZE` and run the same operation on the
same words written directly (CHK-MLDSA, CHK-MLKEM-SEED, CHK-MLKEM-MSG). After
the `D` then `Z` KV read, sample the engine's `Z` through
`abr_mlkem_seed_z_probe_o` (CHK-MLKEM-SEED). Each ABR block has eight
pairwise-distinct words, so these compares fail a delivery that reorders them.
. `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` with all eight destination bits. The firmware response is
a precondition; the hardware effect is graded in the next step.
. HMAC with a software key (CHK-HMAC-CLR); OTBN key dump (CHK-OTBN-CLR); for
each ABR KV lane, `ZEROIZE`, set the read enable, read the KV status and the
engine status, `ZEROIZE`. Then `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` the round's `D` handle to
`MLKEM_SEED_D` only and read the `D` then `Z` lane; `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED`
`MLKEM_SEED_D` and `MLKEM_SEED_Z`, the only two destinations that are valid at
that point, transfer the `Z` handle to `MLKEM_SEED_Z` only and read the lane
again; `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` the same two (CHK-ABR-CLR).
. Round 2: the same steps with a new key whose every word differs from the
round-1 word at the same index. Compare each ABR output with round 1
(CHK-ROUND).
. Keyed KMAC on the delivered key (CHK-KMAC-CLR), then AES with the delivered
key selected (CHK-AES-CLR). These two leave their engines stopped, so they run
last.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-AES-1/2
|The sideload ECB-256 ciphertext equals the AES-256 golden of the delivered key.
|CHK-HMAC-1/2
|The HMAC-SHA256 digest equals the golden of the delivered key while a different software key sits in the KEY CSRs (`doc/hmac.adoc`: the delivered key is used whenever `KEY_VALID` is set).
|CHK-KMAC-1/2
|The sideload KMAC256 digest equals the SP 800-185 golden of the delivered key.
|CHK-OTBN-1/2
|The key-dump program reads the delivered 384-bit key, share0 XOR share1 equals it, the pad is zero and `ERR_BITS` is 0.
|CHK-MLDSA-1/2
|The ML-DSA seed KV read completes (`VALID`, `ERROR` = `SUCCESS`) and the KEYGEN public key equals a direct-seed KEYGEN of the same words in the same order.
|CHK-MLKEM-SEED-1/2
|The 16-dword `D` then `Z` KV read completes, the encapsulation key equals a direct-seed KEYGEN of the same `D` and `Z` in the same order, and the `Z` the engine holds after the KV read (`abr_mlkem_seed_z_probe_o`) equals the delivered `Z` word for word. The encapsulation key depends on `D` only and software cannot read `Z` back, so the probe grades `Z`.
|CHK-MLKEM-MSG-1/2
|The message KV read completes and the ENCAPS ciphertext equals a direct-message ENCAPS of the same words in the same order.
|CHK-ROUND
|Every round-2 ABR output (ML-DSA public key, ML-KEM encapsulation key, ML-KEM ciphertext) differs from its round-1 output. The ABR compares are against the same engine, so this row fails an engine that ignores its seed or message. AES, HMAC, KMAC and OTBN have goldens per round and are not compared here.
|CHK-HMAC-CLR-1/2
|After the shred the HMAC digest equals the golden of the software key in the KEY CSRs: with `KEY_VALID` clear the engine falls back to its software key.
|CHK-OTBN-CLR-1/2
|After the shred the key-dump program stops with `ERR_BITS` = `KEY_INVALID` (`doc/otbn.adoc`).
|CHK-ABR-CLR-1/2
|After the shred each of the three ABR KV reads reports `ERROR` = `KV_READ_FAIL` (`doc/crypto.adoc`, `km-key-delivery-summary`: "Seed read does not complete"; the encoding is the `kv_error_e` enum of `kv_def.rdl`). `D` and `Z` have their own `KEY_VALID` and the `D` then `Z` read fails if either is clear, so each is also graded alone: with only `D` delivered again the read reports `KV_READ_FAIL` (`Z` is clear), and after a shred of `D` and `Z`, with only `Z` delivered again, it reports `KV_READ_FAIL` (`D` is clear). Each single-half leg then delivers the other half too and the read must complete (`VALID`, `ERROR` = `SUCCESS`): the control that the re-delivered half became valid, so each failure belongs to the shredded half. The engine `STATUS` is logged and not graded: no SEP document states it.
|CHK-KMAC-CLR
|A keyed KMAC on the shredded delivered key raises `INTR_STATE.kmac_err` (`doc/kmac.adoc`) with `ERR_CODE[31:24]` = `KeyNotValid` (0x01, the "Error Report" table of the OpenTitan KMAC documentation that `doc/kmac.adoc` names as the authority; the field position is from `kmac.rdl`). Both read clear after the engine is programmed and just before `CMD_START`, so the error belongs to that start and not to an earlier operation.
|CHK-AES-CLR
|With the delivered key selected and shredded, AES produces no output in a bounded window. `doc/aes.adoc` defers core behaviour to the OpenTitan AES documentation, which states that the unit only starts if the sideload key is valid ("System Key-Manager Interface").
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes, and the leaf declares every
checker ID above as required evidence. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope*: the share words are write-only and sit on the KM-private bus, so a
delivered value is graded at its consumer, never by readback. The ML-KEM
`Z` has no consumer output (the encapsulation key depends on `D` only), so it
is graded at the engine through a read-only observation probe. The KV read error
of a block whose `KEY_VALID` is clear is claimed here; the ML-KEM shared-key
write lane stays on `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`. ABR has no independent
golden in this tree for a seed of the run's choosing, so each ABR compare is
against the same engine fed through its registers. KMAC and AES recovery after
the invalid-key refusal is not claimed. The ABR engine state after a failed KV
read is logged, not graded.

*Preconditions*: `rom_main` boots on real entropy and announces
`RESP_KM_READY`; this is the bench build with the boot and unrecoverable-fault
SRAM wipes compiled out (see Known Limitations, "KM application ROM wipe
paths"). Every `CMD_KEY_TRANSFER` and `CMD_ENGINE_SHRED` returns success and
echoes its arguments. These are firmware responses and are asserted, not
graded. The shred clears `key_valid` and overwrites both shares
(`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, "Crypto Engine Drivers"); the
cleared-state rows grade the `key_valid` half at each consumer.

*Anti-vacuity*: every key word is non-zero and differs from the other round's
word at the same index, so a stale share word changes the consumer output. Each
ABR KV leg runs before its direct-register reference and after a `ZEROIZE`:
a lane that delivers nothing reads the zeroized register in round 1 and the
round-1 reference in round 2, and fails the compare. The HMAC cleared-state
check uses the same software key as the decoy of the valid-key check, so the
same software key gives the delivered-key digest before the shred and the
software-key digest after it. Each cleared-state check has its positive control
in the same run: the same operation on the same engine succeeds with the
delivered key in the round before.

'''''

[[sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test]]
===== 7.15 `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, KM held in `SW_RESET_N` (reset default).
No `+km_rom_hex`: this leaf grades the SEP-side registers, not a command stream.

*Source Module*: `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip

*Randomization*: RANDCFG. The 16 inbound fill words and the overflow poke come
from the run seed. Every discrete cell (idle, underflow SLVERR, underflow
OKAY-mode, fill, overflow SLVERR, overflow OKAY-mode, IRQ enable/gate/W1C,
flush) walks in one invocation.

*Objective*: the SEP-side KM mailbox as a REGISTER surface rather than a
transport. `STATUS` depth and full/empty, the two SEP-reachable sticky error
bits, `IRQ_STATUS` / `IRQ_ENABLE` gating onto aggregator `[14]`, both
`SEP_CTRL` response modes, and flush.

*Test Coverage*: SEP-side KM mailbox registers: depth, stickies, IRQ, response modes, flush.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Bring up no_cpu with fuse sense skipped. Require `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 clear.
. Compare reset `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS` against the empty golden.
. Read empty `SEP_READ_DATA`; require SLVERR, data 0, and `outbound_underflow`
  latched in both `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS`.
. Write `SEP_CTRL` with a 32-bit beat immediately after that underflow;
  require OKAY and an exact readback. Then write `SEP_CTRL` with one 64-bit
  beat whose low word sets one more response-mode bit; require SLVERR or
  DECERR with no timeout and log the readback. Require the mailbox register
  block's write-refusal count to step by exactly one across the beat, at
  offset `KM_MAILBOX_SEP_REG_MAP_SIZE`, and not to step across the 32-bit
  writes. Rewrite `SEP_CTRL` with the 32-bit value and require an exact
  readback.
. Set `outbound_underflow_resp`; the same empty read must be OKAY with the
  stickies still set. Write-1-to-clear both copies.
. Write 16 seeded words; after each write, `STATUS` depth/full/empty and
  `IRQ_STATUS.inbound_write_space_avail` must match the golden.
. A 17th write must SLVERR, latch `inbound_overflow`, and leave depth 16.
. Set `inbound_overflow_resp`; the next overrun must be OKAY, drop the word,
  and leave the sticky set.
. Enable the two error IRQs; aggregator `[14]` must rise. Clear the enable;
  the pin must drop while `IRQ_STATUS` stays. Write-1-to-clear both stickies.
. Latch inbound overflow (write to the full FIFO) and outbound underflow (read
  the empty FIFO). Set flush. The inbound FIFO empties, the outbound FIFO stays
  empty, `flush` self-clears, and both stickies survive in STATUS and
  `IRQ_STATUS` until write-1-to-clear.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HELD
|`SW_RESET_N` bit 0 reads 0. Without this the fill/overflow goldens are a race against firmware.
|CHK-IDLE
|Reset `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS` match the empty golden; aggregator `[14]` is 0.
|CHK-UFL
|Empty `SEP_READ_DATA` returns SLVERR and 0, and `outbound_underflow` latches in both `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS`.
|CHK-CTRL
|A 32-bit `SEP_CTRL` write immediately after that underflow is OKAY and reads back exactly. The register is not the FIFO error path. A 64-bit write beat at offset 0x18 reaches past the register file and returns SLVERR or DECERR with no timeout. One `BRESP` covers the beat, so which half was refused is not graded; the readback after it is logged. The 32-bit write to the same register is the positive control.
|CHK-CTRL-EP
|The mailbox register block's own address decode refuses the upper word of that 64-bit beat: its write-refusal count steps by exactly one during the beat, and the refused block offset is the first offset past the RDL window (`KM_MAILBOX_SEP_REG_MAP_SIZE`, the word after `SEP_CTRL`). The 32-bit `SEP_CTRL` writes before and after the beat do not step the count, so the probe does not count legal writes. This shows the `--err-if-bad-addr` check is reachable from the SEP host; the beat's `BRESP` is logged.
|CHK-UFL-RESP
|`SEP_CTRL.outbound_underflow_resp` turns the same empty read into OKAY; the sticky bits stay set.
|CHK-UFL-W1C
|Write-1-to-clear drops both sticky copies.
|CHK-FILL
|16 seeded writes fill inbound. After each write, `STATUS` depth/full/empty and `IRQ_STATUS.inbound_write_space_avail` match the golden.
|CHK-OVR
|A 17th write returns SLVERR, latches `inbound_overflow`, and leaves depth 16.
|CHK-OVR-RESP
|`SEP_CTRL.inbound_overflow_resp` turns the next overrun into OKAY; the sticky bit stays; depth stays 16 (the word is dropped).
|CHK-IRQ
|With the `inbound_overflow` sticky set, enabling the two error IRQs raises aggregator `[14]`. Clearing the enable drops the pin while `IRQ_STATUS` stays unchanged, and setting it again raises the pin. With the enable still set, a write-1-to-clear of the two sticky error bits in `STATUS` and then in `IRQ_STATUS` drops the pin. `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS` then match the golden with both stickies clear and inbound depth 16; `CHK-IRQ-W1C` labels a failure of that compare.
|CHK-FLUSH
|`CTRL.FLUSH` empties the full inbound FIFO, self-clears, and leaves both sticky errors -- `inbound_overflow` and `outbound_underflow`, each latched just before the flush -- set in STATUS and `IRQ_STATUS` until W1C. The outbound FIFO is empty before and after, so its flush is not claimed; see Known Limitations, "KM mailbox outbound flush".
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Probe*: `km_mbox_sep_wr_err_count_o` / `km_mbox_sep_wr_err_addr_o` (`tb/tb_top.sv`) count the write refusals of the SEP-side mailbox regblock decode and hold the last refused offset. Observation-only: they read DUT nets and drive nothing.

*Why it is its own entry*: a transport only ever touches what a well-formed
exchange needs. `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` reaches only part of the SEP-side
registers and `STATUS` bits. Reaching the rest means reading an
empty FIFO and overrunning a full one, and both corrupt a frame in flight.

*Why KM is held*: the SEP-facing mailbox lives on cold reset, so the FIFOs and
SEP registers stay alive while the KM CPU cannot consume inbound words or push
outbound ones. Fill, overflow and underflow are then deterministic, and
firmware cannot flush or fault the mailbox under the host. A `SEP_CTRL` write
after a deliberate underflow is a register-block access, independent of the
FIFO error path, and a 32-bit beat must stay OKAY. A 64-bit beat at
`SEP_CTRL` (offset 0x18) reaches past the register file, and its single write
response must be SLVERR or DECERR. One `BRESP` covers the whole beat, so it
cannot say which half, or which block, refused it. The crypto demux routes the
beat on its start address, so it reaches the Key Manager port; the mailbox
register block's own address decode (PeakRDL `--err-if-bad-addr`) is then
observed through the tb_top probe `km_mbox_sep_wr_err_count_o` /
`km_mbox_sep_wr_err_addr_o`, and it must refuse exactly the upper word, at the
first offset past the RDL window. The `SEP_CTRL` value after the beat is
logged and not graded (known limitation _Dead-space per-beat refusal on
writes_). Negative accesses are marked `expect_error` so the
environment scoreboard does not fail them as real errors.

*Not claimed*: `inbound_underflow`, `outbound_overflow`, and
`flushed_by_km` need the KM CPU as the peer. `STATUS.inbound_separator`
updates only when the KM pops the inbound FIFO, and `STATUS.outbound_separator`
only when the SEP pops a KM-pushed word, so both stay on the KM IP mailbox
vehicles. `outbound_read_data_avail` stays 0 for the same reason.

'''''

[[sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test]]
===== 7.16 `sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_crc.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the vector set, from the run seed.

*Objective*: each mode of the KM CRC engine produces the value an independently
written host-side model produces. The engine has no register interface, so the
proof has to come from KM firmware issuing the PCPI custom-0 instruction.

*Test Coverage*: KM CRC PCPI modes vs an independent host golden.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Release the KM on a dedicated service ROM. The ROM applies one CRC instruction
per request — mode, chaining state, data operand — and returns the raw result.
It decides nothing.
. Walk the seeded operand set through all three modes and compare each result
against the host-side golden.
. Send one word through the word instruction and the same four bytes through
four chained byte instructions.
. Send one state and byte through both polynomial families.
. Repeat each byte-mode operand with garbage in the upper three bytes.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-WORD
|Every seeded CRC-32C word vector equals the independent golden.
|CHK-BYTE
|Every seeded CRC-32C byte vector equals the independent golden.
|CHK-ROHC
|Every seeded CRC-8/ROHC vector equals the independent golden.
|CHK-XCHAIN
|One word instruction equals four chained byte instructions from the same start state. Hardware on both sides, so it holds independently of the golden.
|CHK-POLY
|The two polynomial families return different results for the same state and byte, compared over the low byte so their differing result widths cannot satisfy it. The operand is screened for this: the two agree in the low byte for roughly one input in 256, which is correct hardware, so it is excluded from the draw rather than tolerated by a looser check.
|CHK-NARROW
|CRC-8/ROHC leaves the upper twenty-four result bits clear.
|CHK-UPPER
|Both byte modes ignore the operand's upper twenty-four bits.
|CHK-MOVE
|No result equals its own input state.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Aperture*: the CRC engine has no register interface at all. It is a PicoRV32
PCPI co-processor reached by a custom-0 instruction, so KM firmware issues
the instruction, in the same ROM pattern as `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`. The
assembler has no mnemonic for the instruction, so the image emits encoded words;
the KM core is built without the upper register file, so the encodings target
two registers in the lower half and cannot name one the core does not have.

*Anti-vacuity*: the golden is anchored to published check values for both
algorithms and to a byte-order self-test, all asserted at import, so a
transcription error fails before a run rather than agreeing with an equally
wrong DUT. The operand draw rejects an all-zero state with an all-zero byte,
which every reflected CRC maps to zero and which therefore passes against a dead
engine; words whose four bytes are not all distinct, which cannot separate
little-endian consumption from big-endian; and operands sitting on a fixed point
of their mode, which the engine correctly maps back onto their own input state.
A fixed point is legal hardware behaviour, not a defect -- in CRC-8/ROHC one
byte in 256 is a fixed point for any given state -- so it is screened out of the
draw with the independent golden rather than tolerated by CHK-MOVE, which would
otherwise be unfalsifiable for that vector. CHK-MOVE then fails a co-processor
that hands back its first operand, and CHK-XCHAIN and CHK-POLY need no golden at
all. All three modes walk in this vehicle.

*Not claimed*: the illegal-encoding trap. An unrecognised instruction in this
family produces no handshake and traps the core, which would end the service
loop; the KM firmware's own regression owns that leg.

'''''

[[sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test]]
===== 7.17 `sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`,
`+km_rom_hex=km_rom_kpv_scrambler.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM path / golden

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — two scrambler keys and three logical indices, drawn
distinct, from the run seed.

*Objective*: the key-policy vault scrambler transforms both the stored word and
the address mapping while enabled. Pass-through while disabled is the
