observation aperture this test relies on rather than a contract it asserts --
it is what makes the raw stored words readable frontdoor, and a vault that did
not pass through would fail the round-trip and the count instead.

*Test Coverage*: Vault scrambler enable, key transform, and sticky lock.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Release the KM on a dedicated observation ROM and post two scrambler keys and
three logical key-entry indices.
. With the scrambler off, zero all 1024 key-entry words. That reaches a known
starting state through a legitimate frontdoor, without a wipe and without
relying on power-up values.
. Program the first key, enable the scrambler, write one plaintext at each of
the three logical indices, then disable and scan the whole file.
. Repeat with the second key at the first index only.
. Re-enable and read the first index back.
. Read the key register. With the scrambler still unlocked and enabled, write a
third key (the complement of the second key), read the first index, then write
the second key back.
. Take the sticky lock last and read the first index again.
. Write the third key again and clear ENABLE, then read the first index and
`KPV_SCRAMBLER_CTRL`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-COUNT
|After three writes through the enabled scrambler, exactly three of the 1024 words differ from the zeroed setup.
|CHK-DATA
|No stored word equals any plaintext written.
|CHK-ADDR
|The stored words do not all sit at their own logical index: the address mapping is tweaked, not identity.
|CHK-KEYED
|The same plaintext at the same logical index under a different key stores a different word, so the transform is keyed rather than a fixed obfuscation. The reported index is range-checked and the value required non-zero first, so a second-key pass whose write never landed fails here instead of being read as evidence of keying.
|CHK-RT
|With the scrambler re-enabled, the logical index reads back the exact plaintext.
|CHK-KEYRD
|Before the lock, the key register reads back the key that was written.
|CHK-LOCKRT
|The round-trip still recovers the plaintext after the lock, so the hardware kept the key it was using.
|CHK-SWWEL
|Control first: before the lock, with ENABLE set, a write of a third key (the complement of the second key) moves the round-trip away from the plaintext. Every other key write in the test is made while ENABLE is clear, so without this control the post-lock key compare would also pass on hardware that takes its key copy only while disabled, with no lock. After the lock, a write of that same third key leaves the key the hardware uses unchanged (the round-trip still returns the plaintext), and a write clearing ENABLE is refused (ENABLE still reads set). The write lock on the `KPV_SCRAMBLER_KEY` register itself is not graded: see *Not claimed*.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Aperture*: the vault is reachable only from the KM side, so this needs its own
ROM image, not `rom_main` — that image programs the key, enables the scrambler
and takes the sticky lock during boot, so the enable toggle is already gone
before the host can act. No hierarchical peek is needed: with the scrambler
disabled the vault addresses its register file with the logical index and passes
data through unmodified, so firmware can write plaintext with scrambling on,
disable it, and read the raw stored words back through a legitimate frontdoor.
The lock is taken last because it is sticky. The ROM reports raw observations
and the host holds every verdict.

*Precondition*: `km_kpv_regfile` has no reset, so power-up is undefined and a
whole-vault "exactly N non-zero" scan is not a contract until the file is
known-zero. The zero fill is *setup*, not a wipe security checker.

*Anti-vacuity*: "exactly three" is the load-bearing quantifier, not "at least
one". A write that never landed gives zero differences and fails; a read path
stuck at zero gives zero and fails; a register file left dirty gives more than
three and fails. A bare "the stored word differs from the plaintext" check would
pass in the first two cases, and under a simulator that defaults uninitialised
memory to zero it would also pass on a file that was never written. Round-trip
is a required conjunct, so a scrambler that destroys data cannot pass by looking
scrambled. The plaintext is chosen with mixed nibbles because the substitution
is nibble-wise and a uniform value would weaken the difference signal.

CHK-ADDR is "not all of them" rather than "each of them". The
address transform is a bijection on the 1024 indices, and a bijection may have
fixed points, so a single index mapping to itself is legal; an identity mapping
across three seeded indices is not.

The architecture document requires only that a locked key cannot be modified.
CHK-SWWEL grades that contract on the key the datapath uses.

*Not claimed*: the write lock on the `KPV_SCRAMBLER_KEY` register itself, and
the value a locked key register reads. The hardware keeps its own copy of the
key it uses, and `km_kpv.rdl` does not specify the locked read value, so no
frontdoor observation separates a refused key-register write from a landed one;
the ROM does not read the register after the lock. Also not claimed: the eraser's use of the
shared scrambler, the seal and retire contracts owned by the vault test, and
the wipe security contract.

'''''

[[sep_km_rom_write_err_test]]
===== 7.18 `sep_km_rom_write_err_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_bus_err.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_rom_write_err_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — one directed store.

*Objective*: `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/key_manager.rdl` (memory-map Access Notes) says the KM ROM is read-only and is accessed through the PicoRV32 native memory interface, not AXI-Lite. A write completes with no error response, leaves the ROM unchanged and sets `IRQ_STATUS.ROM_WRITE_ERR`. The KM CPU observes an AXI error response only as the sticky `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR` / `AXI_DECERR` bits (`km_csr.rdl`), so a ROM write leaves both clear.

*Test Coverage*: KM ROM write refusal, ROM content after a write, and the error the KM CPU observes.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Dedicated `km_rom_bus_err` on the KM CPU bus, not `rom_main`. The image makes each access, copies raw words to KM SRAM and grades nothing; the host holds every verdict (`seq_lib/sep_km_bus_err_seq.py` holds the word layout).
. Before each access the image writes 1 to clear `AXI_SLVERR`, `AXI_DECERR` and `ROM_WRITE_ERR` and stores the readback. The host requires it clear, so a bit a row reports belongs to that row's access. After each access the image polls `IRQ_STATUS` a bounded number of times and stores the last value.
. Control: a store to `KM_KPV_SIZE` past the KPV base, inside the 8 KB KPV window.
. Control: a KM load from the Reserved ROM-growth row (ROM base + ROM size) of the `key_manager.rdl` memory-map summary.
. The image loads a ROM word, stores its complement to it, polls, and loads the word again.
. The host waits for the image's marker in KM SRAM word 0 and reads the dump words through `km_sram_probe_o`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KM-BUS-ERR-LIVE
|Precondition, shared by the three `km_rom_bus_err` leaves (`seq_lib/sep_km_bus_err_seq.py`). KM SRAM word 0 holds the image's pre-load or post-load marker within a fixed cycle bound after release. The image stores the pre-load marker after every dump word except the data and `IRQ_STATUS` words of the virtual ROM load, so the image ran and the rows before that load are written. The checker has no PASS line: when the marker does not appear, the leaf stops with this FAIL and the KM ROM fetch count.
|CHK-KM-SLVERR-LIVE
|Control. A store past the KPV register map sets `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR`, so the bit and the image's poll work.
|CHK-KM-DECERR-LIVE
|Control. A KM load from the Reserved ROM-growth row sets `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_DECERR`, so the bit and the image's poll work.
|CHK-KM-ROM-UNCHANGED
|The ROM word reads the image constant before the store and after a store of its complement.
|CHK-KM-ROM-WRITE-ERR
|The store sets `IRQ_STATUS.ROM_WRITE_ERR`.
|CHK-KM-ROM-WRITE-NO-AXI-ERR
|The store leaves `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR` and `AXI_DECERR` clear: `key_manager.rdl` says a ROM write completes with no error response and is reported only as `ROM_WRITE_ERR`. `CHK-KM-SLVERR-LIVE` shows the `AXI_SLVERR` bit sets on an erroring store and `CHK-KM-DECERR-LIVE` shows the `AXI_DECERR` bit sets on an erroring load, in the same image run, so a clear bit here is evidence.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

'''''

[[sep_km_otp_shim_unreachable_test]]
===== 7.19 `sep_km_otp_shim_unreachable_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_bus_err.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_otp_shim_unreachable_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image

*Randomization*: RANDCFG — the value the host programs into `EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME`, from the run seed, distinct from its reset and from the value the KM image stores.

*Objective*: `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/key_manager.rdl` (OTP / eFuse Pass-Through) says only the MAP, CTRL and MMR register maps of the 4 KB OTP window are forwarded to SEP, that any other offset in the window answers SLVERR, and that `efuse_shim_ctrl` is intentionally excluded from the KM AXI-Lite path. A KM CPU load from, and a store to, `OTP_EFUSE_MMR` base + size is the first word past the last forwarded register map, so it must answer SLVERR and must not reach the shim register.

*Test Coverage*: KM OTP window decode past the MMR register map, and the separation of the KM path from the eFuse shim CSR port.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. The host reads `EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME` on its SEP-side window (`SEP_EXTERNAL_EFUSE_SHIM_CTRL`) and requires its RDL reset, writes the seeded value, and reads it back.
. The host releases the KM into `km_rom_bus_err` (see <<sep_km_rom_write_err_test>> for the clear-then-poll rule of each row).
. Control: a KM load from the Reserved ROM-growth row (ROM base + ROM size) of the `key_manager.rdl` memory-map summary.
. The image loads from, then stores a fixed value to, the first word past the MMR register map.
. Control: the image loads from the mapped `OTP_EFUSE_MMR` `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH` register in the same OTP window.
. The host reads `EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME` again on its SEP-side window.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KM-BUS-ERR-LIVE
|As <<sep_km_rom_write_err_test>>.
|CHK-KM-SHIM-HOST-PATH
|Control. The host read of `EFUSE_BANK_INIT_TIME` is OKAY at its RDL reset, and the seeded value the host writes reads back, so the no-alias rows below can fail.
|CHK-KM-DECERR-LIVE
|Control. A KM load from the Reserved ROM-growth row sets `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_DECERR`, so the bit and the image's poll work.
|CHK-KM-OTP-WINDOW-LIVE
|Control. A KM load from the mapped `OTP_EFUSE_MMR` `SEC_DISABLE_TOKEN_MATCH` register sets no AXI error bit, so the OTP window answers a mapped offset and a SLVERR below is about the offset past the MMR register map.
|CHK-KM-OTP-SHIM-EXCLUDED
|The KM load and the KM store each set `AXI_SLVERR` and not `AXI_DECERR`; the load does not return the host value; the shim register still holds the host value after the KM store. All four observations are logged before the verdict.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

*Refusal code*: SLVERR. The Key Manager answers every offset of the 4 KB OTP/eFuse window (`0x0001_1000`–`0x0001_1FFF`) outside the MAP, CTRL and MMR register maps itself, with SLVERR and read data `0xBADCAB1E`.

'''''

[[sep_km_drbg_sampler_timeout_reset_test]]
===== 7.20 `sep_km_drbg_sampler_timeout_reset_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+esrc_noise_force`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_drbg_sampler.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_drbg_sampler_timeout_reset_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image, DRBG scoreboard (`CHK5_km` golden)

*Randomization*: Directed. The image carries one `CFG.TIMEOUT` value.

*Objective*: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc` (DRBG sampler register behavior, Errors and reset) and `km_drbg_sampler.rdl`: an active `DATA` read times out after exactly `CFG.TIMEOUT` active cycles and completes with SLVERR and zero data, sets the sticky `STATUS.TIMEOUT_ERR`, increments `COUNT_BAD` and pulses `IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR`; `TIMEOUT = 0` disables the timeout; `ST_REQUEST` asserts TREADY; a warm KM reset restores configuration defaults, counters, error bits and request control. The sequenced KM reset is the KM warm reset (`reset_controller.adoc`, Resetting the Key Manager), and `BOOT_STATUS.COLD_BOOT_DONE` survives it (`km_csr.rdl`).

*Test Coverage*: sampler `ST_TIMEOUT`, a warm reset in `ST_REQUEST`, and `ST_RESPOND` under the same budget.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Release the KM into `km_rom_drbg_sampler` with CSRNG and EDN at their reset (disabled), so no beat reaches the KM. The host records every TREADY run on `km_entropy_tready_o`.
. Timeout leg: the image sets `CFG.TIMEOUT` = 40, clears the watched `IRQ_STATUS` bits and reads `DATA`.
. Reset leg: the image writes `CFG` = 0 and reads `DATA`. The host waits four reset timeout budgets, then parks and releases the KM `SW_RESET_N` bit.
. The image takes its warm path and stores `BOOT_STATUS`, `CFG`, `STATUS` and `IRQ_STATUS` as found.
. Control leg: the host brings up the entropy chain; the image sets the same `CFG.TIMEOUT`, waits for `STATUS.DRBG_READY` and reads `DATA`.
. After the control leg, the host reads CSRNG `ERR_CODE` and `RECOV_ALERT_STS` and EDN `ERR_CODE` and `RECOV_ALERT_STS`.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SMP-TIMEOUT
|TREADY is high for exactly `CFG.TIMEOUT` cycles with no handshake; `DATA` reads 0; the load sets `AXI_SLVERR` and `DRBG_ERR`; `STATUS` has `TIMEOUT_ERR` 1, `STREAM_ERR` 0, `COUNT_BAD` 1, `COUNT_GOOD` 0. Precondition: `STATUS` read its RDL reset before the read.
|CHK-SMP-NO-TIMEOUT
|With `CFG` = 0 the read holds TREADY with no handshake for four reset timeout budgets and does not complete.
|CHK-SMP-WARM-CANCEL
|The KM park drops TREADY while the read is pending, and TREADY stays low while the reset is held.
|CHK-SMP-WARM-RESET
|The image runs its warm path (`COLD_BOOT_DONE` 1). `CFG` and `STATUS` read their RDL resets and `IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR` is clear, although the image left `CFG` = 0 and `TIMEOUT_ERR`, `COUNT_BAD` and `DRBG_ERR` set before the reset.
|CHK-SMP-CONTROL
|Control for CHK-SMP-TIMEOUT: the same budget with `DRBG_READY` 1 completes with one handshake in less than the budget; `DATA` equals the word on the KM AXI-Stream; no watched `IRQ_STATUS` bit sets; `STATUS` has `COUNT_GOOD` 1 and no error.
|CHK-ALERTS-ZERO
|After the control leg, CSRNG `ERR_CODE` and `RECOV_ALERT_STS` and EDN `ERR_CODE` and `RECOV_ALERT_STS` each read zero, so the control beat came from a chain with no error. The shared base-test step `check_entropy_alerts_zero` (`cocotb/tests/sep_base_test.py`) reads and grades them.
|===

CHK-SMP-TIMEOUT, CHK-SMP-NO-TIMEOUT, CHK-SMP-WARM-CANCEL and CHK-SMP-CONTROL each also fail on any cycle in their window with TREADY X/Z, or TVALID X/Z while TREADY is not low, and log that count.

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes, and the DRBG scoreboard reports PASS. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

*Scope limit*: `ST_STREAM_ERR` is not reached. The only `TVALID` withdrawal the specification describes is a TRNG reset while the KM is not quiesced, and `architecture.adoc` tells software to quiesce the KM first.

'''''

[[sep_km_vrom_decerr_test]]
===== 7.21 `sep_km_vrom_decerr_test`

*Phase*: 2

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, `+skip_fuse_sense`, `+km_rom_hex=km_rom_bus_err.parhex`

*Source Module*: `sep_km_vrom_decerr_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip, KM CPU image

*Randomization*: RAND-NONE — one directed load.

*Objective*: The memory-map summary in `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/key_manager.rdl` does not list the virtual ROM (VROM) window `0x1000_0000`–`0x1000_FFFF` and ends with "Every other address | Reserved/Unmapped | DECERR". The KM CPU wrapper (`hw/ip/key_manager/rtl/picorv32_wrapper.sv`) decodes the window only when the simulation-only `OCAH_KM_VROM` define is set. SEP DV does not set it, so the SEP build must send the range to the AXI crossbar like any other unmapped address. This leaf grades the RDL summary: a KM CPU load from `0x1000_0000` must complete and answer DECERR.

*Test Coverage*: KM CPU access to the VROM range, which the `key_manager.rdl` memory-map summary does not list and the SEP build does not decode.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. The host releases the KM into `km_rom_bus_err` (see <<sep_km_rom_write_err_test>> for the clear-then-poll rule of each row). The virtual ROM load is the image's last access.
. Control: a KM load from the Reserved ROM-growth row, as in <<sep_km_otp_shim_unreachable_test>>.
. The image stores a pre-load marker to KM SRAM word 0, loads from the virtual ROM base, stores the data and the polled `IRQ_STATUS`, then stores a post-load marker.
. The host waits for the post-load marker for a fixed bound after the pre-load marker.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KM-BUS-ERR-LIVE
|As <<sep_km_rom_write_err_test>>.
|CHK-KM-DECERR-LIVE
|Control. A KM load from the Reserved ROM-growth row sets `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_DECERR`, so an erroring load completes on this bus and the bit reports it.
|CHK-KM-VROM-PRE
|The image reached the virtual ROM load, with the watched `IRQ_STATUS` bits clear just before it.
|CHK-KM-VROM-DECERR
|The post-load marker appears within the bound, and the load set `IRQ_STATUS.AXI_DECERR`. The claim is completion, so expiry of the bound is a failure, graded against an image shown alive up to the load.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. `UVM_ERROR` is 0.

'''''

[[sep-vplan-phase3-key-manager]]
==== 7. Key Manager vault, Phase 3

The Phase 3 tests of the Key Manager extend the open Key Manager tests (`hw/sys/sep/dv/testlists/km.toml`) with residual cells, corner cases, error injection and randomized legs. One Phase 3 test owns each family of cells that share a stimulus and a run mode. A test names the open test that it extends and grades only the cells that the open test does not grade. A cell that an open test grades whole runs as a control.

The specification and the register description are the only golden. No RTL and no existing test is a golden. A cell that the specification leaves open is not graded and is not drawn at random. <<sep-vplan-phase3-lists,The combined lists of Phase 3>> name those cells.

Every entry names the probes, the injects and the preloads that it uses. An item is *approved* when `hw/sys/sep/dv/docs/SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` lists it or when it is an *existing bench hook*, an item that `hw/sys/sep/dv/tb/tb_top.sv` has (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>). A *preload* is KM ROM content (a KM image, its parameter block, or a `rom_main` build) that the approved image load `tb_backdoor_mem` of `tb_top.sv` (`+km_rom_hex`) puts into the KM ROM macro at t=0, before the KM leaves reset. A preload is approved and is not a runtime drive. An error inject or a top-port drive that `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` does not list and `tb_top.sv` does not have is an *added hook* (<<sep-vplan-credit-rules>>): the test adds it to the inject exceptions or to the HDL Top port table of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, and it blocks no check. A drive, force or deposit of a net inside `sep` for a purpose other than error injection is *PROPOSED* when neither document has it. A check that needs a PROPOSED item is *blocked* and is marked `Blocked: needs <item>`. A blocked check is not a reached check, and no run of it is evidence. A read-only tap that `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` does not list is an *observation tap* that the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. An observation tap is not PROPOSED and blocks no check. <<sep-vplan-km-proposed>> lists the added hooks, the preloads, the PROPOSED drive and the observation taps.

===== Conventions that apply to every test

====== Vehicles

HOST::
Run mode `no_cpu`, target `lsu_stub_all_live`, KM ROM image `rom_main` (the approved bench build, `KM_BOOT_WIPE=0 KM_UNREC_WIPE=0`), entropy noise forced, real fuse sense. The testbench LSU master is the SEP host. It reaches the KM only through `km_mailbox_sep` (hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc). The response frame is the evidence of a command result, and an engine output checked against an independent golden is the evidence that a key arrived. A timeout is a leaf FAIL and not a verdict about the contract.

HOST-UNREC::
Preload (approved). HOST with `rom_main` built with `KM_BOOT_WIPE=0 KM_UNREC_WIPE=1` and preloaded through `+km_rom_hex` by the image load `tb_backdoor_mem`: the weak predicate `rom_unrec_wipe_enabled()` of the fatal handler is on and the boot wipe is off (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc, section "Error Recovery": the sideload wipe and the SRAM wipe of the fatal handler share that predicate, and the report and the trap are outside it). The approved bench build of `rom_main` is built with `KM_BOOT_WIPE=0 KM_UNREC_WIPE=0` (`SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, section "Key Manager `rom_main` Firmware Builds"), and the test adds the HOST-UNREC build to that section. A check that grades the sideload shred or the SRAM shred of the fatal handler runs in a leaf of this profile. A leaf of that profile logs `ROM-BUILD km_boot_wipe=0 km_unrec_wipe=1`.

HOST-BOOTWIPE::
Preload (approved). HOST with `rom_main` built with `KM_BOOT_WIPE=1 KM_UNREC_WIPE=0` and preloaded through `+km_rom_hex` by the image load `tb_backdoor_mem`; the test adds the build to the section "Key Manager `rom_main` Firmware Builds" of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. The boot sequence shreds the KPV, the engine banks and the Adams Bridge shared key, and then continues: it initializes the message buffers and the key registry, flushes both mailbox FIFOs, sets `COLD_BOOT_DONE` and sends `RESP_KM_READY` (`firmware.adoc`, section "Boot Sequence": the wiping step comes before these steps, and `RESP_KM_READY` is the boot-complete barrier; section "Initial Boot"). The specification states no halt in the boot sequence. A boot of this profile that sends no `RESP_KM_READY` within the bounded wait therefore fails the leaf on that contract (CHK-WIPE-READY of `sep_km_boot_reset_rand_test`). `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` (same section) states that a default build, with both wipes on, halts before `RESP_KM_READY` on this bench; that statement does not change the contract of this profile. A leaf of that profile logs `ROM-BUILD km_boot_wipe=1 km_unrec_wipe=0`.

POKE::
Run mode `no_cpu`, a KM image of the class of `km_rom_vault` (`km_rom_blob`) that executes a list of KM bus operations (read, write, back-to-back write, poll, read loop, trap, jump, interrupt arm with a counting handler) as a legal master of the KM bus. The host posts the list through the mailbox, and the image reports each result (data, `slv`, `dec`) in KM SRAM words 0 to 97. An op marked raw does not clear IRQ_STATUS bits 4 (AXI_SLVERR) and 5 (AXI_DECERR) before it and reports no `slv` and `dec`. `+skip_fuse_sense` unless the entry says real fuse sense. The SRAM scrambler stays off.

Scripted images::
IMG-H, IMG-MBOX-KM and IMG-SCR are KM images of the same class for scenarios that need scripted KM actions. IMG-H is streamed by CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC and reports through the KM mailbox. IMG-MBOX-KM reports through the KM mailbox and KM SRAM words 0 to 97. IMG-SCR reports through the KM mailbox.

The KM CPU does not see the AXI response code. It sees the read data and the IRQ_STATUS bits AXI_SLVERR and AXI_DECERR (hw/ip/key_manager/doc/index.adoc, section "Memory Map"). Every POKE result of an op that is not raw carries the two bits as `slv` and `dec`.

A KM key write to an engine that is held in its `SW_RESET_N` reset completes with SLVERR (hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc, the software reset sequence), and the KM reports AXI_SLVERR as a fatal source. Every procedure therefore leaves all engines out of reset before the first key transfer or key write.

KM release. Every step that releases the KM (`SW_RESET_N` bit 0 high) after a cold reset or a fuse sense first waits (bounded; a timeout fails the leaf) for the `sep_reset_n` release on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (approved), which comes after the sense done. `sep_reset_n` resets the Reset Controller register block, so a `SW_RESET_N` write before that release is lost (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller). This holds for the first bring-up of every entry, including a step that reads only "Release the KM".

====== Observation points and hooks

Approved items. Each exists in `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc` or in `tb_top.sv`.

[cols="2,6",options="header"]
|===
|Item |What it shows
|`HOST-MBX` |The SEP-side mailbox registers and the frames on `SEP_WRITE_DATA` and `SEP_READ_DATA`, through the host LSU master. Frontdoor.
|`KM-RESULT` |`km_sram_word0_o` and `km_sram_probe_o`: KM SRAM words 0 to 97, the words that a KM image writes.
|`KM-COUNT` |`km_rom_req_count_o`, `km_sram_req_count_o` and `km_sram_write_count_o`.
|`KM-SRAM-MON` |`km_sram_rd_*` read-response timing counters, which count the one-cycle response only, and `km_sram_scr_wr_*`, the count, the physical address, the data and the array word of the first four scrambled SRAM writes.
|`KM-ENTROPY` |`km_entropy_tvalid_o`, `km_entropy_tdata_o` and `km_entropy_tready_o`, the KM sink of the entropy stream.
|`KM-ISO` |`hmac_km_isolated_probe_o`, `kmac_km_isolated_probe_o` and `abr_km_isolated_probe_o`.
|`PIC-IRQ` |`sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o`. Bit i is PIC source i+1: bit 14 is the KM mailbox, bit 30 is `km_unrecoverable_err_o` and bit 31 is `km_recoverable_err_o` (hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc; hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc, section "SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map").
|`OTP-TAP` |`secure_tm_o`, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, `efuse_shadow_probe_o` and `km_otp_sep_*_id_o`.
|`ENGINE-OUT` |The output of AES and HMAC, read through the host path and compared with an independent golden. Both witnesses show that an engine holds a key. Only the HMAC witness shows that an engine holds none (its digest equals the golden of its software key); the output of an AES without a key is not stated, so an AES result other than the golden is logged and never graded as no key.
|===

The added hooks, the preloads, the PROPOSED drive and the observation taps are in <<sep-vplan-km-proposed>>. A hook drives the stated net and is never a golden. A preload is ROM content and is never a golden. A tap is read only.

The coverage sampler of `SEP_FCOV.adoc` reads the same nets and the nets of `KM-COV`. The registers that KM-CSR adds beyond `IRQ_STATUS`, `IRQ_ENABLE`, `COLD_BOOT_DONE` and the scrambler registers, the ABR `IRQ_STATUS.key_valid` and `IRQ_ENABLE.key_valid_en` of KM-VALID, the write data and read data of KM-FETCH, and the nets of `KM-COV` serve the coverage points only. No check of this plan uses them. CHK-HO-EXEC uses the write strobe of KM-FETCH, and CHK-HO-CONFIRM uses `IRQ_ENABLE` of KM-CSR.

====== Logging and verdict

* Each check prints `CHK-<NAME> PASS <key>=<value> ...` on pass and `CHK-<NAME> FAIL ...` on fail. The test prints `SEED seed=<S>` first and `DRAW <name>=<value>` for every drawn value. A missing expected CHK line fails the test. A clean simulator exit is not evidence. The verdict comes from `results.xml`.
* A blocked check (marked `Blocked: needs <item>`) has no checker code. The leaf logs `OBS-BLOCKED <check> reason=<item>` and no `CHK-<NAME> PASS`. The line is neither a pass nor a fail, is not evidence and is not counted as a reached check.
* The values in a logged-proof line show the field format. A contract value (a return code, a register value) is fixed by the specification. A value that depends on the seed is an example and is not evidence.
* A line with `control=1` or `LOG` is a control. It shows that the scenario ran and cannot fail the leaf on the controlled value.
* Every check that proves "no effect" or "never" has a control in the same test, where a healthy design drives the same signal. A test with no control hit fails `CHK-NONVAC`.
* A negative stimulus value differs from the legal value. The test asserts the difference before it sends the stimulus.
* A fatal fault halts the KM CPU until a reset, so a test draws one fatal point for each KM boot. A warm reset is SW_RESET_N bit 0 low, then high, with the other bits at 1 (reset value 0x7E, hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc, section "SW_RESET_N"). A test never ends with `km_sw_rst_n` at 0.
* The TRNG is not reset while the KM runs (hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc, section "Resetting the TRNG").

====== Randomization rules

* Every random value is a pure function of the seed, drawn from the seeded stream of the environment. `--stage sim --seed N` replays a leaf.
* RAND-REP: the seed draws points from a legal matrix, and every point has a stated outcome. RANDCFG: the seed draws a configuration, the test runs one scenario under it, and the expected outcome is a function of the configuration. RAND-NONE: the stimulus is directed, for the reason the entry states. A test with two classes names the dimensions of each class.
* The stream is not a CSPRNG. Key words, UIDs, class keys and tokens in a test are labelled test vectors that live only in the simulation. No per-seed value is quoted as evidence.
* A draw never sets SYS_DIS or any disable-vector bit. Bit 0 is SEP_DBG and it admits all inbound traffic (hw/sys/sep/doc/fabric.adoc, section "Filtering and Protection").
* Every drawn key word is non-zero, and the first share-width words are pairwise different, so "cleared" and "loaded" never look equal.
* Unstated outcomes are not drawn and not graded. <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>> lists them. A test may log the response to such a cell.
* A costly cell (an extra boot, a fatal point, a key transfer of real size) is drawn from the seed, and a cheap register-level cell is walked in every leaf. A drawn cell fills on a merge of regressions, as `SEP_FCOV.adoc` <<sep-fcov-rand>> states, and no entry claims that one regression fills it. The seed count stays at 3 and is not raised to fill cells.

====== Run mode and testlist

Every test runs in run mode `no_cpu`, target `lsu_stub_all_live`, and joins the testlist groups `all` and `cpu_stub` in hw/sys/sep/dv/testlists/km.toml with one testlist entry, except `sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`, which has one entry for each of its two preloaded `rom_main` builds (HOST and HOST-UNREC), because the build is part of the run mode. No test has a per-cell testlist variant. No test needs the EL2 firmware, so no test is in group `cpu`.

[[sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test]]
===== 7.22 `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle HOST (Key Manager image `rom_main`, real fuse sense, `+esrc_noise_force`, `+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`), 3 seeds, Verilator and VCS. Testlist: groups `all` and `cpu_stub`, `hw/sys/sep/dv/testlists/km.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (the CPU-LSU master is the SEP host) on the `km_mailbox_sep` window (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`); a host frame builder and response decoder; the CRC-8/ROHC and CRC-32C goldens of `env/sep_crc_golden.py` (parameters from `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, section "CRC Function Library"); a sequence model that holds the expected command sequence number e and the response sequence number r; a model of `SEP_STATUS`, `SEP_IRQ_STATUS` and probe bit 14 written from the register text. Probes and injects. Approved: `HOST-MBX` (`SEP_WRITE_DATA`, `SEP_WRITE_SEPARATOR`, `SEP_READ_DATA`, `SEP_STATUS`, `SEP_IRQ_STATUS`, `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE` and `SEP_CTRL` through the host LSU master) and `PIC-IRQ` (`sep_internal_interrupts_probe_o` bits 14 and 31). `KM-VALID` and `KM-KPV` (CHK-NOCHG-VAULT only) are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. Injects: none. The 257-word frame, the SEP flush and the slow host are legal host stimulus.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed (RAND-REP: the seed draws points from the legal matrices and every point has a stated outcome). The draws are: the fault kind, the forced pick, the pair, the wrong value, the payload length of kind (e) in [1, 255], the wire length of kind (d) in [1, 257] words and its PAYLOAD_LEN, the carrier, the command, the bad field and the bad value, DEST_VALID with an ABR bit, the order of the three transport causes, the position of each cause after the previous cause in [10, 40] frames, the fault source, the order of the gate matrix, the host delay before CMD_RECOV_ACK in {0, 100, 2000} clocks, Q in [2, 24], the word gap in {0, 1, 5, 40} clocks, the read delay in {0, 100, 2000} clocks and the order of the three non-empty subsets of `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE` bits [1:0]. Legal ranges: PAYLOAD_LEN 0 to 255, wire length of kind (d) 1 to 257 words (the receive buffer holds 257 words, so a frame of that length with the separator on the last word is complete), command ID 0x00 to 0xFF, KEY_SIZE 0 to 127, DEST_VALID 1 to 255, the other `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE` bits 0, `SEP_CTRL` bit 2 clear in every configuration write, every payload word non-zero. `reseed = 3` samples different draws. The three transport causes (`rx_buff_oflow`, SEP flush and warm reset), the forced values, both fault sources of the gate matrix and the three handover twins run on every seed.

*Objective*: A complete command frame passes the header CRC, the sequence number, the command ID, the frame length, the payload CRC, the recovery gate and the dispatch length check in that order. The first failing check returns its own code and argument. `header_crc` and `cmd_noseq` leave the expected sequence number unchanged. Every later rejection advances it by one. CMD_KEY_GENERATE, CMD_KEY_LOAD and CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER reject reserved bits and a zero destination mask with invalid_arg and the stated word index. The command sequence counter and the response sequence counter are separate 8-bit counters. Each starts at 0 and rolls over to 0 after 255. A receive-buffer overflow (`rx_buff_oflow`), a SEP flush and a warm reset reset both counters. While RECOVERABLE_ERR is set, only CMD_HW_VER, CMD_ROM_VER, CMD_SRAM_VER, CMD_STAT and CMD_RECOV_ACK reach their handlers. Every other valid command returns failure with no argument, and frame validation still runs first. After `rx_buff_oflow` or a SEP flush the KM sends one RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT with no paired RESP_CMD, the first response has sequence 0, commands resume at sequence 0, and CMD_RECOV_ACK clears RECOVERABLE_ERR. A frame of 257 words with the separator on the last word is not a transport fault. The mailbox keeps the response order when the host is slow. `SEP_STATUS` shows the separator of the last word read. The level sources follow the FIFO state. A SEP flush with responses waiting leaves one fault frame and none of the waiting words. Sources: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc` (sections "Command Message Reference", "0x00 - RESP_CMD", "0x22 - CMD_KEY_GENERATE" through "0x27 - CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER", "Error Recovery", "Incoming Message Handler", "Message Buffers", "Mailbox"); `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc` (section "Mailbox Transport"); `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_mailbox_sep.adoc` (`SEP_STATUS`, `SEP_WRITE_SEPARATOR`, `SEP_READ_DATA`, `SEP_IRQ_STATUS`, `SEP_CTRL`); `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc` (`RECOVERABLE_ERR`, `VERSION`); `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc` (section "SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map").

*Test Coverage*: Frame faults of six kinds, singly and as 13 ordered pairs, with forced corner values, and the sequence update after each rejection. Argument faults of CMD_KEY_GENERATE, CMD_KEY_LOAD and CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER, with a positive twin of each command and a DEST_VALID walk over the ABR bits 4 to 7. A wrap pass of more than 256 frames. The three transport causes on every seed, in drawn order (an overflowing partial frame, a SEP flush, a warm reset), with CMD_RECOV_ACK and no extra boot between them, each with the old counter value refused. The recovery handshake after `rx_buff_oflow` and after a SEP flush. The gate matrix (five admitted commands, ten gated commands) after each of the two fault sources, the validation order under the gate, the clear by CMD_RECOV_ACK and by a warm reset, and the gate-clear twin of each gated command. The 257-word frame with the separator on the last word. The mailbox transport: order under a full outbound FIFO, separator reads, `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE` subsets with a response waiting and after the drain, and a SEP flush with two unread responses. Error cases: the malformed frames and arguments, the 257 words with no separator, the wrong sequence number after a reset cause and the SEP flush are error injection (see the procedure). The test extends the open tests. `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` grades each single-fault code (header CRC, sequence, command ID, length, payload CRC), CMD_STAT after every rejection, and the SEP flush with its fault frame, the latched recov_fault, aggregator bit 31 and CMD_RECOV_ACK. `sep_km_wipe_reset_test` and `sep_km_handover_test` grade the restart at sequence 0 after a `SW_RESET_N` park. `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test` grades the refusal of CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER with no shared key pending. `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test` grades the inbound flush, the inbound_write_space_avail level and aggregator bit 14 with an enable, with the KM held. The frame receive check of every open KM host test grades the response sequence +1 and the per-word separator pattern. The Phase 3 test `sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test` grades the echoed size field and DEST_VALID of every key command and the first handle of a boot, and the Phase 3 test `sep_km_mailbox_km_side_rand_test` grades the empty outbound FIFO after a SEP flush. Those cells run here as controls, only the new cells are graded, and each owner keeps the rest. The new cells are the fault-pair order, the forced values, the kind (d) code and its sequence update, the argument rejections, the wrap of both counters, the `rx_buff_oflow` leg, the refusal of the old counter value, the gate matrix, the validation order, the persistence of the output, the end-to-end recovery handshake, the outbound separator hold, the inbound separator, the order under a full FIFO, the outbound level source and the fault frame after an outbound flush with pending responses. Functional coverage: `sep_km_frame_validation_cg` (`cp_fv_first_fail`, `cp_fv_pair_order`, `cp_fv_seq`, `cp_km_seq_wrap`, `cp_fv_counter_reset`, `cp_fv_invalid_id_gap`, `cp_fv_plen`, `cp_fv_arg_kind`, `cp_fv_rx_buffer`, `cp_fv_reserved_bits`), `sep_km_cmd_reject_cg` (`cp_gate`, `cp_gate_cmd`, `cp_load_arg_order`), `sep_km_command_cg` (`cp_cmd_x_outcome`, `cp_ret_code`, `cp_resp_shape`, `cp_stat_recov`), `sep_km_mailbox_fault_cg` (`cp_mbf_flush`, `cp_mbf_raw_transport`), `sep_km_mailbox_transport_cg` (`cp_mbt_access`, `cp_mbt_occupancy`, `cp_mbt_separator`, `cp_mbt_irq_src_x_en`, `cp_mbt_irq_aggregate`, `cp_mbt_backpressure`), `sep_km_kmcsr_status_cg` (`cp_csr_recov_err`), `sep_km_fault_report_cg` (`cp_fr_code_encoding`, `cp_fr_rec_code`, `cp_fr_resp_pairing`, `cp_fr_recovery_handshake`) and `sep_km_secret_nonexposure_cg` (`cp_nx_permitted_channel`). The test also hits the Phase 1 groups `sep_km_host_cmd_cg` (`cp_cmd`), `sep_km_generate_cg` (`cp_generate`) and `sep_mailbox_pic_cg` (`cp_km_raise`, `cp_km_clear`), and adds no bin to them.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage and sense a PROD fuse image for real. Bring the entropy source up with noise forced. Leave every engine out of reset (the reset value of `SW_RESET_N` bits 6:1 is 1), because a key write that meets an engine held in reset ends in an AXI error (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, section "Isolation and Reset Sequencing"). Before the release, read `SEP_STATUS` and record `inbound_separator` (CHK-SEP-IN): the KM has read no inbound word since the cold reset, so the bit holds its reset value 0 (`km_mailbox_sep.adoc`, `SEP_STATUS`). Release the KM (`SW_RESET_N` bit 0) and wait for `RESP_KM_READY`. Every wait in this procedure is a bounded wait, and a timeout fails the leaf. Print `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for every drawn value.
. Set the models: r = 1 (the RESP_KM_READY frame carries response sequence 0) and e = 0. Every warm reset in this procedure sets both models again after its RESP_KM_READY.
. Define the host routines. The frame writer builds {HEADER_CRC8, PAYLOAD_LEN, COMMAND_ID, CMD_SEQ_NUM}, the payload words and, when PAYLOAD_LEN is above 0, PAYLOAD_CRC32. It writes `SEP_WRITE_SEPARATOR.set` = 1 before the last word and writes a word only while `SEP_STATUS.inbound_full` reads 0. The response reader pops the header, then PAYLOAD_LEN payload words and the CRC, each after a bounded wait on `SEP_IRQ_STATUS.outbound_read_data_avail`. The response check compares the header CRC-8, RESPONSE_ID 0x00, the echoed CMD_SEQ_NUM and COMMAND_ID, the return code in bits [7:0] with bits [31:8] equal to 0, the argument and the response sequence with r, and then increments r. A frame fault of every kind below first asserts that the wrong value differs from the right value.
. Frame-fault leg, kinds (a), (b) and (c). Error injection, point: the header of the command frame on `SEP_WRITE_DATA`; kind: (a) wrong HEADER_CRC8, (b) wrong CMD_SEQ_NUM, (c) COMMAND_ID outside the valid IDs; expected response: RESP_CMD -2 with the CRC-8/ROHC of the header as sent, -3 with the next expected sequence number, -4 with no argument. Send, with a drawn carrier from CMD_HW_VER, CMD_ROM_VER and CMD_RECOV_ACK, (a) a wrong HEADER_CRC8, with the forced values right XOR 0x01, right XOR 0x80 and 0x00 or 0xFF (whichever differs), (b) a wrong CMD_SEQ_NUM, with the forced values e+1, e-1 and 255 (whichever differs from e), and (c) a COMMAND_ID outside the 15 valid IDs, with the forced edges of each ID gap 0x05, 0x0F, 0x13, 0x21, 0x29 and 0xFF. Add drawn values of each kind. Check the code and argument of each response and update e: unchanged after (a) and (b), plus one after (c).
. Frame-fault leg, kind (d). Error injection, point: PAYLOAD_LEN of the command frame; kind: PAYLOAD_LEN contradicts the number of words on the wire; expected response: RESP_CMD -5. Send frames whose PAYLOAD_LEN differs from the number of words on the wire (the header CRC is right for the header as sent, the separator is on the last word), with a drawn wire length L in [1, 257] words (separator on the last word, non-zero payload words), a drawn PAYLOAD_LEN in [0, 255] with 1 + PAYLOAD_LEN + (1 if PAYLOAD_LEN is above 0, else 0) not equal to L, and a carrier from CMD_HW_VER, CMD_ROM_VER and CMD_RECOV_ACK (valid command IDs, so that the command ID check passes). Check the code -5. Log the argument. Update e: plus one.
. Frame-fault leg, kind (e). Error injection, point: PAYLOAD_LEN of a self-consistent frame; kind: PAYLOAD_LEN differs from the length that the command requires, the 257-word frame included; expected response: RESP_CMD -5 with the received payload length. Send self-consistent frames (right header CRC, right payload CRC-32C, non-zero payload words) whose PAYLOAD_LEN differs from the length that the command requires, with carriers CMD_HW_VER, CMD_ROM_VER and CMD_RECOV_ACK (required length 0). Use the forced PAYLOAD_LEN values 1, 2, 3, 254 and 255 and drawn values in [1, 255]. Check the code -5 and the argument (the received length). Update e: plus one. The frame with PAYLOAD_LEN 255 has 257 words with the separator on the last word.
. Frame-fault leg, kind (f). Error injection, point: PAYLOAD_CRC32 of the command frame; kind: one flipped bit in a byte lane; expected response: RESP_CMD -6 with the CRC-32C of the received payload. Send CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0x80 and a wrong PAYLOAD_CRC32: the forced values are the right CRC with one bit flipped in each of the four byte lanes, and drawn values. Check the code -6 and the argument (the CRC-32C of the received payload). Update e: plus one.
. Send the twin of each kind with every field right: CMD_HW_VER, CMD_ROM_VER and CMD_RECOV_ACK each return success, and CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0x80 returns success with argument 0x80. Update e.
. Pair leg. Error injection, point: two fields of one command frame; kind: two frame faults; expected response: RESP_CMD with the code and argument of the earlier check. For each of the 13 ordered pairs of kinds (a), (b), (c), (d), (f), (e), in this order of checks (the pairs (c, e) and (d, e) have no valid frame and are not drawn), send one frame with both faults. Use CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0x80 as the carrier of every pair that contains kind (f), with PAYLOAD_LEN other than 1 for the pair (f, e), and CMD_HW_VER otherwise. Check that the response is the code and argument of the earlier check. Update e by the rule of the earlier kind. Send the twin with only the later fault, check the later code and update e by the rule of the later kind.
. Send CMD_STAT and check that it succeeds with bit 0 of the status word equal to 0 and bits [31:1] equal to 0. Sample `KM-KPV` and `KM-VALID` after every rejection of the frame-fault leg, the pair leg and the argument leg below, and require every KPV CTRL bit and every key_valid bit to read 0.
. Argument leg, CMD_KEY_GENERATE. Error injection, point: REQ_SIZE or DEST_VALID of CMD_KEY_GENERATE; kind: reserved bit set or zero mask; expected response: RESP_CMD -7 with argument 0 for REQ_SIZE and argument 1 for DEST_VALID. Send frames with a self-consistent payload and one bad word: REQ_SIZE with bit 7 set, with bit 8 set, with bit 31 set and with a drawn value of 128 or more (legal DEST_VALID), and DEST_VALID equal to 0, equal to 0x100, equal to 0x8000_0001 and a drawn value with reserved bits set (legal REQ_SIZE). Check -7 with argument 0 for REQ_SIZE and argument 1 for DEST_VALID. Send one frame with both words bad and log the code and argument without grading them.
. Argument leg, CMD_KEY_LOAD. Error injection, point: PAYLOAD_LEN, KEY_SIZE and DEST_VALID of CMD_KEY_LOAD; kind: a fault of one validation check; expected response: RESP_CMD -7 with the word index of the first failing check. Send self-consistent frames, one first failure each, in the order of the validation table (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x26 - CMD_KEY_LOAD"): (1) PAYLOAD_LEN 0, 1 or 2, (2) a KEY_SIZE word with a reserved bit set, (3) a legal KEY_SIZE with PAYLOAD_LEN other than KEY_SIZE+3, (4) a DEST_VALID word with a reserved bit set, (5) DEST_VALID equal to 0. Check -7 with argument 0 for the checks 1 to 3 and argument 1 for the checks 4 and 5. Then send pairs of one fault of the checks 1 to 3 and one fault of the checks 4 and 5 in one frame (PAYLOAD_LEN 2 carries both words for the check 1). Check argument 0, and send the twin with only the later fault and check argument 1.
. Argument leg, CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER. Error injection, point: DEST_VALID and PAYLOAD_LEN of CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER; kind: zero or reserved-bit mask, wrong payload length; expected response: RESP_CMD -7 with argument 0 and -5 with the received payload length. With no shared key pending, send DEST_VALID equal to 0 and a drawn DEST_VALID with a reserved bit set. Check -7 with argument 0. Send frames with 0 and with 2 payload words and a right payload CRC. Check -5 with the received payload length.
. Positive twins. Send CMD_KEY_GENERATE with 5 words (REQ_SIZE field 4) and DEST_VALID 0x04, then CMD_KEY_LOAD with 4 words (KEY_SIZE field 3, non-zero words) and DEST_VALID 0xF0. Check success and that the first handle of the boot equals 1. After the CMD_KEY_GENERATE, sample `KM-KPV` (the control of CHK-NOCHG-VAULT). The echoed size field and mask are logged with `control=1`, because `sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test` grades them. Send CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER with a legal DEST_VALID and no shared key pending: check -1 with no argument.
. DEST_VALID walk. Send 12 CMD_KEY_LOAD frames with 4 words each, with the masks 0x10, 0x20, 0x40, 0x80, 0xF0, 0xFF and six drawn masks in [1, 255] that each contain at least one of the bits 4 to 7. Check success. Each echoed mask is compared with the sent mask and logged with `control=1`, because `sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test` grades the echo. Send CMD_STAT and check that it succeeds with bit 0 equal to 0.
. Wrap and transport-cause leg. Warm reset the KM through `SW_RESET_N` (bit 0 low, then high, the other bits 1) and wait for RESP_KM_READY. Send CMD_STAT with e = 0, then send CMD_STAT with sequence 0 at e = 1 and check -3 with argument 1 (control of the wrap check).
. Wrap pass: send 260 frames with carriers drawn from CMD_HW_VER, CMD_STAT and CMD_SRAM_VER, one response read for each frame, with no fault and no reset. The frame at e = 255 is followed by a frame with sequence 0. Check that the frame with sequence 0 succeeds and that the response after the response with sequence 255 has sequence 0.
. Transport-cause loop. This step and the next three run once for each of the three transport causes (`rx_buff_oflow`, SEP flush, warm reset), in drawn order. Before a cause that follows `rx_buff_oflow` or a SEP flush, send CMD_RECOV_ACK and check success, so that the gate of the previous cause is clear. The loop has no warm reset other than the warm-reset cause. Send p further frames, p drawn in [10, 40] for each cause, so that both counters hold a non-zero value. Record the command sequence number e_old that the next frame would carry.
. Apply the transport cause of the pass. Error injection, point: the KM mailbox FIFO, `SEP_CTRL` or `SW_RESET_N`; kind: an overflowing partial frame, a SEP flush or a warm reset; expected response: for the first two one RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT (-2 for `rx_buff_oflow`, -5 for the SEP flush) with sequence 0 and no paired RESP_CMD, for the warm reset RESP_KM_READY with sequence 0. For `rx_buff_oflow`: write 257 words with no separator (non-zero words, host writes only while `inbound_full` reads 0) and stop. For the SEP flush: read every response word, then write `SEP_CTRL.flush` = 1 with the KM idle (every command is answered) and read `SEP_CTRL` until the bit self-clears. For the warm reset: write `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 low and then high.
. For `rx_buff_oflow` and the SEP flush, read the fault frame (a bounded wait). For the warm reset, read RESP_KM_READY. The frame has response sequence 0. Set r = 1 and e = 0.
. Error injection, point: CMD_SEQ_NUM of the frame after the cause; kind: the old, non-zero counter value; expected response: RESP_CMD -3 with argument 0. Send CMD_STAT carrying the old sequence number e_old. Check -3 with argument 0, and that this response is the first frame after the fault frame or RESP_KM_READY. Then send CMD_STAT with sequence 0 and check success. On the `rx_buff_oflow` and flush causes the status word has bit 0 equal to 1.
. Gate-matrix leg. This step and the next seven run once for each of the two fault sources, in drawn order. Warm reset the KM, wait for RESP_KM_READY and set the models.
. Pre-fault window with the gate clear. Send CMD_KEY_GENERATE (DEST_VALID with bit 2), CMD_KEY_LOAD (DEST_VALID with bit 2), CMD_KEY_REVOKE of the generated key, CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of the loaded key to AES (bit 2), CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0x80 and CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD with mask 0x001. Check that each returns its success. After the CMD_KEY_TRANSFER to AES, sample `KM-VALID` (the control of CHK-NOCHG-VAULT). Then load one more key (8 words, DEST_VALID with bit 2) and keep its handle h. No command of the window uses h.
. Apply the fault source of the boot with the procedure of the transport-cause step (`rx_buff_oflow` or SEP flush), and read the fault frame. Check the code (-2 after `rx_buff_oflow`, -5 after the SEP flush, bits [31:8] all ones), response sequence 0 and the probe bit 31 equal to 1.
. Error injection, point: CMD_SEQ_NUM of the frame after the fault frame; kind: the old, non-zero counter value; expected response: RESP_CMD -3 with argument 0. Send CMD_STAT carrying the old sequence number e_old of the window. Check -3 with argument 0 and that this response is the first frame after the fault frame (no paired RESP_CMD). Send CMD_STAT with sequence 0: check success, bit 0 of the status word equal to 1, bits [31:1] equal to 0 and probe bit 31 equal to 1.
. Gate matrix. In drawn order, send CMD_HW_VER, CMD_ROM_VER, CMD_SRAM_VER, CMD_STAT and the ten gated commands with legal arguments: CMD_KEY_GENERATE, CMD_KEY_LOAD, CMD_KEY_REVOKE of h, CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of h to AES, CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0x80, CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD with mask 0x001, CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER, CMD_EXEC_ROM, CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC with FW_WORDS 0 and CMD_SRAM_EXEC. Check -1 with no argument for each gated command, and the admitted results of the four admitted commands. Sample probe bit 31 after each admitted command.
. Validation first. Send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of h to AES with a wrong header CRC and then with a wrong payload CRC, with the gate set. Check -2 and -6. Send the same frame with right CRCs and check -1.
. Wait the drawn host delay, then send CMD_RECOV_ACK. Check success with no argument. Send CMD_STAT and check bit 0 equal to 0 and probe bit 31 equal to 0.
. Gate-clear twins. Send each of the seven gated commands that can reach a handler again with the same arguments: CMD_KEY_GENERATE, CMD_KEY_LOAD, CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of h to AES, CMD_KEY_REVOKE of h (after the transfer, because the revoke destroys the handle), CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0x80, CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD with mask 0x001, and CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER. Check the success of the six and -1 with no argument for CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER (no shared key is pending).
. Handover-twin leg. This step and the next two run once for each of CMD_EXEC_ROM, CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC and CMD_SRAM_EXEC, each in its own boot. Warm reset the KM, wait for RESP_KM_READY, apply a drawn fault source and read the fault frame. Send the command as the first frame (sequence 0) with the gate set: CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC carries FW_WORDS 0. Check -1 with no argument.
. Warm reset the KM and wait for RESP_KM_READY. Send CMD_STAT and check bit 0 equal to 0 and probe bit 31 equal to 0.
. Send the same command as the first handover command of the epoch. Check success for CMD_EXEC_ROM, -7 with argument 0 for CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC (FW_WORDS 0) and -7 with no argument for CMD_SRAM_EXEC (no stored image).
. Transport leg. Warm reset the KM and wait for RESP_KM_READY. Write `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE` with the first subset of the drawn order of the three non-empty subsets of bits [1:0]. Every write to `SEP_CTRL` in the leg keeps bit 2 clear.
. Pacing and stall, one pass with Q = 2 and one pass with a drawn Q in [3, 24]. Draw the carriers from CMD_STAT, CMD_HW_VER and invalid_len carriers (CMD_HW_VER with a drawn PAYLOAD_LEN in [1, 6], non-zero payload words, right CRCs). Every response has six words. The host writes the Q frames in order, starts a frame only when `SEP_STATUS.inbound_depth` plus the frame length is at most 16, writes with the drawn word gap, and reads no response. If the inbound limit stops it, it writes the frames that remain during the drain of the next step. In the pass with Q = 2, once both frames are written, it waits until `SEP_STATUS.outbound_depth` reads 12 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). In a pass with a Q of 3 or more it waits until `SEP_STATUS.outbound_full` reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf), because three responses of six words exceed the 16-word FIFO. Then it waits the drawn read delay, reads `SEP_STATUS` and records `outbound_full`.
. Drain every response with the response reader, and write the frames that remain unsent with the same inbound limit and word gap between the response reads. After each word read `SEP_STATUS` and record `outbound_separator`. After the last word of each response read `SEP_STATUS` twice more. After the first word of the next response read it again. Check that the count of responses equals Q and that the echoed CMD_SEQ_NUM values are in command order.
. Inbound separator. The frame writer of the two steps above reads `SEP_WRITE_SEPARATOR` after the arm and again after the last word write of every frame. After the response of the last frame of the leg is read, wait until `SEP_STATUS.inbound_depth` reads 0 (a bounded wait), so that no inbound word is pending and the KM is idle, and then read `SEP_STATUS.inbound_separator` once. The other frames of the leg are not sampled, because the KM can be in the middle of a later frame.
. Level sources. For each of the three non-empty subsets of bits [1:0] (0x1, 0x2 and 0x3), in the drawn order, write `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE` with the subset and run this sequence: send one CMD_STAT and wait until `outbound_read_data_avail` reads 1. Read `SEP_IRQ_STATUS` and sample probe bit 14. Read the response. Read `SEP_IRQ_STATUS` and sample probe bit 14 again. Wait until `SEP_STATUS.inbound_depth` reads 0 and read `SEP_STATUS.inbound_separator` (the single-frame sample; taken in the first subset only). After the three subsets write `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE` = 0 and repeat the sequence with one more CMD_STAT (control: probe bit 14 reads 0 in both samples).
. Flush with traffic pending. Error injection, point: `SEP_CTRL.flush` with two responses waiting; kind: SEP flush; expected response: one RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT -5 with sequence 0. Send two CMD_STAT frames and read no response. Wait until `SEP_STATUS.outbound_depth` reads 12 (two responses of six words). Write `SEP_CTRL.flush` = 1 with the KM idle and read `SEP_CTRL` until the bit self-clears. Wait for the fault frame. Read the first word and check that its RESPONSE_ID is 0xFE, then read the outbound FIFO word by word until `SEP_STATUS.outbound_depth` reads 0. Check the code -5 and the response sequence 0 of the frame.
. Send CMD_STAT with sequence 0 and check bit 0 of the status word equal to 1. Send CMD_RECOV_ACK, then CMD_STAT, and check bit 0 equal to 0.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-FRAME
|For the forced values of kinds (a), (b) and (f) and for kind (e) the response is the RESP_CMD of the failing check with its argument: -2 with the CRC-8/ROHC of the header as sent, -3 with the next expected sequence number, -6 with the CRC-32C of the payload as sent, -5 with the received payload length (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, sections "0x00 - RESP_CMD" and "Incoming Message Handler"). For kind (d) only the code -5 is graded and the argument is logged (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The expected sequence number follows the sequence model: unchanged after -2 and -3, plus one after every other rejection, kind (d) included. A DUT that returns another code or argument, or that advances e after -2 or -3, or does not advance it after -4, -5 or -6, fails the next frame of the leg. An altered payload word turns the -5 of kind (e) into -6, so the code also shows that the payload words arrive unaltered. The CRC goldens follow the parameter tables of the specification and not the KM. Anchor: RESP_CMD readback and the sequence model. Control: each kind has a twin with every field right; its non-validation result is logged as `twin_code`. A random single fault of kinds (a), (b), (c), (e) and (f) logs `control=1` and cannot fail on its code. Evidence: `CHK-FRAME PASS seed=<n> kind=hdr_crc draw=forced code=-2 arg=0x<crc> golden=0x<crc> e=<e> e_after=<e> twin_code=0`, `CHK-FRAME PASS kind=len_self plen=254 code=-5 arg=254 e=<e> e_after=<e+1>` and `CHK-FRAME PASS kind=len_wire code=-5 arg_logged=<n> e=<e> e_after=<e+1>`.
|CHK-PAIR
|For each of the 13 ordered pairs the frame with two faults returns the code and argument of the earlier check in the order header CRC, sequence, command ID, frame length, payload CRC, dispatch length, and the twin with only the later fault returns the later code (`firmware.adoc`, section "Incoming Message Handler"). A DUT that answers the later check first fails the pair, and a DUT that returns the earlier code for the twin fails the twin. The model sets e after the pair frame and after the twin by the rule of CHK-FRAME. Anchor: RESP_CMD readback. Control: the twin. Evidence: `CHK-PAIR PASS seed=<n> first=seq second=len_self code=-3 arg=<e> twin_code=-5 pairs=13`.
|CHK-ARG
|A frame with one bad argument field returns invalid_arg (-7) with the payload word index that the specification states: 0 for REQ_SIZE and KEY_SIZE, 1 for DEST_VALID, 0 for the sole word of CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER. A CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER frame with 0 or 2 payload words returns invalid_len (-5) with the received length, because its length is fixed (`firmware.adoc`, sections "0x22 - CMD_KEY_GENERATE", "0x26 - CMD_KEY_LOAD", "0x27 - CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER", "Incoming Message Handler"). A DUT that returns another code or index fails. A CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER frame with a legal DEST_VALID and no shared key pending returns failure (-1) with no argument, so the -7 of the same command with a bad DEST_VALID comes from the argument check and not from the missing key (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x27 - CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER": the payload is validated first, then the pending key is looked up). A frame with both words of CMD_KEY_GENERATE bad logs its argument and is not graded, because the specification states no order for two bad words (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). Anchor: RESP_CMD readback. Control: the positive twin, which differs from the rejected frame only in the bad field (CHK-TWIN). Evidence: `CHK-ARG PASS seed=<n> cmd=0x22 field=dest_valid val=0x100 code=-7 arg=1` and `CHK-ARG PASS cmd=0x27 legal_dest=0x04 no_key_code=-1 arg=none bad_dest_code=-7 bad_dest_arg=0`.
|CHK-ARG-PAIR
|A CMD_KEY_LOAD frame with one fault of the validation checks 1 to 3 and one fault of the checks 4 and 5 returns -7 with argument 0 (the earlier check), and the twin with only the later fault returns argument 1 (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x26 - CMD_KEY_LOAD", validation table). A DUT whose later check answers first returns argument 1 for the pair. Pairs within the checks 1 to 3 return argument 0 for every check, so they cannot show the order and are not drawn. Anchor: RESP_CMD readback. Control: the twin. Evidence: `CHK-ARG-PAIR PASS cmd=0x26 first=key_size second=dest_valid code=-7 arg=0 twin_arg=1`.
|CHK-NOCHG-HANDLE
|A rejected frame does not consume a handle: the first positive twin of the boot returns handle 1 (the first handle of a boot after a warm reset is graded in `sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test`, and this cell grades that the rejections of this test consume none) (`firmware.adoc`, sections "Key Registry" and "Key Management": handles start at 1 and a handle is registered only after the arguments pass). A DUT that registers a handle before it validates returns a later number. No successful key command comes before the first positive twin in the boot. Anchor: RESP_CMD KEY_HANDLE. Control: the twin succeeds. Evidence: `CHK-NOCHG-HANDLE PASS rejected=<n> first_twin_handle=1`.
|CHK-NOCHG-VAULT
|A rejected frame sets no KPV CTRL bit and no key_valid bit (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, section "Host Coordination": a failed check leaves the KPV and the key registry unchanged). A DUT that writes the vault before it validates shows a CTRL bit or a key_valid bit. Anchor: `KM-KPV` and `KM-VALID`. Control on the same taps: after the CMD_KEY_GENERATE of the positive twins `KM-KPV` shows `lock_write` set in at least one CTRL entry (a load or a generate write-locks every slot of its span, `firmware.adoc`, section "Key Management"), and after the CMD_KEY_TRANSFER to AES of the pre-fault window of the gate-matrix leg `KM-VALID` shows the AES key_valid bit (bit 2) at 1. A missing bit fails CHK-NOCHG-VAULT. Evidence: `CHK-NOCHG-VAULT PASS seed=<n> rejected=<n> ctrl_nonzero=0 key_valid=0x00 control_ctrl_lock_write=1 control_aes_key_valid=1`.
|CHK-TWIN
|Control. Each positive twin and each frame of the DEST_VALID walk succeeds (`firmware.adoc`, sections "0x22 - CMD_KEY_GENERATE" and "0x26 - CMD_KEY_LOAD"), so every rejection above differs from a legal frame only in its fault. The echoed DEST_VALID, encoded size (words minus 1) and handle are logged and not graded: `sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test` grades the echo of every key command. Anchor: RESP_CMD readback. Evidence: `CHK-TWIN PASS control=1 cmd=0x26 code=0 echo_size=3 echo_dest=0xF0 handle=2`.
|CHK-WRAP
|The command counter rolls over from 255 to 0: the frame with sequence 0 after the frame with sequence 255 is accepted. The response counter rolls over: the response after the response with sequence 255 has sequence 0 (`firmware.adoc`, section "Command Message Reference": both counters are 8-bit and wrap after 255). A DUT that stops at 255, or rejects the wrapped frame, fails. Anchor: the response header sequence and the acceptance of the frame with sequence 0. Evidence: `CHK-WRAP PASS cmd_e=255 sent=255 next=0 resp_prev=255 resp_next=0`.
|CHK-WRAP-CTRL
|Control. A frame with sequence 0 at e = 1 returns cmd_noseq (-3) with argument 1, so the acceptance of sequence 0 in CHK-WRAP comes from the wrap and not from a DUT that accepts any sequence number. Evidence: `CHK-WRAP-CTRL PASS control=1 e=1 sent=0 code=-3 arg=1`.
|CHK-CNT-RESET
|After `rx_buff_oflow`, a SEP flush and a warm reset both counters are 0 (`firmware.adoc`, section "Error Recovery": the first response after the reset has sequence zero and commands resume at sequence zero). The first response after the cause has sequence 0. The frame that carries the old, non-zero counter value is refused with -3 and argument 0, and the frame with sequence 0 is accepted. A DUT that keeps counting accepts the old value or returns a non-zero argument. The old-counter frame follows the fault frame and precedes CMD_RECOV_ACK on the `rx_buff_oflow` and flush causes. Anchor: the response header and RESP_CMD readback. Control: the counters hold a non-zero value before the cause. Evidence: `CHK-CNT-RESET PASS cause=rx_oflow first_resp_seq=0 old_seq_code=-3 arg=0 first_cmd_seq=0` and `CHK-CNT-RESET PASS cause=flush old_seq_code=-3 arg=0 first_resp_seq=0`, and the same line with `cause=warm_reset`.
|CHK-RECOVER
|After `rx_buff_oflow` and after a SEP flush exactly one RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT arrives with the code of its cause (-2 `rx_buff_oflow`, -5 `flushed_by_sep`, `firmware.adoc`, section "0xFE - RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT") and no paired RESP_CMD (the next frame answers the next command), the first response has sequence 0, CMD_STAT reports recov_fault 1, a gated command returns -1 with no argument, CMD_RECOV_ACK returns success with no argument, CMD_STAT then reports recov_fault 0, the gated command then succeeds, and probe bit 31 (`km_recoverable_err_o`) follows recov_fault: 1 after the fault, 0 after the acknowledge (`firmware.adoc`, section "Error Recovery"; `km_csr.adoc`, `RECOVERABLE_ERR`). A DUT that reports the code of the other cause, pairs a RESP_CMD with the fault, keeps the gate after the acknowledge, or leaves the output at the wrong level, fails. For the cause `rx_buff_oflow` every field is graded. For the cause SEP flush the CMD_STAT value, the CMD_RECOV_ACK result and probe bit 31 are logged with `control=1`, because `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` grades them, and the check grades the first sequence number, the absence of a paired RESP_CMD, the gated -1 and the gate-clear success. Anchor: response frames, CMD_STAT and probe bit 31. Control: the gate-clear success of the same command before the fault. Evidence: `CHK-RECOVER PASS cause=rx_oflow frames=1 code=-2 first_seq=0 stat=0x1 gated=-1 ack=0 stat_after=0x0 gated_after=0 pic31=1,0`.
|CHK-GATE
|Each of the gated commands CMD_EXEC_ROM, CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC, CMD_SRAM_EXEC, CMD_KEY_GENERATE, CMD_KEY_REVOKE, CMD_KEY_TRANSFER, CMD_ENGINE_SHRED, CMD_KEY_LOAD and CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD returns failure (-1) with no argument while RECOVERABLE_ERR is set, and its handler does not act (`firmware.adoc`, section "Error Recovery"). A DUT that runs a handler returns the handler result. Controls: the gate-clear window before the fault gives each of the six key and OTP commands its success, the gate-clear twin after the acknowledge gives each its handler result, and each handover command returns its handler result as the first handover command of a boot (CMD_EXEC_ROM success, CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC with FW_WORDS 0 -7 with argument 0, CMD_SRAM_EXEC with no stored image -7 with no argument). CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER with no pending key and CMD_SRAM_VER return -1 with no argument in both states, so they are logged as `witness=0` and are not gate witnesses. Anchor: RESP_CMD readback. Evidence: `CHK-GATE PASS seed=<n> source=rx_oflow cmd=0x22 code=-1 arg=none pre_fault_code=0 clear_code=0`.
|CHK-ADMIT
|While RECOVERABLE_ERR is set the four admitted commands other than CMD_RECOV_ACK return their normal result: CMD_HW_VER succeeds with the VERSION word 0x0001_0000 (`km_csr.adoc`, `VERSION` reset value), CMD_ROM_VER succeeds with bits [31:24] of the version word equal to 0, CMD_STAT succeeds with bit 0 equal to 1 and bits [31:1] equal to 0, and CMD_SRAM_VER returns failure with no argument (the ROM always returns failure, so it is logged as `witness=0`). A DUT that refuses an admitted command returns -1 for the first three. CMD_RECOV_ACK is the clear step (CHK-RECOVER). Anchor: RESP_CMD readback. Evidence: `CHK-ADMIT PASS cmd=0x03 code=0 stat=0x1` and `CHK-ADMIT PASS cmd=0x00 code=0 ver=0x00010000`.
|CHK-VALID-FIRST
|With the gate set a CMD_KEY_TRANSFER frame with a wrong header CRC returns -2 and one with a wrong payload CRC returns -6, and the same frame with right CRCs returns -1 (`firmware.adoc`, section "Error Recovery": normal frame validation still runs first). A DUT whose gate answers before the validation returns -1 for the faulty frames. Anchor: RESP_CMD readback. Control: the right frame returns -1. Evidence: `CHK-VALID-FIRST PASS cmd=0x24 hdr_crc_code=-2 payload_crc_code=-6 right_frame_code=-1`.
|CHK-PERSIST
|Probe bit 31 stays 1 after every admitted query between the fault and CMD_RECOV_ACK, whatever the host delay (`km_csr.adoc`, `RECOVERABLE_ERR`; `firmware.adoc`, section "Error Recovery": the gate holds until the acknowledge). A DUT whose output drops before the acknowledge fails. Anchor: probe bit 31 sampled after each admitted query. Control: the probe reads 1 after the fault frame and 0 after the acknowledge (CHK-RECOVER). Evidence: `CHK-PERSIST PASS samples=5 err_o=1 delay_clk=<d>`.
|CHK-RESET-CLR
|A warm reset clears RECOVERABLE_ERR: after the boot CMD_STAT reports bit 0 equal to 0 and probe bit 31 reads 0 (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, section "Reset Conditioning": `RECOVERABLE_ERR` is in the warm reset domain). A DUT that keeps the bit or the output fails. Anchor: CMD_STAT after the boot and probe bit 31. Control: both read 1 before the reset (CHK-RECOVER). Evidence: `CHK-RESET-CLR PASS stat=0x0 err_o=0`.
|CHK-257
|A frame of 257 words with the separator on the last word raises no transport fault and is answered as a command: CMD_HW_VER with PAYLOAD_LEN 255 returns invalid_len (-5) with argument 255 (`firmware.adoc`, section "Message Buffers": the receive buffer holds 257 words and `rx_buff_oflow` means a full buffer without a separator). The 257 words without a separator raise RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT -2 in the `rx_buff_oflow` boot of the gate-matrix leg. A DUT that raises the fault for the separated frame, or does not raise it for the unseparated one, fails. Anchor: the response frame. Control: the unseparated frame of the same leaf. Evidence: `CHK-257 PASS sep=1 fault=0 code=-5 arg=255 nosep_fault=-2`, printed after both frames ran.
|CHK-ORDER
|Every response arrives, once and in command order, when the host is slow and the outbound FIFO fills: the count equals the number of frames sent and the echoed CMD_SEQ_NUM values rise in command order (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, section "Mailbox Transport": the mailbox is an ordered FIFO; `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, sections "Mailbox" and "Outgoing Message Handler": the TX path copies at most the free FIFO space and spin-waits, so a full outbound FIFO stalls the KM and no response is dropped). A DUT that drops, repeats or reorders a response fails. Anchor: `SEP_READ_DATA` and `SEP_STATUS.outbound_full`. Control: the pass with Q = 2 delivers every response with no `outbound_full`, and the pass with a Q of 3 or more waits until `outbound_full` reads 1 (a timeout fails the leaf) and records `outbound_full` = 1 before the drain, so the stall is real. The frames that the inbound limit holds back are written during the drain, and the count of responses equals Q. Evidence: `CHK-ORDER PASS q=12 gap=5 delay=2000 stalled=1 received=12 in_order=1`.
|CHK-SEP-OUT
|`SEP_STATUS.outbound_separator` is 1 after the last word of a response and 0 after every other word, and it does not clear on a status read: two further reads after the last word both read 1, and the read after the first word of the next response reads 0 (`km_mailbox_sep.adoc`, `SEP_STATUS`: 1 if the last word read had the separator, read-only, not sticky). A DUT that clears the bit on a read, or sets it on a body word, fails. Anchor: `SEP_STATUS`. Control: the 0 on the body words of the same responses (logged `control=1`). Evidence: `CHK-SEP-OUT PASS frames=12 last_reads=1,1 control_body_flag=0`.
|CHK-SEP-IN
|`SEP_STATUS.inbound_separator` reads 1 when the last word that the KM read from the inbound FIFO carried the separator (`km_mailbox_sep.adoc`, `SEP_STATUS`). The check samples it only with no inbound word pending and the KM idle: after the response of the last frame of the transport leg with `inbound_depth` 0, and after the response of the single CMD_STAT of the level step with `inbound_depth` 0. At those points the last word read is the last word of a frame, so a healthy DUT reads 1. Before the first KM release the bit reads its reset value 0, because the KM has read no inbound word, so the 1 at the sample points is a change. A DUT that never sets the bit, or holds it at 1, fails. Anchor: `SEP_STATUS`. Control: `SEP_WRITE_SEPARATOR.set` reads 1 after the arm and 0 after the write of the last word (the self-clear, logged `control=1`). Evidence: `CHK-SEP-IN PASS before_release=0 inbound_sep=1 single_frame_sep=1 inbound_depth=0 control_arm=1,0`.
|CHK-LEVEL
|`SEP_IRQ_STATUS.outbound_read_data_avail` is 1 while a response waits and 0 after the drain, and probe bit 14 equals (enable 0 AND outbound_read_data_avail) OR (enable 1 AND inbound_write_space_avail), with both levels read from `SEP_IRQ_STATUS` (`km_mailbox_sep.adoc`, `SEP_IRQ_STATUS` and `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, source 15 on vector bit 14). With enable 1 set the bit stays 1 after the drain, because the inbound FIFO has space. A DUT whose source selection ignores an enable, or whose level does not follow the FIFO, fails. Anchor: `SEP_IRQ_STATUS` and probe bit 14. Control: with `SEP_IRQ_ENABLE` = 0 probe bit 14 reads 0 in both samples, and the inbound_write_space_avail level and the aggregator with an enable are logged `control=1`. Evidence: `CHK-LEVEL PASS en=0x1 data_avail=1 probe14=1 after_drain=0 model_after_drain=0`.
|CHK-FLUSH-PEND
|After a SEP flush with two unread responses (12 words) and the KM idle, the first frame that the host reads is RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT (RESPONSE_ID 0xFE) with code -5 and response sequence 0 (`firmware.adoc`, section "Error Recovery"). A DUT that sends the fault frame with another code or sequence number fails. The count of waiting words, the empty outbound FIFO after the flush (`outbound_depth` 0) and the absence of a stale word are logged with `control=1`, because `sep_km_mailbox_km_side_rand_test` grades the outbound FIFO after a SEP flush. Anchor: `SEP_READ_DATA`. Control: `outbound_depth` reads 12 before the flush. Evidence: `CHK-FLUSH-PEND PASS first_frame=0xFE code=-5 resp_seq=0 waiting_words=12 control_stale_words=0`.
|CHK-RESPSEQ
|Control. The response sequence number rises by one on every response frame, wrapping after 255, except where a reset cause restarts it at 0. Evidence: `CHK-RESPSEQ PASS control=1 n=<n> monotonic=1`.
|CHK-ALIVE
|Control. CMD_STAT succeeds with bit 0 of the status word equal to 0 after the rejections of a leg, so a rejection does not set RECOVERABLE_ERR. Evidence: `CHK-ALIVE PASS control=1 stat=0x00000000`.
|CHK-CARRIER
|Control. Each self-consistent frame whose PAYLOAD_LEN differs from the required length returns -5 with the received payload length, so the carriers of the transport leg and kind (e) are real invalid_len frames. Evidence: `CHK-CARRIER PASS control=1 carriers=<n> code=-5 len_match=<n>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The reserved bits of CMD_KEY_REVOKE, CMD_KEY_TRANSFER and CMD_ENGINE_SHRED, and the argument of CMD_KEY_GENERATE with both words invalid, are not stated and are not graded (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). Only the `rx_buff_oflow` and `flushed_by_sep` legs of the mailbox ISR are host reachable. The `mbox_overflow` (-3) and `mbox_underflow` (-4) faults and the ISR order of the three mailbox errors need an injected KM mailbox error and are in <<sep-vplan-phase3-noobs,Features with no observation point>>. A SEP flush while the KM is blocked in the middle of a response, and `SEP_IRQ_STATUS.flushed_by_km` after the boot flush, are not stated. The test grades only the cells listed under Test Coverage as new. The open tests named there keep the cells that they grade. CHK-NOCHG-VAULT grades the unchanged vault through `KM-KPV` and `KM-VALID`, and CHK-NOCHG-HANDLE grades it through the handle.

'''''

[[sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test]]
===== 7.23 `sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle HOST (Key Manager image `rom_main`, real fuse sense, `+esrc_noise_force`, `+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`), 3 seeds, Verilator and VCS. Testlist: groups `all` and `cpu_stub`, `hw/sys/sep/dv/testlists/km.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_km_key_vault_alloc_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (the CPU-LSU master is the SEP host) on the `km_mailbox_sep` window; a host frame builder and response decoder; an allocator model written from the specification: 64 slots of 16 words, ceil(words/16) consecutive slots for a key, handles 1 to 255 in order, with the live keys and their words taken from the host's own load and revoke list. The model never predicts a start slot, because the start slot is DRBG-selected. Probes and injects. Approved: `HOST-MBX` and `KM-ENTROPY` (`km_entropy_tvalid_o`, `km_entropy_tdata_o`, `km_entropy_tready_o`). `KM-KPV` (CHK-SPAN, CHK-REVOKE, CHK-FRAG) is an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. Injects: none.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed (RAND-REP). The draws are: the key sizes (the forced corners 1, 15, 16, 17, 31, 32, 33, 64, 127 and 128 words on every seed, plus 6 uniform sizes in [1, 128]), the command (every forced size runs with both CMD_KEY_LOAD and CMD_KEY_GENERATE), DEST_VALID in [1, 255] (forced to 0xFF on the first CMD_KEY_LOAD and the first CMD_KEY_GENERATE of the leaf, so that every destination bit runs with both commands on every seed), the key words (non-zero, the first 8 words pairwise different), the order of the sizes, the keys to revoke, and the load order of the capacity leg.
