The handle-space leg is directed because its threshold is the fixed handle width. The seed draws only the key word and DEST_VALID of each load in that leg. `reseed = 3` samples different sizes, words, masks and revoke orders.

*Objective*: A key of 1 to 128 words takes ceil(words/16) consecutive free slots inside the vault. A load write-locks every slot of the span. A revoke erases every slot of the span and frees it. Handles are allocated in order from 1 to 255, are never reused in a boot and never wrap, and a boot restores the handle space. With no free run or with no handle left a load returns failure with no argument and changes nothing. The allocator requires every slot of a run to be unassigned, so the slots of a live key never serve a second key. It starts at a DRBG-selected slot, wraps its search once and does not let a run cross the vault end. A generated key draws its words from the DRBG. Every response carries only a handle, a size and a mask. Sources: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc` (sections "0x22 - CMD_KEY_GENERATE", "0x23 - CMD_KEY_REVOKE", "0x26 - CMD_KEY_LOAD", "Key Registry", "Key Management"); `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc` (sections "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)" and "DRBG Sampler": the sampler takes one 32-bit word for each full-strobe beat).

*Test Coverage*: Key sizes at the slot corners 1, 15, 16, 17, 31, 32, 33, 64, 127 and 128 words and drawn sizes, each by CMD_KEY_LOAD and by CMD_KEY_GENERATE (spans of 1, 2 and 8 slots). Revoke churn on live keys and a load after a revoke. Capacity: 64 loads of 16-word keys, a 65th load, one revoke followed by a 17-word load (two slots) and a 16-word load, and two revokes followed by a 2-slot request that is logged. Fragmentation: free slots that are never adjacent, a 2-slot request before and after an adjacent revoke. The handle space: 255 load-and-revoke cycles of a 1-word key, the 256th load, a CMD_KEY_GENERATE after it, a warm reset and one more load. Error cases: the full vault and the used-up handle space are legal resource conditions graded as failure with no argument. No error injection. The test extends `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`, which grades a generated key with a non-null handle and its echo, a second handle that differs from the first, CMD_KEY_REVOKE, and the refusal of the null handle. Those cells run here as controls. Only the new cells are graded, and the open owner keeps the rest. The new cells are the slot span of every key size, the capacity of the vault, the handle space to its end, the restore of the handle space, the DRBG draw of a generated key and the response contents. Functional coverage: `sep_km_cmd_reject_cg` (`cp_key_extent`, `cp_capacity`), `sep_km_kpv_slot_cg` (`cp_kpv_extent`), `sep_km_key_registry_cg` (`cp_reg_handle_value`, `cp_reg_handle_lifecycle`, `cp_reg_run_start`, `cp_reg_span`, `cp_reg_eligibility`, `cp_reg_revoke`, `cp_reg_gen_size`), `sep_km_secret_nonexposure_cg` (`cp_nx_resp_payload`) and `sep_km_sideload_delivery_cg` (`cp_sd_dest_x_cmd`). The test also hits the Phase 1 group `sep_km_generate_cg` (`cp_generate`) and adds no bin to it.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage and sense a PROD fuse image for real. Bring the entropy source up with noise forced. Release the KM (`SW_RESET_N` bit 0) and wait for `RESP_KM_READY`. Every wait in this procedure is a bounded wait, and a timeout fails the leaf. Print `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for every drawn value. Set the models: r = 1, e = 0, next handle 1, no live key. A frame writer and a response reader as in `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` build every frame and check every response frame.
. With `KM-KPV`, read the 64 KPV CTRL words and require every one to read 0.
. Define the entropy counter. It counts the clocks in which `km_entropy_tvalid_o` and `km_entropy_tready_o` are both high, from the write of the last word of a command to the read of its response.
. Size leg. Build the size list: the 10 forced sizes and 6 drawn sizes in [1, 128], in drawn order. For each size s run the next five steps.
. Revoke the oldest live generated keys until at most 7 slots are live. With at most 7 live slots and a request of at most 8 slots a free run always exists, because 64 slots minus at most 7 live slots leave at least 57 free slots in at most 8 gaps.
. Send CMD_KEY_LOAD of s words (non-zero words, drawn DEST_VALID). Read delta_load from the entropy counter. Check success, the echoed DEST_VALID, the echoed size field s-1 and the handle equal to the next handle. With `KM-KPV`, read the CTRL words and require that exactly ceil(s/16) CTRL entries gained `lock_write`, that they are consecutive slot indices, that no run crosses slot 63 and that no other CTRL bit is set. Record the run.
. Send CMD_KEY_REVOKE of that handle. Check success and the echoed handle. With `KM-KPV`, require that the CTRL of every slot of the run reads 0 and that the CTRL of every other live key is unchanged, and log the number of stored words of the whole KPV that change across the revoke.
. Send CMD_KEY_GENERATE of s words with the same DEST_VALID. Read delta_gen from the entropy counter. Check success, the echoed DEST_VALID, the echoed size field s-1 and the handle equal to the next handle. With `KM-KPV`, read the CTRL words and apply the same span check as for the load. The generated key stays live.
. Compare every response frame of the size leg, word for word, with the RESP_CMD frame that the model predicts (CHK-NOEXPOSE).
. Revoke churn. Send CMD_KEY_REVOKE for up to two drawn live generated keys. Check success and the echoed handle of each. With `KM-KPV`, apply the revoke check of step 7 to each. Then send CMD_KEY_REVOKE for the oldest live generated keys, one at a time, until at most 7 slots are live (the model counts the live slots), and apply the same checks to each. With at most 7 live slots a free run of 8 slots always exists, as in step 5. Send CMD_KEY_LOAD of a drawn size and check success and that its handle is the next unused number and not the handle of a revoked key.
. Capacity leg. Warm reset the KM through `SW_RESET_N` (bit 0 low, then high, the other bits 1) and wait for `RESP_KM_READY`. Set the models again.
. Send 64 CMD_KEY_LOAD frames of 16 words each (non-zero words, drawn DEST_VALID). Check success for each and the handles 1 to 64. The vault is full.
. Send a 65th CMD_KEY_LOAD of 16 words. Check -1 with no argument.
. Send CMD_KEY_REVOKE for one drawn live key and check success. Send CMD_KEY_LOAD of 17 words (two slots) and check -1 with no argument. Send CMD_KEY_LOAD of 16 words and check success with a handle above the last returned handle.
. Send CMD_KEY_REVOKE for two further drawn live keys. Send CMD_KEY_LOAD of 17 words. Log the response and the number of free slots of the model. Do not grade it, because the leaf does not know whether the two freed slots are adjacent.
. Fragmentation leg. Warm reset the KM, wait for `RESP_KM_READY` and send 64 CMD_KEY_LOAD frames of 16 words each. Record the slot of every handle from the CTRL change at its load (CHK-SPAN). Revoke two live keys whose slots are not adjacent, so that the longest free run is 1 slot. Send CMD_KEY_LOAD of 17 words and check -1 with no argument and CTRL unchanged. Revoke a live key whose slot is next to a free slot, so that a run of 2 free slots exists. Send CMD_KEY_LOAD of 17 words and check success.
. Handle-space leg. Warm reset the KM, wait for `RESP_KM_READY` and set the models. Repeat 255 times: send CMD_KEY_LOAD of a 1-word key (non-zero word, drawn DEST_VALID in [1, 255]) and check success and a handle equal to the loop index. Then send CMD_KEY_REVOKE of that handle and check success. The revoke keeps the vault free, so only the handle space is consumed.
. Send a 256th CMD_KEY_LOAD of 1 word and check -1 with no argument. Send CMD_KEY_GENERATE of 1 word and check -1. Log the argument of the CMD_KEY_GENERATE response and do not grade it.
. Warm reset the KM, wait for `RESP_KM_READY` and send CMD_KEY_LOAD of 1 word. Check success and the handle 1.
. For every size of the size leg compare delta_gen and delta_load: delta_gen minus delta_load must be at least s.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-HANDLE
|Handles are allocated in order from 1: every handle of a boot is above the previous one, none repeats, and none wraps. The 255 loads of the handle-space leg return the handles 1 to 255, each equal to its loop index. Every response echoes the sent size field and DEST_VALID (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, section "Key Registry": handles are 8-bit, allocated monotonically from 1 through 255, destroyed handles are not reused within a boot). A DUT that returns an out-of-order or repeated handle, wraps, or echoes another size or mask fails. Anchor: RESP_CMD KEY_HANDLE, size and mask. Control: every load before the first capacity failure succeeds. Evidence: `CHK-HANDLE PASS n=255 distinct=255 first=1 last=255 monotonic=1`, and `CHK-HANDLE PASS leg=size monotonic=1` for the other legs.
|CHK-CAP
|With 64 live 16-word keys the 65th load returns -1 with no argument. After one revoke a 17-word key (two slots) returns -1 with no argument, because only one slot is free, and a 16-word key succeeds (`firmware.adoc`, sections "0x26 - CMD_KEY_LOAD" and "Key Management": a key needs ceil(words/16) consecutive free slots; exhaustion returns failure with no argument before the vault changes). A DUT that loads the 65th key, loads the 17-word key into one slot or returns an argument fails. Anchor: RESP_CMD readback; the capacity of 64 slots and the slot count from the specification. Control: the 64 loads and the 16-word load after the revoke succeed. Evidence: `CHK-CAP PASS live=64 load65=-1 arg=none after_revoke_17w=-1 after_revoke_16w=0`.
|CHK-HANDLE-END
|After handle 255 the 256th CMD_KEY_LOAD returns -1 with no argument and a CMD_KEY_GENERATE returns failure, and after a warm reset the first handle is 1 (`firmware.adoc`, section "Key Registry": allocation fails instead of wrapping; boot initialization restores the handle space). The argument of the CMD_KEY_GENERATE failure is logged and not graded, because the specification states "no argument" for CMD_KEY_LOAD only. A DUT that wraps to a used handle, succeeds, or restarts at a handle other than 1 fails. Anchor: RESP_CMD. Control: the load with handle 255 succeeds and the next load fails in the same leaf, and the load after the warm reset succeeds. Evidence: `CHK-HANDLE-END PASS load_code=-1 load_arg=none gen_code=-1 gen_arg_logged=<a> first_after_boot=1`.
|CHK-ENT
|A generated key draws at least `s` more DRBG words than the equal-size load of the same leaf: delta_gen minus delta_load is at least s, where s is the word count (`firmware.adoc`, section "Key Management", Generate: the KM obtains `encoded_size + 1` words from the DRBG; the sampler takes one word for each full-strobe beat). A DUT that takes key words from the firmware PRNG shows a smaller difference. Anchor: the entropy handshake counters on `km_entropy_tvalid_o` and `km_entropy_tready_o`. Control: the load of the same size gives the baseline delta_load. Evidence: `CHK-ENT PASS words=33 delta_gen=41 delta_load=8 diff=33`.
|CHK-NOEXPOSE
|Every response frame of the size leg equals, in length and word for word, the RESP_CMD frame that the model predicts from the specification: the header word (HEADER_CRC8 from the CRC-8/ROHC golden, PAYLOAD_LEN 4, COMMAND_ID 0x00 and the response sequence number r), the four payload words (the source sequence number, the source command ID, return code 0, and the return argument: KEY_HANDLE, REQ_SIZE and DEST_VALID for CMD_KEY_LOAD and CMD_KEY_GENERATE, KEY_HANDLE for CMD_KEY_REVOKE, with every reserved bit 0) and PAYLOAD_CRC32 from the CRC-32C golden (`firmware.adoc`, sections "Command Message Reference", "0x00 - RESP_CMD", "0x22 - CMD_KEY_GENERATE", "0x23 - CMD_KEY_REVOKE", "0x26 - CMD_KEY_LOAD", "Outgoing Message Handler": the response carries only these fields). Every expected word comes from the stimulus and the handle model, so the expected frame holds no key word. A DUT that adds a word, puts a key word in place of a stated field, or sets a reserved bit fails. Anchor: `SEP_READ_DATA`, the response frames. Evidence: `CHK-NOEXPOSE PASS responses=<n> plen=4 frames_equal=<n>`.
|CHK-SPAN
|A key of s words takes exactly ceil(s/16) slots that gain `lock_write` and no other CTRL bit, the slots are consecutive and do not cross slot 63, and the slot count is 1, 1, 1, 2, 2, 2, 3, 4, 8 and 8 for 1, 15, 16, 17, 31, 32, 33, 64, 127 and 128 words (`firmware.adoc`, section "Key Management"; `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, section "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)"). A DUT that takes another slot count, a gap, or a run past slot 63 fails. CHK-SPAN also grades the 64 loads of the fragmentation leg: each load sets `lock_write` on exactly one slot and no other CTRL bit changes, and the 64 slots are pairwise different, so in every leaf one run starts at slot 0 and one run ends at slot 63. Anchor: `KM-KPV` CTRL and the RESP_CMD fields. Control: the keys already live keep their CTRL. Evidence: `CHK-SPAN PASS words=33 slots=3 run=21-23 ctrl_lock_write=3 other_ctrl=0 handle=4`, and `CHK-SPAN PASS leg=frag loads=64 one_slot_each=64 slots_distinct=64`.
|CHK-REVOKE
|A revoke clears the CTRL of every slot of the span and changes the CTRL of no other live key: the CTRL of every other live key keeps the value that it had before the revoke (`firmware.adoc`, section "Key Management", Revoke; `km_kpv.adoc`, `CTRL[64]`, `erase`). The CTRL bits are write-one-to-set, and with no reset or wipe in the step only an erase that completes on an unsealed slot clears them, so CTRL 0 on each slot of the span, which held `lock_write` after the load, shows that an erase of that slot completed. The stored words of the KPV are not graded, and this part is blocked: the specification does not state the physical KPV word position under the scrambler. The ROM enables and locks the KPV scrambler at boot (`firmware.adoc`, section "Boot Sequence"), so a logical key word sits at a keyed physical index that the scrambler text does not state, and the erase fill is not stated either (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The leaf logs the number of stored words of the whole KPV that change across the revoke (`OBS-REVOKE-FILL LOG`). That the revoked key is not used after the revoke is graded by `sep_km_key_transfer_rand_test` (CHK-XFER-REVOKED: a transfer of the revoked handle gives no key to the HMAC witness). A DUT that leaves a CTRL bit of the span set, or changes the CTRL of another live key, fails. Anchor: `KM-KPV` CTRL. Control: every slot of the span shows `lock_write` on the same tap after the load (CHK-SPAN), so CTRL 0 after the revoke is a change; a span slot with CTRL 0 before the revoke fails the check. Evidence: `CHK-REVOKE PASS handle=4 words=33 run=21-23 ctrl_before=lock_write ctrl_after=0 other_ctrl_same=1`, and `OBS-REVOKE-FILL LOG handle=4 kpv_words_changed=<n>`.
|CHK-FRAG
|With no two adjacent free slots a 2-slot load returns -1 with no argument and changes no CTRL bit, and after an adjacent revoke the 2-slot load succeeds (`firmware.adoc`, section "Key Management": a run needs consecutive eligible slots). A DUT that splits a key over two separate slots, or refuses after a free pair exists, fails. Anchor: RESP_CMD and `KM-KPV` CTRL. Control: the load after the adjacent revoke succeeds. Evidence: `CHK-FRAG PASS longest_free_run=1 fail=1 after_adjacent_revoke=ok`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: Trailing words in the last slot of a short key are graded by `sep_km_kpv_rand_test`. A sealed, a retired or a read-locked slot never reaches the allocator, because no ROM flow and no host command sets seal or lock_use before an allocation (<<sep-vplan-phase3-noobs,Features with no observation point>>). A slot-write error during a load, which destroys the registration and keeps the earlier writes, needs an injected slot-write failure (<<sep-vplan-phase3-noobs,Features with no observation point>>). The DRBG-selected start slot has no net and is not graded. The two-revoke 2-slot request is logged only. The open test `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` keeps the cells listed under Test Coverage.

'''''

[[sep_km_key_transfer_rand_test]]
===== 7.24 `sep_km_key_transfer_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle HOST (Key Manager image `rom_main`, real fuse sense, `+esrc_noise_force`, `+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`), every engine out of reset, 3 seeds, Verilator and VCS. Testlist: groups `all` and `cpu_stub`, `hw/sys/sep/dv/testlists/km.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_km_key_transfer_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (the CPU-LSU master is the SEP host) on the `km_mailbox_sep` window and on the AES and HMAC register windows; a host frame builder and response decoder; the independent goldens `env/sep_aes_golden.py` and `env/sep_hmac_golden.py`. Probes and injects. Approved: `HOST-MBX`, `ENGINE-OUT` (the AES and HMAC witnesses) and `KM-ENTROPY` (`km_entropy_tvalid_o`, `km_entropy_tdata_o`, `km_entropy_tready_o`). `KM-BUS` (CHK-DELIV, CHK-ORDER, CHK-SHRED-ORDER) and `KM-VALID` (CHK-BANKS) are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. Injects: none. A witness engine is an engine whose output is checked against its independent golden. The HMAC witness shows a key (the digest equals the golden of the delivered key) or no key (the digest equals the golden of its software key, which the HMAC text states). The AES witness grades only a key (the output equals the golden of the delivered key): the output of an AES without a key is not stated, so no AES result grades the absence of a key. KMAC, OTBN and the ABR seeds have no witness.

*Randomization*: Two classes. RANDCFG for the delivery leg: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed draws the round order, one size class (below, equal to or above the width, equally likely) for each of KMAC, OTBN and the ABR ML-DSA seed, the size inside that class, and the key words (non-zero, the first 12 words pairwise different). AES and HMAC run every forced size on every seed, so their six (engine, size class) cells fill in each run. The nine cells of KMAC, OTBN and the ABR seed are drawn, one for each engine in a leaf, and fill on a merge of regressions: one regression of three seeds misses a given class of an engine with a probability of about 30 %. Legal ranges: size 1 to 128 words, engine bits 0 to 4. RAND-REP for the policy leg: the seed draws the policy P1, the policy P2, the permitted mask M, the mixed mask X, the outside mask Z, the second mask M2, the shred subset S and the order of the revoke and shred legs. Legal range: policy and masks 1 to 255, with P1 not equal to 0xFF, with bit 0 (the HMAC witness) set in P1, in M, in X and in S, with bit 2 (the AES witness) clear in S, and with the ABR bits 5 to 7 set in V and in S, so that the CMD_ENGINE_SHRED cells of the ABR seed and message banks run on every seed. `reseed = 3` samples different round orders, size classes, sizes, words, policies and masks. The forced sizes and the all-bank pass run on every seed.

*Objective*: A transfer writes one full-width share pair to each selected bank (8 words per share, 12 for OTBN), and the XOR of the shares is the key. A key longer than the share width delivers its leading words and is not rejected. A shorter key is padded with DRBG words. Every share word is written once, in a shuffled order interleaved across the two shares, and key_valid is written last and only for the selected banks. A shred writes key_valid 0 first. A transfer succeeds only when every requested destination is in the stored policy. Any disallowed destination refuses the whole request with no engine write. A multi-engine transfer writes every selected bank. A revoke leaves the engine copies. CMD_ENGINE_SHRED of a subset purges exactly that subset. Sources: `hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc` (section "Key Manager Key Delivery"); `hw/sys/sep/doc/hmac.adoc` and `hw/sys/sep/doc/aes.adoc` (the delivered-key rules); `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc` (sections "Key Delivery", "Host Coordination"); `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc` (sections "0x23 - CMD_KEY_REVOKE", "0x24 - CMD_KEY_TRANSFER", "0x25 - CMD_ENGINE_SHRED", "Firmware PRNG", "Register Access Shuffling", "Crypto Engine Drivers (HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN / Adams Bridge)", "Key Management"); `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/abr_wrapper_key.adoc` (section "Adams Bridge Key Sideload Registers").

*Test Coverage*: The five engine classes (AES, HMAC, KMAC, OTBN, the ABR ML-DSA seed) at key sizes below, equal to and above the share width: the forced sizes 1, width-1, width, width+1, 16, 17 and 128 on the two witness engines on every seed, and one drawn size class for each of the other three engines. Repeated transfers of one key to one engine for the write order. A policy P1 of at least 3 bits and a policy P2, a permitted multi-engine mask inside P1, a mixed mask (some bits inside P1, some outside) and a mask fully outside P1. A revoke of the key, a transfer of the revoked handle and a transfer of a second key. A shred of one engine after the transfer, a proper-subset shred of the loaded banks that holds the ABR bits 5 to 7 on every seed, and an all-bank pass with the ABR bits 5 to 7 (its all-bank shred runs as a control). Error cases: a key length above the engine width (success with the leading `width` words, no rejection) and a mixed mask with bits outside the stored policy (failure with no argument, no engine write), both graded as error injection (see the procedure). The mask fully outside P1 is the cell that `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` grades, so it runs as a control. The test extends the open tests. `sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test` grades both shares, the four ABR seed blocks and the word order for key sizes equal to the share width on all eight destinations. `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` grades one transfer outside the stored policy, the refusal of a transfer of a revoked handle and a shred that reaches the key. Those cells run here as controls. Only the new cells are graded, and each open owner keeps the rest. The new cells are the size above the width, the DRBG word count of the padding below the width, the share write count, key_valid written last, the mixed-mask refusal, the multi-engine transfer, the copies kept after a revoke, the absence of a key for a revoked handle and the subset shred. Functional coverage: `sep_km_cmd_reject_cg` (`cp_transfer_policy`), `sep_km_secret_nonexposure_cg` (`cp_nx_resp_payload`) and `sep_km_sideload_delivery_cg` (`cp_sd_mask_shape`, `cp_sd_dest_x_cmd`, `cp_km_size_vs_width`, `cp_sd_write_order`, `cp_sd_shred_order`). The test also hits the Phase 1 group `sep_km_command_sideload_cg` (`cp_dest`) and adds no bin to it.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage and sense a PROD fuse image for real. Bring the entropy source up with noise forced. Leave every engine out of reset (the reset value of `SW_RESET_N` bits 6:1 is 1), because a key write that meets an engine held in reset ends in an AXI error (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, section "Isolation and Reset Sequencing"). Release the KM (`SW_RESET_N` bit 0) and wait for `RESP_KM_READY`. Every wait in this procedure is a bounded wait, and a timeout fails the leaf. Print `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for every drawn value. Set the models: r = 1 and e = 0. A frame writer and a response reader as in `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` build every frame and check every response frame.
. Define the AES witness. Program `CTRL_SHADOWED` with `sideload` = 1 and the 256-bit key length encoding, encrypt one fixed block in ECB mode, and wait for the output. The output equals the AES-256 golden of the delivered key words 0 to 7 (state `key`, graded). An output that differs from that golden, or no output within the bound W, is state `other`. The output of an AES that holds no key is not stated (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), so the leaf logs an `other` result as `OBS-AES-NOKEY` and never counts it as the pass of a refusal, a shred or a revoke: a wrong ciphertext is also `other`. Where a step expects no key, an AES result `key` fails the check of the step (a leaked key). After an `other` result write `SW_RESET_N` bit 2 low and then high and program the AES again, because an AES that waits for a key stays waiting. W is 8 times the duration of the first AES output of the leaf (8 is a test parameter; the duration is a control measured in the same leaf), so the positive result lies inside the window. The leaf prints `aes_first_clk` and `w_clk` with every `other` result. The first AES read of the leaf must produce an output; W does not apply to it, and a timeout of its bounded wait fails the leaf.
. Define the HMAC witness. Program the software key registers with a fixed 256-bit value that differs from every delivered key and set `CFG.key_length` to the 256-bit encoding. Hash one fixed message and wait for the digest (a bounded wait). The digest equals the HMAC golden of the delivered key words 0 to 7 as the engine takes them (the key is most-significant-bit justified in the 1024-bit field, `hw/sys/sep/doc/hmac.adoc`) (state `key`), or it equals the golden of the software key (state `nokey`, because the delivered key is used only while `KEY_CTRL.KEY_VALID` is set). Any other digest fails.
. Delivery leg. Build the round list: for AES and HMAC one round for each forced size (width 8: 1, 7, 8, 9, 16, 17, 128), and for each of KMAC, OTBN and the ABR ML-DSA seed one round in its drawn class (below, equal to or above the width; width 8, 12 for OTBN) with a drawn size in the class. Error injection, point: the key length of CMD_KEY_LOAD; kind: a key longer than the share width of the engine; expected response: success with the leading `width` words delivered and no rejection. Run the rounds in drawn order. Each round runs the next seven steps, the last of which ends with the revoke of the round's key.
. Send CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0xFF and check success with the echoed mask 0xFF. Reset and program the AES witness if it is waiting. With `KM-VALID`, require that every key_valid bit reads 0.
. Send CMD_KEY_LOAD of s words (non-zero words, the first `width` words pairwise different, trailing words above the width different from the leading words) with DEST_VALID equal to the bit of the engine E. Check success.
. Send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of the handle to E. Read the entropy counter delta over the command. Check success and the echoed handle and mask. Record the writes of the five key-path ports with `KM-BUS`, and the key_valid bits with `KM-VALID`.
. For a witness engine read its witness. At or above the width the result is `key` and the output equals the golden of the leading `width` words. Below the width no golden exists, so the AES result and the HMAC digest are logged (`OBS-PAD-OUT`). With `KM-BUS`, require exactly 2 x width share-word writes to the window of E, each (share, word) address once, no write to another bank, and the last write equal to `KEY_CTRL.key_valid` = 1. With `KM-VALID`, require that the key_valid bit of E is the only bit set.
. Below the width only, for a witness engine: send CMD_ENGINE_SHRED for E (success, echoed mask), reset the AES if it is waiting, send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of the same handle to E again, and read the witness. Log the two outputs, whether they are equal, and the DRBG words that `KM-ENTROPY` shows over each short transfer (`OBS-PAD-OUT`). Run the equal-width round of the same engine in the same leaf as the twin: two transfers of one equal-width key give equal outputs. Read delta_short from the short transfer and delta_equal from the equal-width transfer.
. At the width, for AES and for HMAC, repeat CMD_ENGINE_SHRED and CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of the same handle four times and read the witness after each transfer: the four outputs are equal. With `KM-BUS`, log the share-write address sequences of the four transfers (`OBS-ORDER`).
. Send CMD_ENGINE_SHRED for E and check success with the echoed mask. With `KM-BUS`, require that the first write is `KEY_CTRL.key_valid` = 0, followed by writes that cover every (share, word) address at least once, and that `KM-VALID` shows the bit of E clear. The number of passes is not graded. Then send CMD_KEY_REVOKE of the loaded handle and check success and the echoed handle (the engine copies stay, `firmware.adoc`, section "Key Management", Revoke). A round that ends this way leaves no live key, so the vault holds at most the key of the current round and a free run of 8 slots exists for every load of the leg.
. Policy leg. Warm reset the KM through `SW_RESET_N` (bit 0 low, then high, the other bits 1) and wait for `RESP_KM_READY`. Set the models. Draw P1 (at least 3 bits, not 0xFF, bit 0 set), P2, M (a subset of P1 with bit 0 and at least one more bit), X (bit 0, the HMAC witness, which P1 holds, and at least one bit outside P1), Z (a non-empty subset of the bits outside P1) and M2 (a subset of P2).
. Send CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0xFF and check success, and reset the AES if it is waiting. Send CMD_KEY_LOAD of K1 (8 words, DEST_VALID P1) and CMD_KEY_LOAD of K2 (8 words, DEST_VALID P2). Check success.
. Permitted transfer. Send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of K1 to M. Check success and the echoed handle and mask M. Read the witnesses: each witness in M reads `key` with the golden of K1. Read the AES last if it is outside M, because an AES without a key can stay waiting. The HMAC witness is always in M. An AES witness outside M must not read `key` (a leaked key fails CHK-POLICY); its `other` result is logged as `OBS-AES-NOKEY` and is not part of the pass. With `KM-VALID`, require key_valid equal to M exactly.
. Whole-request refusal. Send CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0xFF and reset the AES. Error injection, point: the DEST_VALID mask of CMD_KEY_TRANSFER; kind: a mask with a bit outside the stored policy; expected response: RESP_CMD -1 with no argument and no engine write. Send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of K1 with the mixed mask X and check -1 with no argument. Send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of K1 with the mask Z and check -1 with no argument. Read the witnesses: the HMAC witness, which X always holds, reads `nokey` (graded). An AES witness in X or in Z must not read `key`, and its `other` result is logged as `OBS-AES-NOKEY`. With `KM-VALID`, require key_valid equal to 0x00.
. Revoke leg and shred leg, in drawn order. Revoke leg: send CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0xFF and reset the AES, then send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of K1 to M and read the witnesses (`key`). Send CMD_KEY_REVOKE of K1 and check success and the echoed handle. Read the witnesses again and check that each witness in M still reads `key`. Send CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0xFF, reset the AES, and send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of the revoked handle K1 to bit 0 and a drawn subset of the other bits of P1, so that the HMAC witness is in the mask. Log the response code. Read the witnesses: the HMAC witness reads `nokey`, and the AES result is logged with `control=1`. Send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of K2 to M2 and check success, the echoed mask and the witnesses of M2 (`key` with the golden of K2).
. Shred leg. Send CMD_KEY_LOAD of K3 (8 words, DEST_VALID 0xFF). Send CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0xFF and reset the AES. Send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of K3 to V, a mask that holds both witness bits 0 and 2, the ABR bits 5 to 7 and a drawn subset of the bits 1, 3 and 4. Check success and the echoed mask V, and read the HMAC witness: `key` with the golden of K3 (the pre-state of the shred; any other result fails CHK-SHRED-SUBSET). Draw S as a proper subset of V that holds the HMAC witness bit 0 and the ABR bits 5 to 7, not the AES witness bit 2, and a drawn subset of the other bits of V. Send CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask S and check success with the echoed mask S. Read the witnesses: the HMAC witness (in S) reads `nokey` and the AES witness (outside S) reads `key`. With `KM-VALID`, require key_valid equal to V with the bits of S cleared.
. All-bank pass. Send CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0xFF and reset the AES. Send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of K3 to the mask 0xFF and check success and the echoed mask 0xFF. Read the witnesses (`key`). Send CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with mask 0xFF, check the echoed mask 0xFF and read the witnesses (the HMAC witness reads `nokey`; the AES result is logged). The all-bank echo and the witness reads of this step are logged with `control=1`, because `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` and `sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test` grade the all-bank shred. With `KM-VALID`, require key_valid equal to 0xFF after the transfer and 0x00 after the shred.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-GOLD
|At and above the share width a witness engine that holds the delivered key produces the output of its independent golden over the leading `width` words: the AES block equals the AES-256 golden and the HMAC digest equals the HMAC golden (`hw/sys/sep/doc/crypto.adoc`, section "Key Manager Key Delivery": word index N carries value bits [32N+31:32N] and the XOR of the shares is the value; `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, section "Crypto Engine Drivers (HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN / Adams Bridge)": a longer key delivers its leading words and is not rejected). The trailing words above the width differ from the leading words, so a DUT that uses another word set fails. A DUT that rejects the longer key, or delivers the wrong words, fails. Anchor: `ENGINE-OUT`. Control: the equal-width round gives the same golden and is graded by the open sideload tests. Evidence: `CHK-GOLD PASS eng=hmac size=12 leading=8 digest_match=1`.
|CHK-PAD
|Below the width the key is padded with DRBG words (`firmware.adoc`, section "Crypto Engine Drivers (HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN / Adams Bridge)": short keys are padded with DRBG words). The short transfer draws at least `width - size` more DRBG words than the equal-width transfer of the same engine, and two transfers of an equal-width key give equal outputs. The specification does not state which key-word positions the pad words fill, or where they lie in the DRBG stream relative to the mask words (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), so no golden exists for a padded output: the leaf logs the two outputs of the short key, whether they are equal, and the DRBG words that `KM-ENTROPY` shows over each short transfer (`OBS-PAD-OUT LOG`), and grades no output below the width. A DUT that pads from the firmware PRNG draws fewer DRBG words and fails. A DUT that draws the DRBG words and then pads with zero or with a fixed pattern is not claimed. Anchor: the handshake counters on `km_entropy_tvalid_o` and `km_entropy_tready_o`; `ENGINE-OUT` for the twin. Control: the equal-width twin (`twin_equal`); the PASS line is printed after the equal-width round of the same engine ran. Evidence: `CHK-PAD PASS eng=aes size=1 pad_words=7 twin_equal=1 delta_short=18 delta_equal=11 diff=7 min=7`, where `min` is `width - size`, and `OBS-PAD-OUT LOG eng=aes size=1 out_equal=<0/1>`.
|CHK-POLICY
|A permitted transfer returns success with the requested handle and mask in the response, and every witness in the mask holds the key of the transferred handle (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x24 - CMD_KEY_TRANSFER": the response carries the key handle and the engines to which the key is transferred). The delivery leg grades the same echo for every engine, KMAC, OTBN and the ABR seeds included. An AES witness outside the mask that reads `key` fails; its `other` result is logged as `OBS-AES-NOKEY` and is not part of the pass. A DUT that returns another mask, or leaves a witness in the mask without the key, fails. Anchor: RESP_CMD echo and `ENGINE-OUT`. Control: every witness is shredded before the transfer, so a `key` result comes from this transfer. Evidence: `CHK-POLICY PASS p1=0x5D m=0x15 echo=0x15 witness_aes=golden witness_hmac=golden`.
|CHK-REFUSE
|A mixed mask returns failure with no argument, and the HMAC witness, which lies inside the permitted part of the mixed mask, holds no key afterwards: its digest equals the golden of its software key (an AES witness in X or Z that reads `key` fails; its `other` result is logged and is not part of the pass) (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x24 - CMD_KEY_TRANSFER": if any requested engine is disallowed the request fails and no transfer takes place). A DUT that transfers the permitted part of the mixed mask, or returns an argument, fails. Anchor: RESP_CMD and `ENGINE-OUT` (the leaf shreds every engine before the request). Control: the permitted transfer of the same handle succeeds (CHK-POLICY). The request with the mask Z fully outside the stored policy is graded by `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`; here it runs as a control and logs `CTL-REFUSE-Z LOG` with the same fields. Evidence: `CHK-REFUSE PASS p1=0x5D x=0x23 code=-1 arg=none witness_hmac=nokey aes_key=0`.
|CHK-REVOKE-COPY
|A revoke does not clear the engine copies: a witness in the mask that read `key` before the revoke still reads `key` after it (`firmware.adoc`, section "Key Management", Revoke: existing engine copies are unaffected). A DUT that purges the engine on a revoke fails. Anchor: `ENGINE-OUT`. Control: the witness reads `key` before the revoke. Evidence: `CHK-REVOKE-COPY PASS h=1 witness_before=golden witness_after=golden`.
|CHK-XFER-REVOKED
|A transfer of a revoked handle gives no key to a shredded HMAC witness (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x24 - CMD_KEY_TRANSFER": the handle must be valid). A DUT that transfers from the erased slots gives the HMAC engine the erased words, and its digest equals neither the golden of K1 nor the golden of the software key, so the witness fails. The AES witness cannot show this transfer, because an AES that holds a wrong key gives an output that differs from the golden, the same `other` result as an AES without a key, so the AES result is logged with `control=1`. The failure code is a control and is logged, not graded. Anchor: `ENGINE-OUT` (the HMAC witness). Control: the same transfer of the same key before the revoke gave the HMAC witness a key. Evidence: `CHK-XFER-REVOKED PASS h=1 code_control=-1 witness_hmac=nokey aes_control=<state>`.
|CHK-SHRED-SUBSET
|CMD_ENGINE_SHRED with a proper subset S of the loaded banks returns success with the echoed mask S, the HMAC witness, which S always holds, reads `key` with the golden of K3 before the shred and `nokey` after it, and the AES witness, which S never holds, still reads `key` (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x25 - CMD_ENGINE_SHRED": the response reports the engines that were purged). The all-bank shred returns the echoed mask 0xFF and leaves the HMAC witness without a key; those cells are logged with `control=1`, because `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` and `sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test` grade them. A DUT that returns another mask, leaves a key in a shredded witness, or purges a witness outside S fails. Anchor: RESP_CMD echo and `ENGINE-OUT`. Control: a witness outside S keeps its key. Evidence: `CHK-SHRED-SUBSET PASS v=0x07 s=0x03 echo=0x03 hmac_before=golden hmac=nokey aes=golden`.
|CHK-DELIV
|A transfer to a bank writes exactly 2 x width share words, each (share, word) address once, no word to another bank, and writes `KEY_CTRL.key_valid` = 1 last (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, section "Host Coordination": the engine receives two key shares before its key-valid bit is set; `firmware.adoc`, sections "Crypto Engine Drivers (HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN / Adams Bridge)" and "Register Access Shuffling": the shares are written in a shuffled, interleaved order that is a permutation of the (share, word) addresses, and key_valid is set after the writes). A DUT that writes a share word twice, skips one, writes another bank or sets key_valid before the last share word fails. Anchor: `KM-BUS`. Control: the unselected banks see no write in the same transfer. Evidence: `CHK-DELIV PASS eng=aes size=17 width=8 writes=16 addr_set=ok last=key_valid`.
|CHK-ORDER
|The share words of a transfer leave in a shuffled order interleaved across the two shares (`firmware.adoc`, sections "Register Access Shuffling" and "Crypto Engine Drivers (HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN / Adams Bridge)": a Fisher-Yates shuffle gives a uniformly random permutation). A uniformly random permutation includes the sequential order and repeated orders, and the PRNG state that drives the shuffle depends on every earlier PRNG call, which the specification does not list, so the specification states no deterministic property of one order or of four orders (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). With `KM-BUS` the leaf logs, over four transfers, the number of distinct orders, the transfers that write all share-0 words before all share-1 words, and the transfers with a share-0 write after a share-1 write (`OBS-ORDER LOG`), and grades none of them. CHK-DELIV grades the address set and the last key_valid write of each transfer. Anchor: `KM-BUS` over four transfers. Blocked: the specification states no deterministic property of a shuffled order. It logs an OBS line and not CHK PASS, and no cell is counted as reached. Evidence: `OBS-ORDER LOG eng=aes transfers=4 distinct_orders=<n> sequential=<n> interleaved=<n>`.
|CHK-SHRED-ORDER
|A shred writes `KEY_CTRL.key_valid` = 0 first and then covers every (share, word) address at least once (`firmware.adoc`, section "Crypto Engine Drivers (HMAC / KMAC / AES / OTBN / Adams Bridge)", Shred). The number of passes is not graded (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). A DUT that writes a share word before it clears key_valid, or misses an address, fails. Anchor: `KM-BUS`. Control: the first write of every shred is graded on every engine of the delivery leg. Evidence: `CHK-SHRED-ORDER PASS eng=aes first=key_valid0 share_words=16`.
|CHK-BANKS
|A bystander bank never rises in a transfer, a refused mask raises no bank, and a subset shred clears exactly the banks of S and no other bank, the five banks without a witness included (`firmware.adoc`, sections "0x24 - CMD_KEY_TRANSFER" and "0x25 - CMD_ENGINE_SHRED"). After the transfer of K3 with the mask 0xFF in the all-bank pass every key_valid bit reads 1, and after the shred with the mask 0xFF every key_valid bit reads 0. A DUT that raises a bystander bank, raises a bank on a refusal, clears a bank outside S, misses a bank of the all-bank transfer, or keeps a bank after the all-bank shred fails. Anchor: `KM-VALID`. Control: the selected banks rise in the permitted transfer. Evidence: `CHK-BANKS PASS p1=0x5D x=0x23 refused_keyvalid=0x00 v=0x7D s=0x11 keyvalid=0x6C all_bank_keyvalid=0xFF all_bank_after_shred=0x00`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0. A checker that is blocked logs `OBS-BLOCKED <check> reason=<item>` and no `CHK-<ID> PASS`; the leaf does not count it in `checks=<k>`.

*Scope limit*: The number of shred passes, the key-word positions of the DRBG pad and a deterministic property of one shuffled share order are not stated (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>); the leaf logs the pad outputs and the share orders. The codes for an unavailable, null or never-issued handle and the shred mask 0 are not stated and are not graded. A driver failure after a transfer begins, which stops the transfer and keeps the earlier engine writes, needs an injected driver failure (<<sep-vplan-phase3-noobs,Features with no observation point>>). KMAC, OTBN and the ABR seeds have no witness: their delivery is graded by `KM-BUS` and `KM-VALID`, and by the open sideload tests. The AES witness grades only the presence of a key. The absence of a key is graded on the HMAC witness, whose no-key digest the HMAC text states; an AES result other than the golden is logged as `OBS-AES-NOKEY`, and the window W only bounds the wait. The open tests named under Test Coverage keep the cells that they grade.

'''''

[[sep_km_abr_shared_key_flow_rand_test]]
===== 7.25 `sep_km_abr_shared_key_flow_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle HOST (Key Manager image `rom_main`, real fuse sense, `+esrc_noise_force`, `+km_rom_hex=rom_main.rom.parhex`), every engine out of reset, 3 seeds, Verilator and VCS. Testlist: groups `all` and `cpu_stub`, `hw/sys/sep/dv/testlists/km.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_km_abr_shared_key_flow_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (the CPU-LSU master is the SEP host) on the `km_mailbox_sep` window and on the Adams Bridge register window; a host frame builder and response decoder; the ML-KEM reference vectors of `env/sep_abr_kem_nist.py`. The KEM set-up is the set-up of `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`. Probes and injects. Approved: `HOST-MBX`. `KM-VALID` (CHK-SK-WORDS) is an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. Injects: none.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed (RAND-REP). The draws are: DEST_VALID of each captured key in [1, 255] (forced to 0xFF for the first captured key, so that every destination bit runs with CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER on every seed), the number k of CMD_STAT frames queued for arrival 3 in [0, 3] (k is forced to 0 on arrival 1 and to 3 on arrival 2), the ML-KEM inputs (message and coins from the vectors of the golden), the message-key words (non-zero, pairwise different) and the DEST_VALID of the message key (in [1, 255] with bit 7 set), the DEST_VALID and words of the vault-fill loads. `reseed = 3` samples different masks, queue depths, KEM inputs and key words.

*Objective*: The KM clears the ABR shared-key source key_valid and words before it allocates a slot, so a second transfer and a transfer after an allocation failure both fail. One RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY is sent for each arrival, also when commands are queued. Sources: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc` (sections "0x27 - CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER", "0x56 - RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY", "Adams Bridge ML-KEM Shared Key", "Main Event Loop"); `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc` (section "Adams Bridge Key Sideload Interfaces"); `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/abr_wrapper_key.adoc` (sections "Adams Bridge Key Sideload Registers", "KEY[8]", "MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY_CTRL", "IRQ_STATUS", "IRQ_ENABLE").

*Test Coverage*: An ML-KEM encapsulation on the Adams Bridge block with a message key from the vault, which writes the shared key back to the KM, repeated for three arrivals. For each arrival 0 to 3 CMD_STAT frames are queued: 0 on arrival 1, 3 on arrival 2 and a drawn number on arrival 3. A transfer, a second transfer, a transfer with a full vault (64 live slots), a retry after two revokes, and a further arrival with its transfer. Error cases: the full vault is a legal resource condition graded as failure with no argument. No error injection. The test extends `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`, which grades a successful transfer with a non-zero handle, the refusal with no key pending, one notification for a lone arrival and the consumed key value. Those cells run here as controls. Only the new cells are graded, and the open owner keeps the rest. The new cells are the second transfer, the allocation-failure corner, the notification count and, with `KM-VALID`, the zeroized source words. Functional coverage: `sep_km_command_cg` (`cp_cmd_x_outcome`, `cp_resp_shape`), `sep_km_secret_nonexposure_cg` (`cp_nx_resp_payload`), `sep_km_fault_report_cg` (`cp_fr_isr_disposition`), `sep_km_sideload_delivery_cg` (`cp_sd_dest_x_cmd`) and `sep_km_abr_shared_key_cg` (`cp_sk_delivery_state`, `cp_sk_notify`, `cp_km_sk_consume`). The test also hits the Phase 1 group `sep_km_command_sideload_cg` (`cp_dest`) and adds no bin to it.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage and sense a PROD fuse image for real. Bring the entropy source up with noise forced. Leave every engine out of reset, so that the Adams Bridge key path is open. Release the KM (`SW_RESET_N` bit 0) and wait for `RESP_KM_READY`. Every wait in this procedure is a bounded wait, and a timeout fails the leaf. Print `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for every drawn value. Set the models: r = 1, e = 0. A frame writer and a response reader as in `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` build every frame and check every response frame.
. Send CMD_KEY_LOAD of the 8-word message key (DEST_VALID with bit 7). Check success and the handle 1.
. Define the arrival routine as four parts that always run in this order. Part 1: send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of the message key to the mask 0x80 and check success. Part 2: send k CMD_STAT frames and read none of their responses (with k = 3 the outbound FIFO holds 16 words of responses and the KM waits in the middle of the third response; wait until `SEP_STATUS.outbound_full` reads 1 before Part 3, a bounded wait with a timeout that fails the leaf). Part 3: write `ZEROIZE` of the Adams Bridge block and wait until its `STATUS.READY` reads 1, because the block does not return to READY after an operation without a zeroize (`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`, the note on back-to-back operations). Run the key generation for the encapsulation key of `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test` and read the encapsulation key after its done status. Write `ZEROIZE` again and wait for READY, because the encapsulation is a second back-to-back operation. Then write the encapsulation key, enable the message read from the vault lane and the shared-key write lane, start the encapsulation, and wait for the Adams Bridge done status. The Adams Bridge block writes the shared key to the KM. Part 4: read frames until the k CMD_STAT responses and one RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY frame (ID 0x56, no payload) are read, matched by RESPONSE_ID, and log whether the 0x56 frame lies before or after a response; then send one CMD_STAT, read its response and add the 0x56 frames read to the count of the leaf.
. Arrival 1. Run the arrival routine. With `KM-VALID`, record that the shared-key key_valid reads 1, and log its 8 words. Send CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER with the DEST_VALID of the captured key (0xFF for the first captured key) and check success and a non-zero handle (2). With `KM-VALID`, require that key_valid reads 0 and that the 8 words read 0. Send CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER again and check -1 with no argument.
. Arrival 2. Run the arrival routine. Send CMD_KEY_LOAD of 16 words (drawn DEST_VALID, non-zero words) until a load returns -1 with no argument. Check that the failing load is the 63rd, because the message key and the first captured key hold 2 of the 64 slots. With `KM-VALID`, record that the shared-key key_valid reads 1, and log its 8 words. Send CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER with a drawn DEST_VALID and check -1 with no argument. With `KM-VALID`, require that key_valid reads 0 and that the 8 words read 0.
. Send CMD_KEY_REVOKE for two drawn live 16-word keys and check success. Send CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER and check -1 with no argument.
. Arrival 3. Run the arrival routine. Send CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER with a drawn DEST_VALID and check success and a non-zero handle.
. Check the count of 0x56 frames of the leaf: it equals the 3 arrivals.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SK
|A transfer of a pending shared key returns success and a non-zero handle, and a second CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER with no new arrival returns failure (-1) with no argument, because the capture consumes and clears the source (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, section "0x27 - CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER": the KM reads the key, clears the interface KEY_VALID, and then allocates). A DUT that leaves the source valid lets the second transfer succeed. Anchor: RESP_CMD. Control: the first transfer succeeds before the second (the open test grades it). Evidence: `CHK-SK PASS arrival=1 notify=1 code=0 handle=2 second_code=-1 second_arg=none`.
|CHK-SK-ALLOC
|With a full vault the transfer returns -1 with no argument, and after two revokes a retry returns -1 again, because the capture cleared the source before the allocation failed (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x27 - CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER": an allocation failure cannot be retried against the same pending source key). A DUT that clears the source after the allocation lets the retry succeed. The vault is full when the 63rd load fails (64 slots, 2 live). Anchor: RESP_CMD. Control: the transfer of arrival 3, with a pending key and free slots, succeeds. Evidence: `CHK-SK-ALLOC PASS vault_full=1 loads_ok=62 code=-1 retry_code=-1`.
|CHK-SK-NOTIFY
|The count of RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY frames equals the count of arrivals, with 0 to 3 commands queued and with repeated arrivals (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x56 - RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY": one unsolicited message for the delivery; "Main Event Loop": the loop sends it after the queued work). A DUT that sends none, two, or loses the notification while commands are queued fails. The position of the 0x56 frame relative to a command response is not graded. Anchor: the response frames matched by RESPONSE_ID. Control: arrival 1, which runs with k = 0 queued frames on every seed. Evidence: `CHK-SK-NOTIFY PASS arrivals=3 frames_0x56=3 k=0,3,<k3> interleaved=<n>`.
|CHK-SK-WORDS
|The source key_valid reads 1 when the key arrives, and reads 0 after a successful transfer and after a failed transfer (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x27 - CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER"; `abr_wrapper_key.adoc`, `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY_CTRL`: writing KEY_CTRL = 0 clears key_valid and zeroizes the key words). A DUT that leaves key_valid set fails. Anchor: `KM-VALID` shared-key key_valid. Control on the same tap: the source key_valid reads 1 at arrival 1 and at arrival 2, before each transfer; a leaf in which it does not read 1 at an arrival fails the check. The 8 source words are logged at each arrival and after each transfer (`OBS-SK-WORDS LOG`) and are not graded: no step makes them hold a stated non-zero value (the word order of `MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY[8]` is not stated and the leaf has no golden for them), so a zero read cannot show a clear. Evidence: `CHK-SK-WORDS PASS arrival=1 control_valid_at_arrival=1,1 valid_after_transfer=0 valid_after_alloc_fail=0`, and `OBS-SK-WORDS LOG arrival=<n> words_zero_after=<0/1>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: A second shared key while the first is pending has no stated outcome and is not drawn: every arrival is followed by a transfer attempt before the next arrival. The queued CMD_STAT frames place the arrival while the KM works through its queue only when k is 3, which fills the outbound FIFO. The test forces k = 3 on arrival 2 and k = 0 on arrival 1, and draws only k of arrival 3. The test does not claim the value of the consumed key, which `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test` grades. The open test keeps the cells listed under Test Coverage.

'''''

[[sep_km_boot_reset_rand_test]]
===== 7.26 `sep_km_boot_reset_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle HOST-BOOTWIPE in every leaf (Key Manager image `rom_main` built with the boot wipe on, a preload; the leaf logs `ROM-BUILD km_boot_wipe=1 km_unrec_wipe=0`), real fuse sense, `+esrc_noise_force`, a drawn spin image for the stored-image legs, 3 seeds, VCS (Verilator for the values). Testlist: groups `all` and `cpu_stub`, `hw/sys/sep/dv/testlists/km.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_km_boot_reset_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (the CPU-LSU master is the SEP host) on the `km_mailbox_sep` window and on the AES and HMAC register windows; a host frame builder and response decoder; the goldens `env/sep_aes_golden.py` and `env/sep_hmac_golden.py` for the AES and HMAC witnesses; the ML-KEM vectors of `env/sep_abr_kem_nist.py`; a spin image of drawn size n (word 0 jumps to itself, the other words are drawn), streamed by CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC as a stored image. The test depends on no output of the image. Probes and injects. Approved: `HOST-MBX`, `ENGINE-OUT` (the AES and HMAC witnesses), `KM-ISO` (the HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge isolated probes), `KM-COUNT` (`km_rom_req_count_o`) and `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (the `sep_reset_n` release). Preload (approved): the `rom_main` build `HOST-BOOTWIPE`, loaded at t=0 by the image load `tb_backdoor_mem` (every check). `KM-CSR` (CHK-BOOT-ORDER, CHK-SCR-KEEP), `KM-KPV` (CHK-SHRED-KPV, CHK-NOX), `KM-VALID` (CHK-SHRED-VALID) and `KM-BUS` (CHK-SHRED-BUS) are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. Injects: none. The cold reset is the SEP system cold reset, a legal host stimulus.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed (RAND-REP). The draws are: the number of keys in [1, 6], the key sizes (in [1, 128] words, with the live-slot rule of the resident-state step: the keys before the last and any shared key stored by CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER hold at most 7 slots), the loaded banks, the reset points, the partial-frame length j in [1, 15] words, the stale inbound word count w in [0, 16], the number of unread responses r in [1, 3], the OTP masks (the mask 0 is a forced pick), the image size in [8, 64] words and its words after word 0, and the order of the three reset points. Legal ranges: FIFO depth 16, masks 0 to 511 with the reserved bits 0. The three warm resets of the warm-reset leg use three distinct reset points: the point with unread responses (iv) on every seed, and two points drawn from the other three. The points (i) to (iii) fill on a merge of regressions: one regression of three seeds misses a given point with a probability of about 4 %. The loaded banks always hold AES, HMAC and the ABR message bank (bits 2, 0 and 7). `reseed = 3` samples different keys, banks, points, frame lengths, stale counts, masks and image sizes.

*Objective*: After each KM warm reset the ROM boot runs. The mailbox is flushed, so no stale word acts as a frame. The per-slot KPV lock bits are clear. With the wipe predicate on, every KPV slot and every sideload bank is shredded and the ABR shared key is cleared. The SRAM and KPV scramblers stay provisioned, and a warm boot does not write SCRAMBLER_KEY again. COLD_BOOT_DONE is set before RESP_KM_READY. Each boot with the wipe on ends with RESP_KM_READY. The SEP-facing mailbox accepts host writes during the reset and keeps its words until the boot flush. OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD accumulates across commands, CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD returns the register value after its write, and a mask of 0 changes nothing. The register survives a warm reset and clears on a cold reset. The stored SRAM image size is zero after a cold boot. COLD_BOOT_DONE reads 0 after a cold reset until the ROM sets it. CMD_SRAM_VER fails with no argument while a stored image does not run. Sources: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc` (sections "0x02 - CMD_SRAM_VER", "0x12 - CMD_SRAM_EXEC", "0x28 - CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD", "Memory Scrambler Provisioning", "Boot Sequence"); `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc` (sections "Reset Conditioning", "SRAM Write Locking", "ROM Warm-Persistent SRAM Region"); `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc` (sections "Resetting the Key Manager" and "Isolation and Reset Sequencing": the domain's paths isolate and drain before its reset asserts).

*Test Coverage*: Four warm-reset points (idle, after commands, after a partial inbound frame that the KM consumed, with unread responses in the outbound FIFO), three warm resets in one leaf: the point with unread responses on every seed and two drawn points. Stale inbound words pushed while the KM is held. The boot flush, the shred of the engine banks, the vault and the shared key, the scrambler keep, the order of COLD_BOOT_DONE and RESP_KM_READY, and the first command at sequence 0. Disjoint and repeated masks over the 9-bit space and the mask 0 through CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD, a warm reset, a cold reset, a warm reset. A stored spin image loaded by CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC, then CMD_SRAM_EXEC after a warm reset, after a cold reset and after a warm reset again. Error cases: none; every reset is a legal stimulus and no fault is injected. The test extends the open tests. `sep_km_wipe_reset_test` grades RESP_KM_READY and a non-null CMD_KEY_LOAD handle after a `SW_RESET_N` park. `sep_km_wipe_reset_test`, `sep_km_handover_test` and `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` grade the sequence restart at 0. `sep_km_handover_test` grades CMD_SRAM_EXEC with no image and the CMD_SRAM_EXEC success after a warm reset. `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` grades the first CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD return and the ROM-only return of CMD_SRAM_VER (failure, no argument). `sep_km_drbg_sampler_timeout_reset_test` grades COLD_BOOT_DONE = 1 after a warm reset. The Phase 3 test `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test` grades the warm survive and the cold clear of OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD and of COLD_BOOT_DONE, and the Phase 3 test `sep_km_otp_interface_rand_test` grades the write-one-to-set rules of the two lock registers. Those cells run here as controls. Only the new cells are graded, and each open owner keeps the rest. The new cells are CMD_SRAM_VER with a stored image that does not run (only this cell of CMD_SRAM_VER is graded; `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` keeps the ROM-only return), the boot flush, the shred, the scrambler keep, the COLD_BOOT_DONE order, the accumulation of OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD through its command (its warm survive and cold clear, and those of COLD_BOOT_DONE, are graded in `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test`), the size that is zero after a cold boot, the order of COLD_BOOT_DONE before RESP_KM_READY, and the end of every wipe boot in RESP_KM_READY. Functional coverage: `sep_km_command_cg` (`cp_cmd_x_outcome`, `cp_resp_shape`, `cp_otp_lock_cold_cmd`), `sep_km_memory_boot_cg` (`cp_mem_scr_ctrl`, `cp_mem_boot_order`, `cp_mem_boot_type`), `sep_km_kpv_slot_cg` (`cp_kpv_post_reset`), `sep_km_otp_readout_cg` (`cp_otp_lock_reg`), `sep_km_reset_domain_cg` (`cp_rd_source`, `cp_rd_cbd_history`) and `sep_km_handover_cg` (`cp_ho_entry_path`, `cp_ho_fwsize`). The test also hits the Phase 1 groups `sep_km_host_cmd_cg` (`cp_cmd`) and `sep_km_sw_reset_cg` (`cp_rel`), and adds no bin to them.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage and sense a PROD fuse image for real. Bring the entropy source up with noise forced. Preload the `rom_main` build of HOST-BOOTWIPE and log `ROM-BUILD km_boot_wipe=1 km_unrec_wipe=0`. Leave every engine out of reset (the reset value of `SW_RESET_N` bits 6:1 is 1), because a key write that meets an engine held in reset ends in an AXI error (`hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, section "Isolation and Reset Sequencing"). Keep the KM held (`SW_RESET_N` bit 0 is 0). Print `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for every drawn value.
. Cold-boot reference. With `KM-CSR`, sample `BOOT_STATUS.COLD_BOOT_DONE` while the KM is held (0 expected after the cold reset). With `KM-KPV`, read the 1024 stored KPV words while the KM is held (the control of CHK-NOX). Release the KM (`SW_RESET_N` bit 0 high), read `SEP_STATUS.outbound_depth` back to back until it reads 1 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails CHK-WIPE-READY) and record the cycle of the first read that returns 1. With `KM-CSR`, record the cycle at which COLD_BOOT_DONE rose (CHK-BOOT-ORDER). Every wait for RESP_KM_READY in this procedure, after a cold or a warm release, is graded by CHK-WIPE-READY. Every other wait in this procedure is bounded too, and a timeout fails the leaf. Read the frame: RESPONSE_ID 0x55, PAYLOAD_LEN 0, response sequence 0. Set r = 1 and e = 0. A frame writer and a response reader as in `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` build every frame and check every response frame. Send CMD_STAT with sequence 0 and check success and response sequence 1. With `KM-CSR`, sample COLD_BOOT_DONE after the boot, and record the count of writes to `SCRAMBLER_KEY` since the release and `SCRAMBLER_CTRL`. With `KM-KPV`, read the 1024 stored KPV words again (CHK-NOX).
. Define the witnesses. AES witness: program `CTRL_SHADOWED` with `sideload` = 1 and the 256-bit key length encoding, encrypt one fixed block, wait for the output. The output equals the AES-256 golden of the delivered key (state `key`, graded). An output that differs from that golden, or no output within the bound W, is state `other`: the output of an AES that holds no key is not stated, so the leaf logs an `other` result as `OBS-AES-NOKEY` and never counts it as a pass of a shred (a wrong ciphertext is also `other`). Where a step expects no key, an AES result `key` fails the check of the step (a leaked key). After an `other` result write `SW_RESET_N` bit 2 low and then high and program the AES again. W is 8 times the duration of the first AES output of the leaf (8 is a test parameter; the duration is a control measured in the same leaf). The leaf prints `aes_first_clk` and `w_clk` with every `other` result. The first AES read of the leaf must produce an output; W does not apply to it, and a timeout of its bounded wait fails the leaf. HMAC witness: program the software key registers with a fixed value that differs from the delivered key, set `CFG.key_length` to the 256-bit encoding, hash one fixed message. The digest equals the golden of the delivered key (state `key`) or the golden of the software key (state `nokey`).
. Warm-reset leg. This step and the next nine steps run three times, once at each reset point of the leaf, in drawn order. Resident state: send CMD_KEY_LOAD for k keys with non-zero words. The first key has 8 words and DEST_VALID with the bits 0, 2 and 7; the other keys have drawn DEST_VALID and drawn sizes under one rule: the keys 1 to k-1 hold at most 7 slots in all (the model counts ceil(words/16) slots for each key), so at most 7 slots are live before every load and a free run of 8 slots exists (`firmware.adoc`, section "Key Management": a key needs consecutive free slots; 64 slots minus at most 7 live slots leave 57 free slots in at most 8 gaps). The live slots include the slot of a shared key that a successful CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER of step 12 stored in the previous pass (`firmware.adoc`, "0x27 - CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER": the key goes into a freshly allocated KPV slot; the boot initializes the key registry, so the keys of the earlier passes are not live). The size of key j for j below k is drawn in [1, 16 x (7 minus the live slots)] words, the size of key k is drawn in [1, 128] words, and k is cut to j when no slot is left for key j. Send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER of the first key to the banks 0, 2 and 7, and of each other key to a drawn subset of its permitted banks. Check success.
. Read the witnesses. Both read `key`. Record the pre-state: the logged duration of the AES result; with `KM-VALID`, the key_valid bits; with `KM-KPV`, the KPV CTRL words and the 1024 stored KPV words.
. Leave an ABR shared key pending: write `ZEROIZE` of the Adams Bridge block and wait until its `STATUS.READY` reads 1 (`hw/sys/sep/doc/adams_bridge.adoc`, the note on back-to-back operations), run the key generation for the encapsulation key of `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test` and read the encapsulation key, write `ZEROIZE` again and wait for READY, then run the ML-KEM encapsulation with the first key (bank 7, delivered by the transfer above) as the message from the vault lane and the shared-key write lane enabled, and read the RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY frame. Do not send CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER. With `KM-VALID`, record the shared-key state: key_valid 1, with the words logged.
. Reach the reset point. (i) idle: read every response. (ii) after commands: send c drawn CMD_STAT frames (c in [1, 8]) and read every response. (iii) partial frame: write j non-zero words with no separator and wait (a bounded wait) until `SEP_STATUS.inbound_depth` reads 0, because the KM consumed them. (iv) unread responses: send r CMD_STAT frames, read no response and wait until `SEP_STATUS.outbound_depth` reads min(6 r, 16). Before every reset pop every word that `SEP_STATUS.outbound_depth` shows, except at point (iv).
. Warm reset: write `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 low (the other bits 1) and keep it low. Wait until the KM is held: until the three `KM-ISO` probes (`hmac_km_isolated_probe_o`, `kmac_km_isolated_probe_o`, `abr_km_isolated_probe_o`) all read isolated and `km_rom_req_count_o` (`KM-COUNT`) reads the same value on two successive reads (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf; no cycle count is assumed). With `KM-CSR`, sample COLD_BOOT_DONE (control: it keeps its value 1).
. Only after the wait of the previous step, while the KM is held, write w stale words to `SEP_WRITE_DATA` (non-zero words, `SEP_WRITE_SEPARATOR.set` = 1 before the last word) and check that every write completes OKAY. Read `SEP_STATUS`: `inbound_depth` equals w and `outbound_depth` equals the depth read before the reset (0 for the points (i) to (iii)).
. Release the KM (`SW_RESET_N` bit 0 high). Read `SEP_STATUS.outbound_depth` back to back and wait (bounded) until it reads 1. Record the cycle of the first read that returns 1. With `KM-CSR`, sample COLD_BOOT_DONE at that read and log it with `control=1`: the flag keeps 1 across a warm reset, so a warm boot cannot show the order. Pop the word and check that it is the RESP_KM_READY header (ID 0x55, PAYLOAD_LEN 0, response sequence 0, header CRC-8). Read `SEP_STATUS.inbound_depth` and require 0. Set r = 1 and e = 0.
. Send CMD_STAT with sequence 0. Check success, response sequence 1, and that this response is the next frame after RESP_KM_READY, so that no stale frame answered first.
. Read the witnesses: the HMAC witness reads `nokey` (graded), then the AES witness, read last because an AES without a key can stay waiting: a result `key` fails, and an `other` result is logged as `OBS-AES-NOKEY`. Send CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER with a drawn DEST_VALID and check -1 with no argument (CHK-SHRED-WITNESS). Write `SW_RESET_N` bit 2 low and then high and program the AES again.
. Check the post-boot state, each sub-check with its own item. With `KM-VALID`: key_valid equal to 0x00 and the shared key invalid with all words zero (CHK-SHRED-VALID). With `KM-KPV`: every KPV CTRL equal to 0, and the stored words of each slot logged against their pre-reset values (CHK-SHRED-KPV). With `KM-BUS`: a write to every (share, word) address of each of the eight bank windows between the release and RESP_KM_READY, the banks that held no key included (CHK-SHRED-BUS). With `KM-CSR`: no write to `SCRAMBLER_KEY` since the release, `SCRAMBLER_CTRL` enable and lock equal to 1 (CHK-SCR-KEEP).
. Cold-domain leg. Draw three pairwise disjoint non-zero masks m1, m2, m3 and a non-zero mask m4 in the 9-bit space. Send CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD with m1, then with m2, then with m1 again, then with 0. Check success and the returned value m1, m1 OR m2, m1 OR m2 and m1 OR m2.
. Send CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC with FW_WORDS equal to the drawn image size n and check the confirmation (RESP_CMD success before the stream, no argument). Stream the n words of the spin image and the CRC-32C trailer with the separator on the trailer. The ROM hands over to the image, which spins at word 0. The leaf reads no response of the image.
. Pop every word that `SEP_STATUS.outbound_depth` shows. Warm reset the KM, wait for `RESP_KM_READY` and set the models. Send CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD with the mask 0 and check the returned value m1 OR m2. Send it with m3 and check m1 OR m2 OR m3.
. Send CMD_SRAM_VER and check -1 with no argument. Send CMD_SRAM_EXEC and check success (control: the confirmation of the stored image). Pop every word that `SEP_STATUS.outbound_depth` shows.
. Cold reset: pulse `rst_ni` low and then high. Re-run the fuse sensing, wait (bounded; a timeout fails the leaf) for the `sep_reset_n` release on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`, which comes after the sense done (`reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller: `sep_reset_no` resets the Reset Controller register block), and run the entropy bring-up of step 1 while `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 holds the KM. With `KM-CSR`, sample COLD_BOOT_DONE (expect 0). Release the KM, read `SEP_STATUS.outbound_depth` back to back until it reads 1 (a bounded wait), record the cycle of the first read that returns 1 and, with `KM-CSR`, the cycle at which COLD_BOOT_DONE rose (CHK-BOOT-ORDER). Pop RESP_KM_READY and set the models. With `KM-CSR`, sample COLD_BOOT_DONE (expect 1), the count of writes to `SCRAMBLER_KEY` and `SCRAMBLER_CTRL`.
. Send CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD with the mask 0 and check the returned value 0. Send CMD_SRAM_EXEC and check -7 with no argument. Send CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD with m4 and check the returned value m4.
. Warm reset the KM, wait for `RESP_KM_READY` and set the models. Send CMD_SRAM_EXEC and check -7 with no argument. Send CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD with m1 and check the returned value m4 OR m1.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-BOOT-FLUSH
|Once the KM is held (the isolation probes read isolated and the ROM request count is stable), the SEP-facing FIFOs keep their words: the inbound depth reads w and the outbound depth reads the depth before the reset, and every stale-word write completes OKAY (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, section "Reset Conditioning": the SEP-facing mailbox is in the cold domain and its FIFO contents survive a warm reset). After the release the ROM flushes both FIFOs before it sends RESP_KM_READY: the outbound depth reaches 1, the word is the RESP_KM_READY header, the inbound depth reads 0, and the next frame is the response of the first command, so no stale word acts as a frame (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, section "Boot Sequence": flush both FIFOs, then send RESP_KM_READY). The stale words end with the separator, so a surviving set is answered as a frame. A DUT that loses words while held, or keeps a stale word, shows another depth, another first word or an extra response frame. Anchor: `SEP_STATUS` and `SEP_READ_DATA`. Control: the points without unread responses show outbound depth 0 while held. Evidence: `CHK-BOOT-FLUSH PASS point=iv w=9 parked_depth_in=9 d_out=12 parked_depth_out=12 ready_depth=1 inbound_after=0 stale=0`.
|CHK-SHRED-WITNESS
|After each warm boot (the boot wipe predicate of HOST-BOOTWIPE on), a witness engine that held a key before the reset holds none, and CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER returns -1 with no argument for a shared key that is pending at the reset (`firmware.adoc`, section "Boot Sequence": shred the engine banks and zeroize the ABR shared key when wiping is enabled). The absence of the key is graded on the HMAC witness: its digest equals the golden of its software key. A DUT that keeps an engine key, or keeps the pending shared key, fails: the HMAC digest equals the golden of the delivered key (or any digest other than the software-key golden), the AES output equals the golden of the delivered key, or the transfer succeeds. An AES result other than the golden is logged as `OBS-AES-NOKEY` and is not part of the pass. Anchor: `ENGINE-OUT` (pre-state record: each witness read `key` before the reset) and RESP_CMD. Control: the pre-state witnesses read `key` and the shared key is pending (the host reads the 0x56 frame). Evidence: `CHK-SHRED-WITNESS PASS hmac=nokey aes_key=0 shared_before=1 sk_code=-1 sk_arg=none`.
|CHK-SEQ0
|Control. RESP_KM_READY has response sequence 0 and the first command with sequence 0 succeeds with response sequence 1, after the cold boot and after every warm boot (`firmware.adoc`, section "Error Recovery" and "Boot Sequence": both counters restart). Evidence: `CHK-SEQ0 PASS control=1 ready_seq=0 first_cmd_seq=0 resp_seq=1`.
|CHK-OTP-ACC
|CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD returns the value of OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD after its write: the OR of the disjoint masks sent (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x28 - CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD": write-one-to-set). A DUT that replaces the value with the last mask returns the second mask only. The first return (m1) is a control that the open test grades. The returns after the repeated mask and after the mask 0 are logged with `control=1`, because `sep_km_otp_interface_rand_test` grades the write-one-to-set rules of the lock registers (a repeat and a write of 0 change nothing). Anchor: RESP_CMD readback. Control: m1 returns m1. Evidence: `CHK-OTP-ACC PASS m1=0x004 m2=0x020 ret2=0x024 control_repeat=0x024 control_zero=0x024`.
|CHK-OTP-WARM
|Control. The value survives a warm reset: the mask-0 command after the reset returns the accumulated value, and a further mask accumulates onto it (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x28 - CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD": bits survive warm reset; `architecture.adoc`, section "Reset Conditioning": the register is in the cold domain). The line carries `control=1`, because `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test` grades the warm survive of the register. Anchor: RESP_CMD readback. Control: the value is non-zero before the reset. Evidence: `CHK-OTP-WARM PASS control=1 ret3_zero=0x024 ret3_m3=0x124`.
|CHK-OTP-COLD
|Control. A cold reset clears the register: the mask-0 command after the cold reset returns 0, and the next mask returns that mask only (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x28 - CMD_OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD": the bits are cleared only by a cold reset). The line carries `control=1`, because `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test` grades the cold clear of the register. Anchor: RESP_CMD readback. Control: the value is non-zero before the cold reset (CHK-OTP-WARM). Evidence: `CHK-OTP-COLD PASS control=1 ret4_zero=0x000 ret4_m4=0x080`.
|CHK-SRAM-VER
|CMD_SRAM_VER returns failure (-1) with no argument while a stored image does not run (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x02 - CMD_SRAM_VER": the ROM always returns failure with no argument, also when an image is stored but not executing). A DUT that succeeds, or returns an argument, fails. Only the stored-image case is graded here; the ROM-only return belongs to `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`. Anchor: RESP_CMD readback. Control: an image is stored (`stored=1`, the load of the cold-domain leg verified its CRC and returned the confirmation). Evidence: `CHK-SRAM-VER PASS code=-1 arg=none stored=1`.
|CHK-FWSIZE
|The stored image size is zero after a cold boot: CMD_SRAM_EXEC returns -7 (invalid_arg) with no argument, and still returns it after a following warm reset (`firmware.adoc`, section "0x12 - CMD_SRAM_EXEC" and `architecture.adoc`, section "ROM Warm-Persistent SRAM Region": the size is cleared on a cold boot and kept on a warm boot). A DUT that keeps the size across a cold reset runs the image and returns success. The warm success before the cold reset logs `control=1`. Anchor: RESP_CMD readback. Control: CMD_SRAM_EXEC after the warm reset with the image stored returns success. Evidence: `CHK-FWSIZE PASS cold_exec=-7 arg=none control_warm_exec=0 warm_after_cold_exec=-7`.
|CHK-BOOT-ORDER
|After a cold reset, COLD_BOOT_DONE is 1 when the first word of RESP_KM_READY is visible: it rises before the first host read that returns the READY word (`firmware.adoc`, section "Boot Sequence": the ROM sets COLD_BOOT_DONE and then sends RESP_KM_READY, so anything that samples BOOT_STATUS on seeing the response observes the flag). A DUT that sets the flag after the response shows 0 at that read. The check is graded at the two cold boots of the leaf (the cold-boot reference and the cold reset of the cold-domain leg). A warm boot starts with the flag at 1, so its sample is logged with `control=1` and is not graded. Anchor: `KM-CSR` and the cycle of the first host read that returns `outbound_depth` 1. Precondition: COLD_BOOT_DONE reads 0 while the KM is held before each graded release; a 1 there fails the check. The warm survive and the cold clear of the flag are graded in `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test`. Evidence: `CHK-BOOT-ORDER PASS boot=cold held=0 cold_boot_done_cycle=<c> ready_visible_cycle=<v>`, with the first cycle below the second.
|CHK-SCR-KEEP
