|A cold boot writes `SCRAMBLER_KEY` at least once, a warm boot writes it 0 times, and the SRAM scrambler enable and lock read 1 after every boot (`firmware.adoc`, sections "Boot Sequence" and "Memory Scrambler Provisioning": the key is written `SHRED_ITER+1` times only when the scrambler is not enabled, and `SHRED_ITER` is not stated, so the check grades one or more writes). A DUT that rewrites the key on a warm boot, or drops the enable or the lock, fails. Anchor: `KM-CSR`. Control: the cold boot writes the key. Evidence: `CHK-SCR-KEEP PASS n_cold>=1 n_warm=0 sram_scr=1`.
|CHK-SHRED-KPV
|After the boot every KPV CTRL reads 0 (the per-slot lock bits are clear) (`architecture.adoc`, section "Reset Conditioning": the KPV lock bits are in the warm domain). The boot erase of each slot (`firmware.adoc`, section "Boot Sequence": shred all KPV slots) fills the words from an LFSR whose value the specification does not state (`km_kpv.adoc`, `CTRL[64]`, `erase`; <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), so no check states the stored words after the boot or that they differ from the words before the reset: for each slot that held a key that the leaf loaded, the leaf logs the number of stored words that equal their pre-reset value (`OBS-SHRED-KPV-FILL LOG`). The warm reset clears the lock bits by itself, so CTRL 0 does not show the boot erase, and the erase of the slot words has no graded effect in this leaf. A DUT that keeps a lock bit across the boot fails. Anchor: `KM-KPV`. Control: the pre-reset CTRL is non-zero. Evidence: `CHK-SHRED-KPV PASS ctrl_nonzero=0`, and `OBS-SHRED-KPV-FILL LOG slots=<n> words_same=<m>`.
|CHK-SHRED-VALID
|After the boot every key_valid bit reads 0 and the ABR shared key is invalid with all words zero (`firmware.adoc`, section "Boot Sequence": shred the engine banks and zeroize the shared key). A DUT that keeps a bit or a word fails. Anchor: `KM-VALID`. Control: the pre-state key_valid bits are set and the shared-key key_valid reads 1. Evidence: `CHK-SHRED-VALID PASS key_valid=0x00 shared_before=1 shared_zero=1`.
|CHK-SHRED-BUS
|Between the release and RESP_KM_READY every (share, word) address of each of the eight bank windows is written, also for a bank that held no key (`firmware.adoc`, section "Boot Sequence": shred the HMAC, KMAC, AES and OTBN banks and the four Adams Bridge seed interfaces). A DUT that skips a bank, or an address, fails. Anchor: `KM-BUS`. Control: the banks that held a key are shredded in the same boot. Evidence: `CHK-SHRED-BUS PASS bank_addrs_missed=0`.
|CHK-NOX
|VCS: no stored KPV word is X after the boot erase of the cold-boot reference. A KPV word that the erase missed keeps its power-up value, which is undefined (`km_kpv.adoc`, `KEY_ENTRY`) and X in a four-state simulation. After that boot every word holds a value that an erase or a load wrote, so a word that a later warm boot misses is not X, and CHK-SHRED-KPV logs the words of the warm boots. Anchor: `KM-KPV`, read while the KM is held before the first release and again after the RESP_KM_READY of that boot. Control: the read before the release shows at least one X word, so the tap and the simulation resolve four-state values; a read with no X word fails CHK-NOX. Evidence: `CHK-NOX PASS before_x_words=<n> x_words=0`, with n at least 1.
|CHK-WIPE-READY
|Every boot of the leaf, with the boot wipe on, ends with RESP_KM_READY: after each cold and each warm release the outbound depth reaches 1 within the bounded wait and the word is the RESP_KM_READY header (`firmware.adoc`, section "Boot Sequence": the wiping step comes before the mailbox flush, COLD_BOOT_DONE and RESP_KM_READY; section "Initial Boot": the KM sends RESP_KM_READY once the boot is complete). A DUT whose boot wipe halts, traps or does not end fails at the first boot that misses the wait. The bound is a test parameter, and the leaf logs it with the boot cycle count of every boot. Anchor: `SEP_STATUS.outbound_depth` and `SEP_READ_DATA`; the `ROM-BUILD` line shows the preloaded build. Evidence: `CHK-WIPE-READY PASS build=bootwipe boots=<n> ready=<n> max_boot_clk=<c> bound_clk=<b>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: SRAM contents across a warm reset have no stated erase and are not graded. The SRAM array contents after a cold reset are not stated and are not graded. The number of `SCRAMBLER_KEY` writes of a cold boot is `SHRED_ITER+1`, and `SHRED_ITER` is not stated (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), so the check grades one or more writes. The KPV scrambler enable and lock have no listed tap and are not graded here; `sep_km_kpv_rand_test` grades the KPV scrambler across a warm reset. CHK-BOOT-ORDER cannot separate an order difference that is shorter than one host read. The output of an AES that holds no key is not stated (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), so the absence of a key is graded on the HMAC witness, and an AES result other than the golden is logged as `OBS-AES-NOKEY`. The open tests named under Test Coverage keep the cells that they grade.

'''''
[[sep_km_handover_rand_test]]
===== 7.27 `sep_km_handover_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle HOST (Key Manager image `rom_main`, the bench build), real fuse sense, entropy noise forced, scripted image IMG-H, `reseed = 3`; testlist `km.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_km_handover_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (the testbench LSU master is the SEP host and reaches the KM only through `km_mailbox_sep`); the frame model of the Key Manager area, written from `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc` (header CRC-8/ROHC, payload CRC-32C, response IDs, return codes, fault codes) and never from the RTL. IMG-H is a scripted KM image of about 120 words (one 1 KiB region). CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC streams it, and it reports register state through the KM mailbox write register. Its report words carry no response ID in bits [15:8] of a first word, so the frame tracker never reads a report word as a response header. Its first report word is 0x4B4D4948. Approved probes: `HOST-MBX` (frames on `SEP_WRITE_DATA` and `SEP_READ_DATA`, including the IMG-H report words), `PIC-IRQ` (vector bit 30, `km_unrecoverable_err_o`), `KM-COUNT` (`km_sram_write_count_o`). `KM-KPV` (the logged KPV words of CHK-HO-KPV-SHRED), `KM-BUS` (CHK-HO-SIDELOAD-BUS), `KM-FETCH` (CHK-HO-EXEC) and `KM-CSR` (CHK-HO-CONFIRM) are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list; <<sep-vplan-km-proposed>> defines them. Injects: none. The corrupted image words and the oversize FW_WORDS are legal host stimulus and need no hook.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, two classes. RAND-REP for the load legs: the size n1 of the good image and the size n2 of the corrupted image (each in [8, 256] words); the flipped position (word w in [0, n2 - 1] or the trailer) and the flipped bit b in [0, 31], with the forced picks word 0, last word and trailer, and bit 0 and bit 31; one drawn oversize FW_WORDS in [8193, 0xFFFE] and one drawn mid size s in [n1 + 1, 8191]; the image words. The legal range of FW_WORDS is 1 to 0xFFFF. The boundary oversize values 8192 (the SRAM size in words) and 0xFFFF (the field maximum) run on every seed. RAND-NONE for the handover state leg: the state is a fixed directed set, because every cell of the leg needs a defined pre-state. The seed draws only the key words (non-zero, pairwise different), the staged OTP words (non-zero) and the confirmation delay D in [2000, 8000] host clocks, counted from the clear of IRQ_ENABLE on `KM-CSR`. `reseed = 3` samples different sizes, positions, bits and words. The leaf streams at most 800 image words in total (a test parameter).

*Objective*: CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC returns one RESP_CMD success before the stream, reads FW_WORDS words and a CRC-32C trailer, and sends no second RESP_CMD. A CRC mismatch is the fatal fw_crc (-14) fault. The load clears the stored image size when it starts and sets it only after the CRC verifies. The bench build has the SRAM wipe of the fatal handler off, so after a fw_crc fault the stored size stays cleared; a build with the wipe predicate on shreds SRAM, and the stored size after the fault is then not stated. A zero FW_WORDS, or an image whose top exceeds the static load ceiling `+__km_fw_load_limit+` (below SRAM region 31), returns invalid_arg with argument 0 before any confirmation or image request. At handover the ROM shreds the eight sideload banks and the shared key, read-locks every registered KPV slot and shreds every free slot, locks the secret OTP fields and not the public identities, write-locks the image regions and SRAM region 31, drains and then flushes the mailbox, overwrites the unlocked SRAM, clears every general register except x5 (which holds the entry address 0x8000), arms SRAM_EXEC_MODE and jumps to 0x8000. At the jump all KMCSR and mailbox interrupt enables are clear, the CPU interrupt sources are masked, IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR is 0x10 and IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK is 0. The ROM does not send RESP_KM_READY at handover and does not flush an unread confirmation. Spec: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, sections "0x11 - CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC", "0x12 - CMD_SRAM_EXEC", "0xFF - RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT", "Error Recovery", "SRAM Firmware Handover", "ROM Stack Budget and the Static Load Ceiling"; `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, sections "OTP Inputs and Readout", "ROM Warm-Persistent SRAM Region", "Mutable Firmware Model".

*Test Coverage*: Load legs: a good image of n1 words (the accepted size and the twin of every refused size); a corrupted image with one flipped bit in an image word or in the trailer (error injection: point, the host stream words after the confirmation; kind, data corruption; expected response, one RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT with payload 0xFFFFFFF2 and the KM halted until reset); CMD_SRAM_EXEC after a warm reset, once after the good load and once after a load whose host stream stops after the confirmation (error injection: point, the host stream after the confirmation; kind, the stream stops and the KM is reset warm; expected response, invalid_arg with no argument, because the stored size stays cleared); CMD_SRAM_EXEC after the fw_crc fault and a warm reset (expected response, invalid_arg with no argument, because the bench build has the SRAM wipe off and the load sets the size only after the CRC verifies); FW_WORDS 8192, 0xFFFF and one drawn oversize value, and FW_WORDS 0 (error injection: point, the size payload word; kind, zero or out-of-range size; expected response, invalid_arg with argument 0 and no image request); one mid size in [n1 + 1, 8191] that the leaf logs and does not grade. State leg: pass 1 through CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC with three keys in 4 registered slots, all eight banks holding a key, a pending ABR shared key, non-zero secret OTP words and 6 junk inbound words after the trailer; pass 2 through CMD_SRAM_EXEC after a warm reset with keys in AES and HMAC only (six banks hold no key), no pending shared key and a delayed read of the confirmation. Extends the open test `sep_km_handover_test`, which grades a good load with CMD_SRAM_EXEC after a warm reset, CMD_SRAM_EXEC with no image, FW_WORDS 0 and a set reserved bit, and the inhibit after CMD_EXEC_ROM. Those cells run here as controls. This test grades only the new cells: the absence of a second RESP_CMD, the fw_crc fault, the size cleared at load start and still cleared after a CRC mismatch, the oversize FW_WORDS, the argument 0 and the absence of an image request on FW_WORDS 0, the state at the jump, the entry address in x5 and the handover shred and locks. The owner of the open test keeps the rest. Functional coverage: `sep_km_cmd_reject_cg` (`cp_image_bounds`), `sep_km_command_cg` (`cp_cmd_x_outcome`, `cp_resp_shape`), `sep_km_kpv_slot_cg` (`cp_kpv_ctrl_write`), `sep_km_secret_nonexposure_cg` (`cp_nx_epoch_secret`), `sep_km_kmcsr_status_cg` (`cp_csr_entry_lock`), `sep_km_otp_readout_cg` (`cp_otp_lock_reg`), `sep_km_exec_protection_cg` (`cp_ex_handover_entry`), `sep_km_sideload_delivery_cg` (`cp_sd_shred_order`), `sep_km_fault_report_cg` (`cp_fr_unrec_code`, `cp_fr_code_encoding`, `cp_fr_err_out`), `sep_km_handover_cg` (`cp_ho_entry_path`, `cp_ho_fwsize`, `cp_ho_image_state`, `cp_ho_state_at_jump`, `cp_ho_mailbox_flush`, `cp_ho_order`), `sep_km_vault_wipe_cg` (`cp_vw_handover_shred`); Phase 1: `sep_km_host_cmd_cg` (`cp_cmd`), `sep_km_sw_reset_cg` (`cp_rel`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Stage a fuse image whose four secret fields (chiplet UID, SIP UID, SYS UID, class key) and three public identities hold non-zero drawn words (test vectors). Cold-reset the SEP with real fuse sense and entropy noise forced, bring up entropy, release the KM and wait for RESP_KM_READY (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Log `SEED` and every `DRAW` value.
. Good load. Send CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC with FW_WORDS = n1 (a drawn image: word 0 jumps to itself, the other words are drawn). Read the RESP_CMD before the host writes any stream word: success, no argument. Stream the n1 words and the CRC-32C trailer with the separator on the trailer. Record every frame that arrives until the warm reset of the next step (logged with `control=1`: the IMG-H load of the state leg grades the absence of a second RESP_CMD by structure).
. Warm reset (`SW_RESET_N` bit 0 low, then high, the other bits at 1) and wait for RESP_KM_READY. Send CMD_SRAM_EXEC and read RESP_CMD: success, no argument (the control for step 4). Warm-reset again and wait for RESP_KM_READY.
. Interrupted load. Error injection, point: the host stream after the confirmation; kind: the stream stops after the confirmation and the KM is reset warm; expected response: the stored size stays cleared. Send CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC with FW_WORDS = n2 and read the RESP_CMD success. Send no stream word. Warm reset and wait for RESP_KM_READY. Send CMD_SRAM_EXEC and read RESP_CMD: invalid_arg (-7), no argument. Warm-reset again and wait for RESP_KM_READY.
. Error injection, point: the host stream words after the confirmation; kind: data corruption; expected response: one RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT with payload 0xFFFFFFF2, no RESP_CMD, `km_unrecoverable_err_o` set and held. Sample PIC vector bit 30 over a quiet window (0 expected, the control). Send CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC with FW_WORDS = n2 and read the RESP_CMD success. Assert that the flipped value differs from the original value. Stream the n2 words and the trailer of the unflipped image with bit b of word w (or of the trailer) flipped. Wait for PIC vector bit 30 to rise (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf) and record the clocks `rise_clk` from the write of the trailer to the rise. Read the frames and sample bit 30 in every host clock over a hold of `hold_clk` = 8 times `rise_clk` (the control `rise_clk` is measured in the same leaf; the leaf prints both).
. Warm reset and wait for RESP_KM_READY. Sample bit 30 (0 expected). Send CMD_SRAM_EXEC and read RESP_CMD: invalid_arg (-7), no argument (the bench build has the SRAM wipe of the fatal handler off, and the load sets the stored size only after the CRC verifies).
. Error injection, point: the size payload word of CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC; kind: out-of-range size; expected response: invalid_arg with argument 0 and no image request. For each FW_WORDS in {8192, 0xFFFF, the drawn oversize value}, each in its own warm boot (warm reset, wait for RESP_KM_READY), send CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC with the reserved bits 0, read RESP_CMD, then send CMD_STAT with the next sequence number of the frame model and read its response, which must be the next frame (an image request would arrive first).
. Error injection, point: the size payload word; kind: zero size; expected response: invalid_arg with argument 0. In its own warm boot send CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC with FW_WORDS = 0 and read RESP_CMD, then send CMD_STAT and read its response as in the previous step.
. Mid size, log only. In its own warm boot send CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC with FW_WORDS = s, log the response as `OBS-SIZE-MID`, and send no stream word. The specification states no ceiling value, so the leaf does not grade the response. Warm-reset the KM and wait for RESP_KM_READY.
. State leg, pass 1 pre-state. Load three keys of 8, 20 and 3 words with drawn non-zero words (4 registered slots). Choose DEST_VALID so that the three keys cover all eight engine banks between them, and so that the 8-word key can serve as the ML-KEM message. Transfer each key to its DEST_VALID bits and read the three RESP_CMD frames: success, with the stated completed-engine masks that together give 0xFF.
. Run the ML-KEM encapsulation sequence of `sep_km_abr_shared_key_flow_rand_test` with the 8-word key and read RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY. Send no CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER, so the shared key stays pending. Record the pre-state: the bank mask 0xFF, the pending shared key, the 4 registered slots, the staged non-zero OTP words, and (with `KM-KPV`) all 1024 stored words.
. Handover through CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC. Send CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC with FW_WORDS = the IMG-H size and read the RESP_CMD success. Count `KM-BUS` writes from this command acceptance and record `km_sram_write_count_o`. Stream IMG-H and its trailer, then post 6 junk inbound words with the separator on the last.
. Read the first IMG-H word from `SEP_READ_DATA` (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf): it is 0x4B4D4948, with no RESP_KM_READY before it. When the first IMG-H word arrives, read all 1024 stored words with `KM-KPV` (the log of CHK-HO-KPV-SHRED). Read the rest of the IMG-H report: every general-purpose register other than x0 and the store base register of the image (x5 first), IRQ_STATUS (logged), IRQ_ENABLE, KM_IRQ_ENABLE, IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR, IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK, the CPU interrupt mask (the mask-read instruction with all ones, which leaves the mask unchanged), SRAM_EXEC_MODE.enable, OTP_READ_LOCK, the VAL_0 words of the four secret fields and of the three public identities, SRAM_LOCK, KM_STATUS.inbound_empty, the 64 CTRL registers, a key-word read of each locked slot (data and `slv`), the 16 key words of the lowest-numbered slot with CTRL 0 (logged), then a write of 0x4B4D5756 to word 0 of that slot and its readback (the readback control of CHK-HO-KPV-LOCK), and the nine KEY_CTRL registers (eight banks in DEST_VALID bit order, then the ABR shared key). After the nine reads IMG-H writes `KEY_CTRL.key_valid` = 1 to the KMAC bank, reads it back, writes 0 and reads it back again, and reports both reads (the readback control of CHK-HO-SIDELOAD; `kmac_wrapper_key.adoc`, `KEY_CTRL`: `key_valid` is read-write with reset value 0).
. IMG-H runs the SRAM store test and adds its results to the report. It clears SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION and IRQ_STATUS by write-one-to-clear and reads SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION (0 expected). It stores a value that differs from the old word at 0xFFFC (region 31) and reads back the word and the violation bits. It clears the violation bits, stores a differing value to a data word that the image holds inside its own region, and reads back the word and the violation bits. Read `km_sram_write_count_o` and take the delta from the baseline of this pass (step 12 for pass 1, step 16 for pass 2).
. Pass 2 pre-state. Warm reset and wait for RESP_KM_READY. The handover of pass 1 shredded the banks, so load two keys and transfer them to AES and HMAC only (six banks hold no key). Leave no shared key pending. Record the pre-state again: the bank mask, the staged OTP words, the number of registered slots, and (with `KM-KPV`) all 1024 stored words.
. Handover through CMD_SRAM_EXEC. Send CMD_SRAM_EXEC. At its acceptance record `km_sram_write_count_o` (the baseline of pass 2) and start the `KM-BUS` count. With `KM-CSR`, record IRQ_ENABLE at the acceptance. Do not read the outbound mailbox until `KM-CSR` shows IRQ_ENABLE at 0 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf) and then for the drawn delay D. Then read RESP_CMD: success, no argument. Read the first IMG-H word and the full IMG-H report as in steps 13 and 14, with the write-count delta taken from the baseline of this step (the IMG-H image of pass 1 persists in the write-locked regions and in `sram_fw_size`).
. Check the pass 1 and pass 2 reports against the checkers below, with the `KM-BUS` and `KM-FETCH` taps active.
. Warm reset and wait for RESP_KM_READY, so that `km_sw_rst_n` is high at the end of the leaf.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-LOAD-OK
|CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC with an accepted size returns one RESP_CMD success with no return argument before the host sends the first image word, and no second RESP_CMD follows the stream (`firmware.adoc`, "0x11 - CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC", Response: the KM never returns to the dispatcher after the confirmation and emits no second `RESP_CMD`). A wrong DUT sends the confirmation after the stream, sends a second RESP_CMD, gives the confirmation an argument, or sends none. On the IMG-H load the next word after the stream is the first IMG-H word, so a second RESP_CMD would sit in front of it. The frames that arrive after the spin-loop image of step 2 are logged with `control=1`. Anchor: the response frames on `SEP_READ_DATA`. Control: the confirmation read before the first stream word (`first_resp=0`) shows that the leaf reads RESP_CMD frames. Evidence: `CHK-LOAD-OK PASS: n=<words> first_resp=0 first_arg=none second_resp=0`.
|CHK-FWCRC
|A CRC-32C mismatch (a flipped bit in one image word or in the trailer) raises the fatal fw_crc fault: exactly one RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT with payload 0xFFFFFFF2 (code -14 sign-extended), no RESP_CMD after the stream, and `km_unrecoverable_err_o` (PIC vector bit 30) rises and stays high until a reset (`firmware.adoc`, "0x11 - CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC", "0xFF - RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT": the KM halts and recovery needs a warm or cold reset). A wrong DUT accepts the image and jumps to it, reports another code, sends a RESP_CMD, or drops bit 30. The test asserts before the stream that the flipped value differs from the original. Anchor: the response frame and PIC vector bit 30 sampled in every host clock over the hold `hold_clk` after the rise (8 times the `rise_clk` of the same leaf, printed with it). Control: bit 30 reads 0 in the quiet window before the load, and the good load of step 2 raises no fault. Evidence: `CHK-FWCRC PASS: n=<words> pos=<word index/trailer> bit=<b> code=0xFFFFFFF2 err_o=1 held=1 rise_clk=<r> hold_clk=<8r> second_resp=0`.
|CHK-FWSIZE-CLR
|The load clears the stored image size when it starts (`firmware.adoc`, "SRAM Firmware Handover", step "Confirm": the ROM clears `sram_fw_size` before it sends the confirmation; `architecture.adoc`, "ROM Warm-Persistent SRAM Region", `sram_fw_size`: the structure survives a warm reset). After a load whose host stream stops after the confirmation and a warm reset, CMD_SRAM_EXEC returns invalid_arg (-7) with no return argument (`firmware.adoc`, "0x12 - CMD_SRAM_EXEC": no stored image gives no argument). A wrong DUT keeps the earlier valid image until the CRC verifies, so the command answers success and jumps. The load sets the stored size only after the CRC verifies (`firmware.adoc`, "SRAM Firmware Handover", step "Persist"), and the bench build has the SRAM wipe of the fatal handler off (`firmware.adoc`, "Error Recovery": the SRAM wipe is under the `rom_unrec_wipe_enabled()` predicate; `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, section "Key Manager `rom_main` Firmware Builds"). So after the fw_crc fault of step 5 and a warm reset, CMD_SRAM_EXEC returns invalid_arg (-7) with no return argument. A wrong DUT that sets the size before the CRC verifies answers success. Anchor: the RESP_CMD of CMD_SRAM_EXEC. Control: the same command after the good load of step 2 answers success (`exec_before=0`), so a stored image exists before the interrupted load. Evidence: `CHK-FWSIZE-CLR PASS: exec_before=0 exec_after=-7 arg=none exec_after_fwcrc=-7 arg_fwcrc=none`.
|CHK-SIZE-REJ
|A zero FW_WORDS, or a size whose image top exceeds `+__km_fw_load_limit+`, returns RESP_CMD invalid_arg (-7) with argument 0 and no image request (`firmware.adoc`, "0x11 - CMD_SRAM_LOAD_EXEC", "ROM Stack Budget and the Static Load Ceiling"). The sizes 8192, 0xFFFF and the drawn oversize value exceed the ceiling, because the ceiling is below SRAM region 31 and 8192 words fill the whole SRAM. A wrong DUT sends the confirmation, returns another code or argument, or sends an image request after the refusal. The invalid_arg code of FW_WORDS 0 is a cell of the open test `sep_km_handover_test` and is logged here as a control; the zero leg grades only the argument 0 and the absence of an image request. Anchor: the response frames; the response of the CMD_STAT that follows the refusal is the next frame, so no image request preceded it. Control: the accepted size n1 of step 2 gets the confirmation. Evidence: `CHK-SIZE-REJ PASS: words=<w> code=-7 arg=0 stat_next=1` for the oversize values and `CHK-SIZE-REJ PASS: words=0 control_code=-7 arg=0 stat_next=1` for the zero leg.
|CHK-HO-FIRST
|The ROM sends no RESP_KM_READY at handover, because the mutable image sends its own ready message (`firmware.adoc`, "SRAM Firmware Handover", step "Jump"; "0x55 - RESP_KM_READY"). In both passes the first word that the host reads after the RESP_CMD confirmation (and, in pass 1, the stream) is the first IMG-H word 0x4B4D4948. A wrong DUT sends a RESP_KM_READY frame (ID 0x55) between them. Anchor: `SEP_READ_DATA`. Control: the confirmation arrives as RESP_CMD in both passes, and the IMG-H first word has no response ID in bits [15:8], so a ready frame cannot pass for it. Evidence: `CHK-HO-FIRST PASS: pass=<1/2> ready_between=0 first_word=0x4B4D4948`.
|CHK-HO-CONFIRM
|The ROM waits for the outbound FIFO to drain before the flush, so the flush cannot discard a RESP_CMD confirmation that the SEP has not read (`firmware.adoc`, "SRAM Firmware Handover", step "Disable IRQs and flush the mailbox": the ROM clears all KMCSR IRQ enables, then waits for the outbound FIFO to drain, then flushes both FIFOs). The host leaves the confirmation of CMD_SRAM_EXEC unread until `KM-CSR` shows IRQ_ENABLE at 0, the first action of that step, and for the delay D after it, so the ROM has reached the drain wait while the confirmation is still unread. The host then reads RESP_CMD success with no argument, and the first IMG-H word follows. A wrong DUT flushes the unread confirmation, so the host reads the first IMG-H word or nothing. Anchor: `SEP_READ_DATA`; `KM-CSR` IRQ_ENABLE. Control: IRQ_ENABLE is non-zero at the acceptance of CMD_SRAM_EXEC, because the boot enables the fault and error sources (`firmware.adoc`, "Boot Sequence"), so its clear marks the handover step; pass 1 reads the confirmation at once (CHK-HO-FIRST). Evidence: `CHK-HO-CONFIRM PASS: irq_enable_at_accept=<e> irq_enable_clear=1 delay=<D> resp_code=0 resp_arg=none first_img_word=0x4B4D4948`.
|CHK-HO-REGS
|At the jump IRQ_ENABLE is 0, KM_IRQ_ENABLE is 0, IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR is 0x10, IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK is 0, the mask bits 3, 4 and 5 of the CPU interrupt mask are 1 (the three sources that the specification numbers are masked) and SRAM_EXEC_MODE.enable is 1 (`firmware.adoc`, "SRAM Firmware Handover", steps "Disable IRQs and flush the mailbox" and "Enable SRAM execution mode": all CPU IRQ sources are masked; `architecture.adoc`, "Key Manager CPU Interrupt Processing": the numbered sources are CPU IRQ bits 3, 4 and 5; `km_csr.adoc`, "IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR", "SRAM_EXEC_MODE"; `km_mailbox_km.adoc`, "KM_IRQ_ENABLE"). A wrong DUT leaves an interrupt enable set, programs the entry address or the lock, leaves one of the three numbered CPU sources unmasked, or does not arm the execution mode. The other bits of the CPU interrupt mask have no stated source, so the full mask value is logged and not graded. IRQ_STATUS at the jump is logged and not graded. Anchor: the IMG-H readback. Control: the boot sequence enables the fault interrupts (`firmware.adoc`, "Boot Sequence"), so a zero IRQ_ENABLE at the jump is a change that the handover makes. Evidence: `CHK-HO-REGS PASS: pass=<1/2> irq_en=0 km_irq_en=0 entry=0x10 lock=0 cpu_mask_bits_3_5=0x7 cpu_mask_logged=0x<m> exec_mode=1`.
|CHK-HO-ENTRY
|x5 holds the entry address 0x8000 and every other reported general register reads 0 (`firmware.adoc`, "SRAM Firmware Handover", step "Clear GPRs"). The image reports x5 and the other registers with its first instructions. Its own store base register is excluded and named in the log. A wrong DUT clears x5 or leaves a register non-zero. Anchor: the IMG-H readback. Evidence: `CHK-HO-ENTRY PASS: pass=<1/2> x5=0x00008000 other_regs_zero=1`.
|CHK-HO-OTP
|The handover sets the OTP_READ_LOCK bits of the four secret fields (chiplet UID bit 2, SIP UID bit 3, SYS UID bit 4, class key bit 5) and leaves the bits of the three public identities (bits 6 to 8) and of the life-cycle state (bit 0) clear (`architecture.adoc`, "OTP Inputs and Readout", "Unique Identifiers (UID) - provisioning and access"; `km_csr.adoc`, "OTP_READ_LOCK"). The bit of the demotion state (bit 1) has no stated value; the leaf logs it and does not grade it. After the handover the VAL_0 words of the four secrets read 0, and the VAL_0 words of the public identities read non-zero; those reads are logged with `control=1`, because `sep_km_otp_interface_rand_test` grades the zero read of a locked field. The pre-handover record is the staged fuse image, where every secret and public word is a drawn non-zero test vector. A wrong DUT sets the lock bit of a public identity, or leaves the bit of a secret field clear. Anchor: the IMG-H readback of OTP_READ_LOCK. The public words are the in-leaf twin of the zeroed secret words. Evidence: `CHK-HO-OTP PASS: pass=<1/2> lock_secret=0xF lock_pub=0x0 life_cycle=0 demotion=<bit, logged> control_pre_val0_nonzero=4 control_uid_val0=0 control_sip_val0=0 control_sys_val0=0 control_cls_val0=0 control_pub_val0_nonzero=3`.
|CHK-HO-KPV-LOCK
|Every registered slot is read-locked (lock_use set), every free slot is shredded through the hardware erase and so reads CTRL 0, and a locked key word reads 0 (`firmware.adoc`, "SRAM Firmware Handover", step "Lock sensitive data"; `architecture.adoc`, "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)"; `km_kpv.adoc`, "CTRL[64]"). The expected count of registered slots follows from the key sizes (8, 20 and 3 words give 1, 2 and 1 slots, so 4; pass 2 gives its own count). A wrong DUT sets lock_use on a different number of slots, lets a registered key read non-zero, or leaves a non-zero CTRL on a free slot. Anchor: the IMG-H readback of all 64 CTRL registers and of one key word of each slot with lock_use set. Control: the free slots have CTRL 0 and the registered slots do not. The zero read of a locked key word needs a control on the same read path: IMG-H writes the non-zero constant 0x4B4D5756 to word 0 of the lowest-numbered slot with CTRL 0, which no lock blocks, and reads it back; a readback other than that value fails the check, because the read path then cannot show key data. The 16 words of that slot before the write hold the erase fill, whose value is not stated, and are logged. Evidence: `CHK-HO-KPV-LOCK PASS: pass=<1/2> reg_lock_use=<n> expected=<n> reg_read_zero=<n> free_ctrl_zero=<64-n> control_free_rd=0x4B4D5756`.
|CHK-HO-SIDELOAD
|After the handover no bank key_valid and not the shared-key key_valid is set (`firmware.adoc`, "SRAM Firmware Handover", step "Shred sideload keys"). The pre-state record is that every transfer succeeded and the shared key is delivered (pass 1: bank mask 0xFF and shared key 1; pass 2: the AES and HMAC bits and no shared key). A wrong DUT leaves a bank or the shared key valid. Anchor: the IMG-H readback of the nine KEY_CTRL registers. Control: the pre-state record is non-zero in each pass, and the same read path shows a set bit: after the nine reads IMG-H writes `KEY_CTRL.key_valid` = 1 to the KMAC bank and reads 1, then writes 0 and reads 0. A readback other than 1 then 0 fails the check, because the read path then cannot show a valid key. Evidence: `CHK-HO-SIDELOAD PASS: pass=<1/2> keyvalid_before=<mask> keyvalid=0x00 shared_before=<0/1> shared_valid=0 control_kmac_rd=1,0`.
|CHK-HO-SRAM
|The handover write-locks the regions that contain the image (region 0 for IMG-H, which holds about 120 words from 0x8000) and region 31, so SRAM_LOCK reads 0x80000001 at the jump. A store to region 31 and a store to the image region are dropped, and SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION names exactly the region of each store; those two store and violation readbacks are logged with `control=1`, because `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test` grades the dropped store and the violation bit of a locked region (`firmware.adoc`, "SRAM Firmware Handover", steps "Write-lock the image" and "Scramble"; `architecture.adoc`, "ROM Warm-Persistent SRAM Region"; `km_csr.adoc`, "SRAM_LOCK", "SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION"). The scramble overwrites every unlocked word, so the SRAM write count rises by at least the number of unlocked words (8192 minus 256 for each locked region). A wrong DUT locks another set of regions, or overwrites fewer words. Anchor: the IMG-H readback of SRAM_LOCK (graded) and of the stores and SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION (cleared, and read 0 before each store; control); `km_sram_write_count_o` as a lower bound, taken from the baseline of the same pass (recorded at the acceptance of the handover command in step 12 or step 16). The content of the scrambled SRAM is not graded. Evidence: `CHK-HO-SRAM PASS: pass=<1/2> sram_lock=0x80000001 control_r31_dropped=1 control_viol31=1 control_image_dropped=1 control_viol_img=1 writes_delta=<n> min_unlocked=7680 locked_regions=2`.
|CHK-HO-MBOX
|The ROM drains the outbound FIFO and then flushes both mailbox FIFOs, so no junk inbound word survives the handover (`firmware.adoc`, "SRAM Firmware Handover", step "Disable IRQs and flush the mailbox"). KM_STATUS.inbound_empty reads 1 although the host posted 6 junk words after the trailer in pass 1. A wrong DUT keeps the junk. Anchor: the IMG-H readback of KM_STATUS (`km_mailbox_km.adoc`). Control: the host posted 6 words, so the FIFO is non-empty before the flush; pass 2 posts none and reports `junk=0`. Evidence: `CHK-HO-MBOX PASS: pass=<1/2> inbound_empty=1 junk=<6/0>`.
|CHK-HO-KPV-SHRED
|The ROM read-locks the registered slots and erases only the free slots (`firmware.adoc`, "SRAM Firmware Handover", step "Lock sensitive data": every free slot is shredded through the hardware erase and the erase path is not gated by slot locks), so the stored words of a registered slot keep their values. Blocked: the specification does not state the physical KPV word position under the scrambler. The ROM enables and locks the KPV scrambler at boot (`firmware.adoc`, "Boot Sequence"), so a logical key word sits at a keyed physical index that the scrambler text does not state, and the leaf cannot say which stored words belong to a registered slot (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The erase fill is not stated either, so the count of stored words that change has no stated value. The leaf logs the number of stored words of the whole KPV that change across the handover (`OBS-HO-KPV-WORDS LOG`). The CTRL 0 of every free slot and the lock_use of every registered slot are graded by CHK-HO-KPV-LOCK. Anchor: all 1024 stored words from `KM-KPV`, read before the handover command and when the first IMG-H word arrives. It logs an OBS line and not CHK PASS, and no cell is counted as reached. Evidence: `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-HO-KPV-SHRED reason=kpv-word-position`, and `OBS-HO-KPV-WORDS LOG pass=<1/2> kpv_words_changed=<n>`.
|CHK-HO-SIDELOAD-BUS
|Counted from the acceptance of the handover command of the same pass (steps 12 and 16), the ROM writes every (share, word) address in each of the eight bank windows, also in the banks that held no key (`firmware.adoc`, "SRAM Firmware Handover", step "Shred sideload keys"; the address lists come from `aes_wrapper_key.adoc`, `hmac_wrapper_key.adoc`, `kmac_wrapper_key.adoc`, `otbn_wrapper_key.adoc` and `abr_wrapper_key.adoc` in `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc`). The boot shred and the transfers of the pre-state do not count. A wrong DUT skips a bank that held no key or leaves an address unwritten. Anchor: the request tap `KM-BUS`. Control: pass 2 has six banks with no key. Evidence: `CHK-HO-SIDELOAD-BUS PASS: pass=<1/2> banks_written=8 addrs_missed=0`.
|CHK-HO-EXEC
|The first instruction fetch from outside the ROM window after the first write to SRAM_EXEC_MODE is at 0x8000, and every KM bus write between the first write to SRAM_EXEC_MODE and that fetch is a write to SRAM_EXEC_MODE (`firmware.adoc`, "SRAM Firmware Handover", steps "Enable SRAM execution mode" and "Jump": the ROM writes the enable three times immediately before the jump). A wrong DUT jumps to another address, or writes another address after it arms the execution mode. Anchor: the fetch tap `KM-FETCH` (`mem_valid`, `mem_instr`, `mem_addr`, and `mem_wstrb`: a write is a cycle with `mem_valid` high and `mem_wstrb` non-zero). Control: the writes to SRAM_EXEC_MODE show on the same tap; a pass with no write to SRAM_EXEC_MODE before the fetch at 0x8000 fails the check. Evidence: `CHK-HO-EXEC PASS: pass=<1/2> arm_writes=<n> other_writes_after_arm=0 fetch0=0x8000`, with n at least 1.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker that is not blocked PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0. The checker `CHK-HO-KPV-SHRED` needs a stated physical KPV word position and logs an OBS line, and no cell of it counts as reached.

*Scope limit*: The value of the load ceiling is not stated (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), so the mid size of step 9 is logged and not graded. IRQ_STATUS at the jump is logged. The content of the scrambled SRAM is not graded; the write count is. The stored size after a fw_crc fault in a build with the wipe predicate on is not stated, because that fatal path shreds SRAM, including `sram_fw_size`; the bench build of this leaf has the SRAM wipe off, so the leaf grades the cleared size after the fault. The fw_stack_ovf (-15) guard needs more image words than the ROM reads and has no host stimulus. The open test `sep_km_handover_test` keeps the cells that it grades; this test does not grade them again.

'''''

[[sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test]]
===== 7.28 `sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), real fuse sense, entropy noise forced, `reseed = 3`, Verilator and VCS. Two testlist entries of the one module in `km.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`, which differ in the preloaded `rom_main` build: the HOST entry (the bench build, wipe predicate off) runs steps 1 to 9 and 20 (the clean boot and the real-wipe point) and grades CHK-NOSHRED-HOST; the HOST-UNREC entry runs every step with the hooks HK-IRQSET, HK-PAR, HK-KPV and HK-LAT and grades the sideload shred and the SRAM shred of the fatal handler (CHK-SRAM-SHRED, CHK-SHRED-WITNESS, CHK-SHRED-FIRST). Each entry has its own list of expected CHK lines.

*Source Module*: `sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (the testbench LSU master is the SEP host on `km_mailbox_sep`; it also writes `SEP_CPU_CTRL.KM_WIPE_CTRL`); the frame model of the Key Manager area, written from `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc` and never from the RTL; the independent goldens `env/sep_aes_golden.py` and `env/sep_hmac_golden.py` for the AES and HMAC witnesses, defined as in `sep_km_key_transfer_rand_test` (the HMAC witness shows no key as the golden of its software key; the AES witness grades only a key). Approved probes: `HOST-MBX`, `PIC-IRQ` (vector bits 30 and 31), `KM-COUNT` (`km_sram_write_count_o`, `km_rom_req_count_o`, `km_sram_req_count_o`), `ENGINE-OUT` (the AES and HMAC witnesses). The real SEP wipe through `KM_WIPE_CTRL` is a legal host write. Added hooks: the error injects `HK-IRQSET`, `HK-PAR` and `HK-KPV` and the top-port drive `HK-LAT` on `km_sram_mem_rsp_i`, which the test adds to `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. Preload (approved): the `rom_main` build `HOST-UNREC` of the HOST-UNREC entry, loaded at t=0 by the image load `tb_backdoor_mem` (CHK-SRAM-SHRED, CHK-SHRED-WITNESS, CHK-SHRED-FIRST). `KM-LAT`, `KM-VALID`, `KM-KPV`, `KM-CSR`, `KM-BUS` and `KM-OUTFIFO` are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list; <<sep-vplan-km-proposed>> defines them. A hook drives its stated net and is never a golden. No check of this test needs a PROPOSED item. The checks CHK-FATAL-WIPE, CHK-FLUSH-FIRST, CHK-TRAP-HELD and CHK-NOSHRED-HOST (the report, the flush and the trap sit outside the wipe predicate, `firmware.adoc`, section "Error Recovery"), CHK-STATUS-HELD, CHK-RESTORE and CHK-PARITY-CLEAN need no hook and no build other than the bench build.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, two classes. RAND-REP for the fault legs: the source set (1 to 4 members of the 12 IRQ_STATUS sources); the precondition state (k keys in [1, 4], the first of 8 words; a bank subset V that holds HMAC (bit 0), AES (bit 2) and the ML-KEM message (bit 7) and a drawn subset of the other bits; a pending ABR shared key; r in [1, 3] unread CMD_STAT responses); the wipe hold width W in [8, 200] host clocks; the memory (ROM or SRAM), the lane (0 to 3) and the read index N in [1, 200] counted in read responses; the key size in [1, 128] words, the flipped word and bit, and which key. The winner rom_access has no lower-ranked source and appears alone only. RANDCFG for the latency leg: the fixed latency L in [2, 8] and the per-access latency stream in [1, 8], which holds every value 1 to 8 at least once. L = 0 is not drawn, because it violates the stated minimum. The seed draws the fatal points of the leaf, about ten boots: the real wipe point runs on every seed; 3 of the 10 single-source HK-IRQSET points (rom_parity, sram_parity, rom_write, sram_write_lock, axi_decerr, axi_slverr, drbg_err, otp_sigint, exec, rom_access), with no repeat; 3 multi-source points: the forced priority set {drbg_err, axi_slverr} on every seed and 2 winners drawn with no repeat from the other 9 winners that have a lower-ranked source (wipe_state, rom_parity, sram_parity, rom_write, sram_write_lock, axi_decerr, axi_slverr, otp_sigint, exec), each with 1 to min(3, M) drawn lower-ranked members, where M is the number of sources ranked below the winner (1 for exec, 2 for otp_sigint), and the winner axi_decerr always with the forced set {axi_decerr, axi_slverr}; and 3 of the 8 (memory, lane) parity points, with no repeat. The drawn points fill on a merge of regressions, never in one regression. The used-word class of the key CRC flip (first, middle or last used word, one in each leaf) fills on a merge too. `reseed = 3` samples the points, preconditions, hold widths, read indexes, key sizes, flip positions and latencies. A fatal fault halts the KM until a reset, so every fatal point runs in its own boot.

*Objective*: A fatal source makes the ROM shred all eight sideload banks and the shared key, flush the mailbox, send one RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT with the sign-extended code of the first source in the ISR order, shred SRAM (one Weyl pass and two xoshiro128++ passes over the SRAM) and trap. Fatal handlers leave IRQ_STATUS set. The ROM and SRAM macros store one odd-parity bit per byte; the KM checks every read response, sets the parity status bit of the memory on a mismatch, and the ROM reports -2 (ROM) or -3 (SRAM) and traps. CMD_KEY_TRANSFER checks the key CRC before the policy. A key whose words read back corrupt from the KPV gives RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT key_slot_crc (-1) first and RESP_CMD failure second, sets RECOVERABLE_ERR, writes no engine, flushes nothing and resets no counter; a transfer of the same corrupt key after CMD_RECOV_ACK raises the fault again, and residue words past the key end are not in the CRC. The SRAM macro answers a granted request at least one cycle after the grant, every command result is independent of the macro latency, and the parity bit stays aligned with its data word. Spec: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/firmware.adoc`, sections "0x24 - CMD_KEY_TRANSFER", "0xFE - RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT", "0xFF - RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT", "Error Recovery", "Register Access Shuffling", "Interrupt Priority", "Key Management"; `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, sections "Parameters", "Wipe State", "ROM and SRAM Error Detection", "Parity Error Protection", "Key Manager Control and Status Registers (KMCSR)"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc`, section "IRQ_STATUS"; `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, section "KM_WIPE_CTRL"; `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, section "SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map".

*Test Coverage*: Fatal legs: the real SEP wipe (code -1) with keys in a bank subset that includes HMAC and AES (the two witnesses) and the ML-KEM message bank (bit 7, the message of the encapsulation), a pending ABR shared key and unread responses in the outbound FIFO; HK-IRQSET with the sources rom_parity (-2), sram_parity (-3), rom_write (-4), sram_write_lock (-5), axi_decerr (-6), axi_slverr (-7), drbg_err (-8), otp_sigint (-13), exec (-17) and rom_access (-18) alone (3 drawn in each leaf), and sets of 2 to 4 sources set in one clock, one set per winner that has a lower-ranked source (the forced priority set {drbg_err, axi_slverr} in each leaf and 2 drawn winners, the winner axi_decerr with the forced set {axi_decerr, axi_slverr}). Parity leg (error injection: point, the parity bit of the ROM or SRAM macro read response; kind, single-bit parity error; expected response, the status bit of the memory and the fatal report -2 or -3): one parity-bit flip per boot on the Nth read response of ROM or SRAM in one byte lane, at 3 drawn (memory, lane) points in each leaf, the data bits untouched, with a clean boot and a no-flip twin of each point. Key CRC leg (error injection: point, the KPV read data of one logical word index, after the descrambler; kind, one inverted bit on every read of that word; expected response, RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT key_slot_crc, then RESP_CMD failure with no argument): a flip inside the used words of a key (forced: first, last and a middle word; bit 0 and bit 31), a transfer of the corrupt key to a destination outside its policy, then to a permitted destination after CMD_RECOV_ACK, a transfer of a sibling key while the fault is pending and after CMD_RECOV_ACK, and a flip in a residue word past the end of a key whose size is not a multiple of 16. Sequence leg (error injection: point, CMD_SEQ_NUM of the frame after the fault; kind, sequence 0 while the counter is not 0; expected response, RESP_CMD cmd_noseq (-3) with the next expected sequence number as argument). Latency leg (error injection: point, the SRAM macro response latency; kind, slow macro; expected response, unchanged command results): a fixed latency for a whole run and a latency drawn per access, each against the L = 1 run, with CMD_STAT, CMD_KEY_LOAD, CMD_KEY_TRANSFER to AES, an AES block, CMD_KEY_REVOKE and CMD_ENGINE_SHRED. Extends the open test `sep_km_wipe_reset_test`, which grades that a `KM_WIPE_CTRL` write posts RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT with the wipe_state code and asserts aggregator bit 30, and the open test `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`, which grades the SEP flush recoverable fault and its clear by CMD_RECOV_ACK. Those cells run here as controls. `sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test` owns the KPV wipe and the held `km_unrecoverable_err_o` on the trap kinds of the KM CPU; the KPV wipe runs here as a control. This test grades only the new cells: the halt of the fatal handler with no command answered, the shred of the banks, the flush before the report, the SRAM shred, the sideload keys that stay with the wipe predicate off, the first-source rule, the parity reports, the key CRC fault and the latency independence. The owners of the open tests keep the rest. Functional coverage: `sep_km_command_cg` (`cp_stat_recov`), `sep_km_kmcsr_status_cg` (`cp_csr_irq_source`, `cp_csr_isr_winner`, `cp_csr_recov_err`), `sep_km_cmd_reject_cg` (`cp_transfer_integrity`), `sep_km_integrity_fault_cg` (`cp_if_parity`), `sep_km_memory_boot_cg` (`cp_km_sram_latency`), `sep_km_fault_report_cg` (`cp_fr_unrec_code`, `cp_fr_code_encoding`, `cp_fr_flow_order`, `cp_fr_wipe_predicate`, `cp_fr_rec_code`, `cp_fr_resp_pairing`, `cp_fr_isr_disposition`, `cp_fr_err_out`), `sep_km_vault_wipe_cg` (`cp_vw_pre_contents`, `cp_vw_effect`, `cp_vw_edge`, `cp_vw_isr_flow`); Phase 1: `sep_km_wipe_cg` (`cp_wipe`). The predicate-on bin of `cp_fr_wipe_predicate`, the sideload-shred and SRAM-shred bins of `cp_fr_flow_order` and the sideload-purge and SRAM-shred bins of `cp_vw_isr_flow` fill in the HOST-UNREC entry, and the predicate-off bin of `cp_fr_wipe_predicate` fills in the HOST entry.

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Cold-reset the SEP with real fuse sense and entropy noise forced, bring up entropy, release the KM and wait for RESP_KM_READY (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Draw the plan of the leaf from the seed: the fatal points (the real wipe point, 3 single-source points, the set {drbg_err, axi_slverr} and 2 drawn multi-source points, 3 parity points), their preconditions and every drawn value. Log `SEED`, the `DRAW` values and `ROM-BUILD km_boot_wipe=0 km_unrec_wipe=<0/1>`, where the value is 1 in a HOST-UNREC leaf. Each boot below starts from a warm reset (or this cold reset) and a wait for RESP_KM_READY. In a boot that follows a point with a pending ABR shared key, the leaf then sends CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER with DEST_VALID bit 7 before any other step and logs the result (success when the key is still pending, failure -1 when it is not) and any RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY frame that arrives before it, so that no shared key is pending when the next one arrives (a second shared key while the first is pending has no stated outcome, as the scope limit of `sep_km_abr_shared_key_flow_rand_test` states).
. Clean boot. Send one CMD_STAT and read RESP_CMD: success with recov_fault 0. Run the boot to the largest read index N of the parity points of the leaf. Sample PIC vector bit 30 (0 expected), `km_rom_req_count_o` and `km_sram_req_count_o`.
. Real wipe point. Draw k in [1, 4] keys, load them and transfer them to the bank subset V (HMAC, AES and bit 7 are in V). The first key has 8 words and a DEST_VALID with bit 7, and its transfer mask holds bit 7; the other keys have drawn sizes. Run the ML-KEM encapsulation sequence of `sep_km_abr_shared_key_flow_rand_test` with the first key (bank 7) as the message from the vault lane, and read RESP_ABR_SHARED_KEY_READY. Do not send CMD_ABR_SK_TRANSFER. Run the HMAC witness and one AES block with the sideload key against the independent goldens (the pre-state record: both outputs equal the golden of the delivered key). Send one CMD_STAT and read RESP_CMD (the control: the KM answers), and record its latency `ctrl_stat_clk` in host clocks. Send r more CMD_STAT commands and do not read their responses. Record the outbound depth from `SEP_STATUS` (non-zero), the key_valid set V and the shared key.
. Quiet window. Sample PIC vector bit 30 over a window of `w_clk` = 10 times `ctrl_stat_clk` host clocks (the control `ctrl_stat_clk` is measured in the same leaf and the leaf prints both; 0 expected, the control) and read `km_sram_write_count_o` at the start and at the end of the window (equal, the control). Do not read the outbound FIFO.
. Error injection, point: `KM_WIPE_CTRL`; kind: fault event; expected response: the fatal sequence and one RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT with code -1. Record `km_sram_write_count_o` and the outbound depth. Write `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state` = 1, hold it for W host clocks, then write 0.
. Wait for PIC vector bit 30 to rise (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Read the outbound FIFO to empty and parse the frames. Sample bit 30 20 times over the window `w_clk` of step 4. Send CMD_STAT and record every frame in the window.
. Read `km_sram_write_count_o` at the rise of bit 30, because the trap follows the SRAM shred (`firmware.adoc`, section "Error Recovery"), and take the delta from step 5. Run the HMAC witness again with a second fixed message that differs from the message of step 3. In a HOST-UNREC leaf compare the digest with the golden of its software key (no key), then run the AES witness block again with the sideload key: an output equal to the golden of the delivered key fails CHK-SHRED-WITNESS (a leaked key), and any other result, a stalled engine included, is logged as `OBS-AES-NOKEY`. In a HOST leaf, where the wipe predicate is off, compare the digest with the golden of the delivered key (CHK-NOSHRED-HOST), and log the write-count delta and the AES result with `control=1`.
. With the taps, record `KM-VALID` and `KM-KPV` at the first clock in which the outbound FIFO holds a word of the fault frame (the `KM-OUTFIFO` fill level, sampled on every clock), and record `KM-CSR` IRQ_STATUS after the trap.
. Warm reset and wait for RESP_KM_READY. Send CMD_STAT and read RESP_CMD; sample bit 30.
. HK-IRQSET single-source points of the leaf, each in its own boot. Repeat steps 3 and 4 for the same precondition without the witness runs, then write HK-IRQSET with the bit of the source (error injection, point: the IRQ_SET write path of the KMCSR; kind: spurious fault event; expected response: the report of that source). Repeat steps 6, 8 and 9.
. HK-IRQSET multi-source points of the leaf, each in its own boot. Take the winner of the point and draw 1 to min(3, M) lower-ranked members, M being the number of sources ranked below the winner (the order is wipe_state, rom_parity, sram_parity, rom_write, sram_write_lock, axi_decerr, drbg_err, axi_slverr, otp_sigint, exec, rom_access). Compute the expected code of the first source in this order from the code table. Repeat steps 3 and 4 and write HK-IRQSET with all bits of the set in one clock. Repeat steps 6, 8 and 9. The forced priority sets are {drbg_err, axi_slverr} (expected -8) and {axi_decerr, axi_slverr} (expected -6).
. Parity points of the leaf, each in its own boot with its no-flip twin. The memory M and the lane l come from the drawn point, and N is drawn. Start the boot, send the first CMD_STAT, and arm HK-PAR to flip parity bit l of the Nth read response of M after that CMD_STAT (the data bits stay untouched). Read the report and record IRQ_STATUS and bit 30. If the flipped response is not consumed by the CPU, log `VACUOUS`, redraw N + 1 and repeat, up to 4 redraws. Warm reset. Run the twin boot to the same read count with HK-PAR idle. Sample bit 30 and record the frames.
. Key CRC point. Boot, load key B of 4 words, then load key A (size in [1, 128], DEST_VALID with AES and without at least one other bit). Read the slot run of key A from the `KM-KPV` tap (the slots whose CTRL changed with the load of A). Transfer A to AES (success, the control), record the `KM-BUS` writes of this transfer (the control of CHK-KEYCRC: the tap sees the share writes to the AES key window), and shred the AES bank. Transfer A to the destination outside its policy and read RESP_CMD: failure with no argument and no fault frame (the policy-only control).
. Error injection, point: the KPV read data of one logical word index, after the descrambler; kind: one inverted bit on every read of that word; expected response: RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT key_slot_crc, then RESP_CMD failure. Arm HK-KPV on the logical index s_A x 16 + w, where s_A is the first slot of the run of A and w is a used key word of A (forced picks: first, last and a middle used word), to invert bit b (forced picks: 0 and 31). HK-KPV stays armed until step 18. Send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER for A to the destination outside its policy. Read the two frames: 0xFE with payload 0xFFFFFFFF, then RESP_CMD failure with no argument. Record `KM-VALID`, `KM-KPV` CTRL of A and `KM-BUS` writes in the eight engine key windows.
. Send CMD_STAT with the next command sequence number and read RESP_CMD (the argument has bit 0 set). Error injection, point: CMD_SEQ_NUM of the frame after the fault; kind: sequence 0 while the counter is not 0; expected response: RESP_CMD cmd_noseq (-3) with the next expected sequence number as argument. Send a CMD_STAT frame with sequence 0 and read RESP_CMD.
. Send CMD_RECOV_ACK and read RESP_CMD: success. Send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER for A to AES (a permitted destination) and read the two frames again. Record `KM-VALID` and `KM-BUS`.
. With the fault pending, send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER for B to AES and read RESP_CMD (failure, gated). Send CMD_RECOV_ACK (success). Send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER for B to AES and read RESP_CMD (success) and AES key_valid.
. Residue. Load key C of a drawn size in [1, 128] that is not a multiple of 16. Disarm HK-KPV. Read the slot run of C from the `KM-KPV` CTRL change, and arm HK-KPV to invert one bit of a word past the end of C in its last slot (logical index s_last x 16 + j, j in [size mod 16, 15]). Send CMD_KEY_TRANSFER for C to a permitted destination and read RESP_CMD (success, no fault frame).
. Latency points. Run the L = 1 boot first: set HK-LAT to 1, boot, send CMD_STAT, CMD_KEY_LOAD (8 words, DEST_VALID with AES), CMD_KEY_TRANSFER to AES, run one AES block against the golden, send CMD_KEY_REVOKE and CMD_ENGINE_SHRED, and keep all response words. Repeat with the fixed latency L, and then with the per-access stream. Read `KM-LAT` for every SRAM read.
. Warm reset, wait for RESP_KM_READY and send CMD_STAT after every point (the restore control).

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-FATAL-WIPE
|On the real-wipe point a write of 1 to `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state` makes the ROM send exactly one RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT with code -1 (payload 0xFFFFFFFF), and `km_unrecoverable_err_o` (PIC vector bit 30) rises (`firmware.adoc`, "Error Recovery", "0xFF - RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT"; `sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, "KM_WIPE_CTRL"; `interrupts.adoc`). A wrong DUT sends another code, two frames or none, or never raises bit 30. The open test `sep_km_wipe_reset_test` keeps the grade of these values. The line carries `control=1`: it proves that the point reached the fatal state before the checks of the same point read the effects. Anchor: the response frame and PIC vector bit 30. Control: bit 30 reads 0 in the quiet window before the write. Evidence: `CHK-FATAL-WIPE PASS: control=1 code=0xFFFFFFFF frames=1 err_o=1`.
|CHK-FLUSH-FIRST
|On the real-wipe point the ROM flushes the mailbox before it sends the fault frame, so the host reads only the fault frame (`firmware.adoc`, "Error Recovery": step 2 flushes the FIFOs and sends the report). The outbound FIFO held r unread responses before the write, and after bit 30 rises the host reads exactly one frame. A wrong DUT leaves a stale RESP_CMD next to the fault frame. Anchor: `SEP_READ_DATA` and the outbound depth recorded before the write. Control: the recorded depth is non-zero. Evidence: `CHK-FLUSH-FIRST PASS: out_depth_before=<d> out_frames_after=1 stale_resp=0`.
|CHK-SRAM-SHRED
|On the real-wipe point the SRAM is shredded between the write and the trap: the delta of `km_sram_write_count_o`, read at the rise of bit 30, is at least 8192 writes, one SRAM of words (`firmware.adoc`, "Error Recovery": the SRAM shred is one Weyl pass and two xoshiro128++ passes, so more than one pass; `architecture.adoc`, "Parameters": the SRAM has 8192 words). The extent of each pass on the fatal path is not stated (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), so the leaf logs the delta and the value 24576 of three full passes (`full_extent_logged`) and does not grade the number of passes. A wrong DUT skips the shred. Anchor: `km_sram_write_count_o`. Control: the count is read in a quiet window before the write and does not move. Only a HOST-UNREC leaf runs this check. Evidence: `CHK-SRAM-SHRED PASS: writes_delta=<n> min=8192 full_extent_logged=24576`.
|CHK-SHRED-WITNESS
|On the real-wipe point the HMAC witness holds no key after the report: its digest equals the golden of its software key, although before the write it equals the golden of the delivered key (`firmware.adoc`, "Error Recovery": step 1 shreds all eight sideload banks). A wrong DUT leaves the HMAC key and fails. The AES witness grades only a leaked key: an AES output equal to the golden of the delivered key fails, and any other AES result, a stalled engine included, is logged as `OBS-AES-NOKEY` and is not part of the pass. Anchor: `ENGINE-OUT`. Control: the pre-state record `hmac_before=golden aes_before=golden`. Only a HOST-UNREC leaf runs this check. Evidence: `CHK-SHRED-WITNESS PASS: hmac_before=golden hmac_after=nokey aes_before=golden aes_key_after=0`.
|CHK-NOSHRED-HOST
|In a HOST leaf (the bench build, wipe predicate off) the real-wipe point leaves the sideload keys in place: after the report the HMAC witness digest equals the golden of the delivered key (`firmware.adoc`, "Error Recovery": the sideload and SRAM wipes of the fatal handler share the `rom_unrec_wipe_enabled()` predicate, and the report and the trap are outside it; `architecture.adoc`, "Wipe State": the hardware wipe does not scrub the accelerator sideload registers). A wrong DUT shreds the banks with the predicate off, or clears a sideload register on the wipe edge. Only a HOST leaf runs this check. The SRAM write-count delta is logged and not graded, because the specification states no bound for the writes of the fatal path without the SRAM shred. Anchor: `ENGINE-OUT`. Control: the pre-state record `hmac_before=golden`; the run after the report hashes a second message, so a stale digest cannot match its golden; CHK-FATAL-WIPE shows that the point reached the fatal state. Evidence: `CHK-NOSHRED-HOST PASS: km_unrec_wipe=0 hmac_before=golden hmac_after=golden msg_changed=1 writes_delta_logged=<n>`.
|CHK-TRAP-HELD
|On the real-wipe point `km_unrecoverable_err_o` stays high from the first rise until a reset and the KM answers no command while it is high (`architecture.adoc`, "Error Events": the signal stays asserted while the KM is in the trap state, and the KM does not respond to further commands). The halt of the ROM fatal handler after the report and the silence to a command are new cells here: `sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test` grades the held output on the trap kinds of the KM CPU and sends no command. A wrong DUT drops bit 30 before the reset or answers the CMD_STAT. Anchor: PIC vector bit 30 sampled 20 times; the CMD_STAT of step 6 with no response frame in the window `w_clk`. Control: bit 30 reads 0 and CMD_STAT is answered before the write, with the latency `ctrl_stat_clk`. Evidence: `CHK-TRAP-HELD PASS: control_quiet=0 control_stat=answered ctrl_stat_clk=<c> w_clk=<10c> err_o=1 samples=20 stat_answered=0`.
|CHK-RESTORE
|Control. After every point a warm reset restores a working KM: CMD_STAT succeeds with recov_fault 0 and bit 30 reads 0 (`architecture.adoc`, "Error Events": a warm reset clears the trap). Anchor: RESP_CMD and PIC vector bit 30. Evidence: `CHK-RESTORE PASS: control=1 stat=0x0 err_o=0`.
|CHK-PARITY-CLEAN
|Control. A clean boot with ROM and SRAM reads running gives no unrecoverable report: bit 30 stays 0 and no fault frame arrives, so the later parity points do not fail on a design that rejects its own odd parity (`architecture.adoc`, "ROM and SRAM Error Detection"). The twin of each parity point runs to the same read count as the point. Anchor: PIC vector bit 30 and the frames; `km_rom_req_count_o` and `km_sram_req_count_o`. Evidence: `CHK-PARITY-CLEAN PASS: reads=<n> err_o=0 frames=0`.
|CHK-FATAL-SET
|A single IRQ_STATUS source, or a set of sources set in one clock, gives exactly one RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT with the sign-extended code of the first source in the order wipe_state, rom_parity, sram_parity, rom_write, sram_write_lock, axi_decerr, drbg_err, axi_slverr, otp_sigint, exec, rom_access, and bits [31:8] of the payload equal the sign extension (`firmware.adoc`, "Interrupt Priority", "0xFF - RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT"; `km_csr.adoc`, "IRQ_STATUS" for the bit of each source). The expected code comes from the code table. A wrong DUT reports a lower-ranked member, a wrong extension or two frames. `km_unrecoverable_err_o` and the silence after the trap log `control_err_o=1`. It is graded at every single-source and every multi-source point of the leaf. Stimulus: `HK-IRQSET`. Anchor: the response frame. Control: the real-wipe point reaches the same report path through the wipe input (CHK-FATAL-WIPE), and the clean boot sends no fault frame (CHK-PARITY-CLEAN). Evidence: `CHK-FATAL-SET PASS: set=<mask> order_first=<name> code=<0xFFFFFFxx> frames=1 control_err_o=1`, one line for each point.
|CHK-SHRED-FIRST
|When the report is visible, every engine key_valid and the shared key valid bit are clear (`firmware.adoc`, "Error Recovery": the report follows the sideload shred). The pre-state record is non-zero. A wrong DUT sends the report before the shred. Anchor: `KM-VALID` sampled at the first clock in which the outbound FIFO holds a word of the fault frame (`KM-OUTFIFO`). Control: the pre-state record `keyvalid_before`. Only a HOST-UNREC leaf runs this check. Evidence: `CHK-SHRED-FIRST PASS: keyvalid_before=<mask> shared_before=1 keyvalid_at_report=0x00 shared_zero=1`.
|CHK-STATUS-HELD
|After the trap every IRQ_STATUS bit of the set is still set, because fatal handlers leave the triggering status uncleared (`firmware.adoc`, "Interrupt Priority"). A wrong DUT clears a bit. Anchor: `KM-CSR` IRQ_STATUS, because the CPU is halted. Evidence: `CHK-STATUS-HELD PASS: status=<mask>`.
|CHK-KPV-TRAP
|Control. After the ROM trap of an HK-IRQSET point all 1024 KPV words and all 64 CTRL registers are 0, because the CPU trap triggers the KPV wipe (`architecture.adoc`, "Wipe State"). The real-wipe point is excluded, because the wipe edge clears the vault before the trap. The vault is non-zero before the injection. Anchor: `KM-KPV`. `sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test` owns the grade. Evidence: `CHK-KPV-TRAP PASS: control=1 words_nonzero=0 ctrl_nonzero=0`.
|CHK-PARITY
|A flipped parity bit on the Nth read response of a memory sets the parity status bit of that memory (IRQ_STATUS bit 0 for ROM, bit 1 for SRAM) and leaves the parity status bit of the other memory clear, and the ROM sends one RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT with code -2 (0xFFFFFFFE) for ROM or -3 (0xFFFFFFFD) for SRAM and traps (`architecture.adoc`, "ROM and SRAM Error Detection", "Parity Error Protection"; `firmware.adoc`, "0xFF - RESP_UNRECOVERABLE_FAULT"; `km_csr.adoc`, "IRQ_STATUS"). The SRAM parity bit is computed on the plaintext word before scrambling (`hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc`), so the flip does not depend on the scrambler state. A wrong DUT sets no bit, the other memory's bit, a wrong code, or does not trap. Stimulus: `HK-PAR`. IRQ_STATUS bit 7 is not graded, because the trap that follows the report also sets it (`architecture.adoc`, "Key Manager Control and Status Registers (KMCSR)", the interrupt source table: bit 7 rises on a wipe_state edge or a CPU trap), and the other bits are logged. Anchor: `KM-CSR` IRQ_STATUS; the response frame; PIC vector bit 30. Control: the no-flip twin (CHK-PARITY-CLEAN). Evidence: `CHK-PARITY PASS: mem=<rom/sram> lane=<l> n=<N> par_bit=1 other_par_bit=0 status_logged=<s> code=<0xFFFFFFFE/0xFFFFFFFD> err_o=1`.
|CHK-PARITY-VAC
|A flipped response that the CPU did not consume is redrawn up to 4 times; a point that stays unconsumed fails the leaf, so no parity cell passes by a flip that never reached the check. Stimulus: `HK-PAR`. Evidence: `CHK-PARITY-VAC PASS: redraws=<r>`.
|CHK-KEYCRC
|An inverted bit on the KPV read data of a used key word (HK-KPV) makes CMD_KEY_TRANSFER send RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT key_slot_crc (payload 0xFFFFFFFF) first and RESP_CMD failure (code -1, no argument) second, set RECOVERABLE_ERR (CMD_STAT bit 0 reads 1), write no engine key window and leave the CTRL of the slots unchanged (`firmware.adoc`, "Key Management" Check and Transfer, "Error Recovery", "0xFE - RESP_RECOVERABLE_FAULT"; `architecture.adoc`, "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)"). The engine-write grade comes from the transfer of A to AES after CMD_RECOV_ACK: AES is in the policy of A, so only the CRC check stops the write. On the transfer to the destination outside the policy the policy check alone also stops the write, so the bus record of that transfer is logged and not graded. A wrong DUT sends no fault frame, sends the frames in the other order, leaves RECOVERABLE_ERR clear, changes CTRL, or writes an engine bank. Stimulus: `HK-KPV`. Anchor: the response frames; CMD_STAT; `KM-KPV` and `KM-BUS`. Control: the transfer of A to AES before the flip succeeds (step 13), so the fault comes from the flip, and `KM-BUS` records its share writes to the AES key window, so the tap sees an engine write; a record with no AES write fails the check. Evidence: `CHK-KEYCRC PASS: size=<s> word=<w> bit=<b> first=0xFE code=-1 second_code=-1 arg=none stat=0x1 ctrl_same=1 bus_bank_writes=0 control_bus_aes_writes=<n>`, with n above 0.
|CHK-CRC-FIRST
|For a corrupt key and a destination outside its policy the fault frame comes first, so the CRC check precedes the policy check (`firmware.adoc`, "Key Management": Transfer calls Check first). A wrong DUT answers failure from the policy check without a fault frame. Stimulus: `HK-KPV`. Anchor: the response frames. Control: before the flip the same transfer returns failure with no fault frame (step 13). Evidence: `CHK-CRC-FIRST PASS: policy_dest=<mask> first=0xFE control_first=0x00`.
|CHK-NOFLUSH
|The key fault does not flush the mailbox and does not reset the sequence counters (`firmware.adoc`, "Error Recovery"; "Command Message Reference"): the response sequence numbers of the two fault frames are consecutive and continue the count of the response frames read since the boot, a CMD_STAT with the next command sequence number succeeds with bit 0 set, and a frame with sequence 0 returns cmd_noseq (-3) with the next expected sequence number as argument. A wrong DUT flushes, so a frame with sequence 0 is accepted and the response numbers restart. The expected sequence numbers come from the count of frames in the leaf; the leaf sends fewer than 255 commands per boot, so the counter does not wrap to 0. Stimulus: `HK-KPV`. Anchor: the response headers; RESP_CMD. Control: on the real-wipe point of the same leaf the ROM flushes the mailbox before its report (CHK-FLUSH-FIRST), and after each warm reset the CMD_STAT of CHK-RESTORE succeeds with sequence 0, so a flush and a counter restart are visible to the same decoder. Evidence: `CHK-NOFLUSH PASS: resp_seq_a=<n> resp_seq_b=<n+1> next_cmd_ok=1 seq0_code=-3 seq0_arg=<next>`.
|CHK-GATE-AFTER
|With HK-KPV still armed, the transfer of the corrupt key after CMD_RECOV_ACK raises the fault again, because Transfer calls Check on every transfer (`firmware.adoc`, "Key Management"), and with the fault pending the transfer of the sibling key is gated (RESP_CMD failure, no argument), then succeeds after CMD_RECOV_ACK (`firmware.adoc`, "Error Recovery": while RECOVERABLE_ERR is set only five commands reach their handlers). A wrong DUT skips the CRC check after the acknowledgement, transfers the corrupt key, or does not gate the sibling. Stimulus: `HK-KPV`. Anchor: RESP_CMD. Control: the sibling transfer succeeds after the acknowledgement. Evidence: `CHK-GATE-AFTER PASS: b_gated=-1 ack=0 b_ok=0 a_again=fault`.
|CHK-RESIDUE
|An inverted read-data bit on a word past the end of a key whose size is not a multiple of 16 raises no fault, because the CRC covers only the key words (`firmware.adoc`, "Key Management": the final slot is read only for the remaining key words; Check computes the CRC-32C over the key). A wrong DUT raises the fault. Stimulus: `HK-KPV`. Anchor: RESP_CMD. Control: the flip inside the key (CHK-KEYCRC) raises the fault. Evidence: `CHK-RESIDUE PASS: size=<s> flip_index=<i> code=0`.
|CHK-LAT
|With a fixed SRAM macro latency L in [2, 8] every response frame equals the frame that the frame model states and equals the frame of the L = 1 run (the RESP_KM_READY frame and five RESP_CMD frames), the AES ciphertext equals the independent golden, no fault frame arrives, bit 30 stays 0, and the observed latency equals L (`architecture.adoc`, "Parameters": the macro returns read data at least one cycle after the grant; "ROM and SRAM Error Detection": the parity bit travels with the data word). A wrong DUT gives another response, a wrong ciphertext or a parity fault at some latency. A leaf where the hook did nothing fails `CHK-NONVAC`, because the observed latency differs from L. Stimulus: `HK-LAT`. Anchor: the response words; `ENGINE-OUT`; `KM-LAT`. Control: the L = 1 run is the twin of every other latency, and the parity points of the same leaf raise bit 30 and send a fault frame (CHK-PARITY), so a fault is visible to the same anchors. Evidence: `CHK-LAT PASS: mode=fixed L=<L> observed=<L> resp_equal=<n> aes_ok=1 err_o=0`.
|CHK-LAT-VAR
|The same contract holds with a latency drawn per access in [1, 8]: every response equals the L = 1 run, the AES ciphertext equals the golden, no fault arrives, and `KM-LAT` reports for every read the value that HK-LAT set for that access. Anchor: as `CHK-LAT`. Evidence: `CHK-LAT-VAR PASS: mode=random L=1..8 resp_equal=<n> aes_ok=1 err_o=0`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every checker of the entry PASSes. The HOST entry grades CHK-FATAL-WIPE, CHK-FLUSH-FIRST, CHK-NOSHRED-HOST, CHK-TRAP-HELD, CHK-STATUS-HELD, CHK-RESTORE and CHK-PARITY-CLEAN. The HOST-UNREC entry grades every checker except CHK-NOSHRED-HOST. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The codes -9, -10, -11, -15 and -16 have no stimulus (<<sep-vplan-phase3-noobs,Features with no observation point>>). The code -12 (spurious_irq) needs a CPU interrupt line that the specification does not number (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The code -14 is graded in `sep_km_handover_rand_test`. The OTP change handler is dormant in the ROM, because the boot sequence does not enable the source, so no check can fail on its non-fatal flow and the leaf does not set the source. The return code of CMD_KEY_REVOKE on a corrupt key is not stated and the leaf does not send it. The ROM macro latency has no stated range and stays at the bench default. The extent of each SRAM shred pass of the fatal handler is not stated, so the leaf logs the write count and grades one SRAM of writes only (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The output of an AES that holds no key is not stated (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), so the absence of a key is graded on the HMAC witness, and the AES witness fails only on an output that equals the golden of the delivered key. The open tests `sep_km_wipe_reset_test` and `sep_km_command_set_rand_test` keep the cells that they grade, and `sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test` keeps the KPV wipe and the held output on the trap kinds of the KM CPU.

'''''

[[sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test]]
===== 7.29 `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle POKE, `+skip_fuse_sense`, SRAM scrambler off, `reseed = 3`; testlist `km.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; VCS (Verilator runs the value checks)

*Source Module*: `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (the testbench LSU master is the SEP host: `km_mailbox_sep`, `SEP_RESET_CTRL`, `SEP_STATUS`). The POKE image is a KM image of the class of `km_rom_vault` (`km_rom_blob`) that executes a list of KM bus operations (read, write, back-to-back write, poll, read loop, trap, jump, interrupt arm with a counting handler) as a legal master of the KM bus. The host posts the list through the mailbox, and the image reports each result (data, `slv`, `dec`) in KM SRAM words 0 to 97. The KM CPU does not see the AXI response code. It sees the read data and the IRQ_STATUS bits AXI_SLVERR and AXI_DECERR, so the image clears these bits before an operation and reports them after it as `slv` and `dec`. An op marked raw skips that clear and that report. The ops of the IRQ_STATUS walk (step 4), the IRQ_SET masks (step 6), the enable aggregate (step 7) and the IRQ_STATUS witness with its readback (steps 18 to 20) are raw, so a bit 4 or 5 that a step sets stays set until the step clears it. The counting handler on CPU IRQ bit 3 (KMCSR) counts one entry and clears (write one) the IRQ_STATUS bits that are both set and enabled. The register model of the test is written from `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc` and `architecture.adoc`, never from the RTL. Approved probes: `KM-RESULT` (the POKE readback), `HOST-MBX` (`SEP_STATUS` for the mailbox witness), `KM-COUNT` (`km_rom_req_count_o`), `KM-ISO` (`hmac_km_isolated_probe_o`, `kmac_km_isolated_probe_o`, `abr_km_isolated_probe_o`), `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (the `sep_reset_n` release after `rst_ni`; `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, SEP reset probes). `KM-RST` (CHK-HOLD, and the extra PARK holds W) is an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. Injects: none. The resets are legal: a write of `SOFT_RST_CODE` by the image, a write of `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 by the host, and a pulse of `rst_ni` by the testbench.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, RAND-REP. Dimensions: the bit order of the IRQ_STATUS walk, the IRQ_SET masks and the enable subsets, the entry value, the scrambler key values, the store address and value, the 22 size tags and the 8 drawn size addresses, the reset kind of each round (all four kinds run in each leaf, in a drawn order), the witness subset and values, the wrong reset codes and the extra PARK hold W. Legal ranges: IRQ bits 0 to 11 (reserved bits are written as 0), SRAM_LOCK regions 1 to 31 (region 0 holds the POKE image data and is never locked or written), word-aligned addresses; the round 3 addresses include at least one locked region other than 31 and at least one unlocked region. The extra PARK hold W is the number of host clocks that the leaf keeps `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 low after the reset has asserted at the KM; it is a test parameter, and the specification states no bound for it. The extra holds 1, 2, 5 and 12 host clocks are forced over the four PARK resets of the SRAM rounds (the PARK before round 1 and the PARK after each round). W serves only CHK-HOLD, and the extra holds are timed from `KM-RST`. Every drawn key word, witness value and size tag is non-zero, the witness values are pairwise different, and the size tags are pairwise different. The 8 drawn size addresses are word-aligned in [0x8400, 0xFFFC] and outside the walk set of step 11. The wrong codes 0x00000000 and 0xFFFFFFFF are forced in each leaf, and three more are drawn: at least one from the single-bit neighbours of the code 0x53525354, and the others from the single-bit neighbours or from uniform values. The test asserts that each wrong code differs from 0x53525354 before it writes it. `reseed = 3` samples different orders, masks, addresses, values and codes.

*Objective*: A VERSION write answers SLVERR and leaves the value. IRQ_STATUS has 12 sticky write-one-to-clear bits and reserved bits that read 0. IRQ_SET sets the matching bits. The interrupt is the OR of status AND enable, and a masked sticky bit still sets. IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR resets to 0x10 and accepts any value until IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK, which is write-one-only. SCRAMBLER_KEY reads back the written value while the lock is clear, and SCRAMBLER_CTRL reads 0 at boot. DEBUG reads 0xCAFEBEEF and CTRL reads 0. SRAM_LOCK has 32 write-one-to-set bits, one per 1 KiB region of 0x8000 to 0xFFFF (region = (address - 0x8000) / 0x400; region 31 holds rom_persist). A store to a locked region is dropped and sets the bit of the store's region in SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION (write one to clear) and IRQ_STATUS.SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_ERR. The SRAM has 8192 words, and each of the 13 word-address bits selects a distinct word.
