A warm reset (`km_sw_rst_n` or SOFT_RST_CODE 0x53525354) clears the warm-domain state and keeps the cold-domain state and the SRAM contents. A cold reset clears both domains. The warm reset stays asserted at least MIN_RESET_CYCLES (10) clocks after its trigger releases. Any other SOFT_RST_CODE value does nothing. The soft reset does not isolate the key paths. Spec: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, sections "Reset Conditioning", "Key Manager Control and Status Registers (KMCSR)", "Parity Error Protection", "SRAM Write Locking", "ROM Warm-Persistent SRAM Region"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc`, sections "VERSION", "CTRL", "SOFT_RST_CODE", "IRQ_STATUS", "IRQ_ENABLE", "SCRAMBLER_KEY", "SCRAMBLER_CTRL", "SRAM_LOCK", "IRQ_SET", "SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION", "BOOT_STATUS", "IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR", "IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK", "DEBUG"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, sections "Isolation and Reset Sequencing", "Resetting the Key Manager".

*Test Coverage*: Reset reads of IRQ_ENABLE, IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR, IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK, VERSION, DEBUG, CTRL and SCRAMBLER_CTRL; a VERSION write of a drawn value (error injection: point, KM bus write to VERSION; kind, write to a read-only register; expected response, SLVERR and the value unchanged). A walk over the 12 IRQ_STATUS bits in a drawn order, each raised by IRQ_SET, with W1C of the other bits, a write of 0 and its own W1C. IRQ_SET masks of 1 to 5 bits. Enable subsets against status sets with the counting handler, with every one of the 12 sources set under its enable 0 (the walk) and under its enable 1 (case (c)) on every seed. IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR values (aligned, unaligned, 0xFFFFFFFF) before and after IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK. SCRAMBLER_KEY writes with the lock clear and the enable 0. SRAM size and alias stores at word 8191 (0xFFFC), at the 13 words 8191 - 2^k (k = 0 to 12, 0xFFF8 to 0xBFFC) and at 8 drawn words in regions 1 to 31. Three SRAM rounds with a PARK between them: the odd regions 1 to 31 in two writes, the even regions 2 to 30 in two writes, and a drawn mask of 3 to 8 regions that includes region 31; stores to each locked and unlocked region, with the words written before the lock so that every compared word is defined; region 31 stores at 0xFC00, 0xFC04 and 0xFFFC. Warm-domain witnesses: SRAM_LOCK, IRQ_ENABLE, IRQ_STATUS (masked bits), IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR with its lock, OTP_READ_LOCK, DRBG sampler CFG. Cold-domain witnesses: OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD, COLD_BOOT_DONE (and a write of 0 to it). Data witnesses: SRAM words, one host word in the mailbox. The KPV CTRL bits and the KPV scrambler across the reset kinds are graded in the Phase 3 test `sep_km_kpv_rand_test`. Four reset kinds: SOFT, PARK (`SW_RESET_N` bit 0), COLD (`rst_ni`) and SOFT-WRONG (error injection: point, KM bus write to SOFT_RST_CODE; kind, wrong code; expected response, no reset). Extends the open test `sep_km_command_set_rand_test`, which grades the VERSION value through CMD_HW_VER. It also extends the open tests `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test` and the KM bus-error tests, which grade AXI_SLVERR by a KPV past-extent read, one locked SRAM region (dropped store, violation bit, IRQ status bit, clear), DRBG_ERR through IRQ_SET, their W1C clear and ROM_WRITE_ERR by a ROM store, and the open test `sep_km_drbg_sampler_timeout_reset_test`, which grades the sampler CFG, STATUS and DRBG IRQ_STATUS reset values and COLD_BOOT_DONE = 1 after the PARK path. Those cells run here as controls. This test grades only the new cells: the VERSION write, the W1C of other bits, the write of 0, IRQ_SET of all 12 bits, the enable aggregate, the entry address and its lock, the scrambler key read-back, DEBUG and CTRL, masks of several SRAM regions, the OR of two writes, a write of 0, region 31, the release by a warm reset, the size and alias of the array, and the reset domain matrix with the soft-reset code and the hold. The owners of the open tests keep the rest. Functional coverage: `sep_km_memory_boot_cg` (`cp_mem_iface_req`, `cp_mem_scr_key_access`, `cp_mem_scr_ctrl` (the enable-0 bin), `cp_km_sram_region`, `cp_mem_r31`), `sep_km_otp_readout_cg` (`cp_otp_lock_reg`), `sep_km_kmcsr_status_cg` (`cp_csr_version`, `cp_csr_irq_source`, `cp_csr_status_semantics`, `cp_csr_src_x_en`, `cp_csr_aggregate`, `cp_csr_entry_vector`, `cp_csr_entry_lock`, `cp_csr_debug_ctrl_read`), `sep_km_integrity_fault_cg` (`cp_if_sram_lock_violation`), `sep_km_drbg_sampler_cg` (`cp_smp_reset_defaults`), `sep_km_reset_domain_cg` (`cp_rd_source`, `cp_rd_soft_wrong`, `cp_rd_matrix`, `cp_rd_cbd_history`, `cp_rd_hold`, `cp_rd_isolation`); Phase 1: `sep_km_sw_reset_cg` (`cp_rel`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Cold-reset the SEP with `+skip_fuse_sense`, release the KM and wait for the start marker of the POKE image in the KM SRAM result words (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Draw the plan. Log `SEED` and every `DRAW` value.
. Post a list that reads IRQ_ENABLE, IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR, IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK, VERSION, DEBUG, CTRL and SCRAMBLER_CTRL. Log the VERSION read value as a control.
. Error injection, point: KM bus write to VERSION; kind: write to a read-only register; expected response: SLVERR and the value unchanged. Post a list that clears IRQ_STATUS (write one to all 12 bits), reads VERSION, writes a drawn value that differs from 0x0001_0000 (the `VERSION` reset value in `km_csr.adoc`), reads IRQ_STATUS (`slv`) and reads VERSION again.
. IRQ_STATUS walk. Arm the counting handler for CPU IRQ bit 3 with IRQ_ENABLE at 0, read the handler count after each IRQ_SET write of the walk, and disarm the handler after the walk. For each of the 12 bits i in a drawn order, post a list that writes IRQ_SET with bit i and reads IRQ_STATUS; writes IRQ_STATUS with the other 11 bits set (W1C of the complement) and reads it; writes 0 and reads it; writes IRQ_SET with a drawn second bit j and reads it; writes IRQ_STATUS with bit i and reads it; writes IRQ_STATUS with bit j and reads it. Every IRQ_STATUS read also shows the reserved bits [31:12].
. Real-cause pass, logged only. Raise AXI_SLVERR by a read at 0x13108 (past the KPV extent), AXI_DECERR by a read at 0x4000 (reserved span), ROM_WRITE_ERR by a store to 0x0 (ROM) and DRBG_ERR by a read of the DRBG sampler DATA with CFG.timeout = 1 and no stream data. Read IRQ_STATUS after each, clear it, and log the bits that rose with the cause. No value of this step fails the leaf.
. IRQ_SET masks. Draw three masks of 1 to 5 of the 12 bits. For each, post a list that writes IRQ_SET with the mask, reads IRQ_STATUS and clears all bits. The IRQ_SET register is never read.
. Enable aggregate. Arm the counting handler for CPU IRQ bit 3. For each of four drawn cases, post a list: (a) IRQ_ENABLE = 0 and a non-zero status set S by IRQ_SET; (b) IRQ_ENABLE = E with S AND E = 0, S non-zero; (c) IRQ_ENABLE = E and S with S AND E non-zero, run 12 times, once with each source i of the 12 in S AND E (the sources in drawn order, the other bits of S and E drawn), so that every source raises the line with its enable 1 on every seed; (d) S set with IRQ_ENABLE = 0, then IRQ_ENABLE written with a bit that is already set. After each case read the handler count and IRQ_STATUS, then write IRQ_ENABLE = 0 and clear IRQ_STATUS. Disarm the handler.
. SCRAMBLER registers. Read SCRAMBLER_CTRL. Write SCRAMBLER_KEY = k (non-zero) and read it. Write SCRAMBLER_KEY = k2 (different from k) and read it. Do not set the lock or the enable.
. IRQ entry. With the lock clear write three values in turn to IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR: an aligned value other than 0x10, an unaligned value and 0xFFFFFFFF; read it after each write. Write IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK = 1 and read it. Write a value v2 that differs from the stored value and read IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR. Write 0 to IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK and read it.
. PARK reset: write `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 low (the other bits at 1) and wait until the three `KM-ISO` probes read isolated and `km_rom_req_count_o` (`KM-COUNT`) reads the same value on two successive reads (the isolation has drained and the KM is held, `reset_controller.adoc`, "Isolation and Reset Sequencing"; a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf; no cycle count is assumed). Keep the bit low for W further host clocks after the net `km_sw_rst_n` (`KM-RST`) has fallen, W drawn from {1, 2, 5, 12} (each value forced over the four PARK resets of steps 10 and 16). Then write the bit high. Wait for the start marker. This reset restores IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR.
. Size and alias. Store a distinct non-zero tag at word 8191 (0xFFFC), at each word 8191 - 2^k for k = 0 to 12 (byte address 0xFFFC - 4 x 2^k, from 0xFFF8 to 0xBFFC; no word of this walk set lies in region 0) and at the 8 drawn addresses. Store all 22 tags first, then read all 22 words back.
. SRAM round r = 1, 2 and 3. Draw the store addresses first. Round 1 draws one word-aligned address in each region 1 to 31. Round 2 does the same. Round 3 draws its lock set first (below), then 12 distinct word-aligned addresses in [0x8400, 0xFFFC] with at least one in a locked region other than 31 and at least one in an unlocked region. Store a non-zero word at each of them and at the three region 31 addresses of step 15, because the array has no reset value and only a word that the test wrote has a defined content. Write the mask in two writes m and m2: round 1 locks the odd regions 1, 3 to 31, round 2 locks the even regions 2 to 30 (so every region 1 to 31 is locked in one round and unlocked in the other), and round 3 locks a drawn set of 3 to 8 regions in [1, 31] that includes 31. Read SRAM_LOCK. Write 0 to SRAM_LOCK and read it again.
. For each store address a of the round, with region r_a = (a - 0x8000) / 0x400: clear SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION and IRQ_STATUS, store a value v that differs from the old word, then read the word, SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION and IRQ_STATUS. A locked region is expected to keep the old word and to set exactly bit r_a; an unlocked region is expected to take v and to set no bit.
. After each locked store write 0 to SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION and read it (the bit stays), then write the set bit and read it (clear), and read IRQ_STATUS (bit 3 stays until its own W1C). Write IRQ_STATUS bit 3 and read it.
. Region 31 stores at 0xFC00, 0xFC04 and 0xFFFC. When bit 31 is locked (rounds 1 and 3) read the three words and the violation register. In round 2, where region 31 is unlocked, the same stores land (the twin).
. PARK reset (the extra hold W is drawn as in step 10). Wait for the start marker. Read SRAM_LOCK. Store a value that differs from the old word at one drawn address that the previous round locked, and read the word. Repeat steps 12 to 16 for the next round; the PARK of round 3 ends the SRAM rounds.
. Draw the order of the four reset kinds (SOFT, PARK, COLD, SOFT-WRONG) for four rounds. Each round runs steps 18 to 20.
. Write the witnesses with ops, in three groups. First the cold domain: one OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD bit; COLD_BOOT_DONE = 1, then a write of 0 (it must stay 1). Then the data: a pattern in 8 SRAM words, and one host word pushed into the SEP-to-KM mailbox FIFO after the image has read its list. Then the warm domain: IRQ_ENABLE = 0xAAA; IRQ_STATUS bits 0, 2 and 4 by IRQ_SET (masked by 0xAAA, so no interrupt); IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR = 0x1230, then IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK = 1; one OTP_READ_LOCK bit; DRBG sampler CFG = 0x004D0001 (prefetch 1, timeout 77); last, SRAM_LOCK bits (drawn regions in [1, 31] that do not hold the 8 SRAM words). Read every witness back as the pre-reset record (all non-zero).
. Reset kind SOFT: post an op that writes 0x53525354 to SOFT_RST_CODE. PARK: write `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 low (the other bits at 1), wait as in step 10 (the three `KM-ISO` probes isolated and a stable `km_rom_req_count_o`; a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf), keep the bit low for 200 further host clocks (a test parameter), read `SEP_STATUS` during the hold, and write the bit high. COLD: pulse `rst_ni` low and high, wait for the `sep_reset_n` release on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf; `sep_reset_n` resets the Reset Controller register block and the KM cold domain, `reset_controller.adoc`, "SEP Reset Controller"), read `SEP_STATUS` (the mailbox depth, before the release of the KM), then write `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7F to release the KM (`SW_RESET_N` is back at its reset value 0x7E after `rst_ni`, so the KM is held in reset until the write; `reset_controller.adoc`, "SEP SW_RESET_N fields"). SOFT-WRONG: for each wrong code, post an op that writes it to SOFT_RST_CODE (error injection, expected response: no reset), then read SRAM_LOCK and BOOT_STATUS. From the write or the pulse to the start marker, sample the three isolation probes in every clock. In the PARK round also sample `km_rom_req_count_o` and the `km_sw_rst_n` net (`KM-RST`).
. After SOFT, PARK and COLD wait for the start marker (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf) and post the readback list for every witness. After SOFT-WRONG post the same list without a reset. Remove the mailbox word by a SEP-side flush after its read.
. For every PARK reset of steps 10 and 16 and for the PARK round of step 19, take the first `km_rom_req_count_o` increment after the rising edge of the `km_sw_rst_n` net at the KM (`KM-RST`).
. At the end of the leaf write `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 high if it is low, so that `km_sw_rst_n` is high.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-CSR-VER
|A write to VERSION answers SLVERR and leaves the value unchanged (`architecture.adoc`, "Key Manager Control and Status Registers (KMCSR)": the register is read-only and a write completes with an AXI SLVERR response). A wrong DUT answers OKAY or changes the value. `slv` is the AXI_SLVERR bit of IRQ_STATUS, which the list clears before the write. The expected value of the read after the write is the `VERSION` reset value 0x0001_0000 from `km_csr.adoc` (major 1, minor 0, patch 0), not the read before the write, and the written value differs from it. The read before the write is logged as a control: the open owner grades the value through CMD_HW_VER. Anchor: the POKE result (`slv`, and the read after the write). Evidence: `CHK-CSR-VER PASS: wr_val=<v> wr_slv=1 after=0x00010000 golden=0x00010000 control_rd=0x00010000`.
|CHK-CSR-STATUS
|Each of the 12 IRQ_STATUS bits is sticky: it reads 1 on every read until cleared; a W1C of the other 11 bits and a write of 0 leave it set; a W1C of the bit clears it and only it, so a second set bit j stays set (`km_csr.adoc`, "IRQ_STATUS": sticky, write one to clear). Bits [31:12] read 0. A wrong DUT clears the bit on a write of 0 or on a W1C of other bits, or clears another bit. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: the W1C of the wrong mask is the twin of the right mask. Evidence: `CHK-CSR-STATUS PASS: bit=<i> src=irq_set sticky=1 w1c_other=kept zero_write=kept w1c_self=cleared other_bit_kept=1`.
|CHK-CSR-SET
|IRQ_SET with a mask sets exactly the matching IRQ_STATUS bits, and reserved bits read 0 (`km_csr.adoc`, "IRQ_SET", "IRQ_STATUS"). A wrong DUT sets other bits or reads a reserved bit as 1. The IRQ_SET read is not sent, because its response is not stated. Anchor: the POKE readback. Evidence: `CHK-CSR-SET PASS: mask=<m> status=<m> rsvd=0`.
|CHK-CSR-ENABLE
|The interrupt is the OR of IRQ_STATUS AND IRQ_ENABLE: the handler enters once when S AND E is non-zero, does not enter when S AND E is zero or E is 0, a masked sticky bit still sets, and enabling a bit that is already set enters the handler (`architecture.adoc`, "Key Manager Control and Status Registers (KMCSR)"; "Key Manager CPU Interrupt Processing", CPU IRQ bit 3). A wrong DUT enters with S AND E zero, does not enter with S AND E non-zero, or does not set a masked bit. Anchor: the handler count and the IRQ_STATUS readback. In the IRQ_STATUS walk, with IRQ_ENABLE 0, the handler count does not rise after the IRQ_SET of each of the 12 bits, while the status bit sets. Control: E = 0 is the twin of E non-zero, and case (c) raises the count for each of the 12 sources in the same leaf. Evidence: `CHK-CSR-ENABLE PASS: case=<a/b/c/d> e=<E> s=<S> entries=<0/1> masked_set=<0/1>`, and `CHK-CSR-ENABLE PASS: case=walk e=0 bits=12 entries=0 status_set=12`.
|CHK-CSR-ENTRY
|IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR resets to 0x10 and IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK to 0. While the lock is 0 any 32-bit value is accepted and reads back, and aligned, unaligned and 0xFFFFFFFF are all legal. After a write of 1 to the lock, a write to IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR leaves the value, and a write of 0 to the lock leaves it 1 (`architecture.adoc`, "Key Manager Control and Status Registers (KMCSR)"; `km_csr.adoc`, "IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR", "IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK"). A wrong DUT has another reset value, does not read back a value, accepts a write after the lock, or clears the lock on a write of 0. Anchor: the POKE readback. Evidence: `CHK-CSR-ENTRY PASS: reset=0x10 v=<v> lock=1 v2_kept=1 lock_w0_kept=1`.
|CHK-CSR-SCR
|SCRAMBLER_CTRL reads 0 at boot, and SCRAMBLER_KEY reads back the written value while the lock is clear (`km_csr.adoc`, "SCRAMBLER_KEY", "SCRAMBLER_CTRL"). A wrong DUT has another boot value or does not read the key back. The lock and the enable are not set here, because the scrambler would then be forced on and belongs to `sep_km_sram_scrambler_key_rand_test`. Anchor: the POKE readback. Evidence: `CHK-CSR-SCR PASS: ctrl_rd=0 key_rd=<k> key2_rd=<k2>`.
|CHK-CSR-DEBUG
|DEBUG reads 0xCAFEBEEF and CTRL reads 0 (`km_csr.adoc`, "DEBUG", "CTRL"). A wrong DUT reads another value. The write responses of DEBUG and CTRL are not stated and are not sent. Anchor: the POKE readback. Evidence: `CHK-CSR-DEBUG PASS: debug=0xCAFEBEEF ctrl=0`.
|CHK-SRAM-SIZE
|The SRAM has 8192 words, and each of the 13 word-address bits selects a distinct word (`architecture.adoc`, "Parameters": 8192 x 32-bit words): after all 22 stores, each of the 22 words reads back its own tag. Word 8191 and word 8191 - 2^k differ only in word-address bit k. The check detects on every seed exactly the faults that map two of these 14 words to one cell, so that one of them reads the tag of the other: (1) any set of word-address lines of which each is ignored, stuck at 0 or stuck at 1 (a faulted line k maps word 8191 - 2^k onto word 8191); (2) a bridge of two lines j and k whose value is their AND (words 8191 - 2^j and 8191 - 2^k map to one cell), their OR, or one line that drives the other (word 8191 - 2^k maps onto word 8191); (3) as a case of (1), an array of 2^m words, m at most 12, that decodes only the low m word-address bits. Not claimed: any other decode that maps two words to one cell, for example an array of 4096 words or fewer whose decode is not a drop of address bits (the address modulo 4093 keeps the 14 walk words distinct), an array of more than 4096 and fewer than 8192 words (for example the address modulo 6144), a bridge of three or more lines, or a bridge together with a second fault that separates the pair; only a sweep of the array shows those. The 8 drawn words are graded the same way and add no claim. Anchor: the POKE readback. Evidence: `CHK-SRAM-SIZE PASS: tagged=22 walk_bits=13 drawn=8 own_tag=22 alias=0`.
|CHK-SRAM-LOCK
|SRAM_LOCK holds the OR of two writes, and a write of 0 changes nothing (`km_csr.adoc`, "SRAM_LOCK": write one to lock, 0 has no effect). A wrong DUT keeps only the last write or clears on 0. Anchor: the POKE readback. Evidence: `CHK-SRAM-LOCK PASS: round=<r> mask=<m OR m2> or_ok=1 zero_write=kept`.
|CHK-SRAM-DROP
|A store to a locked region is dropped, SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION has exactly the bit of the store's region (`(address - 0x8000) / 0x400`) and not a mask bit, and IRQ_STATUS.SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_ERR is set with the parity bits and ROM_WRITE_ERR clear. A W1C of the bit clears it, and a W1C of 0 does not (`architecture.adoc`, "SRAM Write Locking"; `km_csr.adoc`, "SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION", "IRQ_STATUS"). A wrong DUT lets the store land, sets the wrong bit, or fails the clear. The old word is a word that the test wrote, so a dropped store differs from a landed store. Anchor: the POKE readback of the word, SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION and IRQ_STATUS. Control: CHK-SRAM-LAND in the same leaf. Evidence: `CHK-SRAM-DROP PASS: addr=<a> region=<r> locked=1 word=old viol=<1<<r> irq=1 parity=0`.
|CHK-SRAM-LAND
|A store to an unlocked region lands and sets no violation bit (`architecture.adoc`, "SRAM Write Locking"). A wrong DUT drops it or flags it. Anchor: the POKE readback. Every locked-region store has an unlocked-region store in the same round. Evidence: `CHK-SRAM-LAND PASS: addr=<a> region=<r> locked=0 word=new viol=0`.
|CHK-SRAM-R31
|With bit 31 locked, stores at 0xFC00, 0xFC04 and 0xFFFC are dropped and bit 31 of SRAM_WRITE_LOCK_VIOLATION sets (`architecture.adoc`, "ROM Warm-Persistent SRAM Region": after the lock any write into 0xFC00 to 0xFFFF is dropped and a violation is raised). A wrong DUT lets a store land or sets another bit. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: in round 2 region 31 is unlocked and the same stores land. Evidence: `CHK-SRAM-R31 PASS: addrs=3 dropped=3 viol31=1`.
|CHK-SRAM-RELEASE
|A warm reset clears every lock: SRAM_LOCK reads 0 and a store to an address of a locked region lands (`architecture.adoc`, "SRAM Write Locking": the lock state is cleared on every warm or cold reset). A wrong DUT keeps a lock. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: the same store is dropped before the PARK. Evidence: `CHK-SRAM-RELEASE PASS: locks=0 store_lands=1`.
|CHK-RST-WARM
|After SOFT or PARK every warm-domain witness reads its reset value (SRAM_LOCK 0, IRQ_ENABLE 0, IRQ_STATUS 0, IRQ_ENTRY_ADDR 0x10, IRQ_ENTRY_LOCK 0, OTP_READ_LOCK 0), every cold-domain witness survives (OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD bit, COLD_BOOT_DONE 1), and the data witnesses survive (the 8 SRAM words unchanged, and, after PARK, the host word still in the mailbox) (`architecture.adoc`, "Reset Conditioning": reset-domain assignment table and the text on warm reset). The KPV CTRL bits and the KPV scrambler across a reset are graded in `sep_km_kpv_rand_test`. After SOFT the DRBG sampler CFG reads 0x01000000 (`km_drbg_sampler.adoc`, "CFG": timeout 0x100, prefetch 0). The sampler CFG after PARK and COLD_BOOT_DONE after PARK are controls that the open owner grades. After SOFT the mailbox word is logged, because the restarted image can read it. A wrong DUT keeps a warm witness or loses a cold or data witness. Anchor: the POKE readback; `SEP_STATUS`. Control: every witness is non-zero before the reset. Evidence: `CHK-RST-WARM PASS: kind=<soft/park> sram_lock=0 irq_en=0 irq_status=0 entry=0x10 entry_lock=0 otp_lock=0 cfg=0x01000000 cold_kept=1 data_same=1`.
|CHK-RST-COLD
|After COLD every cold-domain witness clears (OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD 0, COLD_BOOT_DONE 0), the mailbox holds no word (`SEP_STATUS` depth 0, read before the release of the KM), and every warm-domain witness reads its reset value (`architecture.adoc`, "Reset Conditioning"). The KPV scrambler across a cold reset is graded in `sep_km_kpv_rand_test`. The SRAM words after COLD are not graded, because no array erase is stated. A wrong DUT keeps a cold witness or the mailbox word. Anchor: the POKE readback; `SEP_STATUS`. Control: every witness is non-zero before the reset. Evidence: `CHK-RST-COLD PASS: kind=cold cbd=0 otp_lock_cold=0 mbox_depth=0 warm_cleared=1`.
|CHK-RST-WRONG
|A write of any value other than 0x53525354 to SOFT_RST_CODE does nothing (`km_csr.adoc`, "SOFT_RST_CODE"): after each wrong code SRAM_LOCK, COLD_BOOT_DONE and the other witnesses keep their pre-reset values, and the image does not restart. A wrong DUT resets on a wrong code (SRAM_LOCK would clear). Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: the exact code of the SOFT round is the twin. Evidence: `CHK-RST-WRONG PASS: val=<code> sram_lock_kept=1 cbd_kept=1`.
|CHK-HOLD
|The warm reset stays asserted at least MIN_RESET_CYCLES = 10 clocks after its trigger releases, so the first ROM request after the rising edge of `km_sw_rst_n` comes at least 10 clocks later; the bound has no upper limit (`architecture.adoc`, "Reset Conditioning": `rst_warm_sync_no`). A wrong DUT with no hold counter fetches earlier. Anchor: `km_rom_req_count_o` and the `km_sw_rst_n` net at the KM (`KM-RST`), for each PARK reset and each extra hold W in {1, 2, 5, 12} host clocks; the leaf writes the release only after the net has fallen. Control: the PARK reset of every extra hold W runs the same measurement. Evidence: `CHK-HOLD PASS: w=<W> first_fetch_after_release=<n>` with n at least 10.
|CHK-ISO
|The soft reset does not isolate the key paths: the three isolation probes stay 0 during SOFT (`reset_controller.adoc`, "Resetting the Key Manager": a soft reset takes the warm path without isolation). A wrong DUT raises a probe during SOFT. Anchor: `KM-ISO` sampled in every clock from the write to the start marker. Control: in the PARK round of the same leaf the probes read 1 while the KM is held, which shows that the probes can rise. Evidence: `CHK-ISO PASS: soft_iso=0 park_control=1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The KPV CTRL bits and the KPV scrambler across the reset kinds belong to `sep_km_kpv_rand_test`. The IRQ_SET read response and the DEBUG and CTRL write responses are not stated (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), so the leaf sends none of them. The SRAM scrambler lock is not a witness: the scrambler is forced on once the lock is set (`hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc`), and it belongs to `sep_km_sram_scrambler_key_rand_test`. The isolation order and the AES, OTBN and eFuse isolation stages have no probe (<<sep-vplan-phase3-noobs,Features with no observation point>>). The isolation by the SW_RESET_N path and the scan override of the KM reset belong to `sep_sw_reset_isolation_matrix_test` and `sep_scan_reset_override_test`. The real-cause pass of step 5 is a control and fails nothing; the open owners keep those cells.

'''''

[[sep_km_kpv_rand_test]]
===== 7.30 `sep_km_kpv_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle POKE, `+skip_fuse_sense`, SRAM scrambler off, `reseed = 3`; testlist `km.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; VCS (Verilator runs the value checks)

*Source Module*: `sep_km_kpv_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (the testbench LSU master is the SEP host: `km_mailbox_sep`, `SEP_CPU_CTRL.KM_WIPE_CTRL`, `SEP_STATUS`). The POKE image is a KM image of the class of `km_rom_vault` (`km_rom_blob`) that executes a list of KM bus operations (read, write, back-to-back write, poll, read loop, interrupt arm) as a legal master of the KM bus, and reports each result (data, `slv`, `dec`) in KM SRAM words 0 to 97. A read loop reports the count of non-zero words that it read. The KM CPU does not see the AXI response code, so the image clears the IRQ_STATUS bits AXI_SLVERR and AXI_DECERR before an operation and reports them after it as `slv` and `dec`. The model of the slot states is written from `architecture.adoc` and `km_kpv.adoc`, and the scrambler tables (S-box, inverse S-box and permutation) from `hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc`, section "Data Scrambling Algorithm", never from the RTL. Approved probes: `KM-RESULT` (the POKE readback of CTRL, key words, the HMAC KEY_CTRL and IRQ_STATUS), `HOST-MBX` (the `KM_WIPE_CTRL` write, `SEP_STATUS`), `PIC-IRQ` (vector bit 30), `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (the `sep_reset_n` release after `rst_ni`; `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, SEP reset probes). `KM-KPV` (CHK-KPV-ERASE-RET, CHK-KSCR-CIPHER) is an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list; <<sep-vplan-km-proposed>> defines it. Injects: none.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, two classes. RAND-REP for the slot legs: the slot, the word, the state path, the op order and the value. Legal ranges: slots 0 to 63, words 0 to 15, CTRL bits [3:0]. Twelve slots are drawn, with slots 0 and 63 always in the set and kept for the state walk. The repeats and the control of the seal-during-erase step take slots outside the drawn set. The sweep visits all 64 slots on every seed, in drawn order. RANDCFG for the scrambler and wipe legs: the scrambler key kk (32 bits, non-zero), the lock timing (before or after the data cells) and the wipe hold width W in {2, 3, 8, 40} host clocks (at least 2). The scrambler leg writes the cells with enable 0 (the twin) and with enable 1 on every seed, and the warm survive and the cold clear both run on every seed. Every drawn data word is non-zero and the 24 scrambler cells hold pairwise different values. `reseed = 3` samples different slots, words, paths, orders, keys and widths.

*Objective*: Key word w of slot s is at 0x12000 + s x 0x40 + w x 4 and CTRL[s] is at 0x13000 + 4 s. The four CTRL fields are write-one-to-set. lock_use does not block key writes. A sealed slot refuses writes and still reads. A slot with both locks refuses write and read. No lock blocks erase. A retired slot refuses read and write. A seal that arrives during an erase retires the slot. Unwritten words keep their data, and a neighbouring slot never changes. The KPV scrambles key data with the S-box and permutation of the scrambler spec, and the KM port sees logical data. The KPV scrambler enable and lock survive a warm reset and clear on a cold reset or a wipe. A wipe edge clears all 1024 KPV words, all 64 CTRL registers (sealed and retired slots too) and the KPV scrambler key, enable and lock, and sets IRQ_STATUS.WIPE_STATE. It scrubs neither SRAM, nor the mailbox, nor the engine key registers, and it does not halt the CPU. A level held high gives no second wipe. Spec: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, sections "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)", "KPV Scrambling", "Wipe State"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_kpv.adoc`, sections "Key and Policy Vault (KM Port)", "KEY_ENTRY[64].WORD[16]", "CTRL[64]", "KPV_SCRAMBLER_CTRL"; `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, section "KM_WIPE_CTRL"; `hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc`, section "Data Scrambling Algorithm".

*Test Coverage*: An address walk over drawn slots (always 0 and 63) and words {0, 15, drawn}, and a sweep that covers all 64 slots on every seed; each compared slot is erased first, because the key-entry flops have no reset. CTRL writes of 0, of each field alone, and three identical writes. A state walk per slot through free, W, R, WR, sealed and retired, with key write, key read, CTRL write 0 and erase in each state (the denied accesses are legal bus accesses with a stated SLVERR response, so the test has no error injection). Erase and seal in back-to-back writes, and the same writes with the erase finished first. A partial write after an erase, then lock_use. A drawn KPV scrambler key, enable 0 (the twin) and enable 1, lock before or after the data, and 24 distinct non-zero cells; a PARK reset and a COLD reset. A wipe pulse with drawn hold width, with a sealed slot, a retired slot, an HMAC key_valid, SRAM words and a mailbox word present. A wipe level held high with a second write of 1, then a second rising edge. Pairs of writes to one cell that cover every nibble value in each nibble lane. Extends the open test `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`, which grades the write refusal of a write-locked slot, the zero read with SLVERR of a read-locked slot, the seal write, the retire and the sticky retire of a sealed erase, and the erase of a write-locked and of a free slot, and the open test `sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test`, which grades the logical readback, the scrambled stored word, the moved physical index and the keyed difference. Those cells run here as controls. This test grades only the new cells: the slot-state matrix, the CTRL write rules, the trailing words, the neighbours, the warm survive and the cold clear of the scrambler, the wipe and its scope, the second edge and, with `KM-KPV`, the cipher difference. The owners of the open tests keep the rest. Functional coverage: `sep_km_kpv_slot_cg` (`cp_kpv_slot_index`, `cp_kpv_word_index`, `cp_kpv_post_reset`, `cp_kpv_ctrl_write`, `cp_kpv_lock_write`, `cp_kpv_lock_use`, `cp_kpv_erase`, `cp_kpv_seal`, `cp_kpv_scramble_mode`, `cp_kpv_trailing`), `sep_km_kpv_locked_access_cg` (`cp_kpv_state_x_op`, `cp_kpv_locked_write`, `cp_kpv_locked_read`, `cp_kpv_retired`, `cp_kpv_boundary`), `sep_km_vault_wipe_cg` (`cp_vw_pre_contents`, `cp_vw_effect`, `cp_vw_edge`, `cp_vw_scope`); Phase 1: `sep_km_wipe_cg` (`cp_wipe`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Cold-reset the SEP with `+skip_fuse_sense`, release the KM and wait for the start marker of the POKE image in the KM SRAM result words (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Draw the plan: the 12 slots, the words of each slot, the sweep order of the 64 slots, kk, the lock timing, W and the 24 cells. The drawn slots serve the steps in the drawn order, and slots 0 and 63 serve the state walk of step 7. Steps 6, 7, 9 and the first try of step 8 use at most 10 drawn slots. The repeats and the control of step 8 take slots outside the drawn set. A slot that a step has sealed or retired is not reused by a later step before the next reset. Log `SEED` and every `DRAW` value.
. Reset values. Post a list that reads CTRL of the 12 drawn slots. The key data power-up values are not graded.
. Define the data. Erase each drawn slot s and each neighbour s - 1 (s at least 1) and s + 1 (s at most 62): write CTRL = 0x4 and poll until the erase bit reads 0 (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Every KPV word that a later step compares is then defined.
. Address walk. Write a distinct non-zero value f(s, w) to the words {0, 15, drawn} of each drawn slot and, for s at most 62, to word 0 of slot s + 1. Read every cell back, and read CTRL of each slot. The VCS leaf also counts the X and Z bits in every stored word that a later step compares.
. Sweep. For each of the 64 slots, in the drawn sweep order: erase it, write word 0, read word 0 and read CTRL.
. CTRL write rules. For each of the four fields F (lock_write 0x1, lock_use 0x2, erase 0x4, seal 0x8) on its own drawn slot: erase the slot and wait for the erase bit to clear (CTRL reads 0); write 0 and read CTRL (0); write F alone and read CTRL (lock_write 0x1, lock_use 0x2, seal 0x9 because hardware also sets lock_write, erase 0 after the bit clears); for the three lock fields write 0 again and read CTRL (unchanged: the lock stays set); write the same value F two more times and read CTRL after each (same value; after each erase write wait for the bit to clear); write another field G and read CTRL. G is drawn from lock_write, lock_use and seal, other than F, so CTRL reads F OR G, with seal adding lock_write; for F = erase the slot is erased, so CTRL reads G alone.
. State walk. For each of four drawn slots (slots 0 and 63 among them), draw the order of the states W, R and WR. Run the free state first: a key write and a key read. Then, for each of W, R and WR in the drawn order, write the 16 words and record them, write the CTRL bits of the state (W: 0x1, R: 0x2, WR: 0x3), run a key write of a value that differs from the stored word, a key read and a CTRL write of 0, then write CTRL = 0x4 and wait for the erase bit to clear (the slot is free again). After the erase of an R slot and of a WR slot read the 16 words and CTRL, then write 16 new values, each different from the recorded word, and read them back. Run the sealed state last: write the 16 words and record them, write CTRL = 0x8 (CTRL reads 0x9), run a key write, a key read and a CTRL write of 0, then erase (the slot is retired and CTRL reads 0xB). In the retired state run a key read and a key write, write CTRL = 0x4, wait for the erase bit to clear, run a key read and a key write again, and read CTRL.
. Seal during erase. On a slot that holds data write CTRL = 0x4 and CTRL = 0x8 as back-to-back writes and read CTRL right after the seal write: the erase bit reads 1 when the erase was still in flight at the seal write. Then wait for the erase bit to clear and read CTRL. When the read right after the seal write shows the erase bit at 0, the erase finished before that read: the leaf logs `OBS-KPV-RACE-REDRAW` (case `finished`) with both CTRL values, grades nothing from that slot, and repeats the step on another slot outside the drawn set that no step has sealed or retired, up to 4 repeats. A leaf whose tries all end `finished` grades no cell of `CHK-KPV-RACE`, logs `OBS-KPV-RACE graded=0`, and does not fail. Control: on one more such slot erase, wait for the bit to clear, then seal, and read CTRL.
. Trailing words. Erase a slot and read all 16 words (record E). Write k words, k in [1, 15], and read all 16 words. Write CTRL = 0x2 and read words k to 15.
. Neighbours. Before and after every op of steps 6 to 9 on slot s, read CTRL and one word of slot s - 1 (s at least 1) and of slot s + 1 (s at most 62).
. Scrambler leg. Choose 24 cells (slot, word) in erased slots and 24 values D (non-zero, pairwise different). With the scrambler disabled write the 24 cells and read them (the e = 0 twin). With the scrambler still disabled, erase the slots of the 24 cells (CTRL = 0x4, wait for the erase bit to clear), so that no stored word holds D. Write KPV_SCRAMBLER_KEY = kk and KPV_SCRAMBLER_CTRL.enable = 1. When the lock timing is "before", write lock = 1 at this point. Write the 24 cells again and read each. When the lock timing is "after", write lock = 1 at this point.
. Warm survive. PARK reset (`SW_RESET_N` bit 0 low, then high, the other bits at 1), wait for the start marker, read CTRL of the 12 drawn slots, KPV_SCRAMBLER_CTRL and the 24 cells. Then write CTRL = 0x1 (lock_write) to each of the 12 drawn slots and read CTRL (0x1), so that every drawn slot holds a set bit before the cold reset.
. Cold clear. COLD reset (pulse `rst_ni`), wait for the `sep_reset_n` release on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf), write `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7F to release the KM (the register is back at 0x7E after `rst_ni`), wait for the start marker, read CTRL of the 12 drawn slots, KPV_SCRAMBLER_CTRL and the 24 cells.
. Wipe pre-state. Write KPV_SCRAMBLER_KEY = kk2 (non-zero) and read it, then write enable 1 and lock 1. Write key words in a free slot and a sealed slot, and write, seal and erase a third slot (retired). Write HMAC KEY_CTRL = 1. Write 8 SRAM words. The host pushes one word into the SEP-to-KM mailbox FIFO after the image has read its list. Read all pre-state values (non-zero).
. Wipe pulse. Write `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state` = 1, hold it for W host clocks, then write 0. Sample PIC vector bit 30.
. Read back after the wipe: a read loop over the 1024 key words (count of non-zero words), all 64 CTRL registers, KPV_SCRAMBLER_KEY, KPV_SCRAMBLER_CTRL, IRQ_STATUS, then one further op to show the CPU runs; read the HMAC KEY_CTRL, the 8 SRAM words and `SEP_STATUS`.
. Level held high. Write 1 to IRQ_STATUS bit 7 (clear). Write `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state` = 1 and read IRQ_STATUS (bit 7 set by the rising edge), leave the bit at 1 for 200 host clocks (a test parameter), write 1 to IRQ_STATUS bit 7 and read it. Write 1 to `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state` again while it is high and read IRQ_STATUS over 200 host clocks.
. Second edge. Clear IRQ_STATUS bit 7, write `KM_WIPE_CTRL.wipe_state` = 0, then 1, and read IRQ_STATUS.
. Cipher difference, read through `KM-KPV`. All stored words read 0 after the wipe. Write KPV_SCRAMBLER_KEY = k (non-zero) and enable 1 with the lock clear. For 32 draws: write d1 to a cell, snapshot the stored array through `KM-KPV`, write d2 (different from d1) to the same cell and snapshot again. Draws 1 to 16 give every nibble lane the value i in d1 and (i + 8) mod 16 in d2 (i = 0 to F); draws 17 to 32 are seeded. Count the X and Z bits in the snapshots. Then write a second key k2 and store d1 in the same cell (control, logged).
. Write `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 high if it is low, so that `km_sw_rst_n` is high at the end of the leaf.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KPV-ADDR
|Key word w of slot s is at 0x12000 + s x 0x40 + w x 4 and CTRL[s] is at 0x13000 + 4 s (`memory_map.adoc`, KM port; `km_kpv.adoc`, "KEY_ENTRY[64].WORD[16]", "CTRL[64]"). Each written cell reads back its own value. A wrong DUT aliases two words, two slots, or a word with a CTRL (CTRL reads 0 after the key writes of the walk). Anchor: the POKE readback. The sweep is graded the same way: in each sweep step word 0 of the slot reads back the value that the step wrote, and CTRL reads 0. Control: the neighbour word of the next slot holds another value. Evidence: `CHK-KPV-ADDR PASS: cells=<n> alias=0 sweep_slots=64 sweep_ok=64`.
|CHK-KPV-CTRLW
|The four CTRL fields are write-one-to-set: a write of 0 sets nothing on an erased slot and leaves a set lock unchanged, a write of F alone sets F only (seal also sets lock_write, so 0x9; an erase of an unsealed slot ends with CTRL 0), a repeated write leaves the value, and a second lock field adds its bit without clearing the first (`km_kpv.adoc, "CTRL[64]"; `architecture.adoc`, "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)", table of per-slot controls). A wrong DUT clears a bit on a write of 0, clears a bit on a write of another field, or changes the value on a repeated write. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: CTRL reads 0 after the erase that starts each sequence, so every bit that a later read shows is set by the sequence. Evidence: `CHK-KPV-CTRLW PASS: slot=<s> write0_free=4 write0_keeps=3 set_only=4 triple_same=4 or_ok=4`.
|CHK-KPV-STATE
|In state W (lock_write only) a key read returns the recorded word with no SLVERR, because lock_write blocks writes only; in state R (lock_use only) a key write completes with no SLVERR, because lock_use blocks reads only; in state WR a write is dropped with SLVERR and a read returns 0 with SLVERR; in state sealed a write is dropped with SLVERR and a read returns the old word with no SLVERR (`architecture.adoc`, "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)"; `km_kpv.adoc`, "CTRL[64]"). A wrong DUT refuses a read in W, blocks a write in R, lets a write or read through in WR, or accepts a write or refuses a read in a sealed slot. A key read in state R returns 0 with SLVERR, and a key write in state W is dropped with SLVERR (controls of the open owner). The stored-word effect of the R write is not graded here. Anchor: the POKE readback and `slv`. Control: the free-state access of the same slot is the twin of each locked access. Evidence: `CHK-KPV-STATE PASS: slot=<s> state=<W/R/WR/sealed> wr_slv=<0/1> rd=<data> rd_slv=<0/1>`.
|CHK-KPV-ERASE
|An erase of an unsealed R or WR slot clears CTRL to 0 and frees the slot: after the erase bit clears, the 16 key reads complete with no SLVERR, and 16 new values written to the slot read back (`architecture.adoc`, "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)", `erase[2]`: no lock blocks the erase, and a slot that is unsealed at completion has all CTRL bits cleared and is reusable; `km_kpv.adoc`, "CTRL[64]", `erase`). The CTRL bits are write-one-to-set, and with no reset or wipe in the step only an erase that completes on an unsealed slot clears them, so CTRL 0 after the lock bits of R or WR shows that the erase completed. The erase fill comes from an LFSR whose value the specification does not state (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), so no check states the 16 words after the erase or that they differ from the words before it: the leaf logs the number of words that equal their record (`OBS-KPV-ERASE-FILL LOG`). A wrong DUT is blocked by a lock, leaves a CTRL bit set (a lock_use left set makes the reads return 0 with SLVERR), or refuses the new values. Anchor: the POKE readback and `slv`. Control: the erase of a free slot and of a W slot (the open owner). Evidence: `CHK-KPV-ERASE PASS: slot=<s> state=<R/WR> ctrl=0x0 rd_slv=0 rewrite_ok=16`, and `OBS-KPV-ERASE-FILL LOG slot=<s> words_same=<n>`.
|CHK-KPV-RETIRED
|A retired slot (sealed, then erased) refuses a read (0 with SLVERR) and a write (dropped with SLVERR), before and after a repeated erase, and CTRL reads 0xB (`architecture.adoc`, "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)", sealed and retired slots; `km_kpv.adoc`, "CTRL[64]"). A wrong DUT returns data or accepts a write. The effect of a repeated erase on the stored words is not stated and is not graded. Anchor: the POKE readback and `slv`. Control: CTRL 0xB after the repeated erase is a cell of the open owner. Evidence: `CHK-KPV-RETIRED PASS: slot=<s> rd=0 rd_slv=1 wr_dropped=1 wr_slv=1 after_reerase_rd=0 after_reerase_wr_dropped=1 ctrl_control=0xB`.
|CHK-KPV-RACE
|An erase and a seal in back-to-back writes retire the slot when the erase was still in flight at the seal write: in a try whose CTRL read right after the seal write shows the erase bit at 1, CTRL reads 0xB after the erase bit clears (`km_kpv.adoc`, "CTRL[64]", `seal` and `erase`: a seal taken while an erase is already in flight still retires the slot). The specification states no erase duration and no write gap. A try whose read right after the seal write shows the erase bit at 0 has an erase that finished before that read: it is logged as case `finished`, is not graded, and the step repeats on another slot (up to 4 repeats). A leaf whose tries all end `finished` grades no cell of this check and does not fail. In a graded try a wrong DUT that lets the race lose the seal or the retire frees the slot (0x0) or leaves it only sealed (0x9). Anchor: the CTRL readback right after the seal write and after the erase. Control: erase, wait, then seal gives 0x9 (sealed, not retired). Evidence: `CHK-KPV-RACE PASS: erase_seen=1 tries=<n> ctrl=0xB control=0x9`. The lines `OBS-KPV-RACE-REDRAW slot=<s> case=finished ctrl_after_seal=<v> ctrl_final=<v>` and `OBS-KPV-RACE graded=<0\|1> tries=<n>` record the tries that are not graded.
|CHK-KPV-TRAIL
|After an erase, a partial write of k words changes only those words: the words k to 15 read the recorded values E. With lock_use set the same words read 0 with SLVERR (`architecture.adoc`, "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)", `lock_use[1]`). A wrong DUT changes an unwritten word, or does not force the read to zero. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: the words 0 to k - 1 read the written values after the partial write, so the write landed in the same slot; a written word that does not read its value fails the check. Evidence: `CHK-KPV-TRAIL PASS: k=<k> written=<k>/<k> kept=<16-k>/<16-k> lock_use_zero=1`.
|CHK-KPV-NEIGH
|An op on slot s changes neither slot s - 1 nor slot s + 1 (CTRL and one word each) (`architecture.adoc`, "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)": the slots are independent). A wrong DUT lets an op leak into a neighbour. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: the changed slot is the moving twin of each unchanged neighbour. Evidence: `CHK-KPV-NEIGH PASS: ops=<n> neighbor_changes=0`.
|CHK-KPV-RESET
|After a cold reset (step 2 and step 13) and after a warm reset (step 12) every CTRL of the 12 drawn slots reads 0, and the slots that the earlier steps sealed, retired or locked read 0 after the warm reset (`architecture.adoc`: "CTRL resets to zero" and "The lock/seal bits clear on warm or cold reset"; `km_kpv.adoc`, "CTRL[64]"). A wrong DUT resets to another value or keeps a lock. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: the slots are locked, sealed or retired before the warm reset, and all 12 drawn slots read CTRL = 0x1 before the cold reset of step 13, so each 0 after a reset follows a set bit; a drawn slot that does not read 0x1 before the cold reset fails the check. The power-up read of step 2 has no earlier value. Evidence: `CHK-KPV-RESET PASS: kind=<cold/warm> slots=12 set_before=<n> ctrl=0x0`.
|CHK-KPV-NOX
|VCS: no compared stored word holds X or Z, so a comparison never passes on an undefined value. Anchor: the KM SRAM result words read with every bit known. Evidence: `CHK-KPV-NOX PASS: compared_words=<n> x_words=0`.
|CHK-KSCR-RST
|A warm reset keeps the KPV scrambler enable and lock and the key, so every cell still reads D. A cold reset clears the enable and the lock, so KPV_SCRAMBLER_CTRL reads 0 (`architecture.adoc`, "KPV Scrambling": the enable and lock are preserved across a warm reset and cleared only by a cold reset; scrambling is disabled after a cold reset). The 24 cells after the cold reset have no stated value: the stored words lie at the indices that the address permutation of the KPV scrambler (perm10) selects, which `hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc` does not tabulate, and the other indices hold the erase fill (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The leaf logs the number of cells that read a value other than D (`OBS-KSCR-COLD LOG`) and grades no cell value after the cold reset. A wrong DUT clears the scrambler on a warm reset or keeps the enable or the lock after a cold reset. Anchor: the POKE readback of control and cells. Control: before the reset the e = 0 twin cells read D, and the cells written with enable 1 read D logically (`architecture.adoc`, "KPV Scrambling": the KM port sees logical data), so the warm read follows a scrambled write. The stored word, the moved physical index and the keyed difference belong to the open owner `sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test`. Evidence: `CHK-KSCR-RST PASS: e=1 twin_equal=24 logical_ok=24 warm_kept=1 cells_ok=24 cold_ctrl=0x0`, and `OBS-KSCR-COLD LOG cells_ne=<n>`.
|CHK-WIPE-KPV
|A wipe edge clears all 1024 KPV words, all 64 CTRL registers (the sealed and the retired slot too) and the KPV scrambler key, enable and lock, sets IRQ_STATUS.WIPE_STATE, and leaves the CPU running (`architecture.adoc`, "Wipe State", "KPV Scrambling"; `sep_cpu_ctrl.adoc`, "KM_WIPE_CTRL"). A wrong DUT leaves a word, a CTRL, the key, the enable or the lock, does not set the status, or halts the CPU. The key is read before the lock and after the wipe, where the specification states the clear. PIC vector bit 30 is logged (`LOG err_o=<b>`) and not graded, because no leg of this leaf raises it, so a 0 has no control. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: the pre-wipe vault is non-zero. Evidence: `CHK-WIPE-KPV PASS: words_nonzero=0 ctrl_nonzero=0 kpv_key=0 en=0 lock=0 status=1 cpu_alive=1`.
|CHK-WIPE-SCOPE
|The wipe does not clear SRAM words, the mailbox word or an engine key_valid (`architecture.adoc`, "Wipe State": the hardware action covers the KPV only). The 8 SRAM words keep their values, `SEP_STATUS` still shows the pushed word, and the HMAC key_valid reads 1. A wrong DUT scrubs one of them. Anchor: the POKE readback; `SEP_STATUS`. Control: each scope witness is non-zero before the wipe. Evidence: `CHK-WIPE-SCOPE PASS: sram_same=8 mbox_depth=1 keyvalid_hmac=1`.
|CHK-WIPE-EDGE2
|A wipe level held high gives no second wipe: with the bit high and IRQ_STATUS.WIPE_STATE cleared, the status stays 0 over 200 host clocks, also after a write of 1 while the bit is high. A rising edge after a return to 0 sets the status again (`architecture.adoc`, "Wipe State": return the bit to zero before another rising edge). A wrong DUT sets the status again on a held level or on a write of 1 while high, or ignores the second edge. Anchor: the IRQ_STATUS readback. Control: the first rising edge sets the status. Evidence: `CHK-WIPE-EDGE2 PASS: level_high_no_wipe=1 status_again=1`.
|CHK-KPV-ERASE-RET
|An erase of a sealed slot overwrites its key words, although the slot reads 0 afterwards (`architecture.adoc`, "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)": erasing a sealed slot destroys the contents). The specification does not state the value of the erase fill (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), so no deterministic value states the overwrite. With `KM-KPV` the leaf logs the 16 stored words after the erase and the number that equal the pre-erase record (`OBS-KPV-ERASE-RET LOG`). Anchor: the stored words from `KM-KPV`, because a retired slot cannot be read on the KM port. Blocked: the specification does not state the erase fill. It logs an OBS line and not CHK PASS, and no cell is counted as reached. Evidence: `OBS-KPV-ERASE-RET LOG slot=<s> state=sealed words_same=<n>`.
|CHK-KSCR-CIPHER
|With the scrambler enabled, two writes of d1 and d2 to the same cell give stored words S1 and S2 with ibox(iplayer(S1)) XOR ibox(iplayer(S2)) equal to d1 XOR d2 in every draw, and exactly one stored word changes between the two snapshots (`hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc`, "Data Scrambling Algorithm": the stored word is the permutation of the S-box output of the data XOR the tweaked key, so the key and the address tweak cancel in the difference). The draws cover all 16 values in each of the 8 nibble lanes. A wrong DUT with another S-box, another permutation or a missing XOR shows a mismatch. Anchor: the stored words from `KM-KPV`. Control: under the second key k2 the same cell and data store a different word (logged). Evidence: `CHK-KSCR-CIPHER PASS: draws=32 lane_values=8x16 diff_ok=32`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker that is not blocked PASSes, except that `CHK-KPV-RACE` grades only a try in which the erase bit reads 1 right after the seal write; a leaf with no such try logs `OBS-KPV-RACE graded=0`, and no cell of that check counts as reached. UVM_ERROR == 0. The checker `CHK-KPV-ERASE-RET` needs a stated erase fill and logs an OBS line, and no cell of it counts as reached.

*Scope limit*: The value of the erase fill and the KPV cells read after a cold reset are not stated (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), and the leaf logs them. The effect of a repeated erase on the stored words of a retired slot is not stated (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), and the leaf does not grade it. A key access during an erase is not defined, and the leaf does not send one. The read value of a locked KPV_SCRAMBLER_KEY is not stated (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), and the leaf reads the key only before the lock and after the wipe. Past-extent offsets belong to `sep_km_crossbar_efuse_window_rand_test`. The open tests `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test` and `sep_km_kpv_scrambler_test` keep the cells that they grade; this test grades only the cells listed in the Test Coverage.

'''''

[[sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test]]
===== 7.31 `sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle POKE, `+skip_fuse_sense`, SRAM scrambler off, `reseed = 3`; testlist `km.toml`, groups `cpu_stub` and `all`; Verilator and VCS

*Source Module*: `sep_km_cpu_protect_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: ocah_axi_vip (the testbench LSU master is the SEP host: `km_mailbox_sep`, `SEP_RESET_CTRL`, `SEP_STATUS`). The POKE image is a KM image of the class of `km_rom_vault` (`km_rom_blob`) that executes a list of KM bus operations (read, write, jump, trap, raw instruction word, interrupt arm with a counting handler) as a legal master of the KM bus, and reports each result (data, `slv`, `dec`) in KM SRAM words 0 to 97. The trap kinds and the fetches are instructions that the image executes. Its ROM spin loop copies IRQ_STATUS to a result word on every pass. In the cases that arm the exec_violation handler, the handler copies IRQ_STATUS to a result word and spins in ROM. The image copies a snippet into the drawn SRAM region with KM bus writes. The snippet S is 5 instructions: a store of a marker to a drawn SRAM word, then a jump to the ROM spin loop. The snippet P is SRAM-resident and never fetches from ROM: it stores a marker, clears IRQ_STATUS (write one), reads a ROM data word and stores the read data and an IRQ_STATUS read, clears IRQ_STATUS, stores a word to ROM and stores a second IRQ_STATUS read with the `slv` and `dec` bits, writes 0 to SRAM_EXEC_MODE.enable and stores its read, then spins on a jump to itself. The testbench reads the result words through the KM SRAM macro array handle (`KM-RESULT`). The case table is written from `architecture.adoc`, sections "Key Manager Control and Status Registers (KMCSR)" (execute-permission whitelist and ROM lockout) and "Unrecoverable Faults", never from the RTL. Approved probes: `KM-RESULT`, `HOST-MBX` (`SEP_STATUS`), `PIC-IRQ` (vector bit 30), `KM-COUNT` (`km_rom_req_count_o`), `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (the `sep_reset_n` release after `rst_ni`; `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, SEP reset probes). `KM-VALID` (CHK-TRAP-ENGINE) and `KM-CSR` (CHK-EXEC, CHK-REGION, CHK-TRAP-STATUS, CHK-LOCKOUT-ORDER) are observation taps the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list; <<sep-vplan-km-proposed>> defines them. Injects: none.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed, RAND-REP. Dimensions: the trap kind (each of (a) to (f) runs on every seed), the misalignment of kind (c), the opcode field values, the reset kind after each trap (warm or cold; the six recoveries of a seed hold at least one warm and at least one cold reset), the case (each of C0 to C6 runs on every seed), the enable of C5 and the enable of C6 (0 or 1, one boot each), the snippet region in [1, 30], and the target window of C5. The four (enable, target) cells of C5 and C6 fill on a merge of regressions: one regression of three seeds misses a given cell with a probability of about 12 %. Legal ranges: funct3 3 to 7 for kind (f), because funct3 0 to 2 are the legal encodings of the control; unassigned opcodes only for kind (b); SRAM_EXEC_MODE.enable is written to 1 once per boot. Every drawn data word is non-zero. `reseed = 3` samples different misalignments, opcode fields, reset kinds, enables, regions and windows.

*Objective*: `km_unrecoverable_err_o` asserts while the KM CPU is in the trap state, stays asserted, and clears on a cold or warm reset. The trap entries are EBREAK, an illegal instruction or a misaligned access with the interrupt masked, and EBREAK or an illegal instruction inside an interrupt handler. A CRC accelerator encoding of the custom0 family other than the three defined ones takes the illegal-instruction path. A CPU trap wipes the KPV and sets IRQ_STATUS.WIPE_STATE, and it scrubs neither SRAM, nor the mailbox, nor the engine keys. The execute whitelist checks every committed fetch. With SRAM_EXEC_MODE.enable 0 only ROM is executable. With enable 1 write-locked 1 KiB SRAM regions and ROM (until the lockout engages) are executable. Other fetches pulse IRQ_STATUS.EXEC_VIOLATION. The ROM lockout engages on the first committed fetch from write-locked SRAM. After it, ROM data reads return zero with ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION, ROM fetches feed zero and trap, a ROM write completes with no error and sets ROM_WRITE_ERR, and a write of 0 to the enable has no effect. A build without VROM treats 0x1000_0000 as unmapped. Spec: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, sections "Wipe State", "Key Manager Control and Status Registers (KMCSR)", "CRC Accelerator"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc`, sections "IRQ_STATUS", "SRAM_EXEC_MODE"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/port_table.adoc`; `hw/sys/sep/doc/interrupts.adoc`, section "SEP CPU Interrupt Vector Map".

*Test Coverage*: One case per boot. Six trap kinds: (a) EBREAK with the interrupt masked; (b) an illegal instruction (the all-zero word or an unassigned opcode); (c) a misaligned word load or store; (d) EBREAK inside the handler; (e) an illegal instruction inside the handler; (f) custom0 with funct7 0101100 and funct3 in {3, 4, 5, 6, 7}. Error injection for the traps: point, the KM instruction stream and data bus; kind, illegal instruction, misaligned access, undefined CRC encoding; expected response, a trap with `km_unrecoverable_err_o` asserted. The witnesses before each trap are KPV words and CTRL locks, an SRAM word, HMAC key_valid and a mailbox word; the recovery is a warm or a cold reset, and every seed has at least one recovery of each kind. Whitelist cases: C0 (control): enable 0, a jump into ROM, a ROM data read and a ROM store. C1: enable 0, a fetch from a write-locked and from an unlocked SRAM region. C2: enable 1 while running in ROM (a ROM data read and a ROM store before the lockout), then a jump to an SRAM-resident snippet in a write-locked region that reads ROM data, stores to ROM and writes 0 to the enable. C3: enable 1, a fetch from an unlocked SRAM region. C4: enable 1, a locked-region snippet that jumps back into ROM. C5: a fetch from a peripheral window (0x10000, 0x12000, 0x14000, 0x15000) with a drawn enable 0 or 1. C6: a fetch from 0x1000_0000 with a drawn enable 0 or 1. Error injection for the cases: point, the KM instruction fetch address and the KM data bus; kind, a fetch outside the whitelist, a write to ROM, a ROM access after the lockout; expected response, an EXEC_VIOLATION pulse; for a ROM store, completion with `slv` = 0 and `dec` = 0 and ROM_WRITE_ERR; for a ROM data read after the lockout, zero data and ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION; for a ROM fetch after the lockout, ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION and the trap. The fetch region index: region r locked and r + 1 unlocked, r in [1, 30]. After C2: a warm reset and, in another boot, a cold reset; the image reads SRAM_EXEC_MODE and a ROM word after each. Extends the open test `sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test`, which runs the three legal CRC encodings against a golden, the open test `sep_km_rom_write_err_test`, which grades the ROM store before the lockout, and the open test `sep_km_vrom_decerr_test`, which grades the DECERR of a load at 0x1000_0000. Those cells run here as controls. `sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test` runs the KPV wipe on a trap and the held `km_unrecoverable_err_o` as controls. This test grades only the new cells: the trap kinds, the KPV wipe on a trap, the held output, the whitelist cases, the lockout and the ROM store after the lockout. The owners of the open tests keep the rest. Functional coverage: `sep_km_memory_boot_cg` (`cp_mem_iface_req`), `sep_km_integrity_fault_cg` (`cp_if_rom_write`), `sep_km_exec_protection_cg` (`cp_ex_mode_x_target`, `cp_ex_mode_enable`, `cp_ex_lockout_x_access`, `cp_ex_lockout_order`, `cp_ex_lockout_irreversible`), `sep_km_fault_report_cg` (`cp_fr_err_out`, `cp_km_trap_cause`), `sep_km_vault_wipe_cg` (`cp_vw_pre_contents`, `cp_vw_effect`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Cold-reset the SEP with `+skip_fuse_sense`, release the KM and wait for the start marker of the POKE image in the KM SRAM result words (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Draw the plan. Log `SEED` and every `DRAW` value. Each boot below starts from a warm reset (or this cold reset) and waits for the start marker. In each case boot of steps 7 to 14, before the jump, the image writes a drawn non-zero sentinel, different from every marker, to the snippet marker word, to the result words of P and to the handler result word, and reports them. The KM SRAM keeps its words across a warm reset, so a result word that still holds the sentinel after the jump is a missing result and fails the check that reads it, and a value left by an earlier boot cannot pass.
. Normal-interrupt control. Arm the counting handler and write IRQ_SET with IRQ_STATUS bit 8 (otp_change) with its enable set. Read the handler count. Run the three legal CRC encodings (funct7 0101100 with funct3 000, 001 and 010). Sample PIC vector bit 30 and `km_rom_req_count_o` over a window of 5000 host clocks (a test parameter).
. Trap boot, once per kind (a) to (f), each in its own boot. Write the witnesses: KPV words and CTRL lock bits on 4 slots, one SRAM result word, HMAC key_valid 0 and then 1 (two writes of the HMAC KEY_CTRL, with `KM-VALID` read after each, the control of CHK-TRAP-ENGINE), and one host word pushed into the SEP-to-KM mailbox FIFO after the image has read its list. Sample PIC vector bit 30 over a quiet window (0 expected, the control).
. Error injection, point: the KM instruction stream and data bus; kind: the trap of the drawn kind; expected response: a trap with `km_unrecoverable_err_o` asserted. Kinds (a), (b), (c) and (f) run with the CPU interrupts masked; for kind (b) execute the all-zero word or a drawn word with an unassigned opcode; for kind (c) execute a word load or store at an address plus 1 or plus 2; for kind (f) execute custom0 with funct7 0101100 and a drawn funct3 in {3, 4, 5, 6, 7}. Kinds (d) and (e) arm the counting handler, write IRQ_SET with IRQ_STATUS bit 8 (enable set), and execute EBREAK (d) or an illegal word (e) inside the handler.
. Wait for PIC vector bit 30 to rise (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). Sample bit 30 and `km_rom_req_count_o` over a window of 5000 host clocks. Read the SRAM result word through `KM-RESULT` and the mailbox depth through `SEP_STATUS`. With the taps, read `KM-VALID` and `KM-CSR` IRQ_STATUS.
. Recovery. Apply the reset kind that the plan gives this trap (a warm reset by `SW_RESET_N` bit 0, or a cold reset by a pulse of `rst_ni`, a bounded wait for the `sep_reset_n` release on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (a timeout fails the leaf) and a write of `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7F to release the KM, because the register is back at 0x7E after `rst_ni`) and wait for the start marker. Sample bit 30 (0 expected). Post a read loop over the 1024 KPV words and a read of the 64 CTRL registers.
. Case C0 (control), in its own boot: enable 0, a jump into ROM, a ROM data read and a ROM store; the CPU interrupts stay masked.
. Case C1, in two boots: enable 0, a jump to the snippet S in a write-locked region (boot A) and in an unlocked region (boot B). In each boot copy S with KM bus writes into a drawn region in [1, 30] and read it back; in boot A then write SRAM_LOCK for that region (a write to a locked region is dropped, so the copy comes first). Arm the exec_violation handler. Whether the refused instructions run is not stated and is not graded.
. Case C2, in its own boot. Copy P with KM bus writes into region r (drawn in [1, 30]) and read it back, then write SRAM_LOCK for region r only. In ROM write 1 to SRAM_EXEC_MODE.enable, run a ROM data read, write 0 to the enable and read it, store a word to ROM (the control: it completes with `slv` = 0, `dec` = 0 and sets ROM_WRITE_ERR) and clear IRQ_STATUS. Jump to P with the CPU interrupts masked and wait for the end marker in the result words.
. Reset after C2. Apply a warm reset, wait for the start marker, read SRAM_EXEC_MODE and a ROM data word. In another boot repeat step 9 (C2), apply a cold reset (a pulse of `rst_ni`), wait for the `sep_reset_n` release on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` as in step 6, write `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7F to release the KM, wait for the start marker, read SRAM_EXEC_MODE and a ROM data word.
. Case C3, in its own boot. Copy S with KM bus writes into region r + 1 (the region r of step 9 plus one) and read it back, then write SRAM_LOCK for region r only, so that region r + 1 stays unlocked. Write 1 to the enable, arm the exec_violation handler, and jump to S.
. Case C4, in its own boot. Draw a region in [1, 30]. Copy a snippet that ends with a jump into ROM into it with KM bus writes and read it back, then write SRAM_LOCK for that region and write 1 to the enable. Keep the CPU interrupts masked. Jump to the snippet. Sample PIC vector bit 30 and `km_rom_req_count_o` over a window of 5000 host clocks. With the taps, record the cycles of the IRQ_STATUS.ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION rise and of the bit 30 rise.
. Case C5, in its own boot. Draw a window from {0x10000, 0x12000, 0x14000, 0x15000} and the enable (0 or 1), write the enable, arm the exec_violation handler and jump to the window.
. Case C6, in its own boot. Draw the enable (0 or 1), write it, arm the exec_violation handler and jump to 0x1000_0000.
. For C1, C3, C5 and C6 sample `KM-CSR` IRQ_STATUS on every clock from the jump to the end of a window of 5000 host clocks (a test parameter), and log the result word that the handler stored; for C2 read the result words of P; for C0 read the result words of the ROM loop.
. Write `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 high if it is low, so that `km_sw_rst_n` is high at the end of the leaf.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-TRAP
|After a trap of kind (a) to (f) `km_unrecoverable_err_o` (PIC vector bit 30) is 1, stays 1 over the window, and the KM CPU executes no more instructions: `km_rom_req_count_o` stops moving (`architecture.adoc`, "Unrecoverable Faults": the CPU enters the trap state for EBREAK or an illegal instruction with the interrupt masked, for a misaligned access with the interrupt masked, and for EBREAK or an illegal instruction inside an ISR; the signal remains asserted while the KM stays in the trap state; "CRC Accelerator": an unrecognized custom0 encoding keeps the normal illegal-instruction handling). A wrong DUT does not assert the output, keeps fetching, or drops the output before a reset. Anchor: PIC vector bit 30 and `km_rom_req_count_o`. Control: bit 30 reads 0 in the quiet window before the trap, and CHK-TRAP-CTRL shows that the same image does not trap on legal events. Evidence: `CHK-TRAP PASS: kind=<a..f> detail=<value> err_o=1 halted=1 held=1`.
|CHK-TRAP-WIPE
|A CPU trap wipes the KPV: after the reset all 1024 KPV words read 0 (`architecture.adoc`, "Wipe State": the wipe event is `wipe_state_i` OR `cpu_trap` and it clears the KPV words and the CTRL registers). The reset does not overwrite the key words (`architecture.adoc`, "Key and Policy Vault (KPV)": cold or warm reset alone does not overwrite their contents), so the image reads them after the reset. The reset clears CTRL by itself (same section: the lock/seal bits clear on warm or cold reset), so the 64 CTRL reads after the reset cannot show the trap wipe; they are logged (`LOG ctrl_nonzero_after_reset=<n>`) and not graded. A wrong DUT leaves a KPV word. Anchor: the read loop after the reset. Control: the witnesses are non-zero before the trap. Evidence: `CHK-TRAP-WIPE PASS: kpv_nonzero_before=1 kpv_nonzero_after_reset=0`.
|CHK-TRAP-SCOPE
|A trap scrubs neither the SRAM word nor the mailbox word (`architecture.adoc`, "Wipe State": the hardware action covers the KPV only). The SRAM result word keeps its value, read through `KM-RESULT` while the CPU is halted, and `SEP_STATUS` shows the pushed word. A wrong DUT clears one of them. Anchor: the KM SRAM result words; `SEP_STATUS`. Control: both witnesses are non-zero before the trap. Evidence: `CHK-TRAP-SCOPE PASS: sram_same=1 mbox_depth=1`.
|CHK-TRAP-CLR
|A cold or a warm reset clears the trap: PIC vector bit 30 reads 0 after the reset (`architecture.adoc`, "Unrecoverable Faults": the trap is cleared by resetting the KM CPU, either a cold or a warm reset). A wrong DUT keeps the output set. Anchor: PIC vector bit 30. Control: bit 30 read 1 before the reset. Evidence: `CHK-TRAP-CLR PASS: reset=<warm/cold> err_o=0`.
|CHK-TRAP-CTRL
|Control. A normal interrupt runs its handler once and returns, the three legal CRC encodings run with no trap, bit 30 stays 0 and the CPU keeps fetching (`architecture.adoc`, "CRC Accelerator", "Key Manager CPU Interrupt Processing"). It shows that the image traps only on the stimulus of the trap kinds. Anchor: the handler count in the result words; PIC vector bit 30; `km_rom_req_count_o`. Evidence: `CHK-TRAP-CTRL PASS: control=1 irq_entries=1 legal_crc=3 err_o=0`.
|CHK-EXEC
|A fetch outside the whitelist raises IRQ_STATUS.EXEC_VIOLATION: C1 (enable 0, SRAM region locked or unlocked), C3 (enable 1, unlocked region), C5 (peripheral window, enable 0 or 1) and C6 (0x1000_0000, a build without VROM) (`architecture.adoc`, "Key Manager Control and Status Registers (KMCSR)", "Execute-Permission Whitelist (NX)", the executable regions table and the VROM bullet; `km_csr.adoc`, "IRQ_STATUS"). A wrong DUT runs the fetch with no violation, or reports ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION instead. Anchor: `KM-CSR` IRQ_STATUS, sampled on every clock from the jump to the end of the window: EXEC_VIOLATION reads 0 at the jump and rises in the window, and ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION stays 0. The tap sees the rise in the cycle in which it occurs, so the anchor does not depend on what the CPU executes after the refused fetch, which the specification does not state; the handler result word is logged. Control: CHK-EXEC-OK, where the permitted fetch raises nothing. Evidence: `CHK-EXEC PASS: case=<C1/C3/C5/C6> mode=<0/1> region=<r/window> locked=<0/1> exec_viol_at_jump=0 exec_viol=1 rom_acc_viol=0 handler_word_logged=<w>`.
|CHK-EXEC-OK
|A permitted fetch is accepted and raises no violation: C0 (enable 0, ROM) and C2 (enable 1: ROM before the lockout engages, then the locked SRAM region). The marker of the snippet lands and IRQ_STATUS shows neither EXEC_VIOLATION nor ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION for the fetch (`architecture.adoc`, executable regions table). The ROM data reads of C0 and of C2 before the lockout, at an address whose word in the built POKE image is non-zero, return that image word and raise no ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION (`architecture.adoc`, "ROM Lockout": ROM reads are blocked only after the lockout engages). A wrong DUT refuses the fetch, or blocks a ROM data read before the lockout. Anchor: the snippet marker and IRQ_STATUS in the result words, which hold the sentinel before the jump. Control: the refusals of CHK-EXEC are the twins of this check. Evidence: `CHK-EXEC-OK PASS: case=<C0/C2> mode=<0/1> region=<r> locked=<0/1> pre=sentinel marker=1 viol=0 rom_rd_ok=1`.
|CHK-LOCKOUT
|After the first committed fetch from write-locked SRAM in SRAM mode, a ROM data read returns 0 with IRQ_STATUS.ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION set; a ROM store completes with `slv` = 0 and `dec` = 0, sets ROM_WRITE_ERR and does not set ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION; and a write of 0 to SRAM_EXEC_MODE.enable leaves it 1 (`architecture.adoc`, "ROM Lockout": blocked reads return zero, writes still reach the ROM interface and raise ROM_WRITE_ERR, the lockout cannot be cleared by software; `memory_map.adoc`, note 1: a ROM write completes without an error response and sets ROM_WRITE_ERR). A wrong DUT returns ROM data, leaves ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION clear, gives the store an AXI error or misses ROM_WRITE_ERR, or clears the enable. Anchor: the result words of P. Control: C0 and the ROM accesses of C2 before the lockout return the word of the built image at that address and raise no ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION. Evidence: `CHK-LOCKOUT PASS: rom_rd=0 rom_acc_viol=1 rom_wr_err=1 slv=0 dec=0 enable=1`.
|CHK-LOCKOUT-FETCH
|A ROM fetch after the lockout feeds zero instructions and traps the CPU with the inherited interrupt masks: PIC vector bit 30 rises after the blocked fetch, and the KM CPU stops fetching (`architecture.adoc`, "ROM Lockout": a blocked fetch raises ROM_ACCESS_VIOLATION and feeds an all-zero stream that the CPU traps). A wrong DUT runs the ROM code with no trap. Anchor: PIC vector bit 30 and `km_rom_req_count_o` over the window. Control: a ROM fetch with enable 1 before the first locked-SRAM fetch raises nothing (C2). Evidence: `CHK-LOCKOUT-FETCH PASS: trap=1 halted=1`.
|CHK-REGION
|The fetch-region index is the same 1 KiB slice of the byte address as the write-lock region: with only region r locked, code in region r runs (marker lands, no violation) and code in region r + 1 is refused with EXEC_VIOLATION (`architecture.adoc`, "Key Manager Control and Status Registers (KMCSR)": the fetch-region index is a plain slice of the byte address and the same slice as the data-write check). A wrong DUT uses another slice and runs the code in r + 1 or refuses the code in r. Anchor: the marker and the result-word IRQ_STATUS of the C2 boot (region r), and the `KM-CSR` IRQ_STATUS of the C3 boot (region r + 1), sampled as in CHK-EXEC. Control: the C2 and C3 boots lock the same region r. Evidence: `CHK-REGION PASS: r=<r> in_r=allowed in_r1=refused`.
|CHK-EXEC-RST
|After a warm or a cold reset SRAM_EXEC_MODE.enable reads 0 and a ROM data read returns the ROM word again (`km_csr.adoc`, "SRAM_EXEC_MODE": cleared by warm or cold reset; `architecture.adoc`, "ROM Lockout": every reset that re-enters ROM restores ROM access). A wrong DUT keeps the enable at 1 or still reads zero from ROM. Anchor: the POKE readback after the reset; the expected ROM word is the word at the read address in the built POKE image file (the stimulus), at an address whose image word is non-zero. Control: the ROM data read of C2 before the lockout returns the same image word, and the same read returned zero after the lockout. Evidence: `CHK-EXEC-RST PASS: reset=<warm/cold> enable=0 rom_word_ok=1`.
|CHK-TRAP-ENGINE
|A trap does not clear the HMAC key_valid (`architecture.adoc`, "Wipe State": the hardware action does not touch the accelerator sideload registers). A wrong DUT clears it. Anchor: `KM-VALID`. Control on the same tap: before the trap the image writes HMAC KEY_CTRL = 0 and then 1, and `KM-VALID` shows the HMAC key_valid at 0 after the first write and at 1 after the second (`hmac_wrapper_key.adoc`, `KEY_CTRL`: read-write); a tap that does not show both values fails CHK-TRAP-ENGINE. Evidence: `CHK-TRAP-ENGINE PASS: control_w0=0 control_w1=1 keyvalid_hmac=1`.
|CHK-TRAP-STATUS
|A trap sets IRQ_STATUS.WIPE_STATE, because the wipe event is `wipe_state_i` OR `cpu_trap` and it records the KMCSR wipe event (`architecture.adoc`, "Wipe State"). A wrong DUT leaves the bit clear. Anchor: `KM-CSR` IRQ_STATUS, because the CPU is halted. Control: the bit reads 0 before the trap. Evidence: `CHK-TRAP-STATUS PASS: status_wipe=1`.
|CHK-LOCKOUT-ORDER
|On a blocked ROM fetch the violation bit is recorded before the trap, because the illegal instruction cannot be detected until the zero word has been returned and decoded (`architecture.adoc`, "ROM Lockout": the violation bit is always recorded first). A wrong DUT traps before it records the violation. Anchor: `KM-CSR` IRQ_STATUS and PIC vector bit 30, in cycles. Evidence: `CHK-LOCKOUT-ORDER PASS: viol_cycle=<c1> trap_cycle=<c2>` with c1 below c2.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: An unmasked EBREAK outside a handler has no stated outcome and is not drawn. Whether the refused instructions of C1 execute is not stated and is not graded. The ROM store of the enable-0 case C0 is a control. The ROM data reads of C0 and of C2 before the lockout are graded by CHK-EXEC-OK and are not claimed as a lockout. The ROM store before the lockout belongs to `sep_km_rom_write_err_test`, the legal CRC encodings to `sep_km_crc_pcpi_kat_test`, the DECERR of a load at 0x1000_0000 to `sep_km_vrom_decerr_test`, and the KPV wipe and the held output on the trap kinds to this test. `sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test` grades the halt of the ROM fatal handler and runs the KPV wipe as a control. The open owners keep the cells that they grade. The SRAM words after a cold reset are not graded, because the specification states no array erase.

'''''
[[sep_km_crossbar_efuse_window_rand_test]]
===== 7.32 `sep_km_crossbar_efuse_window_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle POKE with real fuse sense (no `+skip_fuse_sense`) and a fresh fuse image for each leaf, 3 seeds, Verilator and VCS. Testlist: groups `all` and `cpu_stub` in `hw/sys/sep/dv/testlists/km.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_km_crossbar_efuse_window_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: The POKE image is a legal master of the KM bus. It reads and writes through the KM crossbar and reports each result (read data, `slv`, `dec`) in KM SRAM words 0 to 97. The host LSU master (ocah_axi_vip) reaches the eFuse shadow map, the interface control map and the token map at 0x1093_0000, 0x1093_0400 and 0x1093_0500 (`hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`). The test holds a table of the KM windows written from `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc` and a model of the staged fuse image. Approved: `KM-RESULT` (POKE result), `HOST-MBX` and the host eFuse path (frontdoor reads and programs), `OTP-TAP` (`secure_tm_o`, `efuse_shadow_probe_o`), `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (the `sep_reset_n` release). `KM-BUS` (CHK-REMAP-BUS only) is an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list. Injects: none.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed.
