The draws are: the order of the windows; the offset of each drawn word inside its class; the 40 eFuse read draws (map, and a word in 0 to 255 for the shadow map); the fuse image (the words of `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `LC_STATE` (`TEST_DEV`), `SIP_DIS` (0) and `SYS_DIS` (0) are fixed and are not draws; every other shadow word is drawn non-zero and pairwise different); the read-locked field and a different write-locked field (both drawn from the lockable fields, which exclude `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `LC_STATE`, `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS`), the two spare fields and the target bit of each program (a bit that is 0 in the staged image); the order of the KM path and the host path in each pair of accesses. The secure test strap is a fixed pair of boots (0, then 1) and is not drawn. Legal ranges: addresses 0 to 0x1CFFF, 4-byte aligned; map words 0 to 255; successful programs go to spare fields only, and the denied program goes to the write-locked field. `reseed = 3` samples different offsets, fields, bits and orders. The forced cells (the table below, the size boundaries and the key-window ends) are the same on every seed.

*Objective*: The KM crossbar decodes ROM 16 KiB, SRAM 32 KiB, the mailbox, the eFuse window, the KPV, the KMCSR, the DRBG sampler, the four engine key windows and the ABR window. Each hole, past-extent or reserved offset answers with the code and the read data of the memory map table. The KM CPU sees only the read data and the IRQ_STATUS bits AXI_SLVERR and AXI_DECERR. The eFuse window answers every offset outside its three maps with SLVERR and 0xBADCAB1E, keeps address bits [11:0] and replaces bits [31:12] with the base `OTP_EFUSE_REMAP_BASE[31:12]`. The engine key windows end at their key registers. The window forwards the shadow map, the interface control registers and the token registers with the same access filtering and the same per-field locks as a SEP host access. A KM read of a register returns what the host path returns for the same register and state. A KM program request is allowed or denied exactly as the host request. Spec: `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/memory_map.adoc`; `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/index.adoc`, section "Memory Map"; `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, section "OTP Inputs and Readout"; `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, sections "Shadow Register and JTAG Access Control", "Fuse Programming and RMA Authorization", "Shadow Register Updates and Test-Mode Protection"; `hw/ip/efuse/regs/gen/adoc/efuse_interface_ctrl.adoc`, registers `EFUSE_INTERFACE_CTRL_STATUS`, `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL`, `EFUSE_READ_CTRL` and `EFUSE_READ_INTERFACE_READ_DATA`; `hw/ip/efuse/doc/architecture.adoc`, sections "Command-path ownership" and "Secure Test Mode (secure_tm_i)"; `hw/sys/sep/doc/test_mode.adoc`, section "Protection While Active".

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_km_rom_write_err_test`, `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`, `sep_km_otp_shim_unreachable_test`, `sep_km_vrom_decerr_test` and `sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test`. The open tests grade these cells whole, and they run here as controls: the first word of the reserved ROM-growth row (`sep_km_rom_write_err_test`), the store past the KPV register map (`sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`), the first word past the eFuse MMR map, 0x11574 (`sep_km_otp_shim_unreachable_test`), and 0x1000_0000 (`sep_km_vrom_decerr_test`). `sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test` grades the host path program of an unlocked spare bit (OTP readback and resense) and the host path rejection of a program after the lock. Those two host cells run here as controls. The Phase 3 test `sep_efuse_secure_tm_matrix_rand_test` grades the host path program in secure test mode (one of its program classes is a spare word), so the host line of the secure test program runs here as a control. The read of a decoded key-share word belongs to `sep_km_key_bank_register_rand_test`. Only the new cells are graded: the decode table, and the KM eFuse path (its program, its denial and its reads) against the host result. The open owner keeps the rest.

* The 14 decoded rows (ROM, SRAM, mailbox, eFuse map, eFuse control map, eFuse token map, KPV, KMCSR, DRBG sampler, OTBN, AES, KMAC, HMAC and ABR key windows). Per row: the first word, the last decoded word, the first hole word (rows that have a hole), the first word past the decoded extent, and the last word of the span. Reads cover every cell. Writes cover the hole, past-extent and reserved cells, because those cells have no side effect, and one store to the SRAM top (0xFFFC), which is the only write to a decoded cell. The response of the first word or the last decoded word of a register with no stated read response (the write-only mailbox word 0x10000, a key-share word, the DRBG sampler DATA register) is logged and not graded.
* The 10 reserved rows, each with its first word, its last word and one drawn word, by read and by write: 0x4000 to 0x7FFF, 0x10020 to 0x10FFF, 0x11600 to 0x11FFF, 0x14800 to 0x14FFF, 0x15010 to 0x17FFF, 0x18080 to 0x18FFF, 0x19080 to 0x19FFF, 0x1A080 to 0x1AFFF, 0x1B080 to 0x1BFFF and 0x1C800 to 0x1CFFF. The row at 0x11600 lies inside the eFuse window and answers as an eFuse gap.
* The hole and past-extent cells that answer SLVERR with data 0x0: the mailbox word 0x1001C; the KPV from 0x13108 to 0x13FFC; the KMCSR hole word and the span from 0x144C0 to 0x147FC; the OTBN, AES, KMAC and HMAC windows from 0x18064, 0x19044, 0x1A044 and 0x1B044 to the last word of each 128 B span (offset 0x7C); the ABR hole word and the span from 0x1C42C to 0x1C7FC. The hole word of the KMCSR and of the ABR window is the lowest word inside the decoded extent that the generated register map assigns to no register.
* The eFuse gaps, which answer SLVERR with 0xBADCAB1E: 0x1141C to 0x114FC, 0x11574 to 0x115FC and 0x11600 to 0x11FFC.
* The size boundaries 0x3FFC, 0x4000, 0x8000 and 0xFFFC.
* A fuse image with one read-locked field, one write-locked field and the nine spare fields unlocked. Side-effect-free reads of the shadow map, of the interface status registers (0x11400, 0x11404, 0x11408, 0x11410) and of the token match status registers (0x11564, 0x11568, 0x1156C, 0x11570), by the KM and by the host.
* A program of one bit of an unlocked spare field by each path, and a program attempt on the write-locked field by each path.
* A second boot with the secure test strap set before the cold reset and held: the same reads, a program attempt by each path, and a read of the spare word of that target by each path (the default all-zero response).

Error cases: unmapped and past-extent accesses on the KM bus; a program request to a locked field; a program request in secure test mode.

Functional coverage: `sep_km_otp_readout_cg` (`cp_efw_read_class`, `cp_efw_program`) and `sep_km_integrity_fault_cg` (`cp_if_window`, `cp_if_decode_outcome`, `cp_if_offset_class`, `cp_if_efuse_remap`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Print `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for every drawn value. Build the fuse image at life-cycle state `TEST_DEV`. `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `LC_STATE` (`TEST_DEV`), `SIP_DIS` (0) and `SYS_DIS` (0) are fixed. Draw every other shadow word non-zero and pairwise different. Set by hand in `LOCKS` the read-lock bit of one drawn lockable field and the write-lock bit of a different drawn lockable field; lockable fields exclude `LOCKS`, `LOCKS_SPARE`, `LC_STATE`, `SIP_DIS` and `SYS_DIS`. Keep both lock bits of each of the nine spare fields clear in `LOCKS_SPARE`. Draw the two spare fields and one target bit for each of them and for the write-locked field, and two further unlocked spare targets for the secure test boot (step 12). Each target bit is 0 in the staged word.
. Set `test_en_strap_i` to 0. Apply a cold reset and sense the staged image for real. A bounded wait for `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`; a timeout fails the leaf. Then wait (bounded; a timeout fails the leaf) for the `sep_reset_n` release on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`, which comes after the sense done (`reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller: `sep_reset_no` resets the Reset Controller register block). Sample `secure_tm_o`; it reads 0. Release the KM with the POKE image.
. The image writes 0 to the KMCSR IRQ_ENABLE, so no access raises a KM interrupt. Before every KM access of steps 4 to 12 the image clears AXI_SLVERR and AXI_DECERR in IRQ_STATUS by write-one-to-clear and reads IRQ_STATUS; the read returns 0, which is the control for the check. After the access the image reads IRQ_STATUS again and reports the read data, `slv` and `dec`.
. Decode walk (error injection; point: KM bus address; kind: unmapped, reserved or past-extent access; expected response: the table cell, and the matching IRQ_STATUS bit). In a drawn order of the rows, run the cells of the Test Coverage list for every decoded row and every reserved row. Read every cell. Write only the hole, past-extent and reserved cells. The ROM write is not drawn. For each reserved row and for each eFuse gap use the first word, the last word and one drawn word. Each result is compared with the table cell of `memory_map.adoc`.
. Size boundaries. The image reads 0x3FFC (ROM top) and 0x4000 (the first word past the ROM). It stores a drawn non-zero word to 0xFFFC and reads 0x8000 (SRAM base, which holds a result word) and 0xFFFC (SRAM top).
. Controls of the open owners. The image reads the first word of the reserved row at 0x4000, stores to 0x13108, reads and stores at 0x11574 (the first word past the eFuse MMR map), and reads 0x1000_0000. Each result is logged with `control=1` and does not decide the verdict.
. Clean in-map controls. The image reads the mailbox KM_STATUS (0x1000C), the DRBG sampler STATUS (0x15008) and the first word of ROM and of SRAM, and reports `slv` and `dec`.
. KM read against host read. Build 40 draws (a test parameter): the shadow-map words 0 and 255, one word of the read-locked field, one word of the write-locked field, the four interface status registers, the four token match status registers, and drawn words of the shadow map for the rest. Each map appears at least once. For each draw, in order: the host reads the draw at the SEP address with the same bits [11:0] (0x1093_0000 plus the KM offset inside the 4 KiB window); the image reads the draw at its KM address on the next sync word of the testbench; the host reads the draw a second time. The KM data and the KM `slv` and `dec` bits are compared with the host data and the host response class (OKAY, SLVERR, DECERR). The two host reads of a draw are equal.
. Program by each path. In a drawn order, the KM programs its spare target and the host programs the other spare target. For each path: write `efuse_req_error_clear` through the path and read `efuse_req_error` through the path (0, the control); write `read_enable` = 0 and read `program_done`; write `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` (one write with `program_enable` = 1, `efuse_addr` = the bit address of the target and `efuse_data` = 1, then one write that adds `efuse_program_go` = 1). After the go write, a bounded wait until `program_busy` reads 0 and `program_done` or `efuse_req_error` reads 1; a timeout fails the check. Read `program_done`, `program_status` and `efuse_req_error` through the same path. The host then reads back the OTP word that holds the target bit: it writes `program_enable` = 0 and `read_enable` = 1 with `efuse_addr` = the bit address of the target, writes `efuse_read_go`, waits (bounded; a timeout fails the check) until `read_busy` reads 0 and `read_done` reads 1, and reads `EFUSE_READ_INTERFACE_READ_DATA`. The word equals the staged word plus the target bit.
. Denied program (error injection; point: `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` write; kind: program request to a write-locked field; expected response: `efuse_req_error` = 1 and no fuse change). In a drawn order, for each path: write `efuse_req_error_clear`, read `efuse_req_error` (0, the control), write `read_enable` = 0, then program the target bit of the write-locked field as in step 9. After the go write, a bounded wait until `program_busy` reads 0 and `efuse_req_error` reads 1; a timeout fails the check. Read `efuse_req_error` through the same path and log `program_done` and `program_status`. The host reads back the OTP word of the target bit as in step 9 (`program_enable` = 0, `read_enable` = 1, go, bounded wait for `read_done`). Read the OTP word of the spare bit of step 9 the same way as the control.
. Secure test boot. Hold the SEP in cold reset, set `test_en_strap_i` to 1, release the reset with the strap held at 1 and sense the image for real. A bounded wait for `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`, then for the `sep_reset_n` release on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` as in step 8. Sample `secure_tm_o`; it reads 1. Release the KM with the POKE image. Repeat the reads of step 8 with 40 new draws.
. Error injection; point: `EFUSE_PROGRAM_CTRL` write in secure test mode; kind: program request; expected response: no fuse change, and equal results on the KM path and on the host path. In a drawn order, the KM and the host each target one of the two further unlocked spare targets of step 1 (not a target of step 9; each bit is 0 in the staged word). For each path: write `efuse_req_error_clear` and read `efuse_req_error` (0, the control), write `read_enable` = 0, program the bit as in step 9, then poll `program_done`, `program_status` and `efuse_req_error` through the same path for a bounded interval (a test parameter, the same for both paths) and log the last values of each path. The end of the interval is not a verdict, because the specification does not state whether the block shows as `program_done`, `program_status` or `efuse_req_error`. Then read the spare word of the target through each path (`program_enable` = 0, `read_enable` = 1, `efuse_addr` = the bit address of the target, `efuse_read_go`, a bounded wait until `read_busy` reads 0; a timeout fails the check) and read `EFUSE_READ_INTERFACE_READ_DATA`: the blocked read returns the default all-zero response although the staged word is non-zero (`read_done` and `read_status` are logged). The OTP read of this step cannot show the target bit. Step 13 shows the bit through the shadow.
. Hold the SEP in cold reset with the strap at 1, release the reset with the strap held, and sense again. After the sense completes, the host reads the shadow word of each target bit of step 12 and of step 9 through the frontdoor and through `efuse_shadow_probe_o`. The leaf ends with the strap held; the next cold reset ends the secure test session.
. With `KM-BUS`, sample the request on `efuse_req_o` after the remap for every KM eFuse read of step 8.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-XBAR
|Every hole and past-extent cell of the non-eFuse rows of the table answers SLVERR with read data 0x0, by read and by write (the eFuse rows answer 0xBADCAB1E and CHK-XBAR-EFUSE grades them): `slv` = 1, `dec` = 0, and IRQ_STATUS shows AXI_SLVERR and not AXI_DECERR (`memory_map.adoc`, "Hole in Extent" and "Past Extent" columns; `index.adoc`, "Memory Map": the KM CPU sees only the read data and the two IRQ_STATUS bits). A DUT that answers DECERR, returns non-zero data, shows no error bit, or sets both bits fails. The store at the first word past the KPV extent (0x13108) logs `control=1`, because the open owner grades it. Anchor: the POKE result (`slv`, `dec`, data) and the IRQ_STATUS readback, taken after a clear that read 0. Control: the clean reads of step 7 answer `slv` = 0 and `dec` = 0 in the same leaf, so the two bits can read 0. Evidence: `CHK-XBAR PASS seed=S win=otbn off=0x18064 kind=past_extent dir=rd rd=0x0 slv=1 dec=0`, and `dir=wr` lines for the write cells, one line for each cell.
|CHK-XBAR-RSV
|Every reserved span except 0x11600 to 0x11FFF answers DECERR with read data 0xBADCAB1E on a read and DECERR on a write: `dec` = 1, `slv` = 0 (`memory_map.adoc`, "Hole in Extent" column of the reserved rows). A DUT that answers SLVERR, OKAY or other data fails. The first word of the row at 0x4000 logs `control=1`. Anchor: the POKE result and the IRQ_STATUS readback. Control: the clean in-map reads of step 7. Evidence: `CHK-XBAR-RSV PASS win=reserved addr=0x16000 dir=rd rd=0xBADCAB1E dec=1 slv=0`, one line for each cell.
|CHK-XBAR-EFUSE
|Every eFuse offset outside the three maps (0x1141C to 0x114FF, 0x11574 to 0x115FF and 0x11600 to 0x11FFF) answers SLVERR with read data 0xBADCAB1E on a read and SLVERR on a write: `slv` = 1, `dec` = 0 (`memory_map.adoc`, notes on the eFuse rows; `index.adoc`, "Memory Map"). A DUT that answers DECERR or forwards the access to the eFuse controller fails. The read and the store at the first word past the token map (0x11574) log `control=1`, because the open owner grades them. Anchor: the POKE result and the IRQ_STATUS readback. Control: the in-map eFuse reads of step 8 answer OKAY (`slv` = 0, `dec` = 0). Evidence: `CHK-XBAR-EFUSE PASS addr=0x11800 dir=rd rd=0xBADCAB1E slv=1 dec=0`, one line for each cell.
|CHK-XBAR-SIZE
|The ROM is 16 KiB and the SRAM is 32 KiB: the reads at 0x3FFC, 0x8000 and 0xFFFC answer clean (`slv` = 0, `dec` = 0) (`index.adoc`, "Memory Map"). A DUT with a smaller ROM or a smaller SRAM shows an error bit at one of them. A larger ROM shows only at the 0x4000 read, which the open owner grades, so this check does not claim it. The store of step 5 at 0xFFFC, the only write to a decoded cell, answers clean (`slv` = 0, `dec` = 0). The read at 0x4000 answers DECERR and logs `control=1`, because the open owner grades it. The read of the first word and of the last decoded word of every decoded row answers clean (`slv` = 0, `dec` = 0) (`index.adoc`, "Memory Map"), except the first word of the mailbox (the write-only word 0x10000), of the DRBG sampler (DATA, whose read takes a stream word) and of the five key windows (key-share words, whose read belongs to `sep_km_key_bank_register_rand_test`), whose responses are logged. Anchor: the POKE result. Control: the 0x4000 read next to the clean 0x3FFC read shows the same leaf can show an error bit. Evidence: `CHK-XBAR-SIZE PASS rom_top=clean rom_next=dec(control) sram_lo=clean sram_hi=clean sram_hi_store=clean first_last_clean=<n> first_last_logged=7`.
|CHK-EFW-RD
|For every one of the 40 draws, the KM read returns the data and the response class that the host path returns for the same register and state (`architecture.adoc`, "OTP Inputs and Readout": the KM path is treated exactly like a system-host access; `otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, "Shadow Register and JTAG Access Control"). The read-locked field never returns its staged word to either path, and the KM and the host agree on what they return instead. A DUT that drops address bits [11:0], uses a wrong remap base, applies no per-field lock on the KM path, or reaches another register returns different data than the host. The drawn shadow words are non-zero and pairwise different, so a wrong register among them shows a different value. Anchor: the KM readback and the host readback of the same draw, and the staged lock state. Control: the two host reads of each draw are equal, so the register is stable between the reads. Precondition from the stimulus: for a shadow-map draw outside the read-locked field the host read equals the word of the staged fuse image model, so equal KM and host reads cannot pass on a value that neither path should return; a mismatch fails the draw. Evidence: `CHK-EFW-RD PASS seed=S draws=40 equal=40 maps=3 host_stable=40 host_eq_staged=<n> locked_field=<name> locked_words_staged_seen=0 tm=0`.
|CHK-EFW-RD-TM
|The same equality holds in the secure test boot: for every one of the 40 new draws the KM read equals the host read, and the read-locked field never returns its staged word (`otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, "Shadow Register Updates and Test-Mode Protection": register reads keep their normal access controls). Anchor: the KM readback and the host readback, with `secure_tm_o` = 1. Control: `secure_tm_o` reads 0 in the first boot and 1 in this boot. Evidence: `CHK-EFW-RD-TM PASS draws=40 equal=40 tm=1`.
|CHK-EFW-PROG
|A program of one bit of an unlocked spare field through the KM path completes: `program_done` = 1, `program_status` = 0, `efuse_req_error` = 0, and the OTP word read by the host shows the target bit set and every other bit equal to the staged word (`otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, "Fuse Programming and RMA Authorization": the programming function honors the write-lock of each field and nothing blocks an unlocked spare). The tuple of the KM program equals the tuple of the host program of the other spare. A DUT that never completes, reports an error, blocks the KM path, flips another bit, or answers the KM path differently from the host fails. The host path program of an unlocked spare bit is graded by the open owner (`sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test`) and its line logs `control=1`. `program_done` is graded only when the read before the go write returns 0. A 1 left by the earlier program is logged as `done_before=1`, and the OTP readback then is the evidence of completion. Anchor: `program_done`, `program_status` and `efuse_req_error` readback through the path that programmed, and the host OTP readback. Control: `efuse_req_error` reads 0 after its clear and before each program, and the host path program of the other spare is the twin. Evidence: `CHK-EFW-PROG PASS path=km spare=SPARE3 bit=17 done_before=0 done=1 status=0 req_err_before=0 req_err=0 otp_bit=1 otp_word_eq=1 host_tuple_equal=1`, and one `path=host control=1` line.
|CHK-EFW-DENY
|A program of a bit of the write-locked field is refused on the KM path: `efuse_req_error` reads 1 through the KM path and the OTP word read by the host keeps the target bit at 0 (`otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, "Shadow Register and JTAG Access Control": a write-lock blocks fuse programming; `efuse_interface_ctrl.adoc`, `efuse_req_error`). The KM result equals the host result. A DUT that applies no lock on the KM path, or sets the bit, fails. The host path rejection of a program after the lock is graded by the open owner (`sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test`), so the host line logs `control=1`. The `program_done` and `program_status` values are logged and not graded. Anchor: `efuse_req_error` readback and the host OTP readback. Control: `efuse_req_error` reads 0 after its clear and before the program, and the spare bit of step 9 still reads 1, so the OTP readback can show a landed bit and the unlocked program of CHK-EFW-PROG is the twin. Evidence: `CHK-EFW-DENY PASS path=km req_err_before=0 req_err=1 bit_unchanged=1 spare_bit_control=1 host_equal=1`, and one `path=host control=1` line.
|CHK-EFW-TM
|In the secure test boot the KM path and the host path give the same result for a program attempt, and a program through the KM path changes no bit (`hw/ip/efuse/doc/architecture.adoc`, "Secure Test Mode (secure_tm_i)": all fuse commands are blocked, the guard returns default all-zero responses, and secure test mode contributes to the write lock status; `otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, "Shadow Register Updates and Test-Mode Protection": the fuse write-enable inputs are tied inactive while SECURE_TM is 1; `test_mode.adoc`, "Protection While Active"). The tuple (`program_done`, `program_status`, `efuse_req_error`) of the KM equals the tuple of the host. A read of the spare word of the target through each path returns `EFUSE_READ_INTERFACE_READ_DATA` = 0 although the staged word is non-zero, because the blocked command returns the default all-zero response; the zero read is graded on both paths. Both target bits are 0 in the staged image (step 1). After a cold reset with the strap held and a fresh sense, both target bits read 0 in the shadow word and at `efuse_shadow_probe_o`, so a 1 shows a programmed bit. A DUT that programs a bit through the KM path in secure test mode, returns a non-zero word for a blocked read, or answers the two paths differently, fails. The host path program in secure test mode is graded by `sep_efuse_secure_tm_matrix_rand_test`, so the bit-unchanged line of the host path logs `control=1`. The tuple values are logged: the text states the zero default response and the write-lock contribution, and it does not say whether `program_done` and `efuse_req_error` show the block, because `efuse_interface_ctrl.adoc` defines `efuse_req_error` as a block by a read or program lock and no sentence says whether secure test mode sets it. Anchor: the readback of the tuple and of the read word through each path, the shadow word and `efuse_shadow_probe_o`. Control: the shadow words of the boot-one programs (step 9) read 1 after the same sense, so the shadow readback can show a landed bit, and the OTP word of the spare in the first boot reads non-zero. Evidence: `CHK-EFW-TM PASS km_host_equal=1 tuple=1,0,0 read_zero=2 km_bit_unchanged=1 host_bit_unchanged=1(control) control_landed=2 secure_tm=1`.
|CHK-REMAP-BUS
|For every KM eFuse read of step 8, bits [11:0] of the request at `efuse_req_o` equal the KM offset bits [11:0], and bits [31:12] hold the same value for every draw and every map (`architecture.adoc`, "OTP Inputs and Readout": the window keeps bits [11:0] and replaces bits [31:12] with `OTP_EFUSE_REMAP_BASE[31:12]`). The value of the base is logged and not graded. A DUT that changes a low bit, or uses a different upper value for two maps, fails. Anchor: `KM-BUS`. Evidence: `CHK-REMAP-BUS PASS draws=40 low12=ok base_constant=1 base=0x<..>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The test does not claim the value of `OTP_EFUSE_REMAP_BASE`, the read response of a write-only register, the initiator of a crossbar access (it is not observable), or the status values of a program request under the secure test strap (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The decode table, and the KM eFuse path against the host path, are the new cells. The open tests `sep_km_rom_write_err_test`, `sep_km_key_policy_vault_test`, `sep_km_otp_shim_unreachable_test`, `sep_km_vrom_decerr_test` and `sep_efuse_program_lock_matrix_test` keep the cells that they grade, which include the host path program of an unlocked spare bit and the host path rejection after the lock. The read of a decoded key-share word belongs to `sep_km_key_bank_register_rand_test`.

'''''

[[sep_km_mailbox_km_side_rand_test]]
===== 7.33 `sep_km_mailbox_km_side_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle IMG-MBOX-KM with `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds, Verilator and VCS. Testlist: groups `all` and `cpu_stub` in `hw/sys/sep/dv/testlists/km.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_km_mailbox_km_side_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: The IMG-MBOX-KM image runs a script of phases as a legal KM master. It reports each result in KM SRAM words 0 to 97. The image advances to the next phase when the testbench has done the host-side action of the phase (a sync word of the image; it is not DUT stimulus). The host LSU master (ocah_axi_vip) drives the SEP-side mailbox registers. A FIFO model written from the register text gives the expected depth, flags and word order. Approved: `KM-RESULT` (IMG-MBOX-KM report), `HOST-MBX`. PROPOSED: none. Injects: none. The underflow, the overflow and the flush are legal accesses of the KM image. Every check is a reached check.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed. The draws are: the outbound word counts N and the inbound word counts M; the word values (non-zero, pairwise different inside one pass); which of the two response-bit values runs first in each error pair; which FIFO is non-empty in the "one FIFO" flush cells; the order of the six flush cells; the drawn KM_IRQ_ENABLE subset S in [0, 0x1F]; the order of the interrupt sources 0 to 3. Legal ranges: depth 16; reserved bits 0. N and M also take the forced values 1, 15 and 16, one pass each. `reseed = 3` samples different counts, word values and orders. The response-bit pairs, the flush cells and the two enable runs (S and its complement) are the same on every seed.

*Objective*: Each side writes its own producing FIFO and reads the other. The sender arms a separator that hardware clears after the write. A KM read of the empty inbound FIFO and a KM write to the full outbound FIFO drop the access, set a sticky status and answer SLVERR or OKAY as the KM response bit selects. Either side flushes both FIFOs and sets the peer's flushed-by-other status. The enabled OR of the level and sticky sources drives KM CPU IRQ bit 4 and does not set IRQ_STATUS. The words move unaltered. Spec: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, sections "Reset Conditioning", "Mailbox Transport", "Key Manager CPU Interrupt Processing"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_mailbox_km.adoc`, sections "Key Manager Mailbox KM Side", "KM_READ_DATA", "KM_STATUS", "KM_IRQ_STATUS", "KM_IRQ_ENABLE", "KM_CTRL"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_mailbox_sep.adoc`, sections "SEP_READ_DATA", "SEP_CTRL".

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test` and `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test`. `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test` grades the SEP-side inbound flush with its self-clear and the SEP aggregator bit 14 with an enable, with the KM held. `sep_km_command_protocol_rand_test` grades SEP_STATUS.outbound_separator and the fault frame that follows a SEP flush with responses waiting, and the frame receive check of every open KM host test covers its per-word pattern. Those cells run here as controls. Only the new cells are graded: the KM-side registers, the KM-side flush, the outbound half of the SEP flush and the KM interrupt route. The open owner keeps the rest. The SEP inbound overflow and the SEP outbound underflow, with their response bits and sticky bits, belong to `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test`.

* The reset values of the KM-side registers.
* The outbound words, N in [1, 16] with N = 1, 15 and 16 forced, with the separator armed on the last word; the inbound words, M in [1, 16] with M = 1, 15 and 16 forced, with the separator armed on the last word; one further inbound word with no separator.
* The KM-side underflow and overflow with each response-bit value.
* A KM flush and a SEP flush, each with data in both FIFOs, with both FIFOs empty and with one FIFO non-empty (six cells).
* A drawn KM_IRQ_ENABLE subset and its complement, with each source raised and then cleared, with both KM_CTRL response bits at 1 (OKAY).

Error cases: a KM read of the empty inbound FIFO and a KM write to the full outbound FIFO.

Functional coverage: `sep_km_mailbox_fault_cg` (`cp_mbf_cond`, `cp_mbf_resp_cfg`, `cp_mbf_sticky_clear`, `cp_mbf_flush`) and `sep_km_mailbox_transport_cg` (`cp_mbt_access`, `cp_mbt_occupancy`, `cp_mbt_separator`, `cp_mbt_irq_src_x_en`, `cp_mbt_irq_aggregate`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Print `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for every drawn value. Bring up `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped, load IMG-MBOX-KM, reset SEP and release the KM. A bounded wait for the first phase of the image; a timeout fails the leaf.
. Reset phase. The image reads KM_STATUS, KM_CTRL, KM_IRQ_ENABLE, KM_WRITE_SEPARATOR and KM_IRQ_STATUS and reports them. Log the level bits [1:0] of KM_IRQ_STATUS.
. Outbound passes, for N = 16, 1, 15 and the drawn N, in that order. The image writes N non-zero words to KM_WRITE_DATA. It writes the first N - 1 words with no separator, writes 1 to KM_WRITE_SEPARATOR.set and reads it back, writes the last word, and reads KM_WRITE_SEPARATOR.set again. It reads KM_STATUS and reports `outbound_depth`, `outbound_full` and `outbound_empty`. The host reads N words from SEP_READ_DATA and reads SEP_STATUS after each word. The host then reads SEP_STATUS: the outbound FIFO is empty. Log `outbound_separator` after each word as a control.
. Inbound passes, for M = 16, 1, 15 and the drawn M, in that order. The host writes M non-zero words to SEP_WRITE_DATA, with SEP_WRITE_SEPARATOR armed before the last word. The image reads KM_STATUS and reports `inbound_depth`, `inbound_full` and `inbound_empty`. In the first pass (M = 16) the image then reads KM_IRQ_STATUS[1:0], with the M words queued (the first FIFO event). The image reads M words from KM_READ_DATA and reads KM_STATUS after each word. In the first pass the image reads KM_IRQ_STATUS[1:0] again after it has read all M words. Then the host writes one more non-zero word with no separator, and the image reads it and reads KM_STATUS again.
. Error passes (error injection; point: KM_READ_DATA read of the empty inbound FIFO and KM_WRITE_DATA write to the full outbound FIFO; kind: access error; expected response: SLVERR or OKAY as the response bit selects, a sticky status, and the queue unchanged). Run each of the two conditions with each of the two response-bit values, in a drawn order. Before each pass the image writes KM_CTRL with the response bit under test at the pass value and the other response bit at 0, writes 1 to the bits of KM_STATUS and KM_IRQ_STATUS that this condition sets, writes 1 to IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR, and reads all three back as 0 (the control).
. Underflow pass. With the inbound FIFO empty, the image reads KM_READ_DATA. It then reads IRQ_STATUS, KM_STATUS[22] and KM_IRQ_STATUS[3], and the inbound depth. It writes 0 to KM_IRQ_STATUS[3] and reads it (1); it writes 1 to KM_IRQ_STATUS[3] and reads it (0). It then repeats the underflow: it reads KM_READ_DATA on the still empty inbound FIFO, reads KM_STATUS[22] (1, the pre-clear read), writes 1 to KM_STATUS[22] and reads it. Each clear is graded only when the read immediately before it shows 1.
. Overflow pass. The image writes 16 words to KM_WRITE_DATA and reads KM_STATUS (`outbound_full` = 1). It writes a 17th word. It then reads IRQ_STATUS, KM_STATUS[21] and KM_IRQ_STATUS[2]. It writes 0 to KM_IRQ_STATUS[2] and reads it (1), and writes 1 and reads it (0). It then repeats the overflow: it writes an 18th word to the still full FIFO, reads KM_STATUS[21] (1, the pre-clear read), writes 1 to KM_STATUS[21] and reads it. Each clear is graded only when the read immediately before it shows 1. The host reads SEP_STATUS (outbound depth 16), reads 16 words from SEP_READ_DATA and compares them with the 16 words written, and reads SEP_STATUS (outbound FIFO empty).
. Flush cells, in a drawn order, for the initiator KM and the initiator SEP with the pre-flush fill both FIFOs non-empty, both empty, and one non-empty (the drawn FIFO). Before each cell, write 1 to the peer's flushed-by-other bit (KM_IRQ_STATUS[4] or SEP_IRQ_STATUS[4]) and read it as 0 (the control). Fill the FIFOs as the cell states (the image writes the outbound words, the host writes the inbound words) and read both depths from KM_STATUS and from SEP_STATUS (non-zero where filled, the control). The initiator writes its flush bit (KM_CTRL.flush with the response bits kept, or SEP_CTRL.flush). A bounded wait until the initiator's flush bit reads 0; a timeout fails the check. Read both depths from KM_STATUS and from SEP_STATUS. Read the peer's flushed-by-other bit. Write 0 to it and read it; write 1 to it and read it.
. Interrupt phase. The image writes 1 to both KM_CTRL response bits, writes 1 to IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR, reads IRQ_STATUS (0, the control), and unmasks CPU IRQ bit 4 with a counting handler. It also arms a second counting handler on CPU IRQ bit 3. KMCSR IRQ_ENABLE stays 0 until step 13.
. Define the check cycle. Read KM_IRQ_STATUS[4:0], KM_IRQ_ENABLE and IRQ_STATUS. Read the handler count of CPU IRQ bit 4. Wait one quiet window (a test parameter, the same for every sample, with no mailbox access). Read the count again. The count rises when the line is high. The model line is high when the OR over the five sources of (KM_IRQ_STATUS bit AND KM_IRQ_ENABLE bit) is non-zero.
. Run the interrupt leg twice: with KM_IRQ_ENABLE = S, and with KM_IRQ_ENABLE = the complement of S in bits [4:0]. At the start of each run: write 0 to KM_IRQ_ENABLE; the image writes 16 words to KM_WRITE_DATA (the outbound FIFO is full); the inbound FIFO is empty; write 1 to the sticky bits of KM_STATUS and KM_IRQ_STATUS. Write the enable value of the run. Run a check cycle; the line is low because no source is present.
. Per run, raise and clear each source, sources 0 to 3 in a drawn order and source 4 last, with a check cycle after each raise and after each clear. Source 0 (`inbound_read_data_avail`): the host writes one inbound word; the image reads it. Source 1 (`outbound_write_space_avail`): the host reads one outbound word; the image writes one word. Source 2 (`outbound_overflow`): the image writes one word to the full outbound FIFO; write 1 to KM_IRQ_STATUS[2] and KM_STATUS[21]. Source 3 (`inbound_underflow`): the image reads the empty inbound FIFO; write 1 to KM_IRQ_STATUS[3] and KM_STATUS[22]. Source 4 (`flushed_by_sep`): the host writes SEP_CTRL.flush; write 1 to KM_IRQ_STATUS[4].
. Control for the KMCSR route. After both runs, the image writes 1 to IRQ_ENABLE.drbg_err_en, writes 1 to IRQ_SET.drbg_err_set, and reads the handler count of CPU IRQ bit 3 and IRQ_STATUS. It writes 1 to IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR and writes 0 to IRQ_ENABLE.
. Drain the mailbox: the host reads the outbound words that remain and the image reads the inbound words that remain, so the leaf ends with both FIFOs empty.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-KMBOX-RST
|After reset KM_STATUS reads 0x00000005 (`outbound_empty` and `inbound_empty` are the only bits with reset value 1), KM_CTRL reads 0, KM_IRQ_ENABLE reads 0, KM_WRITE_SEPARATOR reads 0, and the sticky bits [4:2] of KM_IRQ_STATUS read 0 (`km_mailbox_km.adoc`, "KM_STATUS", "KM_CTRL", "KM_IRQ_STATUS"). The level bits [1:0] of KM_IRQ_STATUS are logged at reset and are graded after the first FIFO event (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>): with the inbound words queued bits [1:0] read 0b11 (data available, space available), and after the image has read all the words they read 0b10. A DUT with another reset value, or with a level bit that does not follow its FIFO, fails. Anchor: the image readback. Control: the first FIFO event of step 4 shows the level bits move. Evidence: `CHK-KMBOX-RST PASS km_status=0x00000005 km_ctrl=0x0 km_irq_enable=0x0 sticky_irq=0x0 level_logged=0x2 level_after_push=0x3 level_after_drain=0x2`.
|CHK-KMBOX-OUT
|The host reads back the N words that the image wrote, in order (`architecture.adoc`, "Mailbox Transport": reads pop words, writes push words). The per-word pattern is graded by the frame receive check of every open KM host test, so `words_equal` logs `control=1` and does not decide the verdict. KM_STATUS shows `outbound_depth` = N, `outbound_empty` = 0 and `outbound_full` = 1 only for N = 16. KM_WRITE_SEPARATOR.set reads 1 after the arm and 0 after the write (`km_mailbox_km.adoc`, "KM_WRITE_SEPARATOR": cleared by hardware when that write completes). A DUT that reports a wrong depth or full flag, or keeps the set bit, fails. SEP_STATUS.outbound_separator after the last word is logged with `control=1`, because the open owner grades it. Anchor: the image readback of KM_STATUS and KM_WRITE_SEPARATOR, and the host readback of SEP_READ_DATA and SEP_STATUS. Control: N = 1 and N = 15 run next to N = 16, so a flag that is stuck high fails at N = 1. Evidence: `CHK-KMBOX-OUT PASS n=16 words_equal=16 words_control=1 depth=16 full=1 set_reads=1,0 empty_after=1 control_sep_flag_last=1`, one line for each pass.
|CHK-KMBOX-IN
|The image reads back the M words that the host wrote, in order. The per-word pattern is graded by the frame receive check of every open KM host test, so `words_equal` logs `control=1` and does not decide the verdict. KM_STATUS shows `inbound_depth` = M, `inbound_empty` = 0 and `inbound_full` = 1 only for M = 16. KM_STATUS.inbound_separator reads 1 only after the last word is read and 0 after each other word, and reads 0 again after the extra word with no separator is read (`km_mailbox_km.adoc`, "KM_STATUS": read-only, not sticky). A DUT that reports a wrong depth or flag, sets the separator on another word, or keeps it set after the next read fails. Anchor: the image readback of KM_READ_DATA and KM_STATUS. Control: the extra word is the twin of the last word of the message. Evidence: `CHK-KMBOX-IN PASS m=9 words_equal=9 words_control=1 depth=9 full=0 inbound_sep=1 not_sticky=1`, one line for each pass.
|CHK-KMBOX-ERR
|A KM read of the empty inbound FIFO and a KM write to the full outbound FIFO each set their sticky bit in KM_STATUS and in KM_IRQ_STATUS, drop the access and leave the queue unchanged (`architecture.adoc`, "Mailbox Transport"; `km_mailbox_km.adoc`, "KM_READ_DATA", "KM_IRQ_STATUS"). With the response bit 0 the access answers SLVERR and IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR reads 1; with the response bit 1 it answers OKAY and IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR reads 0 (`km_mailbox_km.adoc`, "KM_CTRL"). The inbound depth stays 0 after the underflow. After the overflow the host reads the original 16 words and the outbound FIFO is empty afterwards. A write of 0 to the KM_IRQ_STATUS bit leaves it set, and a write of 1 clears that bit. A write of 1 to the KM_STATUS bit clears that bit. Each clear path has its own error event, and a clear is graded only when the read immediately before the write shows the bit at 1 (`pre_clear_reads` in the evidence line). The coupling between the two registers is not stated, so the value of the other register after a clear is logged and not graded. A DUT with no sticky bit, a changed queue, a response that ignores the response bit, or a sticky bit that a write of 0 clears fails. Anchor: the image readback of KM_STATUS, KM_IRQ_STATUS and IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR, and the host readback of SEP_READ_DATA. Control: each response-bit value is the twin of the other, and the three bits read 0 before each pass. Evidence: `CHK-KMBOX-ERR PASS cond=ovf resp=0 sticky=1,1 slverr=1 queue_kept=16 zero_keeps=1 w1c_clears=1 km_status_clear=1 pre_clear_reads=1,1`, one line for each of the four passes.
|CHK-KMBOX-FLUSH
|A KM flush and a SEP flush each leave both FIFOs empty in every one of the six cells, and set the peer's flushed-by-other bit (`architecture.adoc`, "Mailbox Transport": either side flushes both FIFOs and sets the peer's flushed-by-other status; `km_mailbox_km.adoc`, "KM_CTRL": cleared by hardware when the flush completes). KM_CTRL.flush reads 0 after the KM flush. After a SEP flush the outbound depth is graded; the inbound depth and SEP_CTRL.flush are logged with `control=1`, because the open owner grades them. A write of 0 to the flushed-by-other bit leaves it set, and a write of 1 clears it. A DUT that leaves a word, keeps the flush bit set, does not set the peer bit in a cell with empty FIFOs, or clears the bit on a write of 0 fails. Anchor: the image readback of KM_STATUS and KM_IRQ_STATUS, and the host readback of SEP_STATUS and SEP_IRQ_STATUS. Control: the depths read non-zero before the flush where the cell filled the FIFO, and the peer bit reads 0 before the flush. Evidence: `CHK-KMBOX-FLUSH PASS init=km fill=both km_depths=0,0 sep_depths=0,0 peer_flag=1 zero_keeps=1 w1c_clears=1`, and `init=sep ... sep_out_depth=0 control_sep_in_depth=0 control_sep_flush_bit=0` for a SEP flush, one line for each cell.
|CHK-KMBOX-IRQ
|The line to KM CPU IRQ bit 4 is high exactly when the OR over the five sources of (KM_IRQ_STATUS bit AND KM_IRQ_ENABLE bit) is non-zero, and IRQ_STATUS stays 0 (`architecture.adoc`, "Mailbox Transport", "Key Manager CPU Interrupt Processing": the enabled OR drives one interrupt to the KM CPU; the mailbox interrupt is independent of KMCSR IRQ_STATUS). In every check cycle the handler count rises exactly when the model line is high. An enabled source that raises no handler entry, a disabled source that raises one, a line that stays high after the cause is cleared, or an IRQ_STATUS bit that sets fails. The check runs with the two enable values of every source, S and its complement. Anchor: the handler count and the IRQ_STATUS readback. Control: before each run the line is low with all sources absent. Each source that is enabled in one run is disabled in the other. The count of CPU IRQ bit 3 does not change during the mailbox runs, and the IRQ_SET of DRBG_ERR raises it and sets IRQ_STATUS bit 6, which separates the two interrupt routes. Evidence: `CHK-KMBOX-IRQ PASS en=0x1D run=S enabled_rise=4 disabled_quiet=1 drop_after_clear=4 irq_status=0x000 ctrl_irq_set_bit3=1 bit3_const_during_mbox=1`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The test does not claim the SEP inbound overflow and the SEP outbound underflow with their response bits and sticky bits (the open test `sep_km_mailbox_protocol_rand_test` owns them), the inbound half of the SEP flush and the SEP_CTRL.flush self-clear (controls here), a flush in the middle of a frame, or a second arm of the separator with no write between. The coupling between a sticky bit of KM_STATUS and the same bit of KM_IRQ_STATUS is not stated and is not graded. The open tests keep the cells that they grade.

'''''

[[sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test]]
===== 7.34 `sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle POKE, `+skip_fuse_sense`, entropy noise forced (`+esrc_noise_force`), 3 seeds, Verilator and VCS. The byte-assembly leg (step 13, CHK-SMP-PACK), the partial-beat timeout leg (step 14, CHK-SMP-TO-ASM) and the handshake-control leg (step 16, CHK-SMP-STRM-CTRL) are blocked: they need the PROPOSED drive `HK-STRM-PAT`. Testlist: groups `all` and `cpu_stub` in `hw/sys/sep/dv/testlists/km.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_km_drbg_sampler_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: The POKE image reads and writes the DRBG sampler registers through the KM bus and reports each result (read data, `slv`, `dec`, STATUS) in KM SRAM words 0 to 97. It uses the read-loop operation for the long loops. The host brings up the entropy chain and drives the TRNG reset through the SEP reset register (ocah_axi_vip, host LSU master). A byte-assembly model written from `architecture.adoc` (section "Reads, prefetch and register access") gives the expected word. Approved: `KM-RESULT` (POKE result and STATUS readback), `KM-ENTROPY` (`km_entropy_tvalid_o`, `km_entropy_tdata_o`, `km_entropy_tready_o`), `HOST-MBX` (the entropy chain enable and the TRNG reset through the host). A stalled stream is the entropy chain held at its reset. Added hook: the error inject `HK-STRM` at the KM sink (the withdrawals; CHK-SMP-STRM), which the test adds to the inject exceptions of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. PROPOSED: `HK-STRM-PAT`, the drive of legal beat patterns on `TVALID`, `TDATA` and `TSTRB` at the KM sink, a net inside `sep`: the beat patterns, the partial beat with a stall, the near-expiry pulses with TREADY high and the drop after a handshake (CHK-SMP-PACK, CHK-SMP-TO-ASM, CHK-SMP-STRM-CTRL). Neither serves as a golden.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed. The draws are: the drawn timeout T in [4, 400]; the TIMEOUT of the beat-pattern legs (256 or 0); the beat pattern (non-zero TSTRB values in 1 to 15 and four different byte values for each beat); the TVALID delay of each beat (0, 1, 2 or 5 cycles from the cycle in which `km_entropy_tready_o` first rises for the read); the TIMEOUT of the byte-assembly leg in [4, 20]; the position of the error pulse; whether the leaf runs the COUNT_GOOD saturation loop of 65540 reads (probability 1/3, a test parameter, because the loop is long). The PREFETCH state is 0 in every leg except the prefetch legs. Legal ranges: T 0 to 65535 (drawn values up to 400); TSTRB 1 to 15. Forced: T = 1, 2, 3, 256 and 65535 on the stalled stream, T = 0 and 65535 on the fed stream, every non-zero TSTRB value at least once, every TVALID delay at least once. `reseed = 3` samples different T, beat patterns, bytes and pulse positions. The COUNT_GOOD saturation fills on a merge of regressions: one regression of three seeds runs no loop with a probability of about 30 %.

*Objective*: A DATA read consumes the prefetched word or assembles four valid bytes from the stream in beat and lane order, and bytes beyond four are not carried. CFG.PREFETCH (reset 0) holds one word that only DATA consumes and that PREFETCH_DATA mirrors. CFG.TIMEOUT (reset 256, 0 disables) times a stalled active read out with SLVERR and zero data. A stream error (TVALID high with TREADY low, then TVALID low) sets STREAM_ERR, counts COUNT_BAD and pulses the KMCSR DRBG error even when no read is active, and outranks a timeout. The counters saturate and clear on a non-zero write. Writes to DATA and PREFETCH_DATA return SLVERR. Spec: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, sections "DRBG Sampler", "Reads, prefetch and register access", "Errors and reset"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_drbg_sampler.adoc`, sections "DRBG Sampler", "CFG", "STATUS", "PREFETCH_DATA"; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, section "Resetting the TRNG".

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_km_drbg_sampler_timeout_reset_test`. The open test runs one stalled read at TIMEOUT 40 and grades SLVERR, data 0, TIMEOUT_ERR, COUNT_BAD + 1, DRBG_ERR and the exact cycle count, the read with CFG.TIMEOUT 0 that never times out, the CFG, STATUS and DRBG IRQ_STATUS reset values, and a fed read with COUNT_GOOD = 1. It does not run TIMEOUT 256 and reads 256 only as the CFG reset value. The outcome at the drawn T and at T = 256 has the same fields as the open check and is a control here. Only the new cells are graded: T = 1, 2, 3 and 65535, the counters, prefetch and the write refusals, and, with `HK-STRM`, the stream-error cases. Byte packing, the timeout after a partial beat and the handshake controls need `HK-STRM-PAT` and are blocked. The open owner keeps the rest.

* Stalled stream: T = 1, 2, 3, 65535, a drawn T and the reset value 256, each with one DATA read; the fed stream as the twin.
* COUNT_BAD saturation on every seed, and COUNT_GOOD saturation in a leaf whose seed draws it, with non-zero and zero writes.
* Prefetch on with a stalled stream, a held word, a fresh read, and prefetch off.
* Writes to DATA and PREFETCH_DATA.
* A TRNG reset while the KM is held in software reset.
* With `HK-STRM`: stream errors with no read active, at the first active cycle of a read at timeout 256, 1 and 2 (graded when TREADY is low in that cycle). With `HK-STRM-PAT` (blocked): beats with every non-zero TSTRB value and TVALID delays of 0, 1, 2 and 5 cycles; a timeout during byte assembly after one partial beat; TVALID pulses before the expiry with TREADY high and a drop after a handshake.

Error cases: a stalled stream (starvation); TVALID withdrawal with TREADY low.

Functional coverage: `sep_km_drbg_sampler_cg` (`cp_smp_read_service`, `cp_smp_tvalid_arrival`, `cp_smp_strb`, `cp_smp_freshness`, `cp_smp_prefetch_mode`, `cp_smp_prefetch_mirror`, `cp_smp_count_good`, `cp_smp_count_bad`, `cp_smp_reset_defaults`, `cp_smp_ready`) and `sep_km_drbg_fault_cg` (`cp_df_timeout_value`, `cp_df_entry_state`, `cp_df_timeout_outcome`, `cp_df_stream_violation`, `cp_df_offset_race`, `cp_df_status_clear`, `cp_df_trng_reset`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Print `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for every drawn value. Bring up `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped and the entropy noise forced. Hold the entropy chain at its reset (the host does not run the entropy initialization), so the stream is stalled. Boot the POKE image.
. The image reads CFG (control: `prefetch` 0, `timeout` 256, graded by the open owner) and STATUS (control: all fields 0), and reads IRQ_STATUS.
. Timeout legs on the stalled stream, for T = 1, 2, 3, 65535, the drawn T and 256, in a drawn order. The image writes CFG with `prefetch` 0 and `timeout` T and reads CFG back. It writes 1 to IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR and to STATUS.TIMEOUT_ERR, and reads both as 0 (the control). It reads STATUS (for COUNT_BAD) and issues one DATA read. A bounded wait for the read to end, longer than T clocks (the bound is a test parameter); a timeout of the leaf fails the leaf and is not a verdict about the contract. It then reads STATUS and IRQ_STATUS and reports `slv`, `dec`, the data, STATUS and IRQ_STATUS. It writes STATUS with every field 0 and reads it, then writes STATUS with TIMEOUT_ERR 1 and every other field 0 and reads it, and writes 1 to IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR. Log the number of clocks that `km_entropy_tready_o` stays high.
. Writes (error injection; point: write to DATA and to PREFETCH_DATA; kind: write to a read-only register; expected response: SLVERR). The image writes 1 to IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR and reads it as 0 (the control). It writes a word to DATA and reads IRQ_STATUS and `slv`; it writes 1 to IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR; it writes a word to PREFETCH_DATA and reads IRQ_STATUS and `slv`.
. Stalled prefetch. First the control: the image writes CFG with `prefetch` 0 and `timeout` 8, writes 1 to IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR and to STATUS.TIMEOUT_ERR, and issues one DATA read on the stalled stream. It reports `slv`, the data, STATUS and IRQ_STATUS (SLVERR, data 0, TIMEOUT_ERR = 1), writes 1 to STATUS.TIMEOUT_ERR and IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR, and reads both as 0. Then the image writes CFG with `prefetch` 1 and `timeout` 8 and reads STATUS before and after a window of 1024 clocks (a test parameter) with no DATA read. It then writes CFG with `prefetch` 0.
. Counter clear and saturation, bad side. The image reads STATUS (COUNT_BAD is non-zero from step 3 and below 0xFF), writes STATUS with the COUNT_BAD field non-zero and every other field 0, and reads STATUS (COUNT_BAD reads 0). It writes CFG with `prefetch` 0 and `timeout` 1, writes 1 to the sticky bits, and reads STATUS. It runs a read loop of 258 DATA reads on the stalled stream (a count above the 255 that reach saturation; a test parameter) and reads STATUS. It runs 2 further reads and reads STATUS. It writes 0 to STATUS (no field cleared) and reads it. It writes STATUS with the COUNT_BAD field non-zero and every other field 0, and reads it.
. TRNG reset. The host writes SW_RESET_N with bit 0 low and the other bits at 1 (the KM is held in software reset). With the KM held, the host runs the complete entropy initialization sequence (the bounded entropy-source startup poll and the acknowledged EDN Instantiate command) and waits (bounded) for the entropy chain to produce; a timeout fails the leaf. The leaf samples `km_entropy_tvalid_o` (it reads 1) and `km_entropy_tready_o` (it reads 0): the producer offers a beat while the sampler, held in reset, does not request one. This sample is the control of CHK-SMP-TRNG. The host writes SW_RESET_N with bit 5 (`trng_sw_rst_n`) low, then high, and bit 0 stays low. The host runs the complete entropy initialization sequence again and waits (bounded) for the chain to produce; a timeout fails the leaf. The host writes SW_RESET_N with bit 0 high. The POKE image boots again with the sampler at its reset defaults.
. The first DATA read of the new boot: the image writes the sticky bits as 1, reads CFG and STATUS, and issues one DATA read. It reports `slv`, the data, STATUS and IRQ_STATUS.
. Fed twin. For T = 0 and T = 65535 (a budget that a live stream does not exceed) the image writes CFG with `prefetch` 0 and `timeout` T and issues two DATA reads. It reports `slv`, the data of both reads, STATUS (`drbg_ready`, `count_good`) and IRQ_STATUS. The leaf counts the handshakes at the sink from the end of the first DATA read to the end of the second (CHK-SMP-PRE).
. Counter clear. After the fed reads, with COUNT_GOOD non-zero, the image writes 0 to STATUS (no field cleared) and reads it, and writes STATUS with the COUNT_GOOD field non-zero and every other field 0, and reads it.
. Prefetch with the live stream. The image writes CFG with `prefetch` 1 and `timeout` 256. A bounded wait for STATUS.PREFETCHED = 1; a timeout fails the check. It reads PREFETCH_DATA three times. The leaf waits (bounded; a timeout fails the check) until `km_entropy_tvalid_o` reads 1, and the image then reads STATUS (`drbg_ready`, CHK-SMP-PRE). It issues one DATA read and reads STATUS (`count_good` against the value before). The leaf counts the handshakes at the sink (`km_entropy_tvalid_o` and `km_entropy_tready_o` high in one clock) from the DATA read to the next STATUS.PREFETCHED = 1. A bounded wait for STATUS.PREFETCHED = 1 again; it reads PREFETCH_DATA. It writes CFG with `prefetch` 0 and reads STATUS and PREFETCH_DATA.
. Counter saturation, good side, only in a leaf whose seed draws it (`DRAW good_sat=<0/1>`). The image writes CFG with `prefetch` 0 and `timeout` 0, and writes STATUS with the COUNT_GOOD field non-zero and every other field 0 (the counter reads 0). It runs a read loop of 65540 DATA reads on the live stream (a count above the 65535 that reach saturation; a test parameter) and reads STATUS. It runs 2 further reads and reads STATUS. It writes 0 to STATUS and reads it; it writes STATUS with the COUNT_GOOD field non-zero and every other field 0, and reads it.
. With `HK-STRM-PAT`, byte assembly. This step is blocked: the leaf skips it and logs `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-SMP-PACK reason=HK-STRM-PAT`. The image writes CFG with `prefetch` 0 and `timeout` drawn from 256 and 0. For each drawn pattern, `HK-STRM-PAT` replaces the live stream at the KM sink for the read and releases it after the read. The hook offers the beats of the pattern, each after its drawn TVALID delay counted from the first cycle in which `km_entropy_tready_o` is high. The image issues one DATA read. The leaf samples `km_entropy_tvalid_o` and `km_entropy_tready_o` and counts the handshakes. The image then issues a second read with a new pattern. The patterns include every non-zero TSTRB value, one beat that supplies more than the four needed bytes, and the delays 0, 1, 2 and 5.
. With `HK-STRM-PAT`, timeout after a partial beat (point: TVALID and TSTRB at the KM sink; stimulus: a legal partial beat followed by a stalled stream; expected response: SLVERR with data 0, TIMEOUT_ERR, COUNT_BAD + 1 and DRBG_ERR, and no STREAM_ERR). This step is blocked: the leaf skips it and logs `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-SMP-TO-ASM reason=HK-STRM-PAT`. The image writes CFG with `timeout` T drawn in [4, 20] and `prefetch` 0. During one active DATA read the hook offers one beat with TSTRB 0x1 and then holds TVALID low. A bounded wait for the read to end. The image reports `slv`, the data, STATUS and IRQ_STATUS.
. With `HK-STRM`, stream errors (error injection; point: TVALID at the KM sink; kind: TVALID withdrawal with TREADY low; expected response: STREAM_ERR and DRBG_ERR; COUNT_BAD + 1 with no read active; SLVERR with data 0 and COUNT_BAD + 1 or more for an active read). Read `km_entropy_tready_o` first. Case a: no read active and `prefetch` 0; the hook drives TVALID high for one cycle in a cycle with TREADY low, then low. Case b: with `timeout` 256, one DATA read; the hook drives TVALID high in the first active cycle of the read for one cycle, then holds TVALID low until the read ends. No beat reaches the sampler during the read, so the read ends through the stream error or through the timeout, and each of the two completes the read with SLVERR and data 0 (`architecture.adoc`, "Errors and reset" and table "Key Manager DRBG sampler states"). A bounded wait for the read to end, longer than the timeout; a timeout of the leaf fails the leaf. Case c: as b with `timeout` 1. Case d: as b with `timeout` 2. The leaf classifies each pulse from `km_entropy_tready_o` in the cycle of the pulse: TREADY low is a withdrawal. The request state asserts TREADY (`architecture.adoc`, table "Key Manager DRBG sampler states") and the specification does not state TREADY in the first active cycle, so a case b, c or d pulse with TREADY high is a handshake: the leaf logs `OBS-RACE-MISSED case=<b/c/d> tready=1` and does not grade that case. After each case the image reports STATUS, IRQ_STATUS, `slv` and the data, writes STATUS with every field 0 and reads it, writes STATUS with STREAM_ERR 1 and every other field 0 and reads it, and writes 1 to the other sticky bits.
. With `HK-STRM-PAT`, handshake controls. This step is blocked: the leaf skips it and logs `OBS-BLOCKED CHK-SMP-STRM-CTRL reason=HK-STRM-PAT`. With `timeout` 8 and a stalled stream, the image issues three separate DATA reads. In read k (k = 4, 3, 2; test parameters) the hook drives one TVALID pulse of one cycle with TSTRB 0x1 at the active cycle that lies k cycles before the expiry, in a cycle with TREADY high. The image reports STATUS and the read outcome after each read. In a further read the hook offers a complete beat and drops TVALID in the next cycle after the handshake.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SMP-TO
|A stalled active DATA read at CFG.TIMEOUT = T ends in a timeout: SLVERR (`slv` = 1, `dec` = 0), data 0, STATUS.TIMEOUT_ERR = 1, STATUS.STREAM_ERR = 0, COUNT_BAD + 1 and IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR = 1 (`architecture.adoc`, "Errors and reset": a timeout completes an active read with SLVERR and zero data; `km_drbg_sampler.adoc`, "STATUS"). The CFG readback equals the written value before the read. STATUS.DRBG_READY reads 0 in the STATUS read after the read, because the entropy chain is held at its reset and TVALID is 0 (`km_drbg_sampler.adoc`, "STATUS": DRBG_READY is TVALID from the DRBG). After the timeout a write of STATUS with every field 0 leaves TIMEOUT_ERR at 1, and a write with TIMEOUT_ERR 1 and every other field 0 clears it (`km_drbg_sampler.adoc`, "STATUS": TIMEOUT_ERR is write one to clear; a write of 0 to a counter field has no effect). A DUT that returns data or OKAY, never ends the read, sets STREAM_ERR, misses a count or a status bit, shows DRBG_READY on a stalled stream, or clears TIMEOUT_ERR on a write of 0 or keeps it after a write of 1 fails. It is graded at T = 1, 2, 3 and 65535. The drawn T and T = 256 log `control=1`, because the open owner grades the same fields. The cycle count of the timeout is logged and not graded. Anchor: the POKE result, the STATUS readback and the IRQ_STATUS readback. Control: the fed twin of step 9, at T = 0 and T = 65535, completes with `slv` = 0 and COUNT_GOOD + 1, so the timeout of the stalled legs comes from the stalled stream and not from the register path; and the bits read 0 after each clear. Evidence: `CHK-SMP-TO PASS seed=S t=3 cfg_rd=0x00030000 slv=1 dec=0 data=0 status_lo4=0x4 count_bad_delta=1 drbg_err=1 drbg_ready=0 timeout_err_w0=1 timeout_err_w1c=0`.
|CHK-SMP-CNT
|COUNT_BAD saturates at 0xFF through stalled reads and stays at 0xFF after two more, and COUNT_GOOD saturates at 0xFFFF through fed reads and stays at 0xFFFF after two more (`km_drbg_sampler.adoc`, "STATUS"). A write of 0 to either field leaves it unchanged. A write of a non-zero value clears it to 0, from a non-saturated value and from the saturated value: COUNT_BAD is cleared from a small non-zero value at the start of step 6 and from 0xFF at its end, and COUNT_GOOD is cleared from a non-saturated value in step 10 and from 0xFFFF in step 12. A DUT whose counter wraps, whose zero write clears, or whose non-zero write does not clear fails. The read loop of COUNT_GOOD runs only in a leaf whose seed draws it; a leaf that does not draw it logs `good_sat=skipped` and does not count the COUNT_GOOD saturation leg. Anchor: the STATUS readback. Control: each counter is non-zero before each clear, and the two further reads after saturation are the twin of the saturating reads. Evidence: `CHK-SMP-CNT PASS bad_sat=0xFF bad_after_extra=0xFF good_sat=0xFFFF good_after_extra=0xFFFF zero_write=kept nonzero_write_clear=0 bad_clear_low=0 bad_low_before=<n> loop_reads=258,65540`.
|CHK-SMP-PRE
|With the stream stalled and CFG.PREFETCH = 1, no word is held and no timeout sets over a window that is 128 times the budget (`timeout` 8, window 1024 clocks): STATUS.PREFETCHED = 0, STATUS.TIMEOUT_ERR = 0 and COUNT_BAD is unchanged (`architecture.adoc`, "Reads, prefetch and register access": background prefetch has no timeout). With the live stream a word is held: STATUS.PREFETCHED = 1, three PREFETCH_DATA reads return the same value, and a DATA read returns that value, leaves COUNT_GOOD unchanged (prefetched words are not counted), and consumes the word, so that the sampler takes a new word from the stream: at least one handshake (`km_entropy_tvalid_o` and `km_entropy_tready_o` high in one clock) falls between the DATA read and the next STATUS.PREFETCHED = 1 (`architecture.adoc`, "Reads, prefetch and register access": consuming the word through DATA invalidates it; `firmware.adoc`, "DRBG Driver": only DATA consumes a prefetched word and starts the next fetch). Whether the next held word equals the consumed word is logged and not graded, because the stream can repeat a word. With CFG.PREFETCH = 0 and a word held, STATUS.PREFETCHED reads 0 and PREFETCH_DATA reads 0 (`km_drbg_sampler.adoc`, "PREFETCH_DATA": cleared when PREFETCH = 0). A DUT that times out a prefetch, changes the held word on a debug read, does not consume the word, counts the prefetched word, or keeps the word after the disable fails. Anchor: the POKE readback and STATUS; `KM-ENTROPY` for the handshake count. Control: the stalled active DATA read at `timeout` 8 with `prefetch` 0, run in step 5 just before the window, ends with SLVERR, data 0 and TIMEOUT_ERR = 1, so a timeout can show on this stream at this budget. In the fed twin of step 9, at least one handshake falls between the end of the first DATA read and the end of the second DATA read of each T, because a DATA read with no word held takes its bytes from the stream (`architecture.adoc`, "Reads, prefetch and register access"). With the live stream, CFG.PREFETCH = 1 and STATUS.PREFETCHED = 1, STATUS.DRBG_READY reads 1 once `km_entropy_tvalid_o` reads 1: the sampler holds its word and keeps TREADY low, so TVALID stays high until a handshake (`km_drbg_sampler.adoc`, "STATUS": DRBG_READY is TVALID from the DRBG; `architecture.adoc`, "Errors and reset": a TVALID drop before the handshake is a stream error). Evidence: `CHK-SMP-PRE PASS control_active_timeout_err=1 stalled_window_clks=1024 stalled_prefetched=0 stalled_timeout_err=0 prefetched=1 pdata_reads=3 pdata_stable=1 data_eq_held=1 good_delta=0 refill_handshakes=<n> next_held_differs=<0/1> disable_prefetched=0 disable_pdata=0x0 fed_handshakes=<f0>,<f1> held_drbg_ready=1`, with n, f0 and f1 at least 1 and `next_held_differs` logged.
|CHK-SMP-WR
|A write to DATA and a write to PREFETCH_DATA each answer SLVERR (`km_drbg_sampler.adoc`, "DRBG Sampler" register table: writes return SLVERR; `architecture.adoc`, "DRBG sampler register behavior"). A DUT that answers OKAY fails. Anchor: the POKE result (`slv`) and IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR after a clear that read 0. Control: IRQ_STATUS.AXI_SLVERR reads 0 before the first write and again after the clear that precedes the second write, so each write shows its own SLVERR. Evidence: `CHK-SMP-WR PASS data_wr_slv=1 pdata_wr_slv=1 axi_slverr=1,1 clear_before=0,0`.
|CHK-SMP-TRNG
|The first DATA read after a TRNG reset that runs while the KM is held in software reset completes without error: `slv` = 0, STATUS.STREAM_ERR = 0, COUNT_BAD = 0 and IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR = 0 (`reset_controller.adoc`, "Resetting the TRNG": holding the KM in software reset is sufficient; `architecture.adoc`, "Errors and reset": holding the KM in software reset clears the sampler control state). A DUT that flags a stream error, or returns SLVERR on the first read, fails. The TRNG is never reset while the KM runs. Anchor: the POKE result and STATUS. Control: before the reset pulse the leaf samples `km_entropy_tvalid_o` = 1 and `km_entropy_tready_o` = 0 with the KM held, so the pulse takes the producer from offering a beat to reset while the sampler does not accept it, which is the case that the reset controller names as the hazard; the sampled values are logged. The fed twin of step 9 on the same boot completes the same way. Evidence: `CHK-SMP-TRNG PASS tvalid_before=1 tready_before=0 first_read_ok=1 stream_err=0 count_bad=0 drbg_err=0`.
|CHK-SMP-TO-ASM
|A read that has received one partial beat (TSTRB 0x1) and then sees a stalled stream ends in a timeout after the budget: `slv` = 1, data 0, STATUS.TIMEOUT_ERR = 1, STATUS.STREAM_ERR = 0, COUNT_BAD + 1 and IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR = 1 (`architecture.adoc`, "DRBG Sampler": a timeout completes an active read with SLVERR and zero data; the assembly state keeps TREADY asserted, so the end of the stream is not a withdrawal). A DUT that does not end the read, or that sets STREAM_ERR, fails. Anchor: the POKE result and STATUS. Stimulus: `HK-STRM-PAT`. Blocked: needs the PROPOSED drive `HK-STRM-PAT`. It logs an OBS line and not CHK PASS, and no cell is counted as reached. Control: CHK-SMP-TO at the same kind of T, and the withdrawals of CHK-SMP-STRM in the same leaf set STREAM_ERR, so the bit can set. Evidence: `CHK-SMP-TO-ASM PASS t=8 partial_strb=0x1 slv=1 data=0 timeout_err=1 stream_err=0 count_bad_delta=1 drbg_err=1`.
|CHK-SMP-PACK
|The read returns a word that holds the four valid bytes of the model in beat order, lanes 0 to 3 in each beat, stopping at four bytes, with one consistent orientation (the first valid byte at the lowest byte position on every read, or at the highest byte position on every read); the leaf logs which orientation it sees and does not claim the position (`architecture.adoc` states the beat order and not the position in the word); the bytes of the last beat beyond four are dropped and do not appear in the next read; the number of handshakes at the sink equals the number of beats that the model needs (`architecture.adoc`, "Reads, prefetch and register access"). A DUT with a wrong byte order, an orientation that differs between reads, a carried byte, or an extra or missing handshake fails. Anchor: the POKE result, and `km_entropy_tvalid_o` and `km_entropy_tready_o` for the handshake count. Blocked: needs the PROPOSED drive `HK-STRM-PAT`. It logs an OBS line and not CHK PASS, and no cell is counted as reached. Control: the second read of each pair uses a new pattern, so a carried byte shows in its word. Evidence: `CHK-SMP-PACK PASS read=1 strb=0x3,0xC word=0x<..> orientation=<first_byte_low\|first_byte_high> orientation_consistent=1 handshakes=2 carry=0 delay=1`.
|CHK-SMP-STRM
|A withdrawal (TVALID high with TREADY low, then TVALID low) sets STREAM_ERR and raises IRQ_STATUS.DRBG_ERR, with no read active (case a) and in an active read whose first active cycle has TREADY low (cases b, c and d) (`architecture.adoc`, "Errors and reset": the monitor sets STREAM_ERR, increments COUNT_BAD and pulses the DRBG error even when no read is active). In case a COUNT_BAD rises by exactly 1, because no read is active and no timeout can count. In cases b, c and d COUNT_BAD rises by 1 or more and the exact delta is logged: the TREADY-low cycle of the withdrawal lies before the request state, and the specification does not state whether the read then ends through the stream error (one count) or through the timeout (a second count) (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The hook holds TVALID low until the read ends, so both paths complete the active read with SLVERR and data 0, and that outcome is graded. After each case a write of STATUS with every field 0 leaves STREAM_ERR at 1, and a write with STREAM_ERR 1 and every other field 0 clears it (`km_drbg_sampler.adoc`, "STATUS": STREAM_ERR is write one to clear). In case c the withdrawal and the timeout expiry fall in the same cycle, and the stream error outranks the timeout (`architecture.adoc`, "DRBG Sampler": a stream error takes priority over a timeout): STREAM_ERR, SLVERR with data 0 and COUNT_BAD + 1 or more are graded, and TIMEOUT_ERR and the exact COUNT_BAD delta are logged because the specification does not state them for that cycle. A DUT that does not flag the withdrawal, does not count it, or answers the active read with OKAY or with data, fails. The leaf counts the true withdrawals (TREADY low at the withdrawal) and grades only those cases; a case b, c or d pulse with TREADY high logs `OBS-RACE-MISSED` and is not graded. Case a picks a cycle with TREADY low, so every leaf has at least one true withdrawal, and a leaf with none fails `CHK-NONVAC`. Stimulus: `HK-STRM`. Anchor: STATUS, IRQ_STATUS and the POKE result. Control: in the same leaf the stalled reads of CHK-SMP-TO end in a timeout with STREAM_ERR = 0, and the first read of CHK-SMP-TRNG completes with STREAM_ERR = 0, so the bit does not set without a withdrawal. Evidence: `CHK-SMP-STRM PASS case=first_active_t256 stream_err=1 drbg_err=1 slv=1 data=0 count_bad_delta=<n> timeout_err_logged=<0/1> stream_err_w0=1 stream_err_w1c=0`, with n at least 1 (exactly 1 for case a), and one line for each of the other cases.
|CHK-SMP-STRM-CTRL
|A TVALID drop after a completed handshake sets no STREAM_ERR, and a one-cycle TVALID pulse with TREADY high 4, 3 and 2 cycles before the expiry (`timeout` 8) is a handshake and sets no STREAM_ERR (`architecture.adoc`, "Errors and reset": a TVALID drop after a completed handshake is not the violation; `architecture.adoc`, table "Key Manager DRBG sampler states": the request and assembly states keep TREADY asserted). The read outcome of the pulse reads is logged. A DUT that flags a handshake as a withdrawal fails. Stimulus: `HK-STRM-PAT`. Blocked: needs the PROPOSED drive `HK-STRM-PAT`. It logs an OBS line and not CHK PASS, and no cell is counted as reached. Anchor: STATUS and `km_entropy_tready_o`. Control: the withdrawals of CHK-SMP-STRM in the same leaf set STREAM_ERR, so the bit can set. Evidence: `CHK-SMP-STRM-CTRL PASS pulses=3 before_expiry=4,3,2 tready_high=3 stream_err=0 handshake_then_drop_stream_err=0`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker that is not blocked PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0. CHK-SMP-PACK, CHK-SMP-TO-ASM and CHK-SMP-STRM-CTRL are blocked (they need `HK-STRM-PAT`): the leaf logs `OBS-BLOCKED <check> reason=HK-STRM-PAT` for each and does not count them.

*Scope limit*: The test does not claim the behavior of a beat with TSTRB 0x0, the TIMEOUT_ERR value and the exact COUNT_BAD delta of a read that sees a withdrawal in its first active cycle (cases b, c and d; including the coincident cycle of case c; not stated; see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>), the exact length of the timeout in cycles (the leaf logs it), the placement of the packed bytes in the word beyond the beat-order rule of the text, or a TRNG reset while the KM runs (the host keeps the KM idle before a TRNG reset, the hardware does not enforce it, and the outcome is not stated; see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The open test `sep_km_drbg_sampler_timeout_reset_test` keeps the cells that it grades.

'''''

[[sep_km_otp_interface_rand_test]]
===== 7.35 `sep_km_otp_interface_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle POKE with real fuse sense and `HK-OTP` for the integrity legs, 3 seeds, VCS only. Testlist: groups `all` and `cpu_stub` in `hw/sys/sep/dv/testlists/km.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_km_otp_interface_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: The POKE image reads and writes the KMCSR OTP registers through the KM bus and reports each result in KM SRAM words 0 to 97. The host LSU master (ocah_axi_vip) writes the LCC DEMOTE registers and the eFuse shadow registers, and writes SW_RESET_N for a KM warm reset. The test builds the fuse image and holds the golden for every read word: the staged field, its bitwise inverse for the complement word, and the dual-rail encoding of the staged life-cycle state. The secure test strap is the `test_en_strap_i` input. Approved: `KM-RESULT` (POKE readback against the staged image), `OTP-TAP` (`secure_tm_o`, `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`), `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (the `sep_reset_n` release), `HOST-MBX` and the host life-cycle path (DEMOTE writes, the `REQUIRED_ALGS` and `REQUIRED_SIGNERS` shadow writes). Added hook: the error inject `HK-OTP` (CHK-OTP-SIG, CHK-OTP-CHG), which the test adds to the inject exceptions of `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`. `HK-OTP` flips one bit on one rail of the KM OTP input; a two-rail flip uses it on both rails at the same bit position. It never serves as a golden.

*Randomization*: Two classes. Class one, RANDCFG, for the readout leg: `SepSeededRng` draws the fuse image words (seven 256-bit fields, a set bit in every word, pairwise different fields), the life-cycle state of the image (TEST_DEV or PROD, equally likely), and the lock mask A. `hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/dv_sim_prestage.py` stages the image, the life-cycle state included, at t=0 from the seed of the leaf, one image per leaf, so the two life-cycle states fill on a merge of regressions: one regression of three seeds misses a state with a probability of 25 %. Legal ranges: non-zero words; SIP_DIS and SYS_DIS keep their fixed values; A has 1 to 8 bits over bits [8:0]. The lock rounds use the mask as a fixed schedule (~ is the complement over bits [8:0]): round 1 writes m_w = A to OTP_READ_LOCK, reads the fields, and then adds m_c = ~A in OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD; round 2 writes m_c = A to OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD, reads the fields, and then adds m_w = ~A in OTP_READ_LOCK, and then m_w = m_c = 0x1FF. So on every seed, in two boots, every field reads with no lock, warm lock only, cold lock only and both locks, and each read under one register alone holds fields inside and outside its mask. Class two, RAND-REP, for the integrity and change leg: `SepSeededRng` draws the field, the word, the bit, the rail and which demotion register is written first. Legal ranges: the seven 256-bit fields with words 0 to 7 and bits 0 to 31; OTP_LIFE_CYCLE bits 0 to 3 of the nibble on the chosen rail; OTP_DEMOTION_STATE bits 0 to 3; fields under an OTP_READ_LOCK bit are not injected; the secure test strap is 0. `reseed = 3` samples different images, life-cycle states, masks, flip points and demotion order. The 18 single-rail points (nine fields on each rail), the nine two-rail flips and the strap pair are the same on every seed.

*Objective*: The KMCSR exposes the life-cycle state, the demotion state and the seven 256-bit fields as read-only dual-rail words, and each complement word is the bitwise inverse of its value word. OTP_DEMOTION_STATE holds two flags, each in a complementary 2-bit group. A set bit in OTP_READ_LOCK or OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD makes the matching field read zero in its value and complement words. In secure test mode the four secret fields read zero in the value words and all ones in the complement words with the integrity check quiet, and the three public identities keep their values. A value word that is not the bitwise inverse of its complement word sets the sticky IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT. A change of a monitored field with both rails consistent sets its OTP_CHANGE_STATUS bit and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE (sticky, write one to clear), and the change is suppressed until the capture history is valid after a reset. A write to another fuse shadow word, such as `REQUIRED_ALGS` or `REQUIRED_SIGNERS`, leaves the readout words, OTP_CHANGE_STATUS, OTP_SIGINT and IRQ_STATUS unchanged, because the KM receives only the four secrets, the three public identities, the life-cycle state and the demotion state. Spec: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, sections "Scan and Scan-Dump Protection", "Errors and reset", "OTP Inputs and Readout"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc`, sections "OTP_LIFE_CYCLE" through "OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD", "IRQ_STATUS", "OTP_CHANGE_STATUS"; `hw/sys/sep/doc/otp_fuse_controller.adoc`, sections "Fuse Fields", "RTL Integration"; `hw/sys/sep/doc/lifecycle_controller.adoc`, section "In-Field Debug Demotion"; `hw/sys/sep/doc/test_mode.adoc`, sections "Entry and Exit", "Protection While Active".

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_efuse_km_public_id_test`. `sep_efuse_km_public_id_test` grades the three public identity words, their zero read under OTP_READ_LOCK and the eFuse per-field lock.
