Those cells run here as controls. The Phase 3 test `sep_efuse_secure_tm_matrix_rand_test` grades the masking of the secret fields at the sensed-shadow output only. Only the new cells are graded: the KMCSR readout of the other six fields, the Key Manager view of the four secret fields in secure test mode (zero value words and all-ones complement words), the cold-lock cells of the three public identities, the quiet integrity check under the strap, the demotion change and its suppression, and, with `HK-OTP`, the single-rail and two-rail flips. The open owner keeps the rest, and the Phase 3 test keeps the masking at the sensed-shadow output. The ROM response to OTP_SIGINT (-13) belongs to `sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`.

* A drawn fuse image: seven 256-bit fields with a set bit in every word, pairwise different, with a drawn life-cycle state TEST_DEV or PROD. The reads start after the fuse sense completes. The nine fields are life cycle, demotion, CHIPLET_UID, SIP_UID, SYS_UID, CLASS_KEY, SEP_CHIPLET_ID, SEP_SIP_ID and SEP_SYS_ID.
* Lock masks in two rounds: OTP_READ_LOCK = A alone, then OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD = ~A added; OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD = A alone, then OTP_READ_LOCK = ~A added, followed by m_w = m_c = 0x1FF, with three identical writes of each register. Every field under each of the four lock states (none, warm only, cold only, both) on every seed. The handover secret-field mask 0x03C (a control).
* A pair of boots with the secure test strap 0 and 1, set before the cold reset and held.
* LCC DEMOTE_1 and DEMOTE_2 written well after boot, one before a KM warm reset (PARK) and one after it. A SEP cold reset that clears both. Host shadow writes of `REQUIRED_ALGS` and `REQUIRED_SIGNERS` with the HMAC and AES key_valid set. Both fields have access RW in the register description and are software-writable in the field table. No host write goes to a field with access R, such as `BL1_VERSION` or a token digest, because their write class is a Blocked specification question (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>).
* With `HK-OTP`: single-rail flips on each of the nine fields, on the value rail and on the complement rail; two-rail flips on each field and one window in which 2 to 4 fields change together; a two-rail flip well after the boot with both demotions held.

Error cases: a single-rail bit flip (illegal) and a two-rail flip (a legal value change) at the KM OTP input.

Functional coverage: `sep_km_secret_nonexposure_cg` (`cp_nx_epoch_secret`), `sep_km_otp_readout_cg` (`cp_otp_field`, `cp_otp_word_index`, `cp_otp_read_through`, `cp_otp_change`, `cp_otp_lock_matrix`, `cp_otp_lock_reg`, `cp_otp_secure_tm`) and `sep_km_integrity_fault_cg` (`cp_if_otp_dual_rail`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Print `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for every drawn value. Build the fuse image and the golden words. Log the drawn life-cycle state as `LC lc=<TEST_DEV/PROD>`. Draw A and derive the masks of the two rounds. Set `test_en_strap_i` to 0.
. Boot 1. Apply a cold reset and sense the staged image for real. A bounded wait for `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`; a timeout fails the leaf. Then wait (bounded; a timeout fails the leaf) for the `sep_reset_n` release on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o`, which comes after the sense done (`reset_controller.adoc`, SEP Reset Controller: `sep_reset_no` resets the Reset Controller register block). Every later boot of this procedure waits for the same two events before it releases the KM. Release the KM with the POKE image. Sample `secure_tm_o`; it reads 0.
. The image reads all 114 readout words (the nine fields: 8 value words and 8 complement words for each of the seven 256-bit fields, OTP_LIFE_CYCLE and OTP_DEMOTION_STATE), the two lock registers, OTP_CHANGE_STATUS and IRQ_STATUS. Record the two demotion groups as the first-boot record.
. Round 1 (disjoint masks). The image writes m_w = A to OTP_READ_LOCK three times, reads it after each write, and reads all 114 readout words (warm lock alone). It writes m_c = ~A to OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD three times, reads it after each write, and reads all 114 readout words. It writes 0 to each lock register, reads both registers, and reads all 114 readout words again.
. Boot 2. Apply a SEP cold reset with the strap at 0 and sense again. Release the KM with the POKE image. The image reads both lock registers (0, the control) and runs round 2 as in step 4 with the register order reversed: it writes m_c = A to OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD first and reads all 114 readout words (cold lock alone), then writes m_w = ~A to OTP_READ_LOCK and reads all 114 readout words, then writes 0 to each lock register, reads both registers and reads all 114 readout words. Then it writes 0x1FF to both registers, reads both registers and reads all 114 readout words again.
. Boot 3. Apply a SEP cold reset with the strap at 0 and sense again. Release the KM. The image writes 0x03C to OTP_READ_LOCK three times, reads it after each write, and reads all 114 readout words.
. Boot 4. Apply a SEP cold reset with the strap at 0 and sense again. Release the KM. The image reads OTP_CHANGE_STATUS and IRQ_STATUS over a quiet window of a test-chosen length (a test parameter, the same wherever this plan says "quiet window"); they read 0. Draw which demotion register is written first, D_a, and call the other D_b.
. With `HK-OTP`, run the 18 single-rail points (error injection; point: the KM OTP input; kind: single-rail bit flip, which breaks the dual-rail rule; expected response: IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT set and held): for each of the nine fields and each of the two rails, draw a word and a bit (or the nibble bit for OTP_LIFE_CYCLE, or the group bit for OTP_DEMOTION_STATE). Before the flip the image reads IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT, IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE and OTP_CHANGE_STATUS as 0 (the control). Flip the bit for 8 cycles (a test parameter). A bounded poll for IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT; a timeout fails the check. Read IRQ_STATUS after the hook releases, write 1 to OTP_SIGINT and read it, and write 1 to OTP_CHANGE and the OTP_CHANGE_STATUS bits and read them as 0.
. With `HK-OTP`, run the nine two-rail flips (error injection; point: the KM OTP input; kind: two-rail flip, a legal value change; expected response: the OTP_CHANGE_STATUS bit of the field and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE set, and no OTP_SIGINT): one for each field. Before each flip the image reads OTP_CHANGE_STATUS, IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT as 0 (the control). Flip value and complement at the same bit for 8 cycles. A bounded poll for the OTP_CHANGE_STATUS bit and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE; a timeout fails the check. Then read IRQ_STATUS and OTP_CHANGE_STATUS, write 1 to both, and read both as 0. Then run one window in which a drawn set of 2 to 4 fields flips together, with the same control reads before it and the same poll, readback and clear after it.
. The image reads OTP_DEMOTION_STATE (the pre-write record), OTP_CHANGE_STATUS and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE (both 0, the control). The host writes DEMOTE_a.demote = 1 well after boot (after a quiet window of the test-chosen length). A bounded poll for OTP_CHANGE_STATUS.demotion = 1 and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE = 1; a timeout fails the check. The image reads OTP_CHANGE_STATUS, IRQ_STATUS and OTP_DEMOTION_STATE. It writes 1 to OTP_CHANGE_STATUS.demotion and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE and reads both as 0.
. PARK. The host writes SW_RESET_N with bit 0 low and then high, and the other bits at 1. The POKE image boots again. After a quiet window of the test-chosen length the image reads OTP_CHANGE_STATUS and IRQ_STATUS.
. The image reads OTP_DEMOTION_STATE (the pre-write record), OTP_CHANGE_STATUS and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE (both 0, the control). The host writes DEMOTE_b.demote = 1 after a further quiet window. A bounded poll for OTP_CHANGE_STATUS.demotion = 1 and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE = 1; a timeout fails the check. The image reads OTP_CHANGE_STATUS, IRQ_STATUS and OTP_DEMOTION_STATE, writes 1 to the two change bits, and reads both as 0.
. With `HK-OTP`, with both demotions held and a quiet window after the PARK reboot, the image reads OTP_CHANGE_STATUS, IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT as 0 (the control). Flip one drawn field on both rails at the same bit for 8 cycles. A bounded poll for the OTP_CHANGE_STATUS bit; a timeout fails the check. The image reads OTP_CHANGE_STATUS, IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT, and clears the change bits.
. Boot 5. Apply a SEP cold reset with the strap at 0 and sense again. Release the KM. After a quiet window the image reads OTP_DEMOTION_STATE, OTP_CHANGE_STATUS and IRQ_STATUS.
. The image writes 1 to IRQ_SET.otp_sigint_set, reads IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT (1, the control), writes 1 to IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT and reads it (0). It records the 114 readout words, IRQ_STATUS and OTP_CHANGE_STATUS. The host writes, through the eFuse shadow registers, the staged value with one added bit to `REQUIRED_ALGS` (a used bit that the image stages at 0) and to `REQUIRED_SIGNERS` (one of bits 1:0 that the image stages at 0), and reads both shadow words back through the host path. The image keeps the write-lock bits of both fields at 0. After a quiet window the image reads the same registers again.
. Boot 6. Hold the SEP in cold reset, set `test_en_strap_i` to 1, release the reset with the strap held at 1 and sense again. A bounded wait for `sep_fuse_sense_done_o`. Sample `secure_tm_o`; it reads 1. Release the KM. The image writes 1 to IRQ_SET.otp_sigint_set, reads IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT (1, the control), writes 1 to IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT and reads it (0). The image then reads the 114 readout words, IRQ_STATUS and OTP_CHANGE_STATUS over a quiet window. The leaf ends with the strap held; the next cold reset ends the session.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-OTP-RD
|Each readout word of the six graded fields equals the model: a value word equals the staged field word, a complement word is the bitwise inverse of its value word, OTP_LIFE_CYCLE equals the dual-rail encoding `{~lc[3:0], lc[3:0]}` of the staged life-cycle state, and each of the two demotion groups of OTP_DEMOTION_STATE is a complementary pair (`architecture.adoc`, "OTP Inputs and Readout"; `km_csr.adoc`, "OTP_LIFE_CYCLE", "OTP_DEMOTION_STATE"). The six graded fields are the life cycle, the demotion state and the four secret fields. The three public identity fields are read and compared too, and log `control=1`. A DUT that returns another word, a complement that is not the inverse, a wrong encoding, or a non-complementary group fails. The polarity of the demotion groups is not stated, so only complementarity is graded. Anchor: the POKE readback against the staged image. Control: every staged word is non-zero, so a zero read is a lock or a mask and not a missing fuse. Evidence: `CHK-OTP-RD PASS graded_val=32 graded_cpl=32 lc=<TEST_DEV/PROD> dem_groups_cpl=2 control_public_val=24 control_public_cpl=24`.
|CHK-OTP-LOCK
|A field whose bit is in m_w or in m_c reads zero in its value and complement words, and a field outside both reads its staged words (`km_csr.adoc`, "OTP_READ_LOCK", "OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD": the effective lock is the OR of the two registers). A write of 0 to a lock register clears no bit, and three identical writes of a mask leave the mask on every readback. The warm-lock zero read of a public identity field is graded by the open owner and logs `control=1`; the zero read of a public identity field under OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD, alone or with OTP_READ_LOCK, is graded. The read under OTP_READ_LOCK = A alone (round 1) and the read under OTP_READ_LOCK_COLD = A alone (round 2) each hold fields inside and outside the mask, because A has 1 to 8 of the 9 bits, so each register is graded on a field outside its mask. Rounds 1 and 2 run on every seed, so every field is read with no lock, warm lock only, cold lock only and both locks. A DUT that locks a field outside the OR, leaves a locked field readable, or lets a write of 0 clear a bit fails. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: every field reads its staged words in the read of boot 1 before the first lock write (step 3). Evidence: `CHK-OTP-LOCK PASS round=<warm_only/disjoint/cold_only/swap/all> m_w=0x<m> m_c=0x<m> eff=0x<m> locked_zero=<n> unlocked_ok=<9-n> w0_kept=1 triple_write_reads=0x<m>,0x<m>,0x<m>`.
|CHK-OTP-HANDOVER-MASK
|Control. With OTP_READ_LOCK = 0x03C the four secret fields (bits 2 to 5) read zero in their value and complement words (`architecture.adoc`, "OTP Inputs and Readout", paragraph on UID provisioning and access: the lock sets the readout words of the field to zero), and the three public identity fields still read their staged words. The line carries `control=1`, because `CHK-OTP-LOCK` grades the zero read of every locked field with random masks and `sep_km_handover_rand_test` grades the lock bits that the ROM sets at the handover. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: the three public fields read their staged words in the same round, and the same fields read non-zero in boot 1. Evidence: `CHK-OTP-HANDOVER-MASK PASS control=1 m=0x03C secret_zero=4 control_public_ok=3`.
|CHK-OTP-TM
|In the secure test boot the four secret fields read zero in the value words and 0xFFFFFFFF in the complement words, IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT stays 0 over the quiet window (it is set and cleared through IRQ_SET.otp_sigint_set before the window, so the bit can set in this boot), and the three public identities, the life-cycle word and the demotion word keep their staged values (`test_mode.adoc`, "Protection While Active"; `architecture.adoc`, "OTP Inputs and Readout": the public identities remain on the KM port in secure test mode). A DUT that leaves a secret field readable, flags the all-ones complement words as an integrity error, or masks a public field fails. Anchor: the POKE readback and `secure_tm_o`. Control: in boot 1, with the strap at 0 and `secure_tm_o` at 0, the four secret fields read their staged words, and the IRQ_SET write of step 16 reads OTP_SIGINT as 1 and then 0 after its clear (`km_csr.adoc`, "IRQ_SET", bit 9). Evidence: `CHK-OTP-TM PASS tm=1 secret_val=0 secret_cpl=0xFFFFFFFF sigint_ctrl=1 sigint=0 public_ok=3 lc_dem_ok=1 twin_secret_staged=4`.
|CHK-OTP-DEM
|A write of DEMOTE_a.demote = 1 well after boot sets OTP_CHANGE_STATUS.demotion (bit 1) and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE. Exactly one demotion group of OTP_DEMOTION_STATE differs from its recorded value, and both groups stay complementary. The write of DEMOTE_b.demote = 1 does the same, and it changes the other group (`km_csr.adoc`, "OTP_CHANGE_STATUS"; `lifecycle_controller.adoc`, "In-Field Debug Demotion": the two controls are independent and either may be set first). After each write, a write of 1 to OTP_CHANGE_STATUS.demotion and to IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE clears both, and both read 0 (`km_csr.adoc`, "OTP_CHANGE_STATUS", "IRQ_STATUS": write one to clear). A DUT that sets no change bit, changes both groups or no group, breaks complementarity, or does not clear a change bit on a write of 1 fails. Which group belongs to which register is not stated, so the index of the changed group is logged. Anchor: the POKE readback against the pre-write record. Control: OTP_CHANGE_STATUS and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE read 0 immediately before each write, and the poll after the write is bounded, so a stale bit cannot satisfy the check. Evidence: `CHK-OTP-DEM PASS reg=DEMOTE_1 change_before=0 group=1 change_bit=1 irq=1 group_changed=1 group_cpl=1 w1c_after=0,0`, and one line for DEMOTE_2.
|CHK-OTP-SUPP
|After the KM warm reset with DEMOTE_a held, OTP_CHANGE_STATUS and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE read 0 after a quiet window: the persisting demotion state is not a change (`architecture.adoc`, "OTP Inputs and Readout": spurious change events are suppressed until the capture history is valid after reset). A DUT that reads the persisting state as a change fails. The length of the suppression window is not stated, and the boundary is not graded. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: the write of DEMOTE_b after the reboot is the twin and raises the change (the DEMOTE_b line of CHK-OTP-DEM), so the detector is armed after the reboot. Evidence: `CHK-OTP-SUPP PASS demote_held=1 after_reboot_change=0 after_reboot_irq=0 twin_change=1`.
|CHK-OTP-COLD
|After the SEP cold reset OTP_DEMOTION_STATE equals the first-boot record, and OTP_CHANGE_STATUS and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE read 0 (`lifecycle_controller.adoc`, "In-Field Debug Demotion": a SEP cold reset clears the demote and lock fields). A DUT that keeps a demotion across the cold reset, or reports a change after it, fails. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: the demotion words read a changed value before the cold reset (CHK-OTP-DEM). Evidence: `CHK-OTP-COLD PASS dem_equals_first_boot=1 change=0 irq_change=0`.
|CHK-OTP-NOTKM
|A host shadow write of `REQUIRED_ALGS` or of `REQUIRED_SIGNERS` (access RW in `sep_efuse_map.adoc`, software-writable in the `otp_fuse_controller.adoc` field table) changes none of the 114 KMCSR OTP words, sets no OTP_CHANGE_STATUS bit and no OTP_SIGINT, and leaves IRQ_STATUS unchanged (`architecture.adoc`, "OTP Inputs and Readout": the KM receives the four secrets, the three public identities, the life-cycle state and the demotion state). The host readback of each shadow word equals the staged value OR the added bit, so the write landed. A DUT that routes a fuse field other than the KM inputs into a KMCSR readout word, or sets a change or integrity bit for it, fails. Anchor: the POKE readback of the KM OTP words, OTP_CHANGE_STATUS and IRQ_STATUS, and the host shadow readback. Control: in the same leaf, before the host writes, IRQ_SET.otp_sigint_set sets IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT, which reads 1 and is cleared to 0 (`km_csr.adoc`, "IRQ_SET", bit 9), so the bit can set in this boot; the DEMOTE_a and DEMOTE_b writes of steps 10 and 12 do raise OTP_CHANGE. Evidence: `CHK-OTP-NOTKM PASS fuses=required_algs,required_signers landed=2 change=0 sigint_ctrl=1 sigint=0 words_same=114 irq_same=1`.
|CHK-OTP-SIG
|A single-rail flip of any field on either rail sets IRQ_STATUS.OTP_SIGINT, the bit stays set after the hook releases, a write of 1 clears it, and it reads 0 after the clear (`architecture.adoc`, "OTP Inputs and Readout": any violation raises OTP_SIGINT; `km_csr.adoc`, "IRQ_STATUS"). A DUT that does not set the bit, or that clears it when the force releases, fails. Stimulus: `HK-OTP`. Anchor: the IRQ_STATUS readback. Control: the two-rail flip of CHK-OTP-CHG on the same field does not set the bit, and the quiet window of boot 4 reads 0. Evidence: `CHK-OTP-SIG PASS field=sys_uid word=3 bit=17 rail=cpl sigint=1 w1c=0 held=1`, one line for each of the 18 points.
|CHK-OTP-CHG
|A two-rail flip (both rails flipped at the same bit, so the pair stays complementary) of a field sets that field's OTP_CHANGE_STATUS bit and IRQ_STATUS.OTP_CHANGE, sets no OTP_SIGINT, and a write of 1 clears both. In a window where 2 to 4 fields change together, each field's bit is set. The same holds well after the boot with both demotions held (`km_csr.adoc`, "IRQ_STATUS", "OTP_CHANGE_STATUS"; `architecture.adoc`, "OTP Inputs and Readout": a change of a monitored field value raises OTP_CHANGE). A DUT that sets OTP_SIGINT, misses a bit, or misses a field of the multi-field window fails. Stimulus: `HK-OTP`. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: a consistent flip does not raise OTP_SIGINT, and the single-rail flip of CHK-OTP-SIG on the same field does. Evidence: `CHK-OTP-CHG PASS field=class_key both_rail=1 sigint=0 change_bit=5 irq=1 multi_k=3 multi_bits_set=3`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The test does not claim the readout value before the fuse sense completes, the length of the suppression window, or the bit order and polarity of the two demotion groups (see <<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The ROM response to OTP_SIGINT (-13) belongs to `sep_km_fault_inject_rand_test`. The open test `sep_efuse_km_public_id_test` keeps the cells that it grades. The Phase 3 test `sep_efuse_secure_tm_matrix_rand_test` keeps the masking of the secret fields at the sensed-shadow output. The suppression after a cold reset is read in boot 5.

'''''

[[sep_km_key_bank_register_rand_test]]
===== 7.36 `sep_km_key_bank_register_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu` (`lsu_stub_all_live`), vehicle POKE with the ABR block present, `+skip_fuse_sense`, 3 seeds, Verilator and VCS. Testlist: groups `all` and `cpu_stub` in `hw/sys/sep/dv/testlists/km.toml`.

*Source Module*: `sep_km_key_bank_register_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: The POKE image reads and writes the key registers of the five wrappers through the KM bus, arms a counting handler on CPU IRQ bit 5, and reports each result in KM SRAM words 0 to 97. The host LSU master (ocah_axi_vip) writes the engine SW_RESET_N bits and runs the ML-KEM encapsulation on the Adams Bridge block through the host path, with the set-up of `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test` (encapsulation key, KV message-read enable, KV shared-key write enable). The expected values come from the register text: write-only share words read 0, and the key_valid set and clear rules. Approved: `KM-RESULT` (POKE readback), `HOST-MBX` (the engine SW_RESET_N bits and the Adams Bridge operation through the host), `KM-ISO` (the isolation probes of HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge). PROPOSED: none. Injects: none. Every check is a reached check.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed. The draws are: the 136 share values (non-zero, and the first share-width words of each bank pairwise different); the order of the eight banks in the key_valid walk; the order of the five engines in the reset leg; the enable timing (a drawn count in [0, 8] of idle POKE operations between the shared-key delivery and the write of the enable). Legal range: 32-bit non-zero words. `reseed = 3` samples different values, orders and idle counts. The bank set, the three encapsulations and the five engine resets are the same on every seed.

*Objective*: Every key-share word of the five key blocks is write-only and reads 0 on the KM bus. KEY_CTRL.key_valid resets to 0, a write of 1 makes the key valid and a write of 0 invalidates it. The ABR shared key uses 8 KEY words and a key_valid that hardware sets, firmware clears and software cannot set. The shared-key IRQ_STATUS.key_valid is sticky and write-one-to-clear, and the enabled interrupt drives KM CPU IRQ bit 5. Each engine wrapper, with its generated key register block, is reset by its own SW_RESET_N bit: that reset clears key_valid of the engine's banks and leaves the banks of the other engines unchanged. Spec: `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, sections "Key Delivery", "Adams Bridge Key Sideload Interfaces", "Key Manager CPU Interrupt Processing"; `hw/sys/sep/doc/reset_controller.adoc`, section "Isolation and Reset Sequencing"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/abr_wrapper_key.adoc`, sections "Adams Bridge Key Sideload Registers", "MLDSA_SEED.KEY_SHARE1[8]", "IRQ_ENABLE"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/aes_wrapper_key.adoc`, sections "AES Wrapper Key Registers", "KEY_SHARE0[8]", "KEY_CTRL"; `hw/sys/sep/regs/gen/adoc/blocks/sep_reset_ctrl.adoc`, section "SW_RESET_N".

*Test Coverage*: Extends `sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test`, the sideload KAT tests and `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`. `sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test` and the sideload KAT tests grade that the delivered shares reach the consumer. `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test` grades the shared-key consume through the mailbox. Those cells run here as controls. Only the new cells are graded: the KM-bus view of the key registers, which is the zero read of the share words, the key_valid rules, the software set refusal, the shared-key interrupt gate and the reset of each engine bank. The open owner keeps the rest.

* Non-zero writes to every share word of every bank (OTBN 2 x 12 words; AES, KMAC, HMAC and the four ABR seed groups 2 x 8 words, 136 words in all), then reads, with key_valid still 0.
* A key_valid walk over the eight banks in a drawn order.
* Three ML-KEM encapsulations that deliver the shared key: the delivery and its sticky status, the consume and a software set attempt, and the interrupt gate with the enable 0 and 1.
* Engine resets through their SW_RESET_N bits with all key_valid bits set, one engine per reset.
* A KM PARK with all key_valid bits set (logged only).

Error case: a KM bus write of key_valid = 1 to MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY_CTRL (a software set of a hardware-owned bit).

Functional coverage: `sep_km_secret_nonexposure_cg` (`cp_nx_share_read`), `sep_km_sideload_delivery_cg` (`cp_sd_key_valid_semantics`) and `sep_km_abr_shared_key_cg` (`cp_sk_delivery_state`, `cp_sk_write_dir`, `cp_sk_interrupt`).

*Test Procedure*:

[arabic]
. Print `SEED seed=<S>` and a `DRAW` line for every drawn value. Bring up `no_cpu` mode with fuse sense skipped. Release the KM with the POKE image. The engines and the ABR block are out of reset at their reset values. In this plan the bank vector has bit i for bank i of the order OTBN, AES, KMAC, HMAC, MLDSA_SEED, MLKEM_SEED_D, MLKEM_SEED_Z, MLKEM_MSG.
. The image reads KEY_CTRL of the eight banks (reset values) and reports the vector.
. The image writes the non-zero drawn value to every share word of every bank (136 words, in a drawn bank order), with key_valid at 0. It then reads every share word and reports it.
. Key_valid walk. In the drawn bank order, for each bank the image writes 1 to its KEY_CTRL.key_valid and reads the eight KEY_CTRL registers; writes 1 again and reads them; writes 0 to the same bank and reads them.
. Encapsulation 1. The image writes 1 to the IRQ_STATUS.key_valid of the shared key (clearing it), reads it as 0 (the control) and writes 0 to the IRQ_ENABLE. It stages the message: it writes the MLKEM_MSG share words and sets its key_valid. The host runs one ML-KEM encapsulation with the set-up of `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test`: the encapsulation key set up as in that test, the KV message-read enable set so that the message comes from the vault lane, and the KV shared-key write enable set so that Adams Bridge posts the shared key to the KM. A bounded wait until MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY_CTRL.key_valid reads 1; a timeout fails the leaf. The image reads KEY_CTRL, the 8 KEY words and IRQ_STATUS.key_valid twice. It writes 0 to IRQ_STATUS.key_valid and reads it; it writes 1 and reads it.
. Consume. The image writes 0 to MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY_CTRL and reads KEY_CTRL and the 8 KEY words. Error injection; point: KM bus write of key_valid = 1 to MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY_CTRL; kind: software set of a hardware-owned bit; expected response: no effect. The image writes 1 to MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY_CTRL and reads KEY_CTRL and the 8 KEY words.
. Encapsulation 2, the interrupt gate. The image writes 0 to IRQ_ENABLE.key_valid_en, arms a counting handler on CPU IRQ bit 5, stages the message and sets its key_valid. The host runs one encapsulation with the set-up of step 5 (encapsulation key, KV message-read enable, KV shared-key write enable). A bounded wait for MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY_CTRL.key_valid = 1. The image reads IRQ_STATUS.key_valid (1, set while masked), and reads the handler count before and after a quiet window (a test parameter; no key register access in it). After the drawn number of idle POKE operations the image writes 1 to IRQ_ENABLE.key_valid_en and reads the handler count before and after the same window. The image writes 1 to IRQ_STATUS.key_valid and reads the handler count before and after the same window. The image writes 0 to MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY_CTRL and 0 to IRQ_ENABLE.key_valid_en.
. Encapsulation 3. The image stages the message and the host runs one encapsulation with the set-up of step 5; a bounded wait for MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY_CTRL.key_valid = 1. With IRQ_ENABLE at 0 the image writes 1 to key_valid of the eight banks and reads the eight KEY_CTRL registers, the shared-key KEY_CTRL and IRQ_STATUS.key_valid. A setup that does not read 0xFF, 1 and 1 fails the leaf; it is not a verdict about the contract.
. Engine resets. For each of the five engines in the drawn order (OTBN bit 1, AES bit 2, HMAC bit 3, KMAC bit 4, Adams Bridge bit 6 of SW_RESET_N), the host writes SW_RESET_N with that bit low and the other bits at 1, holds it low until the engine's isolation probe reads isolated (`KM-ISO`, for HMAC, KMAC and Adams Bridge) or for a test-chosen interval (AES and OTBN), then writes the bit high. After the host signals, the image reads the eight KEY_CTRL registers, the shared-key KEY_CTRL, IRQ_STATUS.key_valid and the 8 KEY words, and reports them.
. PARK. The image writes 1 to key_valid of the eight banks and reads the vector (`before`). The host writes SW_RESET_N with bit 0 low and then high, and the other bits at 1. The POKE image boots again and reads the eight KEY_CTRL registers, the shared-key KEY_CTRL and IRQ_STATUS.key_valid, and reports them. The leaf logs `PARK-OBS before=0xFF after=0x<..> sk_valid=<b> sk_irq=<b>`. The effect of a KM PARK on the wrapper key registers and on the shared-key IRQ_STATUS is not stated, so this line is not graded.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-BANK-SHARES
|Every one of the 136 share words reads 0 after a non-zero write, with key_valid still 0 (`aes_wrapper_key.adoc`, "KEY_SHARE0[8]": write-only, reads return 0; `architecture.adoc`, "Key Delivery"). A DUT that returns the written value, or part of it, fails. The response of the read is logged and not graded. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: every read follows a non-zero write of that word in the same leaf, and the written values are non-zero, so a zero read is the register and not an unwritten bank. Evidence: `CHK-BANK-SHARES PASS words=136 nonzero_written=136 reads_zero=136`.
|CHK-BANK-VALID
|KEY_CTRL.key_valid reads 0 after reset in all eight banks. A write of 1 makes the bank key valid, a second write of 1 leaves it 1, a write of 0 clears it, and no other bank changes in any step (`architecture.adoc`, "Key Delivery"; `aes_wrapper_key.adoc`, "KEY_CTRL"). A DUT whose bit does not set or clear, or that moves another bank, fails. Anchor: the POKE readback of the eight KEY_CTRL registers after every step. Control: the write of 1 on a normal bank is the twin of the refused set on the shared key (CHK-BANK-SK-SW). Evidence: `CHK-BANK-VALID PASS banks=8 reset=0x00 set=1 set_twice=1 clear=0 isolated=1`.
|CHK-BANK-SK
|After the encapsulation the shared key is delivered: KEY_CTRL.key_valid reads 1. IRQ_STATUS.key_valid reads 1 on both reads (sticky), a write of 0 leaves it 1, and a write of 1 clears it (`abr_wrapper_key.adoc`, "MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY_CTRL", "IRQ_STATUS": hardware sets key_valid and loads the words; the status is sticky and write-one-to-clear). A DUT that delivers no key, drops the status between reads, clears it on a write of 0, or keeps it after a write of 1 fails. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: the status reads 0 before the delivery. The 8 KEY words are the FIPS 203 shared key of the encapsulation, for which the leaf has no golden, so they are logged (`OBS-SK-WORDS LOG`) and not graded. Evidence: `CHK-BANK-SK PASS valid=1 irq_sticky=1 zero_write=kept w1c=0`.
|CHK-BANK-SK-SW
|A write of 0 to MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY_CTRL clears key_valid and the 8 KEY words read 0. A software write of 1 afterwards leaves key_valid at 0 and the words at 0 (`abr_wrapper_key.adoc`, "MLKEM_SHARED_KEY.KEY_CTRL": write 0 to clear, write 1 has no effect; `architecture.adoc`, "Adams Bridge Key Sideload Interfaces": software can clear key_valid but cannot set it). A DUT that leaves the words after the consume, or lets software set the bit, fails. Anchor: the POKE readback. Control: the bit reads 1 before the consume (CHK-BANK-SK), the words before the consume are logged, and the write of 1 on a normal bank sets it (CHK-BANK-VALID). Evidence: `CHK-BANK-SK-SW PASS consume_zero=1 words_zero=1 sw_set_ignored=1`.
|CHK-BANK-SK-IRQ
|With IRQ_ENABLE.key_valid_en = 0 and the status set, the handler count does not rise over the window and the status reads 1. With the enable 1 the count rises. After a write of 1 to IRQ_STATUS.key_valid the count stops rising (`architecture.adoc`, "Adams Bridge Key Sideload Interfaces": the masked interrupt is IRQ_STATUS AND IRQ_ENABLE, on CPU IRQ bit 5; `abr_wrapper_key.adoc`, "IRQ_ENABLE"). A DUT that enters the handler with the enable 0, never enters it with the enable 1, clears the status while masked, or keeps the line after the clear fails. The second delivery after the clear of step 5 sets the status again. Anchor: the handler count and the POKE readback. Control: the same window length shows the rise with the enable 1, so a window that is too short for the masked case also fails the enabled case. Evidence: `CHK-BANK-SK-IRQ PASS en0_entries_rise=0 en1_entries_rise=1 status_set_masked=1 w1c_stops=1 idle_ops=3`.
|CHK-BANK-RST
|After a SW_RESET_N reset of one engine, key_valid of that engine's banks reads 0 (for Adams Bridge, the four seed banks, the shared-key key_valid and IRQ_STATUS.key_valid, and the shared-key words read 0), and every bank of another engine reads the value that it read before the reset (`reset_controller.adoc`, "Isolation and Reset Sequencing": each `SW_RESET_N` bit resets its own domain; the engine wrapper, with its key register block, is reset by that bit). A DUT that leaves a key_valid set after its engine reset, or clears a bank of another engine, fails. Anchor: the POKE readback of the eight KEY_CTRL registers, the shared-key KEY_CTRL and IRQ_STATUS.key_valid. Control: all eight banks and the shared key read set before the first reset, and each reset starts from the state that the previous one left, so a clear shows against a set bit. Evidence: `CHK-BANK-RST PASS eng=kmac before=0xFF after=0xFB others_kept=1`, one line for each engine, and `eng=abr ... sk_valid=0 sk_irq=0 sk_words_zero=1` for Adams Bridge.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The test does not claim the effect of a KM PARK on the wrapper key registers and the shared-key IRQ_STATUS (not stated; the leaf logs the state after the PARK and does not grade it), the read response of a key-share word, or the delivery of the shares to the consumer. The open tests `sep_km_sideload_share_walk_test`, the sideload KAT tests and `sep_km_abr_mlkem_sideload_test` keep the cells that they grade.

'''''
[[sep_km_sram_scrambler_key_rand_test]]
===== 7.37 `sep_km_sram_scrambler_key_rand_test`

*Phase*: 3

*Area*: Key Manager vault

*Run mode*: `no_cpu`, the KM CPU runs the scripted image IMG-SCR, `+skip_fuse_sense`, VCS with Verilator for the value checks, 3 seeds. Testlist: group `all` and group `cpu_stub`.

*Source Module*: `sep_km_sram_scrambler_key_rand_test`

*BFM / Model*: IMG-SCR, a KM image of the class of `km_rom_blob` with a parameter block (`HK-IMG-PARAM`) that holds the drawn configuration of the leaf. The prestage step (`hw/sys/sep/dv/cocotb/dv_sim_prestage.py`) stages the parameter block from the leaf seed, and the image load `tb_backdoor_mem` (`+km_rom_hex`) preloads the image into the KM ROM macro at t=0, before the KM leaves reset. After each release the host posts the phase of the boot through the KM inbound mailbox (`HOST-MBX`), so no bench write changes the image after t=0. The test uses these probes and preloads. No cipher model exists: every expected value is a parameter of the test, a register value from the register description, or an inequality that follows from the one-to-one stages of the scrambler description.

* Approved: `HOST-MBX` (the frames that IMG-SCR writes to the KM mailbox, read on the SEP side, and the SEP-side mailbox status), `KM-SRAM-MON` (`km_sram_scr_wr_count_o`, `km_sram_scr_wr_addr_o`, `km_sram_scr_wr_data_o` and `km_sram_scr_wr_cell_o`, the first four scrambled SRAM writes), `KM-COUNT` (`km_sram_write_count_o`, the count of accepted SRAM writes), `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (the `sep_reset_n` release after `rst_ni`; `SEP_TB_ARCH.adoc`, SEP reset probes).
* Preload (approved): `HK-IMG-PARAM`, the parameter block of IMG-SCR, preloaded with the image at t=0 (every checker).
* `KM-SRAM`, a read-only tap of the 8192-word SRAM array in stored form (CHK-SSCR-ROWS), is an observation tap the test adds to the SEP_TB_ARCH observation-probe list.
* Injects: none. The test performs no bench write to the SRAM array and relies on the power-up content of the array.

*Randomization*: `SepSeededRng` from the run seed: the keys kA and kB (non-zero, every byte of kA differs from the same byte of kB), 8 word addresses in [0x8400, 0xFFFC] (one adjacent pair, one pair at the same offset in two 1 KiB regions), 8 plaintexts (non-zero, one pair of equal plaintexts at two of the addresses, the pair stored first), 64 further distinct word addresses in the same range (disjoint from the 8) with 64 distinct non-zero words, and, for the lock-without-enable leg, 2 word addresses in the same range (disjoint from the others) with 2 non-zero words. `reseed = 3` samples different keys, addresses and words. The plan shape (the key sequence, the 8-word and 64-word sets and the boot sequence) is the same on every seed. The stream is not a CSPRNG, and no drawn key is used outside the simulation.

*Objective*: With the SRAM scrambler enabled, the stored form of a word depends on the key and on the address. The same plaintext at the same address stores a different word under another key, and the old key reads its data back after a key change. Two distinct logical addresses do not share a physical row, because the read path scrambles the address and does not descramble it. Once the lock is set with the enable at 1, a write of the key and a write of the enable are ignored, a write of 0 to the lock leaves it set, SCRAMBLER_KEY reads 0, and the scrambler stays on. A lock set with the enable at 0 forces the scrambler into the enabled state. The key, the enable, the lock and the SRAM contents survive a warm reset. A cold reset clears the enable and the lock, and a key write after it lands. The outbound mailbox FIFO holds 16 words and drops a word that is written to a full FIFO, so the image reports in groups that the host drains. Spec: `hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc`, sections "Address Tweaking", "Address Scrambling", "Integration in the Key Manager"; `hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, sections "Parameters", "Reset Conditioning", "Key Manager Control and Status Registers (KMCSR)", "ROM and SRAM Error Detection", "ROM Warm-Persistent SRAM Region", "Mailbox Transport"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc`, sections "SCRAMBLER_KEY", "SCRAMBLER_CTRL"; `hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_mailbox_km.adoc`, section "KM_STATUS".

*Test Coverage*: This test extends the open test `sep_km_mem_smoke_test`. That test grades the round trip under one key and the stored form that differs from the plaintext, including the array word at each kept row. Those cells run here as controls. Only the new cells are graded here, and the open owner keeps the rest. This test owns the SRAM scrambler lock.

* Key dependence: one plaintext stored at one address under kA and under kB; 8 words stored under kA, read under kA (control), under kB (logged), and under kA again.
* Address dependence: two addresses with the same plaintext store different words.
* One-to-one address map: 64 distinct words at 64 distinct logical addresses read back under kA, and 64 physical rows change.
* The lock: set with the enable at 1 in one write; then a key write, an enable write and a write of 0 to the lock; SCRAMBLER_KEY reads 0 while locked and reads the written value before the lock (control).
* The lock set with the enable at 0: in a later cold boot a store with the lock clear and the enable 0 is not a scrambled write (control), and the same kind of store after the lock is written with the enable 0 counts as a scrambled write.
* The reset domains: a warm reset with the lock set (enable, lock and the stored words kept) and, in a later boot, a cold reset (enable and lock cleared, a key write lands).

Error cases: the writes after the lock are legal accesses that the register description defines as ignored. The test has no error injection.

Functional coverage: `sep_km_memory_boot_cg` (`cp_mem_iface_req`, `cp_mem_scr_key_access`, `cp_mem_scr_ctrl`, `cp_km_scr_key`).

*Test Procedure*:

The image reports every value through the KM mailbox write register. It performs no SRAM store other than the drawn stores, because a scrambled store can reach any row of the array and a result store at a logical row could overwrite a stored word that is still in use. The image writes one message per group, with at most 16 words in a group, and the separator on the last word. After a group, the image waits with a bounded poll of KM_STATUS.outbound_depth until the depth is 0, so the outbound FIFO never holds more than one group. A timeout of the poll stops the image. The host reads each group through `HOST-MBX` when it appears, with a bounded wait; a timeout of the wait fails the checker that needs the group.

[arabic]
. Draw the configuration from the seed. The prestage step writes it into the parameter block of IMG-SCR (`HK-IMG-PARAM`), and the image load preloads the image at t=0.
. Release the KM. The host posts the phase word of boot 1 through the KM inbound mailbox (`SEP_WRITE_DATA` with the separator), and the image reads it with a bounded poll of the KM-side inbound status (a timeout stops the image). Every later boot reads its phase word the same way. Boot 1: the image writes kA to SCRAMBLER_KEY and sets SCRAMBLER_CTRL to enable 1 with the lock clear.
. The image stores the equal-plaintext pair P at its two addresses a1 and a2, writes kB to SCRAMBLER_KEY, stores P at a1 again, writes kA to SCRAMBLER_KEY, and then stores the 8 plaintexts to the 8 addresses in the drawn order, the pair among them, so that every row that a later kA read uses holds its kA store. The image performs no other SRAM write while the enable is 1, so the first four scrambled writes are the pair, the kB store and the first of the 8 stores. The host reads the four kept writes through `KM-SRAM-MON`.
. The image reads the 8 words under kA with consecutive loads and reports them (group of 8). The host drains the group.
. The image writes kB to SCRAMBLER_KEY, reads the 8 words and reports them (group of 8). The SRAM parity status that a read under kB sets is not graded. The image leaves IRQ_ENABLE at its reset value. The host drains the group.
. The image writes kA to SCRAMBLER_KEY, reads the 8 words and reports them (group of 8). The host drains the group.
. With the key at kA, the image clears the enable, reads SCRAMBLER_CTRL and the 8 words, reports them (group of 9), and sets the enable again. This is the control for the enable write after the lock. The host drains the group.
. The image reports a ready message of one word and waits until the host has read it. Before the host reads the ready message, it takes the first snapshot of the `KM-SRAM` tap and reads `KM-COUNT`.
. The image stores the 64 words to the 64 addresses with the enable at 1 and the key at kA. The image performs no other SRAM write. It then reports a done message of one word and waits until the host has read it. When the done message appears, and before the host reads it, the host takes the second snapshot of the `KM-SRAM` tap and reads `KM-COUNT` again.
. The image reads the 64 words under kA in 4 groups of 16 and reports each group. The host drains each group before the image writes the next one.
. Lock leg. The image writes kA to SCRAMBLER_KEY, reads SCRAMBLER_KEY back and reports the value (group of 1, control for the locked read), then writes SCRAMBLER_CTRL with enable 1 and lock 1 in one value. In boot 1 the image sets the lock with the enable at 1 only; boot 4 sets it with the enable at 0. The host drains the group.
. The image reads SCRAMBLER_CTRL and SCRAMBLER_KEY and reports both (group of 2). The host drains the group.
. The image writes kB to SCRAMBLER_KEY, reads the 8 words and reports them (group of 8). The host drains the group.
. The image writes SCRAMBLER_CTRL with enable 0 and lock 1, reads SCRAMBLER_CTRL, reads the 8 words and reports the SCRAMBLER_CTRL value and the 8 words (group of 9). The host drains the group.
. The image writes SCRAMBLER_CTRL with enable 1 and lock 0, reads SCRAMBLER_CTRL, reads the 8 words and reports the SCRAMBLER_CTRL value and the 8 words (group of 9). The host drains the group.
. The host confirms that it has read every group of boot 1. The mailbox is empty before the reset.
. Warm reset leg. With the lock set, the host writes `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 low (the other bits at 1). Then the host writes `SW_RESET_N` bit 0 high to release the KM and posts the phase word of boot 2. The image reads SCRAMBLER_CTRL, reads the 8 words under kA without any key write, and reports the SCRAMBLER_CTRL value and the 8 words (group of 9).
. The host reads the group of boot 2 from the mailbox.
. Cold reset leg. The host applies the SEP system cold reset (a pulse of `rst_ni`) and waits for the `sep_reset_n` release on `dbg_sep_reset_n_o` (a bounded wait; a timeout fails the leaf). `SW_RESET_N` is back at its reset value 0x7E, so the KM stays held (`sep_reset_ctrl.adoc`, "SW_RESET_N"). The host writes `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7F to release the KM and posts the phase word of boot 3. The image reads SCRAMBLER_CTRL, writes kB to SCRAMBLER_KEY, reads SCRAMBLER_KEY back, and reports the SCRAMBLER_CTRL value and the key value (group of 2).
. The host reads the group of boot 3 from the mailbox.
. Lock-without-enable leg. The host applies the SEP system cold reset and waits for the `sep_reset_n` release as in step 19. The host writes `SW_RESET_N` = 0x7F to release the KM and posts the phase word of boot 4. The image reports a ready message of one word and waits until the host has read it. Before the host reads it, the host reads `km_sram_scr_wr_count_o` (`KM-SRAM-MON`) as c0. The image writes kA to SCRAMBLER_KEY, stores the first drawn word with the scrambler off and the lock clear (the control), reports a message of one word and waits until the host has read it. Before the host reads it, the host reads the count as c1. The image writes SCRAMBLER_CTRL with enable 0 and lock 1 in one value, reads SCRAMBLER_CTRL, stores the second drawn word, and reports the SCRAMBLER_CTRL value and a done message (group of 2). Before the host reads the group, the host reads the count as c2.
. The host reads the group of boot 4 from the mailbox. The bench compares every reported value with the expected value and logs the checker lines.

*Checkers*:

[cols="2,5",options="header"]
|===
|Checker |Contract
|CHK-SSCR-KEY
|With the scrambler on, the key tweaks the data (`hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc`, "Data Scrambling Algorithm", "Address Tweaking", "Byte-Wise Scrambling Mode": the stored word is a fixed permutation of the S-box output of the data XOR the key XOR the expanded address, and each stage is one-to-one). The kept write of P at a1 under kB carries a different data word than the kept write of P at a1 under kA, because kA differs from kB and the same address gives the same expanded address; this holds for every key pair and every plaintext. The 8 words read under kA again, after the key returns to kA, equal the plaintexts. A scrambler that ignores the key stores the same word twice and fails the pair. A key write that damages the stored data, or a key register that does not return to kA, fails the second kA count. The 8 words read under kB are logged (`OBS-SSCR-KB LOG`) and not graded: a read under kB reaches the row that the kB address scramble selects, and neither perm13 nor the content of that row is stated. Control: the first kA reads. The open test `sep_km_mem_smoke_test` owns that cell, and a failed control means the read path itself is broken. The anchor is the DUT writes at the SRAM macro port (`KM-SRAM-MON`) and the frames of boot 1 read through `HOST-MBX`, compared with the plaintexts that the bench drew. Evidence: `CHK-SSCR-KEY PASS ka_ok=8(control) key_pair_ne=1 ka_again_ok=8`, and `OBS-SSCR-KB LOG kb_eq=<n>`. The lines carry counts and no per-seed value.
|CHK-SSCR-STORED
|The address tweaks the key, so the same data at different addresses has a different scrambled value (`hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc`, "Address Tweaking"). The first four scrambled writes are kept by `KM-SRAM-MON`, in the order that the image stores them. The two kept writes that carry the same plaintext at two different addresses carry different data. A scrambler without the address tweak stores the same word twice and fails the pair count. This pair count is the new cell. Control, owned by `sep_km_mem_smoke_test`: the array word at each kept row equals the kept data. Whether each kept data word differs from its plaintext is logged, because a one-round cipher can map a word to itself. The pair lies inside the first four stores because the monitor keeps no later write. The anchor is the DUT writes at the SRAM macro port and the array word. Evidence: `CHK-SSCR-STORED PASS array_eq=4(control) same_p_pair_ne=1`, and `OBS-SSCR-STORED LOG stored_ne=<n>`.
|CHK-SSCR-ALIAS
|The read path scrambles the address and does not descramble it (`hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc`, "Address Scrambling"), so a word returns only if its logical address reaches the row that the store used. Each of the 64 words, at a distinct logical address, reads back equal to the word that the image stored there under kA. Two logical addresses that share a physical row make the later store overwrite the earlier word, and the earlier address reads a different word. The words are distinct and non-zero, so an overwrite is visible. The image performs no other SRAM write, so no result store can overwrite a stored word. The anchor is the 4 groups of boot 1 read through `HOST-MBX`. Evidence: `CHK-SSCR-ALIAS PASS logical=64 readback_ok=64`.
|CHK-SSCR-LOCK
|Once the lock is set, the key register reads as zero, writes to it are ignored, and the scrambler is forced into the enabled state (`hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc`, "Integration in the Key Manager"). SCRAMBLER_CTRL.lock is write-once, and key and enable cannot be modified until reset (`hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc`, "SCRAMBLER_KEY", "SCRAMBLER_CTRL"). After the lock is set with the enable at 1, SCRAMBLER_KEY reads 0; the 8 words read after the write of kB equal the plaintexts under kA, so the key write is ignored; SCRAMBLER_CTRL reads 0x3 after the write of enable 0 and after the write of 0 to the lock, and the 8 words read after each of the two writes equal the plaintexts, so the scrambler stays on and the enable write is ignored. A key that lands shows another value in the reads. An enable that clears shows CTRL bit 0 at 0. A lock that clears shows CTRL below 0x3. Controls: SCRAMBLER_KEY read before the lock returns kA, so a read can show a non-zero key; the kB write before the lock changes the stored word (CHK-SSCR-KEY); SCRAMBLER_CTRL reads 0x0 after the enable is cleared before the lock, so the enable write has an effect before the lock, and the 8 reads with the enable clear are logged. The anchor is the KM mailbox readback of the registers and of the stored words under kA, read through `HOST-MBX`. Evidence: `CHK-SSCR-LOCK PASS key_rd_unlocked_ok=1(control) en0_ctrl=0x0(control) key_rd_locked=0 key_write_ignored=1 enable_write_ignored=1 lock_write0_kept=1 ctrl_rd=0x3 reads_ok=16`, where `ctrl_rd` is the value of every SCRAMBLER_CTRL read after the lock and `reads_ok` is the count of the 16 reads after the two writes that equal the plaintexts.
|CHK-SSCR-RST
|The SRAM scrambler key, enable and lock are cold-domain state and survive a warm reset, and SRAM contents survive a warm reset (`hw/ip/key_manager/doc/architecture.adoc`, "Key Manager Control and Status Registers (KMCSR)", "ROM Warm-Persistent SRAM Region"). A cold reset clears both domains, and the SCRAMBLER_CTRL fields have reset value 0 (`hw/ip/key_manager/regs/gen/adoc/km_csr.adoc`, "SCRAMBLER_CTRL"). After the warm reset with the lock set, SCRAMBLER_CTRL reads 0x3 and the 8 words read under kA, with no key write, equal the plaintexts. After the cold reset, SCRAMBLER_CTRL reads 0x0, and a write of kB to SCRAMBLER_KEY reads back as kB. A warm reset that clears the enable or the lock shows CTRL below 0x3. A warm reset that drops the key or the data fails the reads. A cold reset that keeps the lock ignores the key write and reads 0, and a cold reset that keeps the enable shows CTRL bit 0. Controls: the SCRAMBLER_CTRL value 0x3 before the reset is the non-zero record that CHK-SSCR-LOCK logs, and kB is non-zero, so a locked read of 0 differs from the written value. SCRAMBLER_KEY has no reset value, so the test reads it only after a write. The anchor is the groups of boot 2 and boot 3 read through `HOST-MBX`. Evidence: `CHK-SSCR-RST PASS warm_ctrl=0x3 warm_reads_ok=8 cold_ctrl=0x0 cold_key_write_lands=1`.
|CHK-SSCR-LOCK0
|A lock set with the enable at 0 forces the scrambler into the enabled state (`hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc`, "Integration in the Key Manager": once locked, the scrambler is forced into the enabled state). After the write of enable 0 and lock 1 the second store counts as one scrambled write: `km_sram_scr_wr_count_o` rises by 1 from c1 to c2. The store with the lock clear and the enable 0 does not count: c1 equals c0. A DUT that leaves the scrambler off after the lock fails the count. The value that SCRAMBLER_CTRL.enable reads after the write is logged and not graded, because the register text says that key and enable cannot be modified after the lock and no sentence says which value the enable bit reads (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). Anchor: `km_sram_scr_wr_count_o` (`KM-SRAM-MON`, approved). Control: the store before the lock does not count, so the count follows the enabled state. Evidence: `CHK-SSCR-LOCK0 PASS c1_minus_c0=0(control) c2_minus_c1=1 ctrl_rd_logged=0x<v>`.
|CHK-SSCR-ROWS
|The 64 stores at 64 distinct logical addresses touch no row other than their own (`hw/ip/scrambler/doc/architecture.adoc`, "Address Scrambling": the scrambled address selects the row). The first snapshot is taken before the ready message is read, and the second when the done message appears, so the 64 stores lie between them and no store marker is used. `KM-COUNT` shows that the count of accepted SRAM writes between the snapshots is 64, and at most 64 rows change, counted over the whole array. A scrambler that writes a second row for one store changes more rows. The number of changed rows is logged and is not required to be 64: a row whose new stored word and parity equal its old content does not change, and the old content is the power-up content, which is not stated. Two addresses that share a row are graded by CHK-SSCR-ALIAS. The anchor is the `KM-SRAM` tap in stored form and the `KM-COUNT` write count. Evidence: `CHK-SSCR-ROWS PASS logical=64 changed_rows_max=64 sram_writes=64`, and `OBS-SSCR-ROWS LOG changed_rows=<n>`.
|===

*Pass Criteria*: Every named checker PASSes. UVM_ERROR == 0.

*Scope limit*: The test extends `sep_km_mem_smoke_test`. Only the new cells are graded here: the open owner keeps the round trip under one key, the stored form that differs from the plaintext and the array word at each kept row, and those cells are controls. The byte permutation `perm8` of the KM SRAM and the address permutation `perm13` are not tabulated in the scrambler document, so the stored word and the physical row have no cipher golden (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The cipher golden is graded on the KPV path in `sep_km_kpv_rand_test`. The test grades a lock set with the enable at 0 by the scrambled-write count only: the value that SCRAMBLER_CTRL.enable reads after such a lock is not stated (<<sep-vplan-phase3-blocked,Blocked features>>). The SRAM parity status that a read under another key sets, the SRAM array contents after a cold reset and the SCRAMBLER_KEY value after a cold reset are not stated and are not graded. The number of SCRAMBLER_KEY writes in a boot, and the key provisioning order of the boot ROM, belong to `sep_km_boot_reset_rand_test`. The KPV scrambler belongs to `sep_km_kpv_rand_test`. The SCRAMBLER_KEY write and read with the lock clear belong to `sep_km_kmcsr_reset_rand_test`.
